Growth & Decay

by greysaspire

Summary

“So you’re fucking suing me? My company? Where the hell do you get off?” Agatha spits, her voice raising.
Rio’s expression doesn’t change. “I’m not the one suing you. The organization is.”
“You lead the organization,” Agatha fires back, standing up out of her chair to lean in closer, palms flat on her desk. “Drop the bureaucratic bullshit.”
Rio arches a brow. “It’s not personal.”
“Oh, please- everything with you is personal.”

OR

Agatha is forced into volunteering for Rio’s climate change organization to avoid a lawsuit... but will their past get in the way?

Chapter 1: Chapter One

“Madam Harkness, there’s a- uh- waiter here to see you?” Agatha’s assistant informs her, poking his head around the office door.

“A waiter?” Agatha sighs, lifting her head from the mountain of paperwork in front of her, fixing the teen with a sharp look, eyebrow raised in confusion, “Billy, I never know what the fuck you’re talking about.”

Billy visibly winces at her tone, looking around anxiously. “I’m sorry, Ma’am, I didn’t totally catch what he said when he came in, I was on my phone- not slacking off or anything, I was just checking my emails because the Wifi on my compu-”

He’s cut off by the silent raise of a hand from Agatha. “Billy. Stop rambling. Just send him in for God’s sake,” she groans, letting out a heavy sigh as she returns briefly to her work.

“Yes Ma’am,” Billy responds, eager smile plastered across his face as he ducks back behind the wall, running down the hallway. A few moments later, an old, stout man appears in the doorframe, knocking briefly on the wood trim before entering. Agatha looks up at the sound, placing her pen down as she pushes her glasses onto the top of her head.

“Agatha Harkness?” The man says, smoothing a hand down his grey suit as he approaches her desk.

Agatha studies him with narrow eyes. The universe had already conspired to ruin her morning. She spilled her coffee in the parking lot, ripped her sleeve on the door handle, and the vending machine stole her dollar. So, whoever this man is, she already hates him.

“Yes?” She says flatly. The man opens his briefcase and pulls out a stack of papers, placing them gently on the desk in front of her.

“You have been served.”

Agatha glares down at the papers. She takes a breath as her jaw tightens.

“I’m sorry,” she says through her teeth, “what?” The man clears his throat.

“You have been served,” he repeats, his voice level.

“I heard you the first fucking time,” Agatha snaps, snatching up the stack of papers and flipping through them, so fast she couldn’t catch a single word if she tried, “I mean served for what? What could I possibly be being sued for?”

“The lawsuit was filed by the Coven for Climate organization.” He explains, stepping back like he can physically sense her rage, “They’re claiming your company caused environmental pollution through negligence and is responsible for the resulting damage.”

Agatha drops her head into her hands, “Jesus fucking Christ,” She drags her face upward and fixes him with an exhausted glare. “What’s your name? Fred? Can I call you Fred?”

He hesitates. “It’s Steve actua-”

“Perfect. Fred.” She points a finger in his face. “Listen. I am having the single worst month of my goddamn life. So, if you’re trying to tell me that my company is being sued by some little cock-sucking environmental whores, I might just lose the last thread of sanity I have left.” Agatha yells, voice raising as she slams her hands onto her desk, pushing out of her chair. “Is that what you’re trying to tell me?”

Steve is silent for a minute, glancing around the room, unsure of how to respond. “…yes?” He eventually says, bracing himself for impact.

Agatha takes a deep, slow breath in, closing her eyes as she tries to recenter herself. She slowly picks up the papers and extends them towards him. “Take these and get out of my sight.”

“Legally, Miss Harkness, I can’t take them ba-” He begins to object.

“Then just get out!” Agatha snaps, slamming her hands onto the desk, “get out of my fucking office.” Steve turns and flees as fast as his legs can take him, grabbing his briefcase on the way out. The moment he disappears, Agatha throws the file across the room, sending pages flying through the air. She sinks into her chair, elbows on the desk, forehead burrowed in her palms. She stays like that long enough to consider whether faking her own death is a viable career move.

The door creaks open again.

“Are you okay, Ma’am?” Agatha’s eyes fly back up to see Billy poking his head around the door once again, worried look furrowing his brows.

Agatha stares at him from across the room. “…That was not a fucking waiter, Billy.” She seethes.

“Who was it?” He replies, innocently, stepping fully into the doorway.

“A process server. We’re being sued.”

“Ohh, see that makes a lot more sense, because I when I heard ‘serve’ I just assumed he meant like, serve you food or-” His mouth slams shut when he registers Agatha’s fed-up stare. “Right. Shutting up. Sorry.”

“It’s fine, Billy,” she sighs, waving him away, “I just… need a minute.” She whispers. Despite him being a complete moron, she’s always had a soft spot for the boy. It’s why she hired him in the first place- she just felt an instant connection. Billy slips out, closing the door behind him.

Agatha eventually stands, moving around the office to gather the scattered lawsuit pages in her arms. She drops back into her chair with everything in order, starts her computer, and begins typing the name printed at the top of the file.

Coven for Climate.

The website loads. She skims their bland mission statement, their membership info, the donation options.

Then she scrolls onto the “Our Team” page, a group photo filling her screen, fake-ass smiles plastered on all the volunteers.

Her eyes catch on a familiar face amongst the members.

Her breath catches as her stomach drops.

Scanning the list of names to confirm her suspicions, her gaze lingers on three words.

Rio Vidal (President).

“Fuck,” Agatha mutters under her breath, anger building. She reaches over to grab the phone on her desk, pulling it up to her ear as she punches in the numbers to Billy’s extension. She angles her head sideways, balancing the phone between her ear and shoulder, as she uses her free hands to pick up the suit papers. Billy picks up the line.

“What’s up, boss?” His chirpy voice crackles through the phone into Agatha’s ear.

“I need you to set me up a meeting. ASAP.” She ordered as she flips through the stack, scanning for any useful information. She hears Billy searching for a notepad and pencil on the other end.

“You got it,” he mumbles, “who with?”

“Rio Vidal.”

Just saying her name makes her want to throw up. The sound of Billy’s pencil scratching the paper tells Agatha he’s on the task, and she slams the phone down without a goodbye.

For the next couple of hours, Agatha forces herself to read through the paperwork- actually read it this time, not just skim it like before. Every page is a new reason for her blood pressure to skyrocket. Legal jargon, environmental reports, satellite photos, statements from people she’s never met… she feels like she’s drowning in the pages and pages of information that threaten to destroy her company, her reputation.

She drags a hand through her hair for what must be the fifteenth time, adjusting her glasses up and down her nose with no solution. The clock ticks obnoxiously loud in the corner, taunting her.

Billy pops in at one point, shortly after the call, to announce with a grin, “I got the appointment scheduled, Ma’am. She’ll be here this afternoon.” He scurries away before she can snap at him.

She.
As in Rio Vidal.
The name echoes in Agatha’s mind, clouding her thoughts.

The moment Billy leaves, Agatha’s heart picks up its pace, thudding against her ribs.

He spoke to her. To Rio. Her voice was on the other end of that line. Her beautiful, warm, intoxicating, irritating voice. Her palms are sweating, which she hates. Agatha Harkness doesn’t get nervous. She intimidates other people until they’re nervous, not the other way around.

But every few minutes- okay, every ten seconds- she glances up at her office door. Each creak of the floorboards outside, each murmur of voices, each shuffle of footsteps sends shivers up her spine as she awaits Rio’s arrival, which could literally be any second now.

Agatha tries to refocus on the paperwork, but the words blur. She digs her nails into the palm of her hand, and then digs harder, until she eventually looks down at the crescent shaped indents in her skin and forces herself to stop.

She exhales through her nose. Tries to think about pollution metrics instead of the last time she saw Rio twenty years ago.

There’s another sound outside the door.

Agatha jolts her head up so fast she almost strains her neck. She’s not there. Not yet.

But her pulse won’t slow.

Why was she so nervous?

It’s just a goddamn girl.

No need to get all worked up.

Yet she finds herself picking up her phone and swiping to open her camera, wiping away some stray mascara from under her eyes and adjusting her messy waves. She silently curses herself for not straightening it this morning when she hears it- the soft, familiar click of boots approaching down the hallway.

Her spine stiffens.

Billy’s voice pipes up outside the door, “Hi! You must be- um- she’s inside there-”

Agatha makes a mental note to work with Billy on his greetings.

And then that voice cuts through the hall. The voice that immediately transports Agatha back to their college days.

“Thank you,” Rio says. Just two simple words, but Agatha feels it in her chest, in her pulse.

The doorknob turns.

Agatha swallows, sitting up straighter as she forces a blank, bored expression onto her face, trying to mask her nerves.

The door swings open.

Rio Vidal steps into the office like she owns it. Her light wash jeans are cuffed around her doc martens, and a black, worn-looking tank top hugs her curves in all the right places, causing Agatha’s glance to linger on her torso. Her dark hair is pulled back into a short ponytail, curtain bangs framing her bare face. Rio’s eyes land on Agatha instantly as she smirks.

“Agatha,” Rio says, her voice low and husk, curling around the name like she’s tasting it again after all these years.

Agatha feels her stomach flip.

“Rio,” she replies coolly, “come in.” She crosses her legs and leans back, nodding toward the chair across from her in a deliberate show of control.

Rio closes the door behind her, slowly.

She walks forward, her shoes tapping on the floor. Instead of sitting, she stops at the edge of Agatha’s desk.

“You look good,” Rio murmurs, eyes sliding over Agatha as her tongue presses against the inside of her cheek.

Agatha scoffs, crossing her arms tight over her chest with a frown. “You look old.” The lie is unconvincing as Rio chuckles, looking down as she shakes her head.

“Still terrible at bluffing.”

Agatha feels heat crawl up the back of her neck and immediately hates herself for it.

“Why am I here, Agatha?” Rio lifts her gaze up to meet her eyeline.

Agatha slides the stack of lawsuit files across the desk with her fingers.

“You know why.” She glances up at Rio harshly, waiting impatiently for an answer. An explanation. Anything.

Rio only tilts her head, shrugging lightly. “Your facilities are causing environmental damage.”

“So you’re fucking suing me? My company? Where the hell do you get off?” Agatha spits, her voice raising.

Rio’s expression doesn’t change. “I’m not the one suing you. The organization is.”

“You lead the organization,” Agatha fires back, standing up out of her chair to lean in closer, palms flat on her desk. “Drop the bureaucratic bullshit.”

Rio arches a brow. “It’s not personal.”

“Oh, please- everything with you is personal.” The dig just flows out of her mouth, too easily falling back into the routine of their old banter.

For a moment, Rio’s jaw tenses. She steps closer, placing one hand on the desk, while maintaining eye contact. Agatha senses Rio’s hand mere inches from hers, not quite close enough to brush. “You’re endangering a watershed, Agatha. People live downstream. Kids. Families. Not the mention the hundreds of species whose water supply you’re also contaminating. This isn’t about you.”

Agatha’s mouth opens, then closes, her anger building. She hates that Rio still talks to her like she knows better, like she’s fucking superior. In that condescending fucking tone.

After a beat, Agatha finds her voice. “If you’re trying to intimidate me, it’s not going to work.”

Rio leans in just slightly. “Who said I was trying to intimidate you?” She whispers.

Agatha’s throat goes dry. She forces a scoff. “You haven’t changed.”

Rio’s smile is small, a flicker of enjoyment behind her eyes. “And you have?”

Agatha looks away, inhaling as she tries to gather an answer. She needs something controlled, something that will reestablish the correct balance of power in this room. She’s about to open her mouth to utter a half-wit response when she notices Rio’s gaze shift downward.

At first it’s subtle, a slight flicker of her eyes that Agatha wouldn’t have caught if she wasn’t painfully aware of all her movements.

But then her stare settles onto Agatha’s left hand resting near the files.
On the diamond rock sitting atop her ring finger.

Rio’s smirk falters, just for a second- a moment of genuine surprise she fails to hide quickly enough.

Agatha sees all of it.

Rio inhales slowly, regaining her composure before talking. When she finally speaks, her voice comes out soft.

“You’re married?”

She says in disbelief, leaning closer, tilting her head to inspect the ring with narrow eyes.

Agatha swallows hard, throat suddenly dry. “Yeah.”

Rio’s eyes stay on the ring. “What’s her name?”

Agatha freezes. She didn’t rehearse this moment. It hadn’t even crossed her mind that this would come up.

“His name is—”

“HIS?”

The shriek that Rio lets out might as well have rattled the picture frames on the wall, much too loud for an office space either way. Agatha physically jumps, chair jerking behind her as her hand flies to her chest.

“Jesus Christ, Rio!” she hisses, glancing toward the door, fully expecting Billy to come running in asking for the ‘tea’ again… whatever that means. “Would you keep it down?”

Rio ignores her entirely, stepping closer, hands braced on the desk, face twisted in disbelief. “What the fuck do you mean his?”

“I mean,” Agatha says sharply, “my husband’s name is Ralph.”

Rio’s expression twists the moment the name hits her, a flicker of recognition tightening her jaw. Disgust and anger creep over her face before she masks them, leaning back with her arms crossed as she forces herself to push past Agatha’s choice in men.

“What happened to girls?”

Agatha’s pulse spikes with irritation.

“I left that part of my life behind when all my hopes and dreams were crushed,” she snaps, eyes blazing, “thanks to you.”

Rio’s breath hitches- she wasn’t expecting that. Her posture falters for the first time since she walked into the room.

“Agatha, I—”

“No.” Agatha raises a hand sharply, silencing what was most likely going to be Rio’s half-assed apology. “Don’t you dare do that.”

“Do what?” Rio asks quietly.

“Whatever that pitying, ‘I wish things had gone differently,’ bullshit tone is. Just don’t.”

Rio’s nostrils flare just slightly, wanting to get defensive, but she doesn’t back up. Instead, she takes a deep breath and steps around the side of the desk to where Agatha is still standing. Not close enough to touch her, but close enough that Agatha can feel the warmth radiating from her body.

Rio’s voice drops low, her words barely a whisper. “You really married him?” She asks, tone laced with more sadness and pity than she expected.

Agatha doesn’t answer. The words are stuck at the back of her throat, not wanting to come forward. She gives a slight nod instead.

Rio watches her intently.

“And are you…” Rio hesitates for a moment, considering her choice of words, “…happy?”

Agatha’s stomach twists. She looks down at the floor, not having the guts to meet Rio’s harsh gaze, while she grabs the edge of the desk to keep her hands steady.

“Yes,” she sighs, “I’m happy.”

Rio’s eyes flicker with a mix of emotions- skepticism, hurt, shock- before she masks them with a slow exhale through her lips.

“I know you well enough to know when you’re lying’” Rio murmurs, “you’re not happy.” Her gaze drops to the ring again. “Not with him.”

Agatha feels her defenses snap into place. “You don’t know anything about my marriage.”

Rio’s eyebrows lift, questioning. “Don’t I?”

Agatha’s jaw clenches. Her heart is beating too fast. She feels uncomfortably exposed, like Rio is peeling back layers she hasn’t let anyone touch in years.

Finally, Agatha forces herself to sit, because her knees feel unsteady and she’ll be damned before she lets Rio see it. She flips quickly through the files, trying to shift the conversation away from any feelings.

“We’re here to talk about the lawsuit,” she states, flatly. “Not my past. Not my marriage. Not you.”

The air between them thickens. Rio’s silent for a moment, considering, before she moves back to the other side of the desk and lowers herself into the chair. She crosses one leg over the other, leaning back comfortably.

“Alright,” Rio says, voice steady as she folds her hands in her lap. “What did you want to discuss?”

“I want you to drop the suit.”

Rio audibly laughs. “Not going to happen.”

“Rio, look.” Agatha exhales hard, trying to steady herself, “We’re already taking heat from the media about our environmental… issues. Sales are tanking, Billy says we’ve been cancelling?”

“Cancelled.”

“Same thing.” Agatha waves her off, “my point is… I’m already juggling a lot here, my company is basically falling apart underneath me. I can’t handle a lawsuit on top of it. Please.”

Rio bites the inside of her cheek, studying her. The gloss of tears in Agatha’s eyes. The panic tightening her voice. The exhaustion apparent in her shoulders. Something in Rio’s chest cracks, just barely.

“Okay, Agatha…” she sighs, leaning forward slightly. “Here’s the deal. I’ll drop the lawsuit-”

“Oh, thank you!” Agatha breathes, relief flooding her face.

“I’m not done.” Rio’s tone snaps. “You’re going to overhaul your manufacturing so it stops harming the environment. Immediately. And your company will make monthly donations to Coven for Climate to help repair the damage you’ve already done.”

“Okay, deal-” Agatha starts, but Rio raises a finger to silence her.

“And,” she continues, “you, Agatha Harkness, will personally volunteer with us. Every week.”

“Absolutely fucking not.”

“You said it yourself- you’re being cancelled.” Rio shrugs lightly. “What better redemption arc than having your CEO out in the field doing the dirty work? The media will eat it up.”

Agatha hesitates. “…It would be good for publicity.”

“Exactly. And honestly, sweetheart, you don’t have much of a choice. It’s this or the lawsuit.”

Agatha’s jaw clenches. She hates not being in control, but she knows Rio is right. This is really her only option. After a long pause, she groans into her palms.

“Fine.”

A slow smile curls at Rio’s lips.

“Perfect.” She stands up out of her chair and extends a hand across the desk. “Welcome to the team, Mrs. Harkness.”

Agatha bats her hand away, crossing her arms like a sulking child. “You can go now.”

Rio tilts her head, examining Agatha’s annoyance and defeat, before turning and walking towards the door.

“Te veo,” she says, pulling the handle shut behind her, leaving Agatha alone in her office once again as silence fills the room.

She glances at the lawsuit files on her desk, no longer intimidating, a heavy weight now lifted off her shoulders.

And the ring on Agatha’s finger feels like it gained ten pounds.

Chapter 2: Chapter Two

Chapter Notes

here's chapter two cause i was in flow state last night. this one's pretty happy/silly so enjoy!

See the end of the chapter for more notes

2005

Rio wakes to what sounds like someone trying to break down her door. Three heavy bangs, then a brief pause, then two more. Groaning, she rolls out of her lofted bed, nearly tripping over her own blanket as she grabs a pair of pajama pants and shoves her legs through them. She yanks the door open with a scowl.

Agatha stands there in the bright hallway light, bundled up in a purple Northwestern hoodie, messy curls falling down her face.

“Agatha?” Rio croaks, still half asleep, “It’s two in the morning, what are you doing here?”

Agatha folds her arms across her chest, “my roommate’s being a bitch again.”

Rio groans heavily, head thunking against the doorframe. “What is it this time?”

“She’s a fucking mess is what it is,” Agatha huffs “there are so many clothes on the floor that I can’t even see the carpet. And she refuses to clean it up. Says it’s her side of the room, so it shouldn’t matter to me.”

“Can’t this wait until the morning?” Rio sighs, rubbing her eyes with the back of her hand.

“No.” Agatha responds immediately, “I can’t sleep in these conditions.”

Rio gives her the flattest, most exhausted stare she can muster.

Agatha shrugs. “This is literally your job, Rio. Come on, help me out here. Or I’ll report you to the RA board.”

“There is no RA board.”

“I’ll make one. Just to file my complaint.”

Rio groans loudly. “Fine, God. Let’s get this over with.”

She grabs her keys as Agatha’s face lights up, her fake scowl melting into a wide grin.

“Thanks, Rio.” She loops her arm through Rio’s as they walk down the hallway to Agatha’s dorm, “you’re the best RA I could ask for” she adds dramatically, clearly hoping flattery will soften the 2AM wake up call.

Rio rolls her eyes, “fucking freshmen,” she mutters under her breath.

“What was that?” Agatha asks genuinely as they reach her dorm, fumbling around for her keys.

“Nothing,” Rio responds with a tight smile, pushing open the door.

The moment they step in, the chaos jumps out at her- clothes everywhere, some clean, some definitely not, used silverware sprawled across the desk, and an interesting smell that Rio doesn’t want to try and pinpoint the source of.

“Natasha.” Rio says as she approaches the redhead sprawled across her bed, “I heard there’s another argument.”

Natasha snaps her gaze toward Agatha. “Seriously? You tattled to your little girlfriend again?”

Agatha inhales sharply, ready to send an insult flying back, before Rio cuts in.

“Okay, shut that down right now. I’m not her girlfriend. I’m her RA. And I’m trying, despite my better judgment, to help you both survive sharing eight square feet.”

Natsha rolls her eyes dramatically. “My clothes are on my side of the dorm. I don’t know why she’s freaking out.”

Rio gestures broadly at the disaster zone. “Okay, I don’t know if you passed geometry, but this is… much more than half. And even if it weren’t, it’s still a shared space, and you need to maintain some level of tidiness. It’s a respect thing.”

“Surpise, surprise,” Natasha snaps, “you’re taking her side yet again. You’re biased.”

Rio pinches the bridge of her nose. “It’s not my fault Agatha’s always in the right, Natasha.” Rio sighs, gesturing to the piles of clothing. “Please. Just clean up your mess. That’s all we’re asking.”

Natasha mutters something under her breath but climbs out of bed anyway, starting to pick up clothes with loud, dramatic sighs after each item.

Rio turns back to Agatha, “Are we all good here?”

Agatha gives her a small, relieved smile. “We’re good. Thanks, Rio.”

For a moment, Agatha looks like she might hug her. Her hands twitch like she’s fighting the impulse. It makes Rio’s stomach flip in a way she chooses to ignore for the time being.

Instead, Agatha steps aside, letting Rio move toward the door. “Sorry for waking you up.”

Rio just turns, giving a small smile in response, and silently shuts the door behind her.

2025

Wednesday

Agatha pulls up her email on her work computer, checking for new messages one more time before heading home. She sighs when she sees five unread messages. This was fucking exhausting. Her eyes land on the most recent one.

From: Vidal, Rio

Subject: Volunteer Info

She groans but clicks in open anyway, skimming briefly over the bullet points.

Saturdays, 9AM… forms to fill out… the standard, boring nonsense…

Trying not to fall asleep in the process, she reads the end of the message.

Let me know what shirt size you want for Saturday. I’m assuming you’re still a medium unless you’ve suddenly embraced the oversized look.
– Rio

Agatha feels her jaw clench. The certainty in Rio’s tone. The casual I know you. Someone seriously needed to take her down a peg.

Medium, Agatha types reluctantly, stabbing each key a little harder than necessary before clicking send. Without giving herself time to rethink it, she powers down her computer. She shoves every thought of Rio, and volunteering, out of her head as she heads for the door, refusing to let either follow her home.

Saturday

Agatha stares out the passenger window, watching the bright May trees smear into green streaks as the car slows into the park’s lot. Her heartrate picks up the closer they get, and the sight of dozens of volunteers already bustling around makes her groan. She tugs nervously at the hem of her shirt- Coven for Climate stretched across her chest, complete with a painfully cheerful cartoon Earth grinning up at her. Subtle.

Ralph parks at the far end and hops out before she can unbuckle. Agatha takes a steadying breath, steps out onto the asphalt, and jogs briefly to catch up with him.

“Thanks for coming with me,” she says, trying to sound casual even as her fingers nervously twist together.

“I wanted to get out of the house anyway,” he responds, picking up his pace slightly, keys jingling as he locks the car.

Agatha’s eyes stay glued to the ground while she tries to calm the pounding in her chest. She isn’t sure what part is making her the most anxious-Rio, the crowd, the new environment… probably all of the above.

When she finally forces herself to look up, her gaze catches on Rio standing by the volunteer tent, addressing a small circle of eager listeners. Rio’s team shirt is knotted at her waist, revealing toned abs and a silver belly piercing that catches the early sunlight. Her flared jeans sit impossibly low on her hips. Agatha swallows.

Rio’s eyes flick toward her- then sharpen when she registers Ralph beside her too. A flash of shock and slight disgust crosses her face before she smooths it over with a professional smile, turning her attention back to the group.

By the time Agatha and Ralph reach the crowd of volunteers, the group is already dispersing- some heading to the riverbank, others toward the trail, a few lingering near the tent. Rio crosses the space toward the couple, fake smile plastered across her face.

“Rio,” Agatha says, greeting her with a polite nod. “This is my husband, Ralph. You guys remember each other right?”

“Unfortunately,” Rio mutters under her breath- quiet enough for Ralph to miss but nowhere near quiet enough for Agatha not to hear. Agatha shoots her a sharp warning look. Rio straightens, flashing Ralph a wide grin, who’s eyebrows are currently furrowed in confusion.

“Not really, nah,” he says honestly, shrugging, “where do we know you from?”

Rio scoffs. “College,” she answers at the same time as Agatha.

“Ohhh that makes sense then,” Ralph snorts, “Yeah, I wasn’t sober enough to remember most of college.”

Agatha offers a thin, polite chuckle in response as Rio doesn’t even try to hide her judgement. She claps her hands together, glancing back and forth between the two of them, impatient.

“Great. Well- thanks for dropping her off, Ralph,” she pivots toward Agatha. “You can come with me and I’ll give you the introductory spiel.”

She starts to turn toward away, but Agatha speaks before she can take a step.

“Actually, Ralph is staying to help out.”

Rio stops dead, turning back with a scowl on her face. “What?”

“He’s going to volunteer.” Agatha says evenly.

“He wasn’t invited.”

“It’s volunteering,” Agatha counters, “you don’t need an invitation.”

“He doesn’t have a shirt.”

“So what?” Agatha lifts a brow. “You’re really going to turn away someone who wants to save the planet because they’re not wearing a tacky, matching tee?”

Rio frowns- very much taking the shirt insult personally.

“Fine,” she snaps. “He can stay.”

She whirls around and marches back to the tent, snatching a stack of flyers like they personally offended her. Agatha and Ralph follow, the tension trailing behind them.

“Okay,” Rio begins, still facing the table as she straightens a stack of flyers, “you’ve got three options: river cleanup, trail cleanup, or publicity.” Her tone is clipped, professional, but the annoyance bleeds through. She finally turns, finding Agatha and Ralph standing there like two people already regretting all their life choices.

Rio nods toward a heap of bright yellow dry suits piled beside the tent. “If you pick river duty, you’ll need one of those. They’re heavy, they’re ugly, and yes- you will get wet. You’ll be in the water pulling out trash, fishing through reeds, the whole deal.”

Agatha’s face falls dramatically.

“Or,” Rio continues, “trail detail. That’s walking the park, collecting anything left behind, including plastic buried under leaves or stuck in the dirt. You’ll need to look closely, be thorough-”

Her explanation is cut off by Agatha’s loud scoff.

“Yeah, absolutely not. I don’t do dirt.”

Rio stops. Blinks. Stares at Agatha.

“You don’t do dirt,” she repeats, like she’s trying to process a foreign language.

Agatha just shrugs. “Correct.”

Rio exhales sharply through her nose, rolling her eyes. “Okay, princesa. Last option: publicity. You stand here with me and hand out flyers to passersby, talk about the organization, yada yada.”

Agatha groans under her breath. None of these options sound appealing, but two of them involve mud, bugs, or water, so the decision is painfully obvious.

“I’ll stay here,” she says, crossing her arms tightly over her chest.

“Perfect,” Rio answers- too quickly, too excited. Then she pivots toward Ralph, enthusiasm evaporating. “Ralph?”

“I’ll stay here too,” he mutters, looking like he’d rather be anywhere else on the planet.

Rio nods stiffly. She splits the flyers into three uneven piles, handing stacks to both Agatha and Ralph, keeping the smallest for herself.

“Alright,” she says, slipping back into her clipped leader voice, “your job is simple. Stop anyone walking by, give them a flyer, and try to start a conversation about what we do. If you can, encourage them to donate or consider joining the volunteer team.”

Agatha nods stiffly at the instructions and peels away from the tent- anything to get out of Rio’s direct presence. She stations herself a few yards away, clutching the flyers tightly in her arms. As people stroll by, she calls out with a painfully forced greeting, extending flyers that are mostly ignored. Some avert their eyes, some shake their heads. A few don’t even slow down.

Within minutes her shoulders ache, her ankles roll inward, and the guilt of bothering innocent people weighs heavy on her chest. She manages to rope a couple into brief conversations, but each one ends the same way- a polite, desperate “sorry” before they speed-walk away, wanting to get out of there as quick as they can.

By the time she exhales in defeat, she feels Rio’s presence heavy behind her.

“What?” Agatha snaps, whirling around, her patience worn to threads.

“Oh, nothing,” Rio says casually, tilting her head with exaggerated innocence. “Just observing.”

“Observing what?”

“How you’re presenting yourself.”

Agatha’s jaw clenches. “You think I can’t do my job?”

“Based on the number of flyers still in your hands? No, I don’t.”

Agatha’s nostrils flare. “Fuck you. Watch this.”

She spins toward a blonde woman passing by with a small dog, timed perfectly as they draw near.

“Excuse me, ma’am,” Agatha calls out, voice sharp.

The woman pauses and removes an AirPod. “Me?”

“Yes, you.” Agatha deadpans. “Would you like a flyer? We’re, you know, helping the earth heal and whatnot.”

“Uhh.. no, I’m good. Thanks.” She immediately puts her headphone back in as she scurries quickly away, trying to escape a continued conversation.

Agatha turns at the sound of Rio cackling behind her, head flung backwards. Heat floods Agatha’s face. Her pulse spikes, anger twisting up her spine.

“Can I give you some pointers?” Rio asks, strolling toward Agatha, much too close for comfort. Heat crawls up Agatha’s spine, her pulse increasing dramatically.

“Do I have a choice?” she mutters.

Rio pretends to think about it, tilting her head. “No,” she answers with a smug smile.

Agatha rolls her eyes. Of course.

“First,” Rio begins, “fix your posture. You look like Quasimodo.”

“Who?” Agatha snaps, brow raised.

“The hunchback? Of Notre Dame?”

“Never seen it.”

Rio exhales dramatically. “Why does that not surprise me? You’ve always been so uncultured.”

There it is again- Rio acting like she knows everything about her. Agatha opens her mouth, ready to fire back, when she suddenly feels Rio’s hands on her shoulders. She jumps.

Rio steps behind her, gently tugging her shoulders back, straightening her spine. “Like this,” she murmurs.

Her voice is soft. Her touch is even softer. Agatha’s stomach flips violently- twenty years, and her body still remembers the feel of her hands on her. She jerks forward, knocking Rio’s arms away.

“I get it. Posture. Done. What else?” she snaps, obviously flustered and trying to hide it.

Rio sighs. “You need to be polite. Enthusiastic. Specific.”

“So… change everything about myself.”

Rio shrugs, smirk spreading. “Exactly.”

A young man approaches, nose buried in his phone.

“Okay, just, watch and learn.” Rio says, brushing past Agatha, their arms grazing, sending shivers down Agatha’s spine. “Sir?”

The man glances up and immediately stops, smiling at Rio a little too eagerly.

“So sorry to bother you,” she says sweetly, “but do you have a few minutes to spare? It’s for a good cause.”

“Yeah, totally,” he says, sliding his phone away, eyes glued to her.

“We’re an organization called Coven for Climate,” Rio continues, offering a flyer. “We fund cleanups, renewable energy projects, species protection- you name it. There’s info on the flyer, and we host volunteer days every Saturday for the local community. I’d love if you’d consider donating or joining us sometime.”

The man barely blinks, clearly hypnotized by Rio and absolutely none of the details. “Yeah… yeah, I’ll think about it,” he says, pocketing the flyer and giving her one last stare before walking off.

“Thanks for your time!” Rio calls after him, bright and eager.

She turns back to Agatha, arms thrown open in a ta-da pose.

“See? Not that hard, right?”

“That wasn’t fair,” Agatha grumbles, arms crossed. “He fancied you.”

“Because I was polite,” Rio says pointedly, “and I smiled. And I was direct.”

Agatha scoffs. “Please. It’s because you’re pretty. Anyone in their right mind would stop to hear you rant about the fuck-ass planet.”

The words tumble out before she can stop them. She freezes. So does Rio- her lips part, then curl into a slow, delighted smirk.

“No- wait, I meant-”

Agatha’s flustered backpedaling is cut short by Ralph’s dramatic groan from across the tent. Flyers explode from his hands, fluttering everywhere as the scared woman he was talking to hurries away from the scene.

He storms toward them, face twisted. “Agatha, I’m leaving. I’m not doing this stupid fucking bullshit.”

“Aww, did someone reject your flyer, Ralph?” Rio taunts, lips pushing into a mocking pout.

“You shut up,” he growls, jabbing a finger toward her unbothered face.

Agatha steps between them instantly, pushing his hand down. “It’s fine, Ralph. You can go. I’m okay.”

He grunts in response, grabbing the keys out of his pocket. “I’ll pick you up later. Don’t be too long.” He tells her, leaning in. Agatha gives him a quick kiss, patting his arm.

Rio fights the urge to visibly gag.

“Well,” Rio says once he’s out of earshot, “at least you’re doing better than he is.”

A few grueling hours crawl by, and somehow Agatha manages to unload a handful of her flyers. Not because she’s suddenly found motivation, but because sheer stubbornness keeps her from letting Rio be right. After each successful interaction, she glances over at Rio, wanting to rub it in her face how amazing she is at this task. Rio’s never looking, distracted by her own flyers, which pisses Agatha off even more. By the time the call for lunch echoes across the park, Agatha is hot, sweaty, flustered, and convinced her feet are physically disintegrating into the ground beneath her. How is she supposed to do this all day? Every weekend?

She trudges toward the cluster of picnic benches where the other volunteers have already begun forming a sluggish line, paper plates in hand, waiting their turn for the array of food set out under a separate tent. Agatha joins the back of the line with a dramatic sigh, grabbing a plate and only now realizing that her stomach feels like it’s collapsing in on itself.

The line does not move. Not an inch. Agatha curses everyone in front of her for their inability to choose a meal at a reasonable speed.

She doesn’t need to turn to know Rio has approached. The tiny spike in her heartbeat gives her away. Still, she glances over her shoulder- Rio stands there, arms crossed, expression unreadable except for a faint, deliberate smirk tugging at the corner of her mouth.

“Long line,” Rio says, trying to initiate small talk.

Agatha faces forward again. “No kidding.”

Rio hums. “People really don’t understand the concept of ‘take a scoop and move.’”

Agatha’s jaw tightens. “Don’t hover behind me. It’s weird.”

“That’s a strong accusation for someone who’s been watching the back of my head all morning,” Rio murmurs.

Agatha bristles, turning just enough to shoot her a look. “I haven’t.”

“Mm,” Rio says, unconvinced, “sure.”

Before Agatha can respond, a woman darts toward them from the supply table- her bright red curls glowing in the sun, cheeks flushed, expression frazzled and apologetic.

“Rio,” the woman breathes, clutching a stack of napkins, “sorry- I swear there were more plates here a minute ago. I knowI bought enough yesterday, I counted like three times. I think people grabbed them without telling me. I’m going to go check the car. Just- hold on, okay? I promise everyone will get their food.”

Rio softens, smiling gently. “It’s fine, Wanda. You’re doing great. Breathe.”

Wanda nods quickly, curls bouncing, clearly soothed by Rio’s tone. “Okay. Right. Thank you.” She rushes away, darting towards the parking lot at an increased pace.

Agatha watches the interaction with a raised brow.
“Who’s that?”

“Wanda Maximoff,” Rio answers simply. “She organizes most of our events.”

“She seems… tightly wound,” Agatha remarks under her breath.

Rio gives a small shrug. “She just cares a lot. Unlike some people.”

Agatha snaps her head toward her. “Excuse me?”

Rio smiles, slow and knowing. “Relax. It was a general statement.”

Bullshit.

The line finally moves. Agatha steps forward, loading her plate with whatever looks edible. Rio walks right past the food without touching a single thing.

“You’re not eating?” Agatha asks, more sharply than she intends.

Rio doesn’t look back. “Vegetarian, remember?”

Agatha scoffs. “Right. That thing you do where you pretend plants taste good.” She squints at her. “So you’re just… not eating.”

Rio glances toward the tables. “I’ll live.”

Agatha trails her with her eyes- annoyed that she even cares.

She carries her plate to the farthest table shaded by a large oak tree. The bench is empty, quiet, free of any people. She sits down, finally taking a long, needed bite, right before Rio sits across from her.

Agatha slams her fork down. “Are you serious?”

Rio uncaps a water bottle, calm as ever. “What? I’m sitting.”

“Out of the entire park, you had to sit here?”

Rio meets her gaze, eyes steady and infuriatingly amused. “Yes.”

Agatha stares at her, stunned. “Well, I don’t want you here.”

“That’s too bad,” Rio says, leaning forward, folding her arms on the table. “It’s a public park. I’ll sit wherever I want.”

“Always doing whatever you want,” Agatha mumbles, barely audible, eyes fixed on her plate, “never once thinking about who you hurt in the process. You really haven’t changed.”

Rio’s expression flickers- just enough to show she heard it, to show it hit somewhere she didn’t expect.

“Agatha…” she says quietly, voice cutting through the thick tension.. “From the bottom of my heart, I am sorr-”

“You don’t have a heart.”

Rio blinks, the words landing like a slap. “Yes, I do,” she says, suddenly desperate, the words tumbling out. “It’s full of remorse. For you. For what I did. But you have to try to understand-”

“No,” Agatha says flatly, stabbing a piece of chicken like it personally offended her. “I really don’t.”

Rio’s jaw locks. “Fine.” She stands abruptly, snatching her water bottle with a sharp movement. “Fuck you too.”

Agatha watches her walk away, letting the tiniest flicker of guilt rise- only to shove it right back down and turn back to her food instead.

When the lunch crowd bleeds away and the picnic tables empty, Agatha trudges back toward her volunteer post, dreading the hours still ahead. As she’s walking, the redhead from earlier steps directly into her path, balancing a tray of coffees in her hands.

“Agatha, right?” the woman asks cheerily, presenting a wide smile. “Want a complimentary coffee?”

Agatha actually softens a little, reaching for a cup. “Thank you. Remind me of your name again, hon?”

“Wanda,” she says with a small nod.

“Wanda,” Agatha repeats, giving her a warm, tired smile. “Nice to meet you. It’ll be good having a friendly face around here.”

Wanda tilts her head, genuinely confused. “What about Rio?”

Agatha freezes. Even the mention of the name makes her shoulders creep up.

“What about her?” she asks carefully, voice flat.

Wanda studies her intently, unsure of how to proceed. “I just assumed you two were close. I mean- based on what I’ve heard. How she talks about you.”

Agatha’s eyes narrow. “And how exactly does she talk about me?”

Wanda blanches. “I- umm- I should go. Coffee duty.” She spins away so fast a bit of coffee sloshes over the edge, leaving Agatha standing alone with her drink and a knot in her chest.

She chugs the coffee on her way back to the volunteer tent, needing the caffeine to get her through the rest of the day. The stack of flyers she abandoned earlier still sits there- mostly untouched. She grabs them all with a sigh, the weight awkward and annoying. As she turns, she throws the empty cup onto the ground, the plastic bouncing along behind her.

“Agatha.”

Rio’s voice snaps through the air like a warning.

Agatha stops and turns with exaggerated innocence, batting her lashes. Rio stands a few feet away, arms crossed, glare sharp enough to cut.

“You can’t just throw trash on the ground,” Rio says. “It’s literally the opposite of what we’re here for.”

“Then pick it up. If you love the environment so much.” Rio just glares at her, in disbelief, frown plastered across her face. Agatha shrugs as she continues, “it’s your job.”

“It’s your job too,” Rio snaps, stepping forward. “Remember?”

Agatha flips her off without breaking eye contact, then turns on her heel and storms away, flyers wobbling in her grip.

“You’re so fucking difficult!” Rio shouts after her. She bends, scoops up the cup, and chucks it into the trash with more force than necessary.

Before Agatha can fire something back, a car horn blares aggressively. Both women turn to see Ralph leaning out the open window a few feet away, looking extremely irritated.

“Agatha! Let’s go!” he barks.

Agatha stiffens. “Honey, I can’t leave yet. I have to stay until the event ends,” she calls back, voice tight, eyes flicking nervously to Rio as if she’s waiting for an explosion from either side.

“Do I look like I give a damn?” Ralph snaps. “Get in the car.”

Agatha hesitates, glancing between the two of them- Ralph’s impatience, Rio’s expression unreadable.

“It’s fine,” Rio sighs, suddenly sounding drained. “Just go.”

“Are you sure? I can probably talk him into-”

“Just go, Agatha.” Rio says sternly, gesturing loosely towards the car. The message is clear. She’s done.

“Okay,” Agatha says, swallowing hard. She gives Rio a small, apologetic look before hurrying toward the car.

As much as she hates everything about this stupid organization, it feels wrong to bail on Rio early, to not hold up her end of the deal. The guilt barely has time to settle before an irritating voice cuts through the air behind her, thick with smug satisfaction.

“See you next week, sweetheart.”

Chapter End Notes

guys I promise it will get more angsty, so enjoy the lighthearted banter while you still can.

Chapter 3: Chapter Three

Chapter Notes

I lowkey love this chapter a lot so enjoyyy!

See the end of the chapter for more notes

2005

“Agathaaaaa,” Rio singsongs as she bursts into the dorm without knocking, kicking the door shut behind her. She strides across the room with that irritating confidence of hers and drops herself onto Agatha’s bed like she owns the place. “Why aren’t you getting ready? We’re leaving in ten.”

Agatha doesn’t bother looking up. Her fingers keep flying across her keyboard. “I don’t think I can go tonight, Rio. I have too much work to do.”

Rio groans loudly, dramatic as ever, then crawls across the comforter and settles right against Agatha’s side. She drops her head onto Agatha’s shoulder, hair brushing her cheek. “Come on,” she whines, “it’s Friday night. You never party with me.”

“I don’t have time to get drunk with you,” Agatha mutters, still typing. “Do you want to explain to Ralph why our project is half-finished?”

“I don’t know who the fuck Ralph is,” Rio says bluntly, “so sure. I’ll explain it.” She gives a crooked grin, proud of her own stubbornness.

Agatha sighs. “He’s in my pre-law seminar. We’re presenting on Monday. We’re already behind.”

“Okay, but hear me out,” Rio says, rolling onto her back dramatically. “What if… I help you finish it tomorrow?”

Agatha actually stops typing at that, turning her head to look at her, considering the offer. Rio’s lying there sprawled across the bed, eyes wide and imploring, basically baring into Agatha’s soul. “I don’t want Ralph to be mad,” Agatha says quietly. “I said I’d get it done tonight.”

“Then invite him,” Rio says instantly, shrugging. “If he’s there, he can’t be mad at you.”

“He’s in a frat, he’s probably already going.”

“Great,” Rio says, pushing up on her elbows. “So I doubt he gives a single fuck. Everyone’s out tonight. Literally everyone. No one is expecting you to sit here doing homework like a lonely sixty-year-old woman grading papers.”

“I expect me to,” Agatha shoots back. “You know how important this class is to me. I actually- God, I hate admitting this- I like the work. It’s pretty fun.”

“Neeeeerd,” Rio teases.

“I am not.”

“You totally are. And the only cure for being a nerd is coming out with me.”

Agatha gives her a pointed look. “That is not a cure.”

Rio crawls closer, close enough that Agatha feels her breath on her cheek. “Pleeeease,” Rio begs, lashes fluttering, voice dipping into something dangerously soft. “I really… I really want you there.”

Agatha swallows hard. Her heartbeat stutters at the sincerity in Rio’s eyes- warm, hopeful, too intense to be casual. She hates how easily Rio gets under her skin, how quickly her resolve melts.

“Fine,” Agatha mutters, defeated. “I’ll go.”

“YES!” Rio shrieks, launching herself forward and wrapping Agatha in a hug so forceful they topple sideways. They roll across the bed until Agatha hits the mattress flat on her back with Rio hovering above her, wearing a triumphant grin.

For a split second- barely long enough to register- Rio’s gaze flicks to Agatha’s mouth. Agatha feels it like a spark straight through her spine.

But then Rio clears her throat, pats Agatha’s arm like nothing happened, and springs up. “Okay! Go get ready. Something cute. Actually, no- something hot. Actually…” She pauses, eyes raking her up and down. “Just… change.”

Agatha huffs a laugh, finally closing her laptop. “The things I do for you, Rio.”

Rio flashes a wicked smile on her way to the door. “You love it.”

And Agatha realizes, as she gets up to pick an outfit, that she’s not wrong.

2025

Thursday

Agatha strides into the lobby, heels clacking with authority. Coffee in one hand, jacket draped over the other, she pushes her sunglasses up into her hair as she reaches Billy’s desk.

“Morning, Mrs. Harkness!” Billy chirps- far too loud, far too happy for eight A.M.

Agatha winces. “Billy, for the last time, please just call me Agatha. ‘Mrs. Harkness’ makes me sound like a Victorian widow.”

“Right. Sorry- Agatha.” He straightens up. “What can I do for you?”

“You’re good at all that social media crap, right?” she asks, circling her hand around in the air.

Billy brightens. “Well, I do have fourteen thousand followers on TikTok and about six thousand on Twitter- sorry, X- so I’d say I’m pretty good at creating posts and-”

“Okay, great. Perfect.” Agatha cuts in. “You’re coming with me on Saturday.”

He blinks. “Coming with you to…?”

“Volunteer,” she says, already bored with the explanation. “At Rio’s little climate organization thing. I need someone to take pictures of me looking environmentally conscious. Or whatever.”

“Oh!” Billy’s smile twitches, trying and failing to look sincere. “Yay! Can’t wait!”

“You don’t have to pretend,” Agatha says, scrolling through her phone as she talks. “I don’t want to be there either. Rio said to wear clothes we don’t mind getting dirty. Apparently, there’s required manual labor involved this time.” She rolls her eyes.

Billy’s forced smile wavers as he nods.

Agatha pivots toward her office, then stops and gestures back at him with her coffee cup. “Oh, and tell Jen she has to come too.”

Saturday

“I don’t want to go to this fuckass event,” Agatha groans, taking the final watery slurp from her empty soda cup before tossing it out the open window without thinking. One hand stays lazily hooked over the steering wheel.

“Yeah, Agatha, we know,” Jen says flatly, not looking up from her phone until she shoots Agatha a stubborn glare. “You’ve said it like twelve times since we left my house.”

“I’m actually kind of looking forward to it!” Billy pipes up from the backseat, leaning forward so his head hovers awkwardly between them. “I know I was hesitant before, but I’m trying this thing where I manifest positivity? And today, I get to help the environment.”

Agatha flicks her eyes at him. “Save the forced enthusiasm for the Instagram post, Billy.”

He shuts his mouth immediately and melts back into the seat. Agatha turns up the music- not because she likes it, but because it fills the silence she doesn’t want to deal with- and drives into the forest preserve parking lot at the slowest speed known to man, trying to delay the inevitable.

She finally pulls into a spot, shutting off the engine like it personally wronged her. The three of them get out, Agatha tugging down her volunteer T-shirt and adjusting the waistband of the old jeans she absolutely did not want to wear in public.

Down the trail, clusters of volunteers mingle around tables and tool bins. Agatha releases a long, weary sigh, bracing herself for a day that will require more sweat and humility than she believes any person should be forced to give on a Saturday.

As they walk toward the group, Agatha spots Rio instantly- because of course she does. The ponytail. The clipboard. The irritating glow of someone who got eight hours of sleep and moral purpose. Agatha looks away fast, about to pivot and take the world’s longest scenic detour, when Billy raises his entire arm and chirps, “hi!”

Agatha actually mutters, “Traitor,” under her breath before speed-walking to catch up with them.

Rio turns at Billy’s voice, her face brightening politely- until her gaze lands on Agatha. Then it drops to a stubborn glare.

“Hey,” Rio says, offering Billy a quick smile. “Billy, right?”

He nods, practically vibrating with pride that she remembered. Jen stands beside him, arms crossed and eyebrow raised, silently observing the dynamic.

Rio’s attention shifts, unavoidably, toward the figure reluctantly approaching. “Agatha.” Her tone is curt and annoyingly steady.

“Rio.” Agatha mirrors it, stuffing her hands into her back pockets. She tries to maintain eye contact but fails for half a second when Rio shifts her stance, revealing very short running shorts- enough that Agatha’s brain practically short-circuits.

She drags her eyes back up quickly, hoping no one noticed. Rio definitely did.

“No Ralph today?” She asks, looking around while trying to hide the smirk peeling at her lips- unsuccessfully.

Agatha shakes her head. “I don’t think he’ll be coming back any time soon.”

Rio full on grins.

“Where should we start, Rio?” Billy asks, practically vibrating with excitement.

Agatha rolls her eyes at the sudden personality transplant from Thursday morning. Now she’s the only bitch here who clearly doesn’t want to be doing this. Well- besides Jen. She glances over at her friend just in time to see her grinning at a dark-haired woman a few feet away, the woman jamming a shovel into the soil with one strong stomp of her boot.

Jen immediately adjusts her tank top, subtly pushing her chest up in preparation.

Nevermind. Looks like Jen has found her motivation.
So it really is just Agatha suffering today.

“You guys can be on weed-pulling duty,” Rio says, businesslike. “You see that patch of shrubs over there?”

She points toward a thicket of tangled, overgrown bushes- honeysuckle, buckthorn, a whole mess of invasive garbage choking out the native plants.

“We need those gone before we can replant,” she explains. “There are gloves and tools over there if you want them, but honestly? You should be fine pulling with your hands.”

“Sounds good, thanks so much, Rio!” Billy beams, already pulling out his phone. “I’ll get some photos for the Instagram, if that’s okay?”

Rio nods, offering him a small, polite smile. Billy practically sprints toward the shrubs, Jen trailing close behind him- though her eyes flick back to the dark-haired woman, clearly already plotting her next move.

Agatha hangs back. She’s not ready to start sweating and wrestling with plants yet. She takes an extra breath- unfortunately long enough for Rio to step into the quiet beside her.

They stand shoulder to shoulder, both watching Billy skid to a stop in the dirt. Rio doesn’t look at her, but Agatha can feel her presence like static.

“He seems sweet,” Rio says softly.

“Yeah,” Agatha replies, just above a whisper. “He’s a good kid.”

Silence settles between them- not comfortable, not hostile, just… full.

Then Rio speaks again, voice gentler than Agatha expects.
“That’s always what I imagined Nicky would’ve looked like.”

Agatha inhales, sharp and involuntary.
The world seems to shrink to a pinpoint.

Nicky.
His name.
A name she hasn’t heard spoken aloud in twenty years. A name she’s only whispered to herself in dark rooms and bad dreams and on anniversaries she pretends she doesn’t remember.

Her eyes sting, vision blurring as tears threaten. She swallows hard, forcing them back down where everything else lives. The anger hits her in a wave- how casually Rio said it, how thoughtlessly she dragged up her past, like it was a mere conversation starter or fond memory.

Agatha feels her heart twist, feels old wounds splitting open beneath the surface. She can’t face it. Not now.

“Don’t.” Agatha tries to sound cold, but it comes out cracked, raw, more like a plea than anything harsh. She shakes her head, a tiny, broken movement. “Don’t.”

Rio doesn’t speak at first. She just looks at Agatha- really looks- eyes tracing the tightness in her jaw, the way she’s fighting the tremble in her breath. Something unreadable passes over her face, softening it.

“…Sorry,” Rio says at last, and the sincerity in her voice cuts sharper than any insult ever could.

Agatha shakes her head, shutting the moment down before it can become anything. She turns on her heel and walks away, fast, leaving Rio standing alone among the volunteers and tools and sunlight, watching her retreat like she wishes she could take the words back.

Agatha beelines toward the farthest edge of the work site- somewhere quiet, shaded, away from everyone else. Away from Rio. Her throat burns, her chest tight, the ghost of that name echoing in her mind. She kneels by the shrubs, hoping the physical labor will ground her, hoping the shaking in her hands stops before anyone notices.

She manages- barely- to yank out one weed.

Just one.

Before Billy’s face suddenly appears inches from hers, phone camera raised like a paparazzi ambush.

Agatha jumps so hard she nearly falls backward, clutching her chest.
“Jesus Christ, Billy! You scared the hell out of me.”

“Sorry!” he chirps, completely unbothered. “I just wanted to get a couple shots before you get all sweaty and gross.” He squints at her through the camera. “But maybe… I don’t know… try to look less- how do I put this… uh, miserable?”

Agatha stares at him, expression murderous.

“Come on, Agatha,” he whines. “You told me we need decent pictures for the post.”

She groans dramatically, flinging her arms up. “Fine!”
She grabs a nearby shovel, leaning on it like some exhausted Civil War soldier, and stretches a stiff, unnatural smile across her face.

The smile instantly drops when she catches sight of Rio watching her from several yards away, arms crossed, mouth ticking upward in amusement.

“Oh, that’s cute,” Rio calls out, voice carrying. “Very cover-girl of you.”

Agatha wants the earth to swallow her whole.

Billy beams at the interaction- of course he does. “Rio! That’s perfect. Why don’t you hop in? I’ll get some shots of both of you together!”

Rio freezes. Agatha’s stomach plummets.
Billy remains blissfully unaware of the tension unfolding in front of him.

Rio opens her mouth- clearly searching for an excuse- but then glances at Agatha. At the red around her eyes. At the way her shoulders still tremble from earlier. She swallows.

“Go on,” Billy insists, waving them together. “Come on, I need something wholesome for the feed.”

To Agatha’s horror, Rio actually steps forward- slow, cautious, like she’s approaching a wounded animal that might bolt. She stops at Agatha’s side, leaving a wide, careful gap between them.

Billy groans. “Okay, great, but can you two look like you don’t hate each other? Just… a little?”

Rio hesitates. And then closes the distance.

She slips an arm around Agatha’s back, her hand settling on Agatha’s far shoulder. Not gripping. Not grabbing. Just resting there, warm and gentle, like she’s asking permission without saying anything aloud.

Then Rio tilts her head in, temple brushing Agatha’s cheekbone. She smells like sunblock and pine and something achingly familiar.

Agatha’s whole body goes rigid. A cold shiver shoots up her spine, chased by heat pooling low in her stomach, an infuriating betrayal by her own body.

She forces a smile, bright and poisonous.
Rio’s smile is soft, almost genuine- too genuine.
Agatha hates how her chest flutters.

“Perfect!” Billy cheers behind the camera. “Hold it! Hold it- okay, yes! That’s it!”

Agatha grits her teeth until he finally lowers the phone, praying the moment ends before her lungs collapse.

Immediately, Agatha steps away from Rio, brushing off her shoulder like she’s trying to rid herself of something crawling on her- something unwelcome. Her skin still tingles where Rio touched her, and she hates it. She forces her face into neutrality, turns her back, and trudges toward her patch of shade, determined not to give Billy any excuse to suggest “just one more” picture.

Out of her peripheral vision, she catches him scurrying off- thank God- to bother Jen instead. Jen, who has already abandoned all pretense of volunteering and is now shamelessly flirting with the dark-haired woman she’d been ogling earlier. Agatha snorts when she sees Jen’s bright, brand-new baby-pink maxi skirt gathered in her hands, tied up at her hips to keep it off the dirt.

“I’m not ruining a good outfit for some weeds,” Jen had announced earlier, hand on hip.

Agatha, on the other hand, kneels in the dirt, grateful she wore jeans she doesn’t care about. She drives her gloves into the soil, fingers wrapping around a stubborn root, grounding herself in the physical struggle of ripping something intrusive out of earth.

She’s mid-pull when she senses movement beside her- too close, too familiar.

Of course. Rio.

Without saying a word, Rio sits down next to her in the patch of shade. She tucks her knees to her chest, arms draped loosely around them, posture casual but her presence anything but. The position hikes her shorts up slightly, exposing the backs of her thighs to Agatha’s line of sight.

Agatha forces her eyes downward, heat prickling up her neck in irritation. Or something else.

“Need some company?” Rio asks lightly, as though their last ten interactions hadn’t been a minefield.

“No. I’m good, thanks.” Agatha answers quickly, snapping a stem with more force than necessary.

Rio doesn’t leave.
“You sure?” she asks, glancing around at the laughter and chatter of the other volunteers. “Everyone else is being social.”

“I hate being social,” Agatha bites out, yanking a weed from the ground so hard she nearly loses her balance.

Rio hums. “Yeah, I know. But from over there you look like a reclusive old witch. Just thought I’d sit in case you were lonely.”

Agatha’s jaw clenches.

Stop.
She thinks with a brutal intensity.

Stop caring. Stop hovering. Stop trying. Stop being gentle.
Because every time Rio shows softness, every time she treats Agatha like someone worth checking on… it hurts.

“I don’t need your company,” she says finally, voice flat, eyes locked on the dirt so she won’t have to see Rio’s face when she says it.

Rio is still for a moment. Agatha feels the silence like pressure in her chest.

“Okay,” Rio murmurs- not cold, not angry, just… tired.

Then she rises to her feet, brushes the dirt from her legs, and walks away.

Agatha watches her go. She shouldn’t. But she does.

The moment Rio disappears behind a cluster of volunteers, something inside Agatha deflates, collapsing inward.

Good, she tells herself.
You don’t want her here.

So why, then-

Why does her chest feel hollow now?

Why does the absence feel worse than the tension?

Why does she already miss the warmth Rio left beside her?

Agatha digs her fingers into the earth, swallowing hard, determined to focus on the weeds and not the ache tightening behind her ribs.

An hour drags by, Agatha lost in her own little bubble of dirt, roots, and stubborn silence. She’s tuned out the rest of the volunteers- ignoring their chatter, their laughter, their annoying sense of community- as she tears another clump of weeds from the earth. Finally, she sits back on her heels, breathing hard. A decent-sized patch of soil lies in front of her, cleared and neat. She allows herself the smallest flicker of satisfaction.

Is volunteering… actually rewarding?

No. Absolutely not. If anyone asks, she will deny this feeling until the day she dies.

She grabs her water bottle and takes a long drink, wincing as she stretches her aching back. Movement catches her eye- instinct, not intention- and her head turns automatically when she hears Rio’s laugh- bright, loud, impossible to ignore. A few yards away, Rio kneels beside Wanda, their heads bent close together over a stubborn patch of weeds. Agatha tells herself she’s only looking because the noise disrupted her focus, but her eyes stay locked on them longer than she’d like to admit.

Rio’s hand lands on Wanda’s arm as she throws her head back in laughter, fingers curling lightly as if they’ve done it a hundred times. Wanda leans into her, shoulder brushing Rio’s in a much too intimate way for Agatha’s liking. Rio doesn’t move away. She leans closer, actually- smiling at Wanda with this warm, open expression that Agatha hasn’t seen directed at her in twenty years.

Agatha’s jaw tenses. She tries to focus on her cleared patch of soil, but her eyes keep sliding back to them like there’s a magnet in Rio’s stupid face. Wanda whispers something too close to Rio’s ear, and Rio tilts her head to listen, their hair nearly brushing. Wanda’s hand rests on Rio’s back for balance. Rio doesn’t flinch. She laughs again.

Heat crawls up Agatha’s chest, sharp and ugly.
Why is Wanda touching her so much?
And why is Rio letting her?

Wanda tugs at a deep-rooted stalk, struggling, and Rio quickly shifts in behind her, reaching around to help. Their hands overlap. Stay overlapped. Wanda giggles, Rio grins, and Agatha’s frown deepens so hard her forehead aches.

This is ridiculous. It’s volunteering, not a dating mixer. They’re supposed to be pulling weeds, not giggling and brushing hands like they’re in some kind of cheesy rom-com.

Agatha glares at the two of them, gripping her water bottle so tightly it crumples. She tells herself she’s annoyed on principle. She tells herself Rio is being unprofessional.

None of these explanations ease the burning feeling in her stomach.

Rio tucks a strand of hair behind her ear- Wanda watches her do it- and Agatha has to look away before her scowl becomes visible from a mile away. But even turned toward the dirt, she can still hear them laughing together. Close. Comfortable. Too comfortable.

She yanks a weed out of the ground with unnecessary force, but the sharp satisfaction doesn’t help. The laughter continues, and with every sound, that bitter little spark in her chest flares hotter.

Why does it matter?
Why does she care?

She does not have an answer.
She just knows she hates the way Wanda keeps making Rio smile.

Something in Agatha snaps.

“Rio!” she calls out, voice slicing through the volunteer chatter before she can stop herself. Rio’s head jerks up instantly, eyes scanning until they find Agatha. Confusion flashes across her face- because Agatha never calls her name, never willingly asks for her attention.

“Can you help me with this?” Agatha adds quickly, pointing at the weed patch in front of her like that was always the plan. She raises her voice just enough to carry, trying to sound irritated instead of desperate.

Rio hesitates. Not long, but long enough to make Agatha’s stomach clench. She turns to Wanda, saying something too quiet for Agatha to catch, before pushing up from the ground. Her hand slips away from Wanda’s arm as she dusts her palms on her shorts, then stands, stretching her back with her hands on her hips.

Agatha watches- too closely- as Rio starts walking toward her. Not hurriedly. Not reluctantly. Just… intrigued. Like she’s trying to figure out what this little outburst was for.

Panic flares. Agatha tears her gaze away so fast her neck almost cracks, suddenly fascinated by the patch of dirt at her feet. She kneels, pretending she’s actually doing something productive and not trying to calm a heart that has- apparently- decided to start racing.

“What do you need?” Rio asks when she reaches her, voice steady but eyes sharp.

Agatha forces herself to stand up, planting her hands on her hips like she has some authority over the situation. “I- uhhh…”
Nope. No plan. Nothing. Complete and total mental wipe.

Her eyes land on a weed near her foot. “I need help with this one. It’s stuck.”

She crouches, tugs it softly, and of course, because the universe despises her, it pops right out like it wasn’t even pretending to be attached.

Rio gives her a slow, teasing smirk. “Wow. Tragic.”

“Wrong weed,” Agatha mutters through clenched teeth. She grabs another, silently bargaining with every cosmic entity that it’ll actually stay rooted. She pulls.

Nothing. Thank god.

“See?” she says, perhaps too triumphantly for someone asking for help.

Rio sighs but steps closer, crouching beside her. Their hands brush at the stem, hips bumping lightly. It’s almost nothing. Almost.

Rio gives a tug once- no movement. Her eyes widen, mildly surprised. “Huh. Okay. Let’s both pull on three.”

Agatha tries not to breathe too loudly.

Rio counts. “One. Two… Three.”

They pull.

The weed springs out of the ground with shocking force, and gravity immediately betrays them. Rio hits the dirt first- hard. Agatha manages to twist in the air, and lands on top of her with a graceless grunt, palms braced against Rio’s shoulders to catch herself.

It’s a split second. A heartbeat.
But it’s enough.

Enough for Agatha to feel the warmth beneath her.
Enough for Rio’s breath to stutter against her neck.
Enough for Agatha to momentarily forget how to move.

She jolts back, scrambling off Rio so fast she almost trips over her own feet. She dusts herself off aggressively, like she can brush away what just happened.

Rio props herself up on her elbows, exhaling sharply as she looks up at her. There’s something unreadable- annoying, soft, careful- in her eyes.

Agatha hesitates for a moment, looking at Rio, and eventually extends a hand with a sigh. “Come on.”

Rio grabs it, grip strong and warm. Agatha hauls her up. Rio lets go immediately- faster than she’d pulled her hand away from Wanda earlier.

The thought hits Agatha with a sharp twist in her chest.

Before Rio can comment, a shriek slices through the air. Both women turn at the same time.

A few yards away, Jen and the dark-haired volunteer are in the middle of a miniature dirt war. Alice tosses a handful of soil at Jen, who blocks it with her arms, laughing uncontrollably.

“Stoooop,” Jen giggles, maniacally. “Oh my gosh, stop!”

“I see Jen has met Alice,” Rio says, amusement tugging at her mouth. She watches the two carefully.

Agatha says nothing- her eyes glued to the scene as Jen scoops up a bit of dirt and flings it back. Alice dodges, badly, and Jen reaches forward to wipe a streak of dirt off her cheek. Slowly. Deliberately. Her fingers linger.

Agatha narrows her eyes. The softness between them makes something bitter rise in her throat.

“Gross,” Agatha mutters, forcing an eyeroll.

“Right?” Rio says, laughing under her breath. “Like get a room.”

Agatha chuckles, shaking her head. The sound is genuine, quiet, careful. She glances down at the dirt, suddenly aware of how close Rio is standing next to her.

“Well, if that’s all you need, I’ll head back over-”

She’s cut off by Agatha. “Or… you could stay.” It slips out before she can stop herself. “Just in case there are any more tough weeds, y’know?”

Rio freezes for half a second, shock flickering across her face- because Agatha never asks her to stay. Not like that. She glances over her shoulder at Wanda, who’s still kneeling in the dirt, waiting, then back at Agatha with an uncomfortable twist of her mouth.

“Sorry,” Rio says quietly. “I told Wanda I’d be back to help her replant.”

Agatha’s heart drops so hard she swears it thuds against the ground. Her smile falters- just a flash, barely a heartbeat- before she forces it back into place. “Oh. Yeah. No, that’s fine.”

“I can come back to help you later?” Rio offers, almost tentative.

“No, that’s okay. I’m a big girl, I can handle it myself.”

Agatha is already crouching again, pretending to adjust her gloves, anything to keep Rio from seeing the embarrassment rising in her face. She fixes her stare on the weeds like they personally offended her, because right now she can’t look at either of them without feeling that sharp twist of jealousy again.

Rio hesitates, then nods and walks back toward Wanda. Wanda beams up at her, brushing soil off her hands before handing Rio another plant. The sight makes something ugly rear up in Agatha’s chest- Wanda’s easy smile, Rio’s soft laugh, the way their shoulders brush like it’s the most natural thing in the world.

Agatha grits her teeth and yanks a stubborn root from the ground with more force than necessary.
Fine. Whatever. If Rio wants to go play gardener with Wanda, she can.

Agatha tears into the next weed like it’s personally to blame for how her stomach feels.

When they break for lunch, Agatha grabs not one, but two sandwiches- mostly out of spite, wanting to take as much from this stupid organization as she cans- and drops onto the bench beside Jen. Her plate hits the table with enough force to make the plastic rattle.

“Jeez,” Jen says, raising an eyebrow as Agatha tears into her sandwich like it personally wronged her. “Someone’s in a bad mood.”

“I’m not in a bad mood,” Agatha snaps through a mouthful of bread.

“Okayyy…” Jen drawls, unconvinced. She copies Agatha’s aggressive bite, chewing exaggeratedly. “Can you do something for me?”

“Hm?” Agatha grunts, only half-listening- because her eyes are glued to Rio at a far picnic table.

Rio is sitting sideways on the bench, one leg tucked up under her, laughing at something Wanda just said. She pops a piece of gum into her mouth, then starts chewing with slow, deliberate rhythm. Each chew reveals the edge of her teeth, her jaw flexing, her tongue brushing the gum toward her cheek. It’s a tiny, stupid thing, but Agatha can’t look away. She suddenly feels like a teenager in a cafeteria, staring at the girl she has absolutely no business staring at.

“I need you to invite Wanda out for drinks tonight,” Jen says.

“What?” Agatha’s head snaps toward her, scowl forming instantly.

Jen holds up her hands. “I want to get drinks with Alice, and Wanda is Alice’s ride. So if you get drinks with Wanda, that means Alice has to come, which means I have to come, and then boom- we’re all hanging out.” She grins like she’s solved world peace.

“Why don’t you just… ask Alice out yourself?”

Jen barks a laugh. “Yeah, no. I’m trying to play it cool. Subtle. Like I don’t care if she shows up or not. So we need a group hang.”

“Absolutely not. I’m not in the mood for drinks.”

“Oh, so you are in a mood then?”

Agatha glares. “Fine. I’m in a mood. And my answer is still no.”

Jen crosses her arms. “I came to this stupid volunteer event for you, Agatha. This is literally the least you could do for me.”

Okay, she’s got a point there.

Agatha tilts her head back and groans dramatically. “Ugh, fine. I’ll ask her.”

“Yay!” Jen squeals, throwing her arms around Agatha in a hug that knocks Agatha off balance.

Agatha endures it, patting Jen’s back like she’s some overexcited pet, but her attention drifts almost immediately back to Rio. Rio, who is blowing a small, perfect bubble with her gum, lips curling in concentration. Rio, whose knee bumps against Wanda’s under the table and makes her grin.

Agatha’s jaw tightens. She takes another vicious bite of her sandwich.

Jen keeps talking, something about outfits and shot specials, but Agatha isn’t listening.

She’s too busy staring at Rio and the stupid piece of gum that has no right being that distracting.

The rest of the afternoon drags by at a glacial pace. Agatha spends the remaining hours alone, planting the new bushes in her own little pocket of shade, away from the rest of the group. She keeps her head down and her eyes glued to the dirt, determined not to look at Billy, who’s skipping around the site with boundless energy, or Jen, who’s giggling and flirting like she’s in a rom-com, or Rio, who-

Well. Agatha wants absolutely nothing to do with Rio.

Out of sight, out of mind.

That was the intention.

Unfortunately, Rio seems to be taking up a lot more mental space that Agatha had anticipated.

When the call finally comes to start wrapping up, Agatha exhales a weary groan, wiping the sweat beading along her hairline. Her entire body feels like it’s about to collapse. She pulls off her gloves, tosses them into the bin with exhaustion, and returns the shovel to its correct bin. As volunteers begin dragging equipment back to their cars, she catches sight of Wanda’s red hair-and is abruptly reminded of Jen’s stupid, stupid plan.

She sighs deeply, then plasters on the most painful fake smile she’s ever produced.

“Wanda!” she calls out, forcing a bright tone that does not reach her eyes. “How are ya, hon?”

Wanda turns, eyes lighting up. “Hey, Agatha! I’m doing good, thanks for asking! What about you?”

“Good. Great.” Agatha blurts, already impatient. “So, I was just wondering if you’d like to come to a little hangout I’m planning tonight? Jen and I thought we’d grab a drink at the bar.”

“Oh my gosh, Agatha!” Wanda gushes, clasping her hands. “That’s so sweet of you to think of me! You know, it’s been a really long-”

“Are you in or you out, Maximoff?” Agatha interrupts, desperate to end this before her fake enthusiasm dissolves into visible misery.

“I’m so in!” Wanda chirps. “Though, um, a few people depend on me for rides, so would it be okay if they came along too?”

“Yeah, of course! The more the merrier!” Agatha lies through clenched teeth. “You mean Alice, right?”

“Yes! And Yelena and Rio!”

Agatha bites the inside of her cheek so hard she tastes blood.

“Perfect,” she grinds out, clapping her hands once with the energy of someone barely holding it together. “See you later then!” And she spins on her heel before Wanda can finish another sentence, practically sprinting toward Jen.

“Jen!” she hisses, marching up to her friend. “I see you conveniently forgot to mention that Rio is also part of Wanda’s carpool.”

Jen blinks innocently, feigning surprise. “Did I?” she asks, lips twitching. “Oops. Must’ve slipped my mind.”

“You owe me. Big time,” Agatha growls, pointing a rigid finger in her face.

“Deal,” Jen says immediately- because she fully expected Agatha to have thrown something at her by now.

They gather the final supplies just as Wanda pulls up her car, window rolled down, elbow draped casually over the door.

“Okay! Wanda’s group, hop on in!” she calls out cheerfully.

Yelena slides into the passenger seat. Alice skips around to the far side, opening the back door. Rio steps towards the car as well… but not fast enough.

Rather, Jen sprints past her at full speed, dives into the backseat next to Alice, and slams the door shut so aggressively the whole car shakes.

“Jen!” Agatha screeches, feeling utterly betrayed. Jen rolls down the window long enough to blow her a dramatic kiss.

“Sorryyy, Agatha, love you!” she sings as Wanda pulls away, leaving a swirl of dust in their wake.

Agatha and Rio stand alone in the settling cloud. Rio processes the chaos in slow motion, her mouth slightly open, eyes blinking with slight shock.

She turns back towards Agatha slowly, shrugging.

“Guess I’m riding with you then.”

Chapter 4: Chapter Four

Chapter Notes
