2005

Rio wakes up on the floor.

It hits her all at once- the pounding in her skull, the dryness in her mouth, the full-body ache from having apparently slept on wood, with only a pathetic, paper-thin rug between them. She groans, slowly propping herself up on her elbows. The room tilts left slightly, then right, and she closes her eyes until it settles.

When she opens them again, she sees Agatha.

She’s sitting on her bed, knees tucked tightly to her chest, arms wrapped around them. Her eyes are glassy, unfocused, fixed somewhere beyond the dorm room wall. It takes Rio a second to realize she hadn’t even noticed her waking up.

Rio rubs her face, wincing as her spine stabs into her.
“Jesus Christ,” she mutters. “Why am I on the ground?”

Agatha’s head tilts slightly, as if the question only barely reaches her.
“I offered for you to sleep up here with me,” she whispers. “You said you’d rather be ‘one with the ground.’”

“Huh.” Rio scoffs softly in disbelief. That… does sound like her. Well, the tequila version of her. The last thing she remembers is dragging Agatha into the frat house, taking 4 shots, and then- nothing. Just a haze of lights, spilled drinks, and the bragging confidence of someone who’d clearly had too much alcohol way too fast.

She pushes herself up with a grunt and climbs onto Agatha’s bed without asking, sitting beside her with her back against the wall. Agatha still doesn’t look at her.

“You okay?” Rio asks, narrowing her eyes. “You look pretty rough. Bad hangover?” She tries to tease, but the joke hangs in the air, heavy and wrong.

Agatha doesn’t smile. Doesn’t flinch. Doesn’t even blink.

“Rio,” she says softly.

Rio stills.
“Yeah?”

Agatha’s voice cracks on the first word.
“I think I need to take a pregnancy test.”

Rio freezes.

Then her brows pull together, confused, dismissive- because it doesn’t make sense.
“A pregnancy test? Wh- you’re a lesbian. Why would you need-”

Agatha turns her head toward her for the first time.

Her eyes are full of tears she’s clearly been holding back for hours. Maybe all night. It’s a look Rio has never seen on her before- empty, afraid, humiliated, shattered. It tells Rio everything she needs to know without saying a single word.

Rio’s breath leaves her in a quiet, horrified exhale.
“Oh.”

Her face breaks, pain flashing through it like she’s been struck.

“Oh, Agatha…” she whispers, shaking her head. “No. No, sweetheart.” Her voice cracks as tears rise to her own eyes. “Oh my sweet baby, what happened?”

Agatha doesn’t answer. She can’t. Her jaw trembles as the dam finally breaks, and she collapses into Rio’s arms with a choked sob.

Rio catches her without hesitation, pulling her tight against her chest like she can shield her from the world- like she can rewind time if she just holds her hard enough. Agatha shakes violently, breath hitching, tears soaking into Rio’s shirt. Rio’s hand finds her hair, combing through the strands gently. Her other arm circles her waist, grounding her, anchoring her.

“It’s okay,” Rio whispers, though she knows it isn’t. “I’ve got you. I’ve got you.”

She presses a soft, trembling kiss to Agatha’s temple because she doesn’t know what else to say- what else could possibly be enough?

Rio doesn’t know.

So she holds Agatha tighter.

Ten minutes go by- ten long, quiet minutes where Agatha stays curled against Rio’s chest, face buried in her shirt, fists gripping the fabric like it’s the only thing holding her together. Her sobs eventually soften from violent tremors to small, broken exhales. Rio doesn’t rush her, doesn’t speak, just holds her and traces small circles into her back with steady hands.

When Agatha finally pulls away, Rio immediately moves with her, cupping her cheek with gentle fingers. Her thumb brushes away the tear tracks, slow and soft, like she’s afraid Agatha will shatter if she applies too much pressure.

“Who did this, Agatha?” Rio whispers. It’s barely a breath, but it carries every ounce of fear and fury coiled in her chest. She squeezes Agatha’s hand once- firm and grounding.

Agatha inhales shakily. Once. Twice. Then three times. Her throat bobs as she swallows down another wave of panic.

“Remember my project partner?” she finally gets out, voice splintering on the edges.

Rio’s face tightens.
“Ralph?”

Agatha nods, small and stiff.

Rio’s entire body goes rigid. Her jaw clenches so hard the muscle ticks, and her fists curl, knuckles whitening as rage surges hot and fast through her.

“I’m gonna kill him,” she mutters, voice trembling with fury. “No, seriously- I’m going to literally murder-”

“No, no, Rio,” Agatha cuts in, panicked, reaching for her arm. “It’s not his fault. He was really drunk, he probably doesn’t even remember.”

“That is not an excuse,” Rio snaps, louder than intended, breath catching with anger. Her fingernails dig into her palms as she fights the urge to scream.

Agatha wipes at her eyes with the sleeve of her hoodie. She’s trembling again. “I didn’t say no,” she whispers. “I… I got scared. I froze. I just- God, I’m so stupid. I should’ve just said no.”

The moment the words leave her mouth, something in Rio breaks.

“No.” Rio’s voice steadies- not soft, not gentle, but firm in a way that demands Agatha’s attention. She shifts closer, taking Agatha’s face between both hands, forcing her to meet her eyes.

“Don’t you dare do that. Don’t blame yourself.” She shakes her head slowly. “This was not your fault, Agatha. Freezing isn’t consent. Being scared isn’t consent. Him being drunk isn’t consent.” Her voice wavers, but she pushes through it. “Someone took advantage of you. And I am so, so sorry.”

Agatha’s lip wobbles, tears sliding down again despite her best effort.

“But you are not to blame,” Rio says again, more insistent, like she’s trying to carve the truth into Agatha. “Not for a single second.”

She wraps her arms around her once more, gentler this time but with a kind of protective hold that feels unbreakable.

“I’m right here,” Rio murmurs into her hair. “I’m not going anywhere.”

In that moment, Rio makes a silent vow to herself. A vow to Agatha- to hold her, to protect her, to carry all of her pain if she must. She swears that she will never let harm come to her again, that she will shield her from hurt, from loss, from grief, in every way she possibly can. She tightens her arms around her, feeling the small, shaky breaths against her chest, and the promise settles deep in her bones. Without uttering a word, she vows to be her shield, her safe place.

Always.

2025

Agatha marches toward her car, Rio trailing closely behind her.

“Ground rules,” Agatha snaps, turning her head back to Rio, hair whipping across her face as Rio jogs to catch up. “No feet on the dashboard. No dirty shoes,” she makes up on the spot, “No eating. No singing. No talking. Got it?”

Rio grins, amusement flickering across her face. “Yes, Ma’am,” she says, giving a sharp, exaggerated salute.

Agatha ignores the flutter in her stomach at the nickname, opening the car door and sliding into the driver’s seat. Rio swings open the passenger side but pauses to wipe the dust off her shoes, scattering gravel across the lot. Agatha smirks, slightly surprised that she actually listened.

The engine rumbles to life. Agatha backs out slowly, arm braced over the passenger seat to check the rearview, painfully aware of Rio’s presence next to her. Heat radiates from Rio’s shoulder, her breath brushing Agatha’s neck whenever she shifts. The feeling makes her chest tight. She puts the car in drive and slams the accelerator, sending the car forward with a sudden jolt, pressing her back into the seat.

Silence falls, thick and heavy, broken only by the hum of the engine and tires on the asphalt. Agatha’s peripheral vision catches Rio pulling out her phone and typing. Curious, she glances over, trying to see who it is, but Rio’s screen is annoyingly angled just right.

“Who’s that?” Agatha asks casually, though her voice is sharper than she means.

“Just Wanda,” Rio replies, eyes glued to the screen.

Agatha’s stomach knots. “What does she want?”

“Oh, just letting her know we’re a few minutes behind,” Rio says casually.

Agatha grits her teeth, forcing herself to keep her eyes on the road. What an unnecessary ass text, she thinks. She shouldn’t care. She doesn’t care. But the tightness curls in her chest anyway, crawling under her skin.

Then Rio speaks again, voice cutting through the quiet, turning her phone screen towards Agatha. “Billy posted our picture. Cover photo.”

Agatha glances up at the phone. Her stomach drops. The image on the screen shows her looking miserable, just like Billy had said- and Rio, god, Rio. Her arm is flung casually around Agatha’s shoulder, fingers grazing lightly across her arm. Her lips curve into that infuriating, soft, open smile. Even in a photo, it’s magnetic.

Agatha freezes, heart hammering. The heat in her chest spreads, clenching tight in her stomach.

“He got my bad side,” she mutters, forcing herself to return her gaze to the road.

“Oh, please,” Rio teases, but her eyes linger on Agatha, “you don’t have a bad side.”

Agatha bites the inside of her cheek hard, heat rushing up the back of her neck. It’s infuriating. She murmurs, barely audibly, “You broke a rule. No talking.”

Rio sighs, slow and heavy, blowing air through her mouth as though it could erase the tension. She notices the music playing on the radio and reaches forward, turning up the volume. “I love Chappell Roan,” she says, smirking as she reaches up to tighten her ponytail. Agatha notices, of course, how the shirt rides up just slightly, brushing against her skin, and immediately regrets thinking about it.

The chorus blares through the speakers, much too loud for any reasonable person, but right now- Agatha doesn’t mind. At least it’s filling the silence.

When you wake up next to him in the middle of the night
With your head in your hands, you’re nothing more than his wife

Rio glances at her subtly, just the tiniest movement, but Agatha notices. She pretends not to, but she can feel her pulse spike.

And when you think about me, all of those years ago
You’re standing face to face with “I told you so”

This time, Agatha can’t resist. She looks. Rio is looking too. Their eyes meet for a millisecond- just long enough to register- before both snap away.

Agatha’s hands tighten on the wheel before reaching out a hand. She spins the volume down to zero, desperate for any ounce of control in this goddamn car. “She’s problematic,” she mutters, pressing the accelerator harder, willing the ride to end before her chest bursts.

Rio leans back slightly, arms crossed, as her smirk fades. The silence between them hums now, heavy and suffocating in the absence of music.

Rio looks over at Agatha. There’s hesitation in the movement, like she’s afraid even turning her head might set something off between them. “I’m sorry about bringing up Nicky earlier,” she says softly- voice barely audible over the hum of the engine, careful and unsure like she’s testing the air. “It was inconsiderate.”

Agatha exhales sharply through her nose, her breath fogging faintly against the window. “Yeah, it was.” Her tone is flat, stripped of its usual bite. Rio is easy to snap at these days- too easy- but when it comes to Nicky, there isn’t anger left for anyone, not even her. All there’s room for is a hollow ache in her chest that feels like something was ripped out of her years ago and she’s been walking around with the empty space ever since.

Rio’s fingers tighten and relax in her lap, twisting together nervously as the seconds stretch. She stares down at them- her hands always give her away. “I just-” she swallows, shoulders slightly curling in on themselves, “I want you to know that if you need someone to talk to- about him- or anything, really- then I’m here.” Her voice trembles as she speaks.

Agatha’s stomach constricts as she turns toward her, taking in the sight of Rio folded in on herself, trying so hard to be gentle, to be sincere. It makes something sharp rise in Agatha’s throat. “Oh, now you’re here for me?” she spits, the words slicing out before she can soften them. Her voice is cold, void of any compassion. “Now you want to help? You’re about twenty years too late, hon.”

Rio’s lip quivers. Just barely- but Agatha sees it. She sees how Rio’s shoulders stiffen, how her jaw tenses, how she chooses to keep her gaze fixed on the dashboard because looking at Agatha right now would break something inside her. “Yeah,” Rio whispers, the word so small it almost gets lost over the sound of the engine, “I know.”

Silence drops over them once again- heavy and suffocating. The only sound present is the faint whir of the air vents and the distant bass of music coming from inside the bar they’re approaching. Agatha’s fingers tighten around the steering wheel, knuckles whitening, as guilt creeps slowly up her spine. She tells herself she doesn’t care. She tells herself Rio deserves it- deserves the cruelty. But the hollow ache consuming her argues otherwise.

Agatha presses her foot down, speeding into a parking spot outside the bar. Rio’s breath stutters, almost too quiet to notice, before she unclips her seatbelt with trembling fingers. She practically launches herself out of the car, the door slamming behind her hard enough to rattle the frame. The sudden cold air scoops up the lingering warmth of her presence from the passenger seat.

Agatha stays where she is for a second, alone in the dim car. Her hands remain on the wheel, even though the engine has already stopped. She inhales slowly, then again, grounding herself with the faint trace of Rio’s perfume still hanging in the air.

Finally, Agatha pushes the door open and steps out into the cold, following Rio into the bar.

As soon as she steps inside, the blaring music hits her like a physical force, the bass shaking through her ribs. For the first time all day, her thoughts stop clawing at her. The noise is a relief, a merciful distraction.

She spots the group squeezed into the corner of the bar, laughter rising above the music. Rio is already seated- of course- right beside Wanda, their heads tilted close in conversation.

Lovely.

Jen notices her first, waving her over with an excited grin. Agatha inhales sharply, steels herself, and heads toward the only empty seat next to Jen- the one safely far from Rio. She plans to sit, keep her mouth shut, and pretend Rio doesn’t exist for the entire night. She can go ahead and giggle with her new soulmate. Agatha is unbothered. Totally fine.

Just in time to ruin her perfect plan, some random man swoops in and drops into the chair Agatha was reaching for, already deep into a conversation with his friend.

Agatha stops. Stares. Lets out an audible noise of pure, exhausted outrage. Then she spins on her heel, jaw clenched, and marches to the other side of the table. She slides into the seat next to Rio, making sure their arms don’t touch, refusing to look at her.

The universe officially hates her.

Drinks are ordered- except for Agatha and Wanda, the responsible chauffeurs. Agatha would kill for a shot, or seven, but no, she has to be the adult. She has to be sober. She has to sit next to Rio sober.

Fucking fantastic.

The bartender turns to Rio.

“Vodka soda,” Rio says coolly, like she’s been drinking them her whole life.

Agatha’s head snaps toward her before she can stop herself. “Since when do you drink vodka sodas?” The bite in her voice surprises even her. As if Rio changing her drink is some kind of personal betrayal. As if Agatha has any right to care.

Rio lifts an eyebrow, scowling. “I’m trying something new.” She pauses, studying Agatha’s expression carefully, frown still plastered across her face. “What’s your problem?”

“Nothing,” Agatha mutters, whipping her gaze back to the bar. Heat crawls up her neck. She waves down the bartender with more force than necessary. “Virgin mojito.”

The bartender nods.

Drinks arrive, and once everyone has a little alcohol in their system, the conversation starts to flow. Well- among the others. Despite the new seating arrangement, Agatha refuses to abandon her plan to ignore Rio.

Luckily, Rio seems just as committed to silence. The two sit stiffly beside each other, listening to the rest of the group chatter while they sip their drinks and avoid even glancing in each other’s direction.

Every so often, Agatha swears she can feel Rio’s eyes on her- hovering, lingering, burning a hole in the side of her face. She doesn’t dare look over. Instead, she watches the others, in a desperate attempt to focus on anything else. Jen flirting shamelessly with Alice, Yelena stirring her drink with slow precision, Wanda twisting the silver bracelets on her wrist. Anything. Anything but Rio.

“Rio,” Jen suddenly says, loud enough to cut through the whole table’s noise. Both Agatha and Rio stiffen. “The picture you reposted earlier is so cute.” Jen beams down at her phone.

“Wait, show me,” Wanda says from Rio’s other side, leaning in. Jen flips her phone around. On the screen is Billy’s photo from earlier- Rio and Agatha- sitting on Rio’s Instagram story, captioned with a bright red heart emoji.

Agatha turns, glare sharp enough to pierce. Rio doesn’t defend herself. She just looks away, cheeks coloring, clearly not expecting anyone here to have seen it.

“Since when do you have my Instagram?” Rio asks, voice clipped. Whether it’s real irritation or just the alcohol talking, Agatha can’t tell.

“Oh, I’m a shameless stalker. It’s my talent,” Jen says cheerfully, oblivious. She turns to Alice. “Speaking of- what’s your user? I’m following you.”

That’s when Agatha mentally checks out, her thoughts crowding in too fast, too loud.

She shoots Rio a sidelong look. Rio, obviously, doesn’t notice, as she’s once again, deep in conversation with Wanda. This bipolar ass bitch, Agatha thinks to herself.

“I’m going to the bathroom,” Agatha says abruptly, shoving back her chair and storming off before anyone can say a word.

She slips into a stall, locks the door, and sinks onto the toilet seat, burying her face in her hands. Her phone buzzes weakly in her pocket. When she pulls it out, the screen lights up with 7 unread messages, 2 missed calls- all from Ralph.

Shit. Did she forget to text him?

She exhales hard, dragging her palms down her exhausted face before opening his texts. A whole string of frantic, irritated messages demanding to know where she is. Agatha types back with stiff fingers:

Sorry, just checked my phone. I’m out with friends at the bar. Be home soon.

She hits send, shoves the phone away, and forces herself to breathe. A minute passes- long enough for the tightness in her chest to loosen just a little. When her pulse finally slows, she gets up and walks out of the bathroom.

She’s halfway back to the table when someone steps into her path. The chair-stealer from earlier. He reeks- alcohol, cigarettes, and something sour underneath it all.

“Hiya, gorgeous,” he bellows, far too loud for the space between them. His breath fans across her cheek, sour and hot.

Agatha’s jaw tenses. “Sorry,” she says, curt and clipped, “not interested.” She steps to the right.

He steps too, instantly following.

What a dick.

She tries the left.

He blocks her again, leaning in with a pout that would be comical if it weren’t so revolting. His eyes are glazed, unfocused, scanning her up and down.

“Oh, come on, give me just one chance,” he pleads, slurring as he reaches out and drags the back of his fingers along her arm.

It’s not even a rough touch- just a soft, entitled graze- but every nerve in Agatha’s body goes sharp. A white-hot pulse of disgust shoots up her spine.

“Don’t touch me,” she snaps, ripping her arm away aggressively.

“Hey!”

The shout echoes through the room, causing the man to turn, revealing Rio approaching behind him. She jolts forward, shoving the man hard in the chest, causing him to stumble backwards, liquid toppling over the side of his glass.

“Get your fucking hands off her, motherfucker!” Rio lunges again, posture wild, protective in a way that’s almost animal.

Wanda reacts immediately, stepping up from the table. She grabs Rio from behind, arms hooking around her upper body, heels digging into the sticky floor as Rio tries to twist out of her grip. Wanda tightens her hold.

“Rio, stop- what are you doing?” she mutters sharply, dragging her back step by step.

Rio keeps her glare locked on the man, chest rising and falling fast, her body vibrating with adrenaline. The man, cheeks flushed and eyes wide, shuffles backward toward the safety of his friends, muttering something under his breath but refusing to look at Rio again.

“I’m protecting Agatha from that fucking pervert,” Rio seethes as Wanda pushes her toward the table. Even seated, she looks like she could launch herself back up at any second.

Agatha follows, pulse still racing, confusion tightening in her throat. She can feel eyes on them- Yelena watching with eager interest, Jen with shock written all over her face, and Alice blinking like she’s not sure if she missed a crucial plot point.

Wanda plants Rio firmly in her seat. Rio sighs, shoulders hunched, jaw clenched, breathing through her teeth.

“Yeah, well that pervert is twice your size,” Wanda says, hands on her hips, her voice low but edged. “What exactly was your plan? To get beat up?”

Rio scoffs, tongue against her teeth, head tipped back against the chair.

“No-” Her words slur around the edges. “I was gonna beat him up.”

Wanda lets out a soft, disbelieving snort. “Sure you were, sweetheart.”

Sweetheart.
Agatha’s eye twitches slightly. She would like nothing more than to beat Wanda up.

“How much has she had to drink?” Agatha whispers to Jen, concerned, wondering if Rio had somehow downed 5 shots in the short time since she left.

Jen leans in. “Only two vodka sodas,” she whispers, brows pinched. “Is she a lightweight?”

“Not at all.” Agatha’s confusion deepens.

“Well, she’s fucking drunk either way.”

She sits beside Rio who is slouched low in her chair, one leg bouncing, arms crossed tight over her chest. If Rio was quiet before, now she’s practically dead silent- no reactions, no snark, not even the effort of pretending to follow the conversation. She just stares at the wood grain of the table, eyes unfocused.

Agatha keeps stealing glances at her- quick, nervous, then away again. She hates how Rio looks right now- small, withdrawn, defeated in a way that doesn’t fit her. It scrapes at something uncomfortable inside Agatha, something she’s trying desperately to ignore.

The conversations around them eventually fade into scattered murmurs. Yelena makes a joke about someone at the bar, Alice responds with a snort, Wanda’s drink topples as she gestures.

Then Agatha notices Rio shift.

Slowly, like every movement is heavy, she leans forward and lowers her head onto the table. She folds her arms, rests her cheek on top, and closes her eyes.

The table goes dead silent.

“Is she… going to sleep?” Alice whispers, blinking like she’s not sure whether to be concerned or entertained.

“I guess so,” Wanda answers with a soft laugh, sliding a gentle hand onto Rio’s back. Her fingers begin tracing slow, comforting circles between Rio’s shoulder blades.

Agatha’s jaw locks instantly. Her eyes burn holes into Wanda’s hand- soft and lingering far too long. It makes something ugly flare up inside her chest, a twisted mix of jealousy and protectiveness she absolutely does not have the patience to examine.

She forces herself to look away and pulls out her phone.

Her screen lights up.

Fuck.
10 unread messages.
3 missed calls.

Cold dread crawls up her spine at Ralph’s notifications. She doesn’t even bother reading them- just types back quickly with shaky fingers.

I’ll leave now.

Her stomach turns. She slides the phone into her pocket, breath tightening.

“I have to go,” Agatha says abruptly, pushing her chair back. She snatches her jacket and keys, eyes glued to the floor.

“Already?” Jen asks, disappointed. “It’s only 8:30.”

“Yeah, sorry,” Agatha mutters, throat tight. “Ralph wants me home.”

At that, Rio’s head snaps up like she’s been shocked awake. Her eyes are dazed, unfocused, but locked directly on Agatha.

“Wait, you’re leaving?”

Wanda giggles beside her, brushing a strand of hair from Rio’s face. “Morning, sleepyhead.”

Agatha’s stomach flips- disgust, jealousy, irritation, all tangled together. She refuses to look at Wanda. She refuses to look at Rio too long, either.

Rio doesn’t even glance at Wanda. Her gaze stays on Agatha, heavy and pleading.

“Can you drive me home?” she asks, voice soft and slurred, edges worn down by exhaustion and vodka.

Agatha scoffs. “Why can’t Wanda drive you?” Her tone is sharp, curt- more tired than angry, but irritated enough to cut.

Wanda answers with that obnoxious bright smile that makes Agatha want to hurl a glass across the bar. “Oh, I’m actually taking Jen- besides, I don’t plan on leaving any time soon.”

“Then I’ll call you a cab,” Agatha says, pulling out her phone again.

But she’s immediately met with glares- from everyone at the table. Alice looks at her like she’s heartless, Jen seems disappointed, even Yelena whispers something under her breath in Russian that definitely sounds like seriously?

And then Rio reaches out.

Her hand finds Agatha’s wrist- not forceful, just warm and desperate. Her fingers curl lightly around her, thumb brushing against the inside of Agatha’s arm in a way that sends a hot, unwelcome shiver up her skin.

Rio looks up at her with big, unfocused, heartbreakingly sad eyes. “No, pleaseee,” she slurs, lips pushing into a soft pout.

Agatha feels her phone buzz in her pocket- Ralph, again.

Her chest constricts painfully.

She looks from the glowing missed call notification to Rio’s hand on her wrist- to Rio’s face, vulnerable in a way she hasn’t seen in a while.

She closes her eyes. Exhales.

“Fine,” she mutters, voice low and resigned. “Let’s go.”

Rio’s fingers tighten around her wrist like she was afraid she’d disappear. As Agatha pulls her up and turns toward the door, Rio doesn’t drop her hand. Instead, she lets Agatha tug her along through the bar, fingers loosely grasping her wrist like a lost child. The moment they step outside, cool night air rushes against their faces, a stark difference from the thick, sticky warmth of the bar. Agatha inhales sharply- fresh air filling her lungs, bringing relief, clarity- before letting out a deep breath.

They walk toward the car, slowly, relaxed. Agatha opens the passenger door for Rio, and only then does Rio finally release her wrist. The warmth disappears immediately, swept away by a gust of cold wind that leaves Agatha’s skin feeling strangely exposed.

Rio bends to climb in but stumbles, knees buckling. Agatha reacts instinctively, grabbing her elbow, steadying her with a firm hand.

“Careful there,” she murmurs.

Rio giggles- soft, airy, almost childlike- and Agatha feels her chest loosen. For the first time in what feels like forever, she doesn’t feel anger when she looks at her. She doesn’t see betrayal, or tension, or twenty years of unresolved mess. She sees youth. Innocence. A pure, unfiltered joy lighting up Rio’s face at the smallest things.

And God, how can anyone hate that?

Agatha hands Rio her phone. “Put your address in.”

Rio types it in clumsily, tongue peeking slightly out of the corner of her mouth in concentration. Agatha shuts the passenger door, trying to shut down her feelings with it, trying to rebuild the emotional armor she somehow keeps losing.

She circles the car and climbs into the driver’s seat. The headlights sweep across the dim street as she pulls away from the curb, the world outside tinted a soft yellow by streetlamps.

“What’s your favorite plant?” Rio asks suddenly, leaning toward her, stretching out her seatbelt.

She’s too close- far too close- elbows braced on the center console, breath warm on Agatha’s cheek.

“Sit in your seat properly,” Agatha orders, giving her a sharp glare.

Rio obeys, flopping back into her seat with an exaggerated compliance.

“Okay, but tell me though. What’s your favorite plant?”

“I don’t have a favorite plant.”

Rio looks genuinely offended. “How do you not have a favorite plant?”

“Because I’m not an environmental freak.”

“Just pick one.”

“Someone’s chatty,” Agatha mutters, shooting another glare.

“Pick one, Agatha.”

“Fine. Broccoli.”

Rio cackles- head thrown back, loud and delighted.

“That doesn’t count.”

“What’s yours?” Agatha asks, mostly to shut her up.

“Easy. Azaleas.” Rio brightens instantly, voice energizing. “Y’know they belong to the genus Rhododendron in the Ericaceae family? And they’re native to hilly regions in Asia and North America? Oh, and did you know they’re toxic? It’s cause they have grayanotoxins, so I wouldn’t recommend eating them if you were planning to.”

“I wasn’t.”

“Good,” Rio says, before launching right back in- waving her hands, rambling about soil acidity, watering schedules, and shade tolerance.

Agatha barely hears any of it. Not the words, anyway.

She hears the excitement in Rio’s voice, sees the animated way she moves, the way her eyes sparkle when she talks about something she loves. She watches Rio glow- really glow- and something soft unfurls inside her chest, warm and tender and terrifying.

She smiles quietly to herself.

The rest of the drive passes in a blur of plant facts and Rio’s laughter until Agatha pulls up to the curb outside her house.

Rio blinks at the window. “When did we get here?”

“Jesus Christ,” Agatha mutters with a laugh under her breath. “How’d you get so drunk?”

Rio shrugs and fumbles for the door handle. Agatha climbs out and walks around the car to open her door. She offers a hand, and Rio takes it immediately- tight grip, unwavering- as Agatha helps her out.

But Rio doesn’t let go.

Instead, she clings to Agatha like she needs her to stay upright, leaning into her side. Agatha presses an arm around her waist automatically, guiding her toward the porch. Their hips bump with each uneven step, and every brush of contact sends a shock of heat through Agatha’s pulse.

“Agatha,” Rio mumbles, wobbling, “I don’t feel so good.”

Agatha freezes, turning toward her. “As in-?”

Rio lurches forward, fumbling in her pocket for her keys. “I’m gonna throw up.”

“Shit.”

Agatha grabs Rio’s shaking hands, helping her slot the key into the lock. Their fingers brush- warm, clumsy, and frantically. The door swings open just in time for Rio to bolt inside, racing down the hallway.

Agatha slams the door shut and runs after her.

She steps into the bathroom just as Rio collapses over the toilet, gripping the bowl as she heaves. Clear liquid splashes into the porcelain, her shoulders trembling with the force of it.

Agatha moves toward her without thinking. Her stomach twists- not with disgust, but something sharper, something protective.

Rio groans, and Agatha kneels beside her, brushing hair away from her face. For a moment, everything else vanishes- the bar, Wanda, Ralph, the messages waiting on her phone.

It’s just Rio, fragile and sick and small. She gathers Rio’s hair back into a loose ponytail, the strands sliding soft between her fingers as Rio retches violently into the bowl, throat raw. The sound echoes off the tiles, too loud for the tiny bathroom. Agatha doesn’t even realize when her other hand begins rubbing slow, comforting circles along Rio’s spine- instinct, muscle memory, something old and buried rising up in her like second nature. Eventually Rio’s stomach empties, and she slumps sideways, back thudding softly against the wall. She wipes her mouth with the back of her hand and lets her head fall back, eyes glassy.

“Why don’t you want me?” she whispers.

Agatha goes still.

Her heart stops, then starts again too fast, thudding against her ribs. The giggles from earlier are gone, washed out of Rio’s bloodstream with the liquor, replaced with something stripped and aching and unbearably honest.

“Rio, you’re drunk,” Agatha says, standing up because sitting still makes the room feel too tight. Her tone hardens on instinct- her emotional armor sliding back into place.

“No I’m nooot,” Rio mumbles, head rolling weakly from side to side.

Sure you aren’t, Agatha thinks, mentally rolling her eyes.

“Just tell me,” Rio breathes.

“We’re not doing this right now,” Agatha snaps. “You need to go to bed.”

Rio pouts like an immature kid, crossing her arms before staggering to her feet. She brushes past Agatha in a wobbling march toward the stairs, muttering something unintelligible under her breath. Agatha blows out a shaky exhale and heads to the kitchen, opening every wrong cabinet until she finally finds a glass. She fills it to the brim with water, rummages through drawers until she finds Advil, and shakes two pills into her palm.

By the time she reaches the top of the stairs, the house is silent again. She follows the sliver of light coming from the room at the end of the hallway. Rio is sprawled on her bed, already fully unconscious, hair plastered messily across her face, mouth open in the ugliest, most endearing sleep known to man.

Agatha sets the water and pills on the nightstand and turns to leave- but then her eyes land on a photo frame. Before she can stop herself, she picks it up.

It’s them. In college. Rio’s arm slung over her shoulder, mid laugh as her eyes are lit up. Agatha’s own smile- bright, unguarded, real- hits her like a punch to the gut.

Has she really kept this all this time?

Her throat tightens. She sets the frame down carefully, trying not to make a noise.

Rio’s head is tilted at an uncomfortable angle, so Agatha grabs a pillow from the opposite side of the bed, leans over, and gently lifts Rio’s heavy head, sliding the pillow underneath, sitting her upright. Just in case she throws up again.

As she turns away, something shiny on the dresser catches her eye- glittery and impossible to miss. A scrapbook.

Her stomach drops.

She walks towards it without thinking, gravity pulling her over. The cover is decorated in sparkly silver stickers.

Agatha + Rio

Her breath stutters. Her thumb drifts over the raised letters, memories slamming into her all at once- laying together on the dorm floor, Rio’s sparkly nail polish getting everywhere, both of them laughing until they couldn’t breathe.

She opens it.

Pages after pages are plastered with their faces. Agatha chuckles softly, remembering Rio’s deep photography phase, laid out here in chaotic, loving detail. Them at parties. At coffee shops. Agatha studying, much too often. Their first date. Rio’s kiss on Agatha’s cheek while Agatha beams like she has no idea life can hurt yet.

Agatha smiles.

Then she turns the page.

And the smile falls.

The next few pages are pregnancy photos- Agatha with her swollen belly, Rio kissing her stomach, both of them glowing, laughing, planning a future that never came.

Her throat closes around a sob.

She flips ahead- one page, then two, then the rest of the book- empty. Blank. It’s as if their life just… stopped.

Agatha slams the scrapbook shut, almost flinching at the sound. She wipes her face with her sleeve, breath shaking, and forces herself to step out of the room. She flicks off the light, letting the darkness swallow everything behind her.

At the door, she looks back one last time.

Rio sleeps quietly, face soft and barely visible in the moonlight. Beautiful. Familiar.

Agatha swallows hard, heading downstairs, before she steps into the cold night, pulling the front door shut behind her- chest aching like something inside has finally broken apart.

2005

“Agatha?” Rio’s voice calls from behind the door, quiet and trembling around the edges. “Are you almost done?”

“Yeah… almost.”

Agatha’s own voice sounds distant to her. She stands up on unsteady legs, flushes the toilet, and sets the pregnancy test on the cold bathroom counter. Her hands are shaking so badly she has to steady herself on the edge of the sink.

The bathroom feels too small, too bright, like the fluorescent dorm light is exposing every thought she’s trying to push away.

She exhales once, then walks to the door and pulls it open.

Rio is right there. Waiting.

Her expression is tight, nervous. Without saying a word, she slips past Agatha and into the bathroom, closing the door gently behind them. Rio pulls out her phone, thumb hovering over the screen before she finally taps start on a three-minute timer.

The girls stand shoulder to shoulder over the test.
Neither breathes properly.

Agatha crosses her arms, uncrosses them, then grips the counter so hard her knuckles go white.
Rio keeps glancing between her and the small plastic stick like she’s trying to measure which one she should be focusing on.

The seconds crawl by slowly.
Agatha can hear the blood rushing in her ears.
Rio hesitates, then reaches out and takes Agatha’s hand- fingers threading through hers, giving the gentlest squeeze. The gesture isn’t enough to calm her, but it keeps her upright and stable.

A minute passes. Then another. Neither moves. Neither speaks.

When the timer finally goes off- an abrupt, shrill beep that slices through the silence- both of them jump, already on edge.

Rio’s hand clenches around Agatha’s.
Agatha steps forward.

She reaches out with trembling fingers and flips the test over.

Two bright pink lines. Not faint. Not ambiguous.

Bold and undeniable.

Rio inhales sharply over Agatha’s shoulder, the sound almost a gasp. Agatha’s vision blurs for a second, her eyes locked on those lines as if they’re burning themselves permanently into her memory.

Rio squeezes her hand again- firmer this time, but she’s still careful and delicate in her movements, like she’s afraid Agatha might shatter.

“Agatha… I-” Rio starts, but the words fail to leave her mouth. She tries again and fails, lips parting with nothing but a soft exhale.

Agatha doesn’t look at her.
She can’t.
Her eyes are glued to the test, mind running a mile a minute.

“I can call the doctor,” Rio finally says, voice low and cautious, reassuring. “We can book you an abortion as soon as you feel-”

“No.”

Agatha turns to her, tears already gathering along her lower lashes. The fear is still there, the sense of dread, but something else has broken through- something raw and vulnerable, something filled with just a silver of hope and purpose.

“No, I- I want to keep it.”
Her hand drifts down, hovering lightly over her abdomen as if she could feel something there already. “That’s my baby in there. My baby.”

Rio stares at her, stunned into silence. Her brows knit together, worry and disbelief twisting in her expression, not wanting Agatha to rush into anything she’ll regret.

“Agatha, are you sure?” she asks gently, “this is a difficult situation. No one will judge you for-”

“Rio, I’m sure.”

Her voice is steadier now, though tears spill over the moment she blinks. A small smile forms on her lips- fragile, trembling, but genuine.

Agatha presses her palm more firmly against her stomach, the gesture instinctive, protective. She looks back up at Rio, fear etched onto her face, but smile breaking through anyway, eyes filled with a drive that Rio has never seen. Agatha laughs softly, heart fluttering.

“I’m gonna be a mom.”

2025

Sunday

10:42 Hey it’s Rio. Thanks for taking care of me last night.

Rio stares at the message as if it’s taunting her. She reads it once. Twice. Then ten more times, combing through every letter, every space, trying to predict all possible interpretations before she finally squeezes her eyes shut and hits send.

The moment it sends, panic hits.

She tosses her phone onto the bed like it’s burning her, scrambling to her dresser and yanking out a pair of jeans. She’s halfway through stepping into them- pants unbuttoned, hanging off her hips- when the anxiety becomes unbearable.

She sprints back to her bed.

No response.

She flops down, groaning dramatically into the quiet room, thumb hovering over the power button as if turning off the phone will make everything easier.

Just as she’s about to do it, the three dots appear, indicating that Agatha is typing.

Rio jerks upright, pulse skyrocketing. Her leg bounces wildly as she gnaws at a nail already bitten raw.

The message appears.

10:44 Oh yeah, it was nothing. How do you have my number?

Her stomach sinks a little.
Before she can psych herself out again, she types back.

10:44 I have a vague memory of begging Jen for it when you went to the bathroom. Not totally sure though.

She hits send, then holds the phone in both hands, squeezing softly with adrenaline.

The bubbles appear again- and disappear just as fast.

Rio stares, blinking.
Waiting.
They don’t return.

She tries to brush it off, but her face stays tight. Her chest feels heavy. She reads the message again.

It was nothing.

Then again.

It was nothing.

The logical part of her brain tries to remind her that it’s a common phrase. People use it lightly all the time. It can absolutely just mean “don’t worry about it.”

But the rest of her brain is clawing at her ribs.

Because she remembers last night- after they left the bar, anyway. She was drunk out of her mind, yet somehow every moment with Agatha felt razor-sharp and impossible to forget. The heat of Agatha’s palm on her wrist, the way Agatha steadied her elbow when she nearly fell. The quiet whisper of “careful,” the warm breath brushing her cheek. The way her hand moved gently on Rio’s back, rubbing slow circles the same way she used to in college, the same way that made Rio feel safe.

It was nothing.

She bites her cuticle until it stings, until she tastes blood.

Agatha drove her home. Held her hair. Got her water and Advil and tucked her in like she wasn’t someone she supposedly hated. Like she wasn’t someone Agatha had every reason to avoid.

Rio can’t stop thinking.
It meant something.
At least to her.

It felt like a shift. A tiny one, but a shift nonetheless. One she didn’t dare hope for, but still- Agatha had cared. Even a little.

Right?

She presses the heel of her hand to her forehead, willing the optimistic thoughts away. Forcing herself to breathe. Because she knows what the safer narrative is. The realistic one.

Agatha was just being a decent human being. Kind in the way anyone would be to a sick, drunk acquaintance. Nothing more. Anyone would’ve done the same. Anyone would’ve held her hair.

She lies back on the bed, staring at the ceiling, stomach twisting.

If there’s one thing she knows for sure, it’s that Agatha doesn’t want her back in her life. Doesn’t want reminders. Doesn’t want her.

Because Agatha said it herself.

It was nothing.

Saturday

Rio picks up her phone, unlocking it with one hand while she brushes her teeth with the other. She opens Instagram, finds Billy’s account, and opens a new chat.

7:43 Did Agatha get the flowers I sent yesterday?

7:43 oh so it was you who sent them? ya she did they arrived before she got to work so i just left them in her office. what were they for?

7:44 None of your business. Just making sure she got them. Did she seem okay yesterday?

7:44 well she was pretty short tempered but its agatha so im used to it..

Rio frowns and leaves a thumbs-down reaction on the message before locking her phone. She spits toothpaste into the sink, checks the time, and her stomach drops. She tosses her toothbrush back into the cup, bolts down the stairs, then doubles back to the kitchen to grab her daily unsweetened iced tea- because she truly can’t leave the house without it. She mutters at herself for being late- again- while sprinting to the car.

She slams the accelerator, speeding toward the park in her worn Coven for Climate t-shirt.

Her mind is already spiraling.
Seeing Agatha again makes her chest tighten in a way she can’t name. She regrets sending the flowers. Regrets writing the note. Regrets every decision she made yesterday, even though at the time it felt necessary. Obvious. The only thing she could do.

Now she’s not so sure.

Was the note too forward? Too personal? Too condescending?
Did Agatha think she was crossing a line?

Again?

Rio winces, tapping the steering wheel with restless fingers. Maybe she should’ve stuck to a text. Something neutral, something easy to dismiss. Flowers were a statement. A note was even worse. And that message- “take care of yourself today”- felt right yesterday, but now she can’t stop overanalyzing it from every angle until it sounds dramatic, or patronizing, or just plain embarrassing.

She takes a sip of iced tea, but it just sits in her empty stomach like a stone, swirling around until she feels nauseous.

She tries to redirect her brain to work- to the volunteers she has to manage, to the payments needing double-checking, to the mostly unplanned fundraising gala next week that she still has yet to organize- but none of it sticks. Her mind keeps drifting back to Agatha. To the way she looked last week when she drove Rio home, held her hair. To the small things she hasn’t stopped replaying even though she knows she shouldn’t.

And now she’s sent flowers.
She curses herself, remembering Agatha’s reaction from the last time she brought up Nicky.

And now she’s gone and done it again.

Perfect.

She tightens her grip on the wheel as she turns into the parking lot, her pulse thudding in her ears.

“Just act normal,” she mutters, parking crooked. “Just… be normal.”

But her hands are already shaking when she climbs out of the car- because nothing about Agatha has felt normal for a long time.

She jogs over to where she sees the tents already set up. Volunteers are scattered around the park, picking up trash and chatting among themselves. Rio scans the area for Agatha, hoping that actually seeing her will calm the chaos in her chest.

Wanda catches her first.

“Rio!” she calls, walking up quickly.

Rio looks at her- then immediately regrets looking, because Wanda’s shirt rides up a bit in the breeze, showing off her toned stomach, the way her waist sinches perfectly. Rio forces her eyes back up.

“Hey, Wanda,” Rio says, trying not to sound frazzled. “Sorry I’m late. Everything set up okay?”

“Yep! Tents are up, and the volunteers already started.”

“Perfect. Thanks.” Rio exhales, relieved- until her eyes drift and land on Agatha a few yards away. Agatha is using a trash-grabber like she’s handling radioactive waste, arm locked straight out, upper lip curled. Rio’s heart jumps and stutters. She starts to move toward her.

But Wanda steps into her path.

“Hey, um- Rio?”

Rio’s whole body tenses. “Yeah?” she says, eyes sliding past Wanda again toward Agatha.

“So… about the gala next week…” Wanda shifts nervously. “I was wondering if you maybe- wanted to go with me? As my plus one?” She laughs awkwardly. “Or I guess technically I’d be yours, since it’s your event and everything.”

Rio freezes. She looks at Wanda.
Then at Agatha.
Then back at Wanda.
Then Agatha again, who is now glaring at a plastic bottle like it personally offended her.

Rio’s chest tightens. She takes a quiet breath.

“That’s really sweet, Wanda,” she says softly. “But I think I’m actually gonna go with someone else.”

“Oh!” Wanda says, voice shooting up an octave. She forces a smile. “No, totally fine! I’ll just-ask someone else, yeah.”

Rio gives her a small, apologetic smile. “Sorry.”

Before Wanda can say anything else, Rio turns on her heel and heads straight for Agatha, because if she doesn’t talk to her right now, she might actually combust.

Agatha shakes the grabber aggressively, trying to fling a crumpled fast-food wrapper into the trash bag. It clings stubbornly to the metal prongs. She growls under her breath, giving it another sharp shake.

“You know you can also use your hands,” Rio says as she approaches, voice lighter than she feels. Agatha jumps- actually jumps- before whipping around to face her. “It’s why we have gloves.”

Agatha’s nose scrunches in disgust. “No, thank you. That’s much more intimate than anyone should be with garbage.”

Rio has to bite back a smile. “Right…” she murmurs, bending to grab a pair of gloves. She slides them on with practiced ease, stepping into Agatha’s space before her brain can overthink it. Agatha stiffens, but doesn’t move away.

Rio gently detangles the wrapper from the prongs, her fingers brushing the metal where Agatha’s had been. She tosses it cleanly into the bag.

“Thanks,” Agatha says, tone clipped and stubborn, as if gratitude physically pains her.

Rio opens her mouth to say something neutral, something normal- but what comes out is, “Did you get my flowers?”

Her stomach drops. Oh my god. She pinches her own thigh behind the trash bag, heat shooting up her neck. She can practically feel her pulse pounding in her ears.

Agatha freezes. Shoulders tightening. Spine straightening. She looks down at the dirt, the grass, anywhere but Rio.

“I did,” she says softly.

Rio holds her breath, waiting. Waiting for a lecture about boundaries. Or a “don’t do that again.” Or cold silence. Her nails dig crescent moons into her palms, piercing the skin.

Agatha swallows. “It was… nice.” Her voice wavers just slightly, barely noticeable. “Thank you. For remembering.”

Rio lets out the breath. The relief hits her so hard her knees nearly give out. “Of course,” she says, voice barely above a whisper.

A breeze blows between them, tugging at Agatha’s hair. Rio watches as she tucks a strand behind her ear before twirling it around her fingers- an old nervous habit Rio hasn’t seen in ages- and her chest tightens.

“Are you doing okay?” Rio asks, forcing her voice to stay steady.

Agatha hesitates. She toes at a pebble with her shoe, jaw tightening, eyes flicking toward the woods like she’s afraid to meet Rio’s gaze.

“I’m hanging in there,” she says finally. And then, in classic Agatha fashion, she locks onto a stray piece of trash like it’s suddenly the most urgent task on earth. She reaches out with the grabber, arm outstretched, every muscle saying please don’t make me talk about my feelings.

Rio watches her for a moment- how her fingers tremble just slightly, how the grabber wobbles, how she tries so hard to look composed.

And Rio’s heart aches in her chest.

She considers stepping forward- just one step- and pulling Agatha into her arms. She imagines smoothing her palm over Agatha’s hair, whispering you’re okay, I’ve got you, the way she used to back when comfort didn’t feel like trespassing.

But she stops herself.
She’s already used up all the boldness she’s allowed for the week- maybe the month. One more step, one more touch, and Agatha might retreat, slam every door shut, lock every window. And Rio can’t risk going back to square one. Not after the fragile softness she finally saw in Agatha’s eyes.

So instead, she gives her a gentle, careful smile- the kind that doesn’t push, doesn’t pry- and forces herself to turn away. Her chest aches at the sound of Agatha’s grabber clicking behind her, loud and busy.

At her car, Rio pops the trunk and lifts out two pots of white azaleas, handling them with care. Their delicate petals tremble in the breeze. She grabs a small shovel, balancing everything in her arms as she makes her way to a quieter part of the park- the edge of the woods, where the world feels a little softer, a little sweeter.

She kneels, pressing her knees into the damp soil. The ground is cool beneath her palms as she works the shovel into the earth, pulling up chunks of dirt. The repetitive motion steadies her breathing, though guilt nips at her.

She should’ve done this yesterday.
It was supposed to be yesterday.

But she’d been swamped- meetings, emails, volunteers, logistics- and she’d told herself she’d get to it afterward. She didn’t. And the shame of being late claws at her ribs.

It’s fine, she tells herself. It’s only one day. He wouldn’t mind.
But she feels the lie lodge itself in her throat.

The hole deepens, dark and clean. She reaches for the azaleas. White- symbol of purity, innocence, rebirth. She loosens each plant from its pot, lowering them gently into the ground like they’re fragile, about to break. She fills the gaps with soft handfuls of soil, patting the earth gently.

When she’s done, she sits back on her heels. The flowers stand bright against the muted forest backdrop, glowing almost unnaturally where the sunlight filters through the leaves. Her chest tightens and her vision blurs.

19 years ago yesterday, everything was ripped apart.
19 years ago yesterday, their entire world was swallowed whole.
19 years ago yesterday… Nicky died.

Her throat closes as the name echoes inside her. A lifetime ago, yet still very raw.

Every year, no matter what, she plants azaleas for him. For their baby boy who never got to open his eyes. For the life that ended before it ever began. For the part of her heart she never got back.

“I love you, Nicky,” Rio whispers, voice cracking as tears spill down her cheeks. She brushes them away quickly, almost scolding herself, even though no one is around to see.

She stands, dusting dirt off her knees, taking one last steadying breath before turning back toward the noise of volunteers and tents and responsibility.

Back to her duties.
Back to holding herself together.

When Wanda arrives with catering and calls lunch, Rio takes that as her cue to get away from the chaos of volunteer management and finally- finally- open her laptop to do the work she’d been dreading all month. She drops into the seat across from Billy, who is hunched over his phone, scrolling with intense focus.

“Hey,” Rio says, nudging his foot under the table, “wanna help me with something?”

Billy doesn’t even look up. “Depends. Is it fun?”

“The fundraising gala is one week today,” Rio says, clasping her hands together. “And I’ve procrastinated all of it. Wanna help me plan?”

“Uhh-”

“Great! Thank you!” Rio chirps, dragging the laptop closer.

Billy blinks at her slowly. “I didn’t say yes.”

“You were going to.”

Billy sighs, defeated. “I absolutely was not. But fine. What do you have so far?”

Rio spins the laptop around, revealing a completely blank document- unnamed and untitled.

Billy stares at it. Then at her. Then back at it.

“Rio,” Billy says solemnly, “you are the reason I’m on anxiety medication.”

“Oh relax,” Rio says, waving a hand. “We’ll be fine. This is how I do all my work.”

“That’s the problem.” Billy mutters. “Okay, first question: what’s the theme?”

“Gala.”

Billy looks up in slow motion. “That’s… not a theme.”

“It could be.”

“It’s literally not.”

“Ugh. Fine,” Rio huffs. “Then I don’t know.”

Billy taps his chin thoughtfully. “Okay. What about… enchanted forest?”

Rio squints. “Like… sparkly trees?”

“Sure… but more elegant,” Billy explains. “Twinkly lights, lots of greenery, maybe some fog machines if you want to be dramatic-”

Rio tilts her head, considering. “I actually don’t hate that… you’re pretty good at this.”

Billy beams. “Thank you. I’m gay, so, trust me- I know how to plan a good party.”

Rio rolls her eyes at the stereotype before pushing the laptop toward him, smiling. Billy groans, taking the hint and pulling the computer fully in front of him. He cracks his knuckles before settling his fingers on the keyboard. “Fine. Let’s make a checklist of what we need to get done.”

Rio clasps her hands and rests her chin on them with a bright smile. “I’ll supervise.”

“You mean you’ll sit there and stare at me doing the work?” Billy says, already typing.

“Same thing.”

Billy glances up, eyes narrowing. “You know supervisors actually do things, right?”

“Not in my world.”

He shakes his head, muttering something about needing to be paid, but he keeps typing anyway- filling the document with notes, bullet points, links.

Rio watches him work, impressed. He’s actually really good at this- organized, thoughtful. His typing grows faster as more ideas flow through him.

“Okay,” Billy says after a minute, “we need invitations, decor, catering-”

“Catering’s covered,” Rio says automatically.

Billy suddenly stops typing, eyes narrowing at Rio. “Can I assume that means Wanda’s doing it?”

Rio blinks, genuinely confused at his tone. “She offered.”

Billy hums, drawing out the sound like he knows something she doesn’t. “Mhm. Okay…” His voice drifts off as he glances to the side. “Speaking of…”

“Hey guys!” Wanda’s voice rings out like a bell, bright and warm.

Rio looks up and forces a smile. “Hey, Wanda.” She nods politely before redirecting her attention to her iced tea, taking a long sip as if it might save her from further conversation.

Wanda slides into the seat beside her, their legs brushing. Rio stiffens- just barely- but enough that Billy notices.

“We were just talking about catering for the gala,” Billy says, still typing furiously. “Wanda, can you give me an itemized list of what you’re planning so I can get that finalized?”

He doesn’t look up- not even once. He types like he’s possessed, like the fate of the event rests entirely on his shoulders, which, honestly, it kind of does now.

Wanda raises an eyebrow and turns toward Rio, curiosity flickering across her face. “You’re having Billy plan the gala for you?”

“He’s just helping out,” Rio says quickly, fidgeting with her hands under the table. She suddenly feels guilty- like she’s exploiting a child volunteer, which technically she sort of is.

“Seems to me like he’s doing all your dirty work,” Wanda teases, her laugh light as she flashes Rio a playful grin.

“He’s getting paid for it, so…” Rio blurts, improvising the defense instantly.

Billy freezes mid-keystroke. “I am?” he asks, finally looking up.

Rio glares at him as if he’s supposed to already know what she decided five seconds ago. “Duh,” she says, voice flat.

Wanda snorts. Billy mutters something under his breath that she can’t quite catch.

“I’ll send you my list later, Billy,” Wanda says, turning back to her lunch and taking a bite of her burger.

Rio exhales, shoulders lowering. The tension that had been crawling up her spine loosens, replaced by a small swell of relief. For the first time all month, the gala doesn’t feel like it’s looming over her. Billy’s help meant she could finally breathe. Focus. Think.

Now she can redirect that mental space to more important things. More complicated things.

Her eyes drift.

Agatha sits across the park with Jen, laughing loudly at something, the sound carrying on the breeze. It’s not her usual polite chuckle or unimpressed sigh. It’s a real laugh. A sudden, soft burst of joy. Rio watches her head tilt back, the sunlight catching on the curve of her cheekbone, making her smile glow.

Rio’s heart stutters.

She’s relieved- genuinely- that Agatha isn’t completely devastated today. Not hollow, not snapping at everyone. Not burying herself in work to avoid thinking about… everything.

She’s okay. Enough to laugh, to smile at least.

As people finish lunch and drift back to their stations, Rio sees Agatha push back her chair and stand. Something inside her jolts- a spark of impulsive bravery.

She calls out, “Agatha!”

Agatha stops mid-step. Turns. Her expression, neutral at first, hardens when she sees Rio jogging toward her- which makes Rio’s heart climb up her throat.

Rio slows as she reaches her, breath uneven- not from running, but from the many nerves that feel like they’re crawling under her skin.

“Hey,” Rio says, voice gentler now, almost careful.

Agatha holds her gaze for a beat, unreadable as always, before raising a brow. Waiting.

Rio regrets every choice she’s ever made.

The second Agatha’s hard eyes land on her, flat and unreadable, Rio feels her stomach drop to the ground. Obviously, the flowers weren’t enough. Obviously, one small gesture wasn’t anywhere close to dissolving years of hurt or anger or whatever lives inside Agatha’s chest when she looks at her.

Rio swallows hard. Too late to back out now. Too late to pretend this wasn’t a mistake.

“Do you want to go to the gala with me next week?” she blurts, aiming for casual but landing somewhere between breathless and terrified. Her voice shakes. She hates that it shakes.

Agatha frowns. Frowns. Like Rio had just hurled a stack of insults at her.

“You and me?” Agatha spits, disbelief dripping from every syllable.

Rio panics. “Look, I don’t wanna go either,” she says with a shrug she hopes seems effortless, “but we could just- y’know- get drunk and stand in the corner judging people like pretentious assholes. Like old times?”

A soft memory flashes through her mind- Rio and Agatha leaning against grimy frat house walls, whispering brutal commentary into each other’s ears, laughing until they were bent over.

Agatha tilts her head, eyes narrowing just slightly. Rio thinks- hopes- for half a second that she’s considering it, that she might actually say yes.

But then Agatha mutters, tone completely flat, “I already told Ralph about it, so I’m gonna go with him. Sorry.”

The sorry isn’t apologetic. Not in the slightest.

Rio’s heart sinks.

“Oh,” she manages, nodding too quickly, too stiffly, trying her best to keep her face neutral even though it feels like it’s cracking. She can’t think of a single word to say. Her throat feels full. Heavy. Her mouth opens- then snaps closed.

There’s nothing she can say that won’t make this worse.

So she turns. Walks away.

“Rio-” Agatha calls, but it’s barely audible. Rio’s ears are ringing like she’s underwater. Her chest feels tight, her breath shaky and uneven.

It was stupid of her to think that Agatha would even consider going with her.

Stupid of her to think that they were on better terms.

Stupid of her to think Agatha had stopped hating her, even slightly.

Stupid of her to think the ride home, the hair holding meant anything to Agatha. Like how it did to her.

It was stupid of her to push a step too far. To cross the hard-drawn, obvious line. Once again.

So she doesn’t turn back. She can’t. If she sees Agatha’s face right now- any version of it- she’ll crumble.

Instead, she beelines toward Wanda, who’s sorting through a box of old gloves. Rio taps her shoulder, harder than necessary.

Wanda spins around, smiling. “Hey, Rio-”

“Change of plans,” Rio says, voice clipped. “I’m free to go to the gala with you.”
