Chapter 6: Chapter Six

Chapter Notes

this might be my peak

See the end of the chapter for more notes

2005

Rio watches Agatha slice through the blue paper, her movements sharp, clipped- like she’s concentrating too hard, like she’s trying not to think too much. Rio pretends to focus on her own uneven star, but really she can’t stop watching the crease between Agatha’s brows.

“Hand me the glue dots,” Agatha says.

Rio passes them over, squinting at the mess on her own craft, looking nothing like what a crib mobile should be. “We should’ve gone with green. Or yellow. Y’know… something neutral. We don’t know the gender yet.”

“It’s a boy,” Agatha insists, “I have a gut feeling.”

Rio can’t help it- she snorts. “Yeah? And when was the last time your gut feeling was right? The ice cream dilemma?”

Agatha shoots her a look. “That was one time.”

“It’s happened many times.”

“Whatever,” Agatha mutters. “This time I’m right.”

Rio softens. She knows Agatha’s pretending at confidence- she can hear the tiny wobble under the surface. So she lets her tease, lets her feel normal for a moment. “Okay. Fine. But we’re buying gender-neutral clothes. I’m not letting us waste money on boy clothes just to replace them later.”

Agatha freezes- just barely- but Rio sees it. She always sees it.

“Oh,” Agatha says slowly. “We’re buying clothes?”

Rio’s heartbeat skips. She hears the real questions buried underneath this one.

Are you sticking around? Are we in this together?

Rio’s mouth goes dry. She hates how scared Agatha looks asking something so small.

“Yeah.” She tries to sound casual but it comes out soft. “I mean… yeah, of course.”

Agatha stops cutting. For the first time all afternoon, she looks Rio full in the face, and Rio feels like her ribs are too tight.

“We are?” Agatha asks- quiet, unsure.

Rio gently takes the scissors from her hand, sets them aside. She makes sure her voice stays steady. She makes sure her hands stay calm.

“Agatha,” she says, “of course I’m helping. I’m not letting you do this alone.”

Agatha doesn’t say anything, but Rio sees her shoulders ease, sees the tension behind her eyes shift into something like relief- something brittle, fragile.

“You mean that?” Agatha whispers.

Rio nods, slowly, deliberately. “I wouldn’t be here cutting crooked paper stars if I didn’t. I’m here. For real. Every late night, every early morning. I’ve got you.”

Agatha’s breath hitches. Rio feels it in her chest.

“You’re staying,” Agatha says, soft, like she’s afraid she’ll scare her away.

“I’m staying,” Rio repeats.

And then Agatha leans in- sudden, desperate, emotional in a way Rio recognizes but has tried so hard not to touch.

Rio’s heart stutters. She wants it- God, she wants it- but not if Agatha isn’t fully present, not after everything. Not after what Ralph took from her.

Rio lifts a hand, stopping Agatha gently, fingers barely brushing her arm.

“Agatha,” she whispers, voice trembling, “wait- are you sure? I need you to be sure. After… everything… I don’t want you to rush into this, not if you’re not ready.”

Agatha’s lips part, and Rio swears she’s going to break in half from the weight in her eyes.

“I’m sure,” Agatha breathes. “Rio… I’ve never been more sure.”

Rio searches her face- her eyes, her breath, her steadiness. She sees it. The clarity. The choice. Not panic, not fear.

So Rio nods.

And Agatha kisses her.

It’s soft at first, barely there, almost like she’s rediscovering what it’s supposed to be like. Rio melts into it, her hand sliding hesitantly- gently- to Agatha’s waist. She keeps the pressure light, careful, giving Agatha every chance to pull away.

But Agatha doesn’t. She leans closer, surer, and Rio feels something in herself finally unclench.

When they part, Agatha is smiling- tired, shaky, but real.

Rio smiles back, brushing her thumb across her cheek. “Okay,” Agatha murmurs. “Maybe we can get the green paper too.”

Rio lets out a breathy laugh. “Good. Because your blue stars were ugly anyway.”

“Hey!”

Rio kisses her again- small, safe, sweet.

Agatha smiles softly. “You’re really sticking around?”

“I’m not going anywhere,” Rio whispers. “I promise.”

2025

“Agatha, let’s go!” Ralph shouts from downstairs, his voice echoing through the house.

“I’m coming!” she calls back, breath already tight in her chest as she drags a brush through her hair one last time. She smooths the front of her dress, forcing her expression into something neutral when the corner of her mouth itches to curl downward. She stares at her reflection in the mirror- satin clinging perfectly, the dark purple fabric rippling around her thighs as she moves. She doesn’t feel beautiful, exactly, more-so just put-together.

She lifts the dress with one hand and hurries down the stairs. Ralph stands at the door in his suit, jaw tense, foot tapping impatiently.

“Jesus Christ, you take so long,” he groans, snatching the keys from the table and yanking open the door.

Agatha’s frown forms before she can stop it. “I would’ve been ready fifteen minutes ago if you hadn’t insisted I straighten my hair.”

“Well, it looks better like that,” he snaps, already halfway down the porch steps, not bothering to check if she’s behind him.

Agatha mutters something under her breath- nothing loud enough to cause a fight- and crouches to fasten the strap of her heel. She hitches the skirt up lightly so it doesn’t drag. Her clutch slips slightly in her hand as she rummages through it, searching for her lip gloss. The small metallic tube finally surfaces, cool against her fingers. She grips it in her palm as she hurries after Ralph.

He unlocks the car but doesn’t open her door. He never does.

She slides into the passenger seat, pulling down the visor to apply gloss in the tiny mirror. Her reflection stares back at her, the glossy sheen on her lips too bright for how she feels. Her now pin-straight hair falls in a neat curtain down her shoulders, sharp and sleek and not at all her.

Ralph throws the car into drive the second she slams the visor shut.

The ride starts in silence- uncomfortable, delicate silence- so Agatha reaches forward and turns up the radio before Ralph can start complaining about anything else. The music fills the car, thin and tinny, but it’s better than him trying to start a conversation that’ll inevitably end in arguing.

He’s in one of those moods. The kind where every exhale sounds like an accusation.

Agatha shifts her gaze to the window. Buildings smear into streaks of color as they pass, neon signs and brick storefronts blending into one long blur. Her hands find the skirt of her gown, fingertips running along the smooth satin. She twists the fabric absentmindedly, the deep purple rich against her pale thigh peeking through the slit. The chilled air from the vent makes goosebumps rise on her skin.

She inhales slowly, willing her heartbeat to settle.

The gala. People. Photographs. Donors. Fake smiles. Ralph glued to her side much too close. And somewhere in that room-

No. She stops herself before her mind says Rio’s name.

She sits up straighter, forces her jaw to unclench, and watches the city rush by. She anxiously plays with her fingers, twisting them together in her lap, her nails digging lightly into her skin. She keeps her gaze pinned downward, watching the shifting folds of her dress instead of looking at Ralph. But he reaches forward anyway, turning down the music with a sharp flick of his wrist before glancing at her.

“We don’t have to stay long, right?” he snaps, one hand slung lazily over the wheel, the other sliding onto her thigh. His fingers press into the silk of her dress, rubbing slow circles like he’s claiming the fabric- and the skin beneath it- as his.

Agatha goes rigid.

“No, just an hour or so,” she answers quietly, still not meeting his eyes. “I just need everyone to see our faces. Get a few pictures. Then we can go.”

“Good.” His voice drops, smug and hungry. “Because you look incredible in that dress. I want to get home as soon as possible. I’d take you right here, right now if I could.”

Agatha forces a laugh, the polite, practiced kind, with no real emotion behind it. Her heart gives a painful squeeze. She pulls her dress tighter over her exposed leg, closing the slit, shielding herself.

The next ten minutes are silent except for the drone of the radio and the thrum of her pulse. When they finally turn into the parking lot of the event hall, Agatha exhales with a sigh of relief.

The building looms ahead- a massive white event hall glowing under warm uplighting, tall columns lining the front. A wide marble staircase leads up to the grand entrance, where guests in shimmering gowns and tailored suits filter in slowly.

Ralph parks aggressively, barely bothering with the lines. He gets out with the usual impatience, while Agatha steps out more carefully, one hand gripping the door frame to balance herself on her tall heels. She releases her dress, letting the dark satin fall in a smooth, heavy cascade to her shoes. She smooths the fabric with trembling palms.

She takes a deep, slow breath.

She prepares herself for an evening of small talk, flashing fake smiles, and doing everything she can to avoid running into Rio. Especially Rio.

Ralph is already halfway up the stairs by the time she starts up them, taking delicate steps so she doesn’t trip. She feels tiny under the towering white pillars, swallowed by the grandeur and the expectation of it all. A doorman opens the entrance for them with a stiff bow.

Inside, the world swallows her whole.

The ballroom is enormous- larger than she imagined- and alive with sound. Music swells from hidden speakers, the bass humming under her feet. Guests cluster in small groups, laughter and conversation echoing through the space.

To the left stretches a polished bar, already crowded with people ordering cocktails. To the right, couples sway on the ballroom floor under warm, sparkling lights. Straight ahead stands a low stage, draped in soft fabric, a podium waiting for speeches later in the night. Round tables fill the space before it, each draped in crisp white linens, floral centerpieces sitting in polished glass vases in the middle.

On the far wall, a massive screen displays the Coven for Climate logo, rotating in slow, purposeful elegance. Faux trees and long vines are tucked into every spare corner, the greenery softening the room until it feels almost enchanted.

The size of the crowd hits her at once. Heat crawls up her neck and her heart jumps into a faster, uneven rhythm. Her eyes dart across the space, searching- desperately- for someone familiar. Someone safe.

Someone who can anchor her.

With her luck, her gaze lands on Rio.

Agatha braces for the rush of nerves, or irritation, or something sharp. But instead, to her absolute confusion, her heartbeat steadies. The noise of the ballroom dims for a moment as she stands frozen in the entrance, watching Rio from across the room.

Rio is radiant. Unreachable. Effortless.

She wears a long dark-green dress that clings beautifully at the waist, fabric gathered into a halter neck that exposes the smooth lines of her ribs and shoulders. A deep slit cuts down the center, revealing the elegant slope of her sternum. The jewelry on her hand catches the chandelier light as she lifts her champagne glass, gesturing lightly while she speaks to a group of guests. Her head tips back in a laugh- bright, unguarded- and the people around her seem to lean closer, drawn in.

Agatha’s hands fall loosely at her sides. Her pulse stays slow, steady. She hates that. Or maybe she doesn’t.

Ralph’s hand lands abruptly on her waist, snapping her back.
“I’m gonna get a drink,” he mutters in her ear, breath hot and impatient. He doesn’t wait for her response- just peels away toward the bar, leaving her with nothing but the ghost of his touch and a tightening in her jaw.

When she looks back, Rio’s gaze has already found hers.

Rio’s mouth curves into a small, instinctive smile- the kind she used to reserve just for her. Agatha freezes. Rio murmurs something to her circle of guests before excusing herself, turning and making her way toward Agatha with slow, confident steps. Agatha watches her approach, taking in the dress, the walk, the way Rio’s eyes travel down the length of her gown before stopping at her hair

Rio’s expression cracks into a frown.

“What did you do to your hair?” she asks, raising her champagne to her lips.

Agatha’s spine stiffens. “I straightened it.”

“I can see that. Why?”

“Ralph said my natural hair was too messy, so I-”
She’s cut off by Rio’s scoff- loud and immediate.

“Jesus. Fuck that,” Rio responds. “I love your natural hair.” She adds, voice almost a whisper as she leans in slightly.

Agatha’s eyebrows shoot up. “I don’t remember asking for your opinion.”

“Well,” Rio fires back without missing a beat, “I’m giving it.” She tilts her head slightly at Agatha, lips pursed as she studies her.

The heat crawls up Agatha’s neck- annoyance, embarrassment, something tangled and old. She rolls her eyes and clenches her jaw, already done with this conversation and the feelings she’s not ready to deal with.

“I need a drink,” she mutters, spinning sharply on her heel and stalking toward the bar, her dress swaying behind her. She doesn’t look back- mostly because she can still feel Rio watching her go.

She eases herself into the empty stool beside Ralph- who is quietly nursing a scotch, oblivious to the rest of the world.

“You didn’t get me a drink?” Agatha asks, keeping her voice even, though the urge to sigh is overwhelming.

Ralph doesn’t turn his head. He just cuts his eyes toward her, taking a long, deliberate sip.
“I didn’t know what you’d want.”

Agatha presses her tongue to the back of her teeth, choosing not to snap. She lifts her hand instead, flagging the bartender down with a strained smile.
“Martini, please.”

He nods quickly and hurries off. Agatha rests her elbows on the bar, twisting a strand of straightened hair around her finger. Ralph says nothing. He doesn’t touch her. Doesn’t look at her. Just stares at the mirrored bottles lining the shelves like they’re more interesting than anything she could possibly say.

The bartender slides her drink across the counter, and Agatha breathes out a quiet, relieved, “Thank you.” She takes a long sip, feeling the cold burn seep down her throat. Her shoulders drop instantly.

“Hey gorgeous.”

She spins sharply to the left, prepared to bite someone’s head off- but her scowl softens when she sees Jen and Alice slip into the seats beside her.

“Hi Jen.” Agatha’s smile forms without effort for the first time all night. “Hey Alice.”

“Okay, that dress?” Jen says, fanning herself. “I’m obsessed. You look absolutely stunning.”

Agatha waves her off quickly. “Oh please.”

“Bitch, I’m serious,” Jen says, leaning in like she’s delivering state secrets. “You’re hot as fuck.”

Agatha meets her eyes, but no words come out. Her throat closes. She’s never been good at taking compliments- never believed people meant them, even when she wanted to.

She clears her throat and tugs lightly at the edge of her gown. “I see you two have gotten cozy.” She nods toward their joined hands- fingers knotted together, Alice leaning into Jen’s shoulder like she lives there.

“She’s determined, this one, I’ll give her that,” Alice says with a grin, nudging Jen.

Jen shrugs, smug. “What can I say? I know what I want.”

Agatha rolls her eyes, dramatic and exaggerated. “You guys are disgusting.” She takes a long sip.

But her chest aches- with jealousy, with longing, with irritation, with something she doesn’t have the energy to identify. It sits heavy in her ribs, pressing.

The second martini arrives before she even asks for it. Ralph notices immediately.

He shoots her a sideways glare.
“What?” she snaps.

“Nothing.” He sighs, shaking his head. “Just- take it easy, for God’s sake. We just got here.”

Agatha curls her fingers into her palm until her nails bite skin. She lifts the glass, takes a slow sip, and sets it back down with care.

She forces a smile at Jen, who’s watching her a little too closely.

But soon enough, the conversation finds its rhythm again. Jen tells some crazy story about falling into a mud puddle while volunteering, Alice keeps chiming in with corrections, and Agatha realizes- with a flicker of surprise- that she’s actually enjoying herself.

Like, genuinely.

The minutes slip away unnoticed, the bar bubbling with laughter, clinking glasses, and the kind of smiles that come from real warmth rather than forced politeness. The alcohol helps smooth the edges, sure, but it’s more than that. For the first time in weeks, Agatha feels herself loosen- shoulders dropping, jaw unclenching, the tension in her spine unwinding inch by inch.

For a little while, she forgets everything.
Forgets the tightness in her chest whenever she thinks of Rio.
Forgets the stress piling up at work.
Forgets Ralph sitting beside her like a storm cloud waiting to burst.

She just lets herself exist in the small bubble of her friends’ company- safe, light, uncomplicated.

It feels like coming up for air.

Until Jen and Alice quietly sneak off- Jen tugging Alice by the hand toward some dark hallway- and Ralph excuses himself for a “work call,” already darting halfway across the room before Agatha can question why he has a work call at this hour.

And just like that, she’s alone again.

Well, that was short-lived, she thinks, swirling the last inch of her martini. She considers striking up a conversation with the bartender- something painfully dull just to fill the silence- when she feels a tap on her shoulder.

A tap she feels all the way down her spine.

Rio.

Agatha turns on her stool, arms crossing across her chest instinctively. Rio stands there with her head tilted, an infuriatingly soft smirk curling at the edge of her mouth. She extends a hand playfully, palm up.

“May I have this dance,” Rio asks, dipping into a small, teasing bow, “m’lady?”

Agatha actually laughs- a real, dumbfounded, this-must-be-a-joke laugh. “I don’t dance.”

“Yes, you do.”

“No, I don’t.”

Rio sticks out her bottom lip, pouting in a way that should be ridiculous but somehow isn’t. Agatha hates that pout. Hates it because she knows exactly how effective it is.

And sure enough, her hand betrays her- slipping into Rio’s before she can think twice. Rio’s face lights up like she just won a bet.

“You’re infuriating,” Agatha mutters.

“You love it.”

Agatha shoots her a look- the kind that says absolutely not- but her mouth refuses to back it up with actual words.

Rio leads her to the center of the dance floor, weaving seamlessly between couples, never letting go of her hand. When she finds a spot, Rio lifts their joined hands gently, settling her other hand at Agatha’s waist.

Agatha inhales sharply.

Even through the satin, Rio’s touch is unmistakable- warm, steady, sending tiny sparks along her skin. Agatha hesitates, fingers hovering awkwardly before they finally land on Rio’s shoulder, brushing the edge of her shoulder blade.

It feels familiar.
It feels dangerous.
It feels like twenty years of history pressing in all at once.

Rio meets her eyes, soft but steady, and Agatha’s pulse stumbles.

For someone who “doesn’t dance,” she is already falling into step. They sway to the music. Agatha stares at the floor, at her own hands, at the other couples- anywhere but at Rio. She can practically feel Rio watching her.

“You enjoying the party?” Rio asks, forcing Agatha’s gaze up.

“It’s better than I thought it would be,” she whispers, eyes darting away again. Her heart won’t stop sprinting.

“Yeah, Billy sure knows how to plan a nice event.” Rio smiles, shaking her head. “That kid is something.”

“Billy planned this?” Agatha raises a brow. “I thought that was supposed to be your job.”

“I delegated.”

“Don’t go stealing my assistant now.”

“I wouldn’t dare.” Her voice drops, warm breath brushing Agatha’s jaw.

Rio’s thumb grazes hers- barely anything, but enough to send a cold shiver ripping down her spine. Agatha pretends she doesn’t notice.

“Where’s your date?” she blurts before thinking, her tone sharper than intended.

“Wanda?” Rio frowns, as if only now remembering the woman exists. “Probably talking up donors somewhere.” She tilts her head. “Why?”

“No reason,” Agatha says, too casually. “Just wondering why you’re here dancing with me instead of her.”

Rio’s smile turns razor-thin. “I could ask the same thing about your husband. Where is the little bitch, anyway?”

“Rio.” Agatha snaps, glaring.

“What?” Rio says blandly. “I could call him a lot worse.”

Agatha exhales, suddenly exhausted. “He’s taking a work call.”

“A work call,” Rio repeats, flat and unconvinced.

“Yeah?”

“At 8 p.m. on a Saturday?”

The words hit harder than they should. Harder because Agatha has wondered the same thing. Harder because it’s Riosaying it. Her stomach drops.

Without thinking, she yanks her hand out of Rio’s.

“How about you stay out of my personal life?”

Rio lifts her hands in surrender. “Agatha, I’m just pointing out-”

“Well, don’t.” Agatha’s voice is ice. “I’m not looking for marriage advice. Especially not from you.”

She turns sharply and pushes off the dance floor, heat rising under her skin, air suddenly too thick to breathe. She needs a wall. A shadow. A corner. To be anywhere but here.

She slips through the crowd, barely aware of the music or the laughter or the bodies brushing past her. Every step feels like she’s pushing through warm, suffocating air. Finally, she finds a far corner- dark, empty, and quiet- and presses her back against the wall as if it can hold her together.

Agatha drags in a shaky breath and closes her eyes, shutting out the room, the lights, the voices, her thoughts. Her hand flies instinctively to her chest, palm flattening over her racing heart like she can pin it down and force it to behave. She inhales again, slower this time, trying to remember how to breathe like a normal person.

She focuses on the rhythm beneath her fingertips. Steady. Then steadier. She counts each inhale, each exhale, letting the noise of the party blur into a distant, meaningless hum. No Rio. No Ralph. No wandering eyes or whispered suspicions.

Her shoulders drop bit by bit. Her pulse eases. She can finally unclench her jaw.

For a moment- just a moment- Agatha lets herself disappear from the room entirely. Then she opens her eyes.

Rio is standing in front of her, quiet, holding out a martini as if it’s a white flag.

“I’m sorry,” she murmurs, extending the glass.

Agatha blinks. “What’s this?”

“A peace offering,” Rio says softly. “You look like you could use a drink.”

Agatha crosses her arms, lifting an eyebrow. “And who says I wanted a martini?”

Rio gives her a look- annoyed and fond at the same time- and keeps holding the drink out patiently. Agatha exhales through her nose and finally reaches for it.

“Fine,” she mutters. “Thank you.”

“You’re welcome.” Rio’s smile is soft, almost apologetic- then, surprisingly, she actually turns and walks away, leaving Agatha alone in the quiet again, a hollow ache developing in her chest.

A few minutes of slow, steady breathing later, Agatha straightens her posture and steps back into the party. She can survive this. She can make polite conversation, laugh at the right moments, and then get the hell out of here. She can hold herself together.
Just for tonight.
Just long enough.

She spots Ralph across the room, returned to his place at the bar, and makes her way over. She slides onto the stool beside him.

“Hey,” she says quietly. “What did work want?”

“Oh, they were just asking for my opinion on something,” he mutters, eyes fixed on his drink.

Agatha nods, slow and hesitant, that uneasy weight settling low in her stomach again. Her gaze wanders- anywhere but her husband- and lands on Rio a few feet away. She’s surrounded by a small circle of donors, laughing at something someone said, all warmth and charisma. And next to her, arm snug around Rio’s waist, is a redheaded woman in a maroon backless dress.

Wanda.

Agatha’s jaw tightens just as the lively music shifts- softening, slowing, melting into something intimate. Couples drift toward the dance floor in pairs, as if pulled by invisible strings.

Rio’s earlier words echo in her mind.
I could ask the same thing about your husband.

Agatha’s hand closes around Ralph’s arm before she can think better of it.

“Let’s go, hon.”

He startles. “What?” he snaps, being tugged to his feet. “Where?”

“To dance,” she says firmly.

Before he can object, she pulls him toward the shifting crowd- her steps a little too sharp, her pulse a little too loud, anything to keep her eyes from drifting back to Rio.

She rests her arms on Ralph’s shoulders- stiff, unnatural- as they begin to sway to the music. Over his shoulder, her eyes drift, and she spots Wanda dragging a visibly reluctant Rio by the hand toward the dance floor. Agatha’s chest tightens instantly, painfully. Wanda loops her arms around Rio’s neck, clinging too tightly, too close. Rio’s hands settle at Wanda’s waist as they fall into the rhythm.

Then Rio’s gaze lifts.
And finds Agatha’s.

The room seems to dim, the music fading into a dull hum in the background. Agatha doesn’t look away- and shockingly, neither does Rio. Her brain screams at her to look at her husband, to remember where she is, to remember who she’s with. But her eyes refuse to obey.

Their stares lock- unblinking, raw- speaking truths Agatha could never find the words to say out loud.

You hurt me. I never want to see you again. We can’t go back. I can’t forgive you. I want nothing to do with you.

But I think I might still love you.

Agatha’s pulse thunders in her ears. The face she once loved- might still love- looks back at her with that familiar ache. And for a terrifying second, she feels herself slipping.

No.
She won’t.
She can’t.

Agatha drops her arms from Ralph’s shoulders and storms off the dance floor without a word. She doesn’t look back. She barely hears Ralph’s confused shouting behind her. The blood rushing in her ears is too loud, drowning everything else out. She pushes through the door into the hallway, heels clicking sharply against the floor.

Another set of footsteps follows.
Of course they do.

She spins around, breath heavy, fury simmering just beneath the surface.

“Stop.” Her voice slices through the hallway like glass.

Rio halts. “Stop what?” she asks cautiously, taking another small step forward.

“Stop pursuing me.”

“I’m not- ”

“No. Stop talking. Stop looking at me like that. Stop showing up and saying all the right things at all the wrong times.” Her voice cracks into something halfway between anger and desperation. “Because it’s exhausting. Because you’re insane- because you’re driving me insane. So just… stop. Please.”

Rio’s expression falters. For a moment she looks genuinely startled, flustered- young in a way Agatha hasn’t seen in years. Tears sting at the corners of her eyes.

“I can’t,” she whispers.

“Why.”

“I don’t know.”

“Why, Rio?”

“I don’t know!” The last word breaks in the middle.

Agatha’s face softens- not forgiving, not yielding, just tired. Bone-deep tired. She shakes her head slowly, heartbreakingly.

“That’s not good enough.” She turns away. “Just leave me alone.”

She darts into the bathroom, slamming the door behind her, pressing her back hard against the wood as if she can physically hold herself together. Her breath shudders out of her chest.

And for the first time all night, she finally lets herself feel it.

The tears stream down her cheeks before she can stop them, and she brings a trembling hand to her mouth, biting down hard to stifle the sob building in her throat.

She can’t face Rio. She can’t face their past, their history, the feelings she’s buried so deep that are finally clawing their way back up. She can’t even face Ralph.

Because she’s weak.
She’s always been weak, just like her mother said.

The thought loops in her head, heavy and cruel, as her chest tightens with each shaky, broken breath. She forces herself upright, peeling her back from the door despite how badly she wants to sink down and disappear. She turns toward the mirror.

And doesn’t recognize the woman looking back.

Mascara streaks down her swollen cheeks. Her eyes are glassy, unfocused, like she’s watching herself from somewhere far away. Her pin-straight hair hangs limp and uneven around her shoulders- Ralph-approved, Rio-hated, and wrong in every way.

Agatha inhales slowly, tasting salt and humiliation.

No wonder Ralph probably has a side piece.
The thought hits sharp and fast.
Look at me. I’m pathetic. A fucking embarrassment.

I wouldn’t want to be with me either.

She drags a rough paper towel under her eyes, wiping away the dark smudges. Her hands shake as she runs her fingers through her hair, trying- failing- to tame it into something normal, something presentable.

After a long moment of gathering herself- rebuilding whatever tiny shell she has left- she reaches toward the door. Her hand hesitates for a second, hovering in the air, but she forces herself to grasp the handle anyway.

She exhales, opens it slowly, and steps back into the empty hallway.

Agatha sits at the round table in silence, moving bits of salad around her plate with her fork. The low hum of conversation blurs around her, blending with the clinking of silverware. She glances up at the stage- Rio stands at the wing, notecards in hand, scanning the crowd with an anxious energy Agatha can feel from across the room. Agatha drops her gaze immediately. Ralph is talking beside her, words spilling out in a steady stream she doesn’t process- not even a syllable registers. She forces herself to lift her fork, taking a mechanical bite despite the nausea curling in her stomach.

Yelena’s voice booms across the room as servers weave between tables with plates.
“And now, ladies and gentlemen, please give a warm welcome to your president and founder… Rio Vidal!”

Applause rises all around. Agatha barely lifts her hands.

Rio steps into the center of the stage, returning Yelena’s grin as she takes the podium.
“Thank you, Yelena. And thank you all for being here tonight,” she begins, voice bright through the speakers. “Seriously. I know it’s cold, parking is a nightmare, and half of you probably skipped dinner because your outfits are tight as hell- so, honestly, thank you.”

The room echoes with laughter. Agatha stays silent, face flat.

Rio glances at her notecards- holding them delicately, almost nervously- before hesitating. After a long second, she sets them down on the podium, palms flattening against the wood. When she raises her head again, her gaze is steady, her shoulders squared.

“You know,” she starts slowly, “as Yelena was reminiscing on her story of joining this organization, I realized I’ve never really shared mine. I’ve never told anyone why I started this in the first place.”

Her voice is low, honest in a way that makes the room quiet. She takes a deep, shaky breath, clears her throat once, and then- almost unconsciously- turns her eyes toward Agatha.

“A long time ago… I loved someone.” Her voice doesn’t waver, but there’s a tremor behind it. “And I had to do something that I did not want to do. Even though it was my job. And it hurt them.”

A silence settles over the room. Rio presses her tongue into her cheek, grounding herself before continuing.

“There was so much I wanted to fix. So much I wanted to change. I wanted so badly to help her.” Her eyes flick across the audience, unable to stay on Agatha too long. “But some things are out of your control. Some things can’t be fixed. No matter how much you want them to be.”

She exhales, the words clearly costing her something.

“So when I got out of college, I kept thinking about that. About her. About how powerless I felt. And I realized I needed… something. Something that I could actually make better. Something I could pour myself into and know that the work mattered.”

She looks back at Agatha- just for a breath, soft and unguarded.

“That’s why I founded Coven for Climate.”

Her voice grows stronger, firmer.

“Because this,” she gestures to the room, to the volunteers, the donors, the photos on the walls, “this is something we cancontrol. The earth is dying because of us. But we don’t have to let it go any further. We can offer our planet more time to thrive, to prosper, to live.”

A small, sad smile touches the corner of her mouth.

“I want to help the environment because it’s something that- with hard work- I can fix. That I can change. For the better.” She pauses. “I just wish I could’ve done the same for her.”

She turns fully toward Agatha now, her voice soft.

“So, my love… this is all because of you. This is all for you.”

A wave of chatter rolls through the room- confused whispers, surprised murmurs, the shocked rustle of gowns and jewelry. Rio pulls her gaze away, blinking hard, gathering herself.

She clears her throat, returning to her practiced professional tone- though it’s a bit thinner, frayed at the edges.

“Once again, thank you so much for being here tonight. And I hope you recognize how important this organization- and our mission- is. Every dollar of your donations goes directly to our cause, so if you’d like to make any additional deposits, I’ll direct you back to Yelena over here.” She gestures at Yelena, who is standing stiffly at the side of the stage.

“Have a wonderful rest of your night, folks.”

Applause erupts again, though Agatha barely hears it. Her heart is thundering, stomach twisting painfully. She watches Rio step off the stage- ignoring Wanda’s bright, expectant smile- and walk straight toward their table with purpose in every step.

Rio stops briefly beside Agatha’s chair. She leans down, close- too close- and her lips brush Agatha’s ear.

“You wanna know why?” She whispers. “That’s why.”

Her fingertips graze Agatha’s shoulder, light and devastating, before she pulls back and disappears into the crowd.

Agatha doesn’t breathe. Doesn’t blink.
Her heart splits clean in two.

2005

“Did you feel it?” Agatha asks, shifting her weight on her aching feet, peering down at Rio- who’s currently kneeling on the carpet, face plastered with determination.

Rio presses her palm harder into Agatha’s stomach, brows furrowed. “No! He’s not doing anything.” She groans dramatically, flopping back onto her heels and crossing her arms. “He hates me.”

Agatha laughs softly. “He doesn’t hate you, sweetheart.”

“He hasn’t kicked for me even once.”

“That’s because you’re impatient,” Agatha teases. She reaches for Rio’s hand, uncurling her stubborn fingers and guiding her palm back onto the round swell of her belly. “Just… give him a minute.”

Rio’s lips press together as she leans in, focused. The room falls into a gentle hush- just the hum of the lamp, the faint rustle of the curtains, the shared rhythm of their breaths.

Then a soft thump.

Agatha inhales. Rio’s eyes widen instantly, a spark lighting up behind them.

“Wait-” Rio whispers.

Another small kick.

Rio’s mouth drops open. “I felt it!” she gasps, the sound excited and breathless. “Agatha, I felt it!” Her hands fly up before she slaps them back down on Agatha’s stomach as if she’s afraid she’ll miss the next one. “Do it again, Nicky! Do it again!”

Agatha snorts. “He’s not a performing seal.”

“I don’t care. I want more kicks.”

“Mm-hm.” Agatha smirks, brushing the bangs out of Rio’s face. “And you said he hated you.”

Rio ignores the jab entirely, cupping both hands around Agatha’s belly with a reverence that makes Agatha’s chest squeeze. She leans forward slowly- almost shyly- and presses a kiss right beside her own palm.

“I can’t believe he’s actually in there,” Rio murmurs. “Like- right now. Growing ears and eyelashes and stuff.”

Agatha raises a brow. “Well, I sure hope he’s in there. Otherwise, I should really see a doctor about this.”

Rio snorts, leaning her forehead against Agatha’s stomach before kissing it again. She whispers into the soft skin, voice warm and thick with emotion, “Our precious Nicky… I can’t wait to meet you.” Her nose scrunches.

Agatha’s heart tugs at the sound of it- Rio’s voice cracking just a little, her fingers trembling ever so slightly where they rest.

She reaches toward the desk with a grunt, stretching until she grabs the camera. She flicks it on just as Rio leans in to place another lingering kiss over the spot where Nicky kicked.

The shutter clicks.

Rio freezes. She lifts her head sharply. “Hey! Warn me first! That was not a good angle.”

“Oh hush,” Agatha says, laughing. “You look beautiful.”

Rio blushes immediately- pink blooming across her cheeks in a way Agatha has always loved. “Well,” she mutters, pretending not to preen, “fine. Maybe one of them is decent. I’ll add it to the scrapbook.”

Agatha brushes her knuckles down Rio’s cheek.

Rio glances down at the belly again, eyes softening as if someone dimmed the rest of the world and all she can see now is Nicky and Agatha. All she’ll ever need.

“Hey, Nicky,” Rio whispers against Agatha’s skin. “Kick again if you love Mama.”

Agatha smacks her arm lightly. “You’re shameless.”

But Rio just grins, dimples showing. “Yeah. So what?”

The baby kicks again, and Rio squeals loud enough to wake the whole dorm. “See?” She glances at Agatha before returning her gaze to her stomach and whispers, “I love you too, Nicky.”

2025

Rio grabs the glue stick with one hand while lifting her mug with the other, taking a careful sip of tea that settles uneasily in her empty stomach. She flips the photo over, dragging the glue along the back in slow, deliberate strokes. Her tongue pokes into her cheek, concentrating, as she presses the picture into the scrapbook, smoothing out the edges, making sure it lands perfectly straight.

She lifts her hands, steps back, and lets the page come into focus.

Agatha and Rio ‘25
Coven for Climate

The title sits at the top in neat handwriting, framed by earthy stickers, pressed leaves, and patterned paper she spent way too long sorting through. The new photo sits just beneath it-printed, trimmed, and laminated by her own two hands. She stares at it the way she’s been staring at it for days- Agatha leaning lightly into her side, Rio’s arm draped over her shoulder like it belongs there. And Agatha’s smile- God, that smile- still hits her like a blow to the ribs.

She’s really going to wear this picture out.

Rio traces Agatha’s outline with her eyes and silently thanks Billy for insisting they take it. Though, she’ll never tell him that, of course.

Her phone buzzes on the desk- an alarm so loud it makes her visibly jump.

Oh, shit.

She’s late. Again.

Rio barely has time to put the cap back on the glue before she grabs her keys, shoves her feet into her boots, and barrels out the door. She fully sprints down the porch steps and onto the sidewalk, hair flying behind her, praying she doesn’t look as frazzled as she feels by the time she reaches the car.

She takes a moment- both hands braced on the wheel, eyes squeezed shut- just to steady herself. Then she throws the car into drive and peels out onto the street, the engine humming as she merges into traffic. Her fingers tap an anxious rhythm against the steering wheel, teeth biting the inside of her cheek raw.

She gives herself a rule.
A strict, unbreakable rule.

No talking to Agatha.
No starting conversations.
In fact, no looking at her.

Because if she does, if she even glances at her, she knows exactly what’ll happen. She’ll fold. She’ll soften. She’ll find some excuse to hover around her like a lovesick idiot.

And after that speech? After whispering that’s why into her ear like some dramatic, self-sabotaging maniac?

God. She winces just thinking about it.

Right after Agatha told her to stop pursuing her.
Right after she begged her to stop.

“Fucking idiot,” Rio mutters under her breath, gripping the wheel tighter.

So this is her punishment. A self-imposed time out. No more talking to Agatha. No more dreaming about Agatha. No more imagining her smile or replaying the past or pretending there’s anything left to salvage- at least when she’s around others.

Agatha’s made very clear that she wants nothing to do with Rio.

She has to respect that.

She forces a slow exhale, the sound covering the complaining growl of her empty stomach as she turns onto the main road, cruising toward the park. The sky is pale and overcast, the kind of gray that makes everything feel quieter. She welcomes it. She needs the quiet, the blankness, the space to shove her feelings somewhere deep where they can’t claw their way out.

Just the park. Just the event.
Just work.

No Agatha.

She repeats it to herself like a mantra as the trees come into view.

Rio slams the box down on the folding table harder than she means to, the thud echoing across the small clearing. She grabs a pair of scissors, slices the tape open, and pulls out stacks of gardening gloves- new, stiff, smelling faintly of rubber and cardboard. She lines them up across the table.

“Okay, new gloves have arrived,” she calls, turning toward the circle of volunteers. The breeze carries her voice farther than she expects. “If you’re on weeding duty, come grab a pair. Everyone else, head to your assigned stations.”

The group breaks apart immediately- some drifting toward her, some scattering across the park with rakes, trash bags, and bright-orange buckets. Rio lifts her voice one more time, “Lunch is at one today instead of twelve!”

A few people give a thumbs-up over their shoulders. Most don’t look back.

She turns to the table again, rubbing her hands briskly along her bare arms. The thin volunteer T-shirt isn’t doing her any favors. Her skin prickles. Her teeth almost chatter. She curses herself for not grabbing the jacket she’d tossed into her closet that morning.

“It’s seventy-nine degrees out. How are you cold?”

The voice behind her makes her spine jolt- makes the whole world stop for a second.

Her voice.

Rio turns so fast she almost trips over her own feet. Agatha stands there with a clipboard tucked against her chest, eyes sharp, eyebrows raised.

“I’m not cold.” Rio blurts, too quick, too defensive. She lifts her chin, trying to look composed instead of startled and inwardly spiraling. Why is Agatha talking to her? After everything at the gala?

Agatha doesn’t even blink. She just stares at Rio’s arms. “You literally have goosebumps.”

Rio glances down. Shit.
“I’m in the shade,” she says, shrugging one shoulder.

“So am I.” Agatha crosses her arms, unimpressed.

Rio exhales sharply through her nose. “Okay, so fucking what? What do you want?”

It comes out harsher than she intended- almost snapping- and she instantly regrets it. But it’s too late- Agatha’s eyes widen a fraction before flattening into a cool, unimpressed stare.

“Jeez.” Agatha says. “I left my purse at the gala. I was told you have it.”

Oh. Fuck.

Of course it would be that.  

Of course that purse just happens to be Agatha’s, out of all people.

Rio clears her throat, trying not to look like she’s malfunctioning. “Yeah. I- I do. Or, well…” She winces. “I accidentally left it at home. I was running late.”

Agatha sighs, long and clearly annoyed. She shifts the clipboard to her other arm.

“I can bring it next week,” Rio offers quickly.

“Can I just swing by after this and grab it?” Agatha asks, tone flat but not unkind. “I have a dinner tonight.”

Rio freezes for a beat too long. Swing by. To her house.

Her heart trips over itself.

“I- uh- yeah,” she manages, trying to sound like a normal human person. “Yeah, sure. That works.”

Agatha nods once. “Thanks.” She gives a tight, polite smile- one that doesn’t reach her eyes- before turning and walking down the hill toward the riverbank where her group is gathering.

Rio watches her go, pulse thudding, breath uneven.

Her hands are still trembling when she turns back to the table.

Wanda scurries up the path, her arms overflowing with six grocery bags packed to the brim.
“I’m here, I’m here!” she calls, practically jogging. “Sorry- line was taking ages!”

Rio steps forward to intercept her before she drops something, taking half the bags from her hands. Warm air carries the smell upward- sliders, fries, garlic, something fried and sinful. Her stomach twists so hard she nearly folds.

“No worries, Wanda,” Rio says, forcing a small smile. “Thanks for going.”

Wanda nods, already breathless, and the two of them move toward the picnic tables. Together they unload the feast- catering boxes, foil trays, tongs. Wanda sets them out in neat rows like she’s running a luxury buffet at a wedding instead of a volunteer clean-up.

“There.” Wanda claps her hands, satisfied. “I’ll go tell everyone lunch is ready. You help yourself!”

Before Rio can protest, Wanda darts off across the grass toward the nearest group.

Rio turns back to the spread. Juicy sliders glistening under the sun. Salted fries still steaming. Big bowls of salads absolutely drowning in dressing. Her stomach tightens again- sharp, hollow, pleading.

She bites the inside of her cheek hard enough to taste metal.

No.

She turns away abruptly, dug-out gum already between her fingers. She pops it into her mouth and walks quickly to the farthest picnic table she can find- empty and quiet.

She sinks onto the bench and pulls out her phone, scrolling mindlessly. Reels, news updates, memes. Anything to distract from the ache in her stomach, the way her body keeps reminding her she hasn’t eaten since yesterday morning.

She’s focused on pushing the thoughts away when someone sits across from her.

Yelena.

She sets her plate down- a mountain of food Rio refuses to look at.

Yelena frowns immediately. “You’re not eating?”

Rio keeps her eyes glued to her screen. “Not hungry.”

Yelena spears a fry, holding it up towards her. “Are you sure? ‘Cause-” She takes a bite, talking through it, “-this is sofucking good.”

Rio’s heart lurches- sharp, panicked.

“I said I’m sure, Yelena. Just drop it.”

Her voice is too loud, too sharp, and Rio feels the guilt hit before the sentence even finishes leaving her mouth.

“I’m sorry,” she says instantly, voice dropping. “I didn’t mean it like that.”

“It’s okay,” Yelena replies softly, looking back down at her plate. She doesn’t push. Doesn’t pry. Just eats quietly.

But Rio can’t stop staring at her food- at the bite marks, at the seasoning, at the way Yelena’s mouth moves deliberately.

She chews her gum harder, jaw aching, leg bouncing uncontrollably beneath the table.

Her stomach growls so loudly she’s terrified Yelena might hear it.

For a second- just a second- Rio imagines it.

Imagines standing up, walking over to the table, piling a plate with sliders and fries until the paper bends under the weight. Imagines taking a huge bite, warm bread and grease and salt exploding on her tongue. Imagines chewing without guilt, swallowing without fear, letting herself feel full for the first time in months- full and human and alive.

The fantasy hits her so hard her throat tightens.

And then, like always, she slams the door on it.

No. You don’t need it. You’re doing great. You’re in control.

She sits perfectly still, nails digging crescent moons into her palm. She forces herself to imagine the regret instead- the bloat, the disgust, the shame that comes crawling in afterward and stays for hours. The feeling of being weak. Ruining progress. Starting from hour zero again.

She knows that feeling too well. She’s lived in it.

So she pushes the thought away as quickly as it came, burying it under every rigid rule she’s carved into herself.

This is always the worst part- the window of time where every pang is razor-sharp, where hunger isn’t hollow yet but loud and insistent. Eventually it’ll fade into that familiar numbness, the dull background ache she can usually ignore. But right now it’s all she can feel, pulsing through her ribs. She hates this stage. But she still doesn’t let herself give in.

Because giving in would undo everything. It would erase every hour she’s white-knuckled her way through. It would confirm the things she already fears are true- that she can’t trust herself, that she can’t control anything, that she is always two seconds from slipping back into the routine she swore she’d never enter again.

Her stomach aches so sharply she has to press a hand against it.

Just ignore it. Ignore it. You’re fine.

She straightens her back, ignoring the way her vision sways for a moment. She ignores the hunger, the fatigue humming under her skin, the cold sweat gathering between her shoulder blades. She ignores the guilt gnawing at her- guilt for snapping at Yelena, guilt for lying again, guilt for caring so much about something she wishes she didn’t care about at all.

Instead, she looks back down at her phone and scrolls as if she’s perfectly comfortable, perfectly calm, perfectly okay.

Pretend long enough and maybe it’ll feel true.

You’re doing great, she tells herself.

You’re doing so, so great.

And maybe if she repeats it enough times, she’ll finally believe it.

After lunch, Rio drifts toward the tree line with the group assigned to weed out invasive plants. She’d originally signed herself up for river cleanup, but that would’ve put her within twenty feet of Agatha- and she doesn’t trust herself with that kind of proximity. Not today. So instead she grabs a fresh pair of gloves, hooks her fingers around the handle of a heavy shovel, and drags it across the grass behind her. She inhales slowly, already dreading the tedious work ahead, wanting nothing more than for this long, complicated day to end.

Out of the corner of her eye, she notices Wanda standing by a cluster of overgrown shrubs, hands on her hips as she surveys the mess of roots. Rio’s gaze flickers up before she can stop it- Wanda’s slim waist, the effortless curve of her thighs, the way she naturally looks like a 90s supermodel despite taking seconds at lunch. Something tightens in Rio’s chest, small and ugly. Wanda always looks like that. And Rio? She’s starving herself half to death and still doesn’t measure up. Maybe she never will.

She tears her eyes away, jaw clenching, a hot pulse of shame moving through her before she forces it down. Focus. Just focus.

She drives the shovel into the dirt, hard enough that the handle rattles in her grip. The blade cuts into the ground, splitting through tangled roots and stubborn weeds. She digs again. And again. Her arms shake from the impact, muscles stinging, but the repetition gives her something her brain hasn’t offered her all day- silence. With each downward swing, her heartbeat steadies. With each heave of dirt, the noise in her head thins out.

For a few minutes- just a few- there’s nothing but the weight of the shovel, the cool earth, and the sound of her own breathing.

Until someone clears their throat beside her.

Rio jumps- actually jumps- and whips around to see Agatha standing there, holding up her trash collector. A huge tear runs straight down the middle of the net.

“Jesus, you scared me,” Rio exhales, pressing a hand to her chest.

“This got torn,” Agatha says, frowning down at the shredded net. “Are there extras?”

Rio pulls off her gloves, wiping her sweaty palms onto her jeans. “Uh- yeah. There should be a box by the supply tent.” She points toward the entrance across the park.

Agatha nods and turns to go, right before a hand slides along Rio’s shoulder. She spins.

Wanda.

The touch snags Agatha’s attention instantly. She turns her head, eyes narrowing just enough to register it before her expression smooths over.

“Rio,” Wanda says brightly, smiling up at her, “do you want to help me with these roots over here? I’m having some trouble.” Her gaze finally flicks to Agatha, as if she’s just realized there’s another human present. “Oh- hi, Agatha.”

Agatha gives her a forced smile. “Hiya, dear,” she chirps, way too sweet, way too eager, head tilted just a little too far.

Rio feels the awkward silence clamp down on all three of them. She remains silent, looking back and forth between the pair.

“Rio? The roots?” Wanda prompts again, eyebrows raised in that expectant way that makes Rio feel trapped.

Rio turns to Agatha, desperation creeping into her voice. “Did you need help finding the other nets?” she asks- because God, she really does not want to be doing… whatever this is.

She watches Agatha’s eyes flick to Wanda’s hand on her shoulder, then to Wanda’s smile. Something tightens in Agatha’s jaw before she forces her expression neutral.

“I’m sure I can manage to find them myself,” Agatha says flatly. She spins on her heel and strides toward the tent without waiting for a response.

Rio swallows hard. That went well. Exactly the kind of interaction she was trying to avoid today.

She sighs internally and turns back to Wanda. “Okay,” she mutters reluctantly, “the roots.”

Rio bends down to grab her shovel, but when she stands too fast her vision bursts into static around the edges. She steadies herself- slow breath in, slow breath out- and trails after Wanda, wishing she were following literally anyone else.

By the end of the day, her muscles ache so badly she feels like she could just crumple into the dirt. Every step sends a dull throb up her calves, her shoulders burning from hours of digging into hard soil. She pushes herself upright anyway, wiping sweat from her forehead with the back of her hand. Her fingers come away gritty with dust. Everything in her body begs for rest as she trudges back to the work tent and starts packing up the supplies, movements clumsy from sheer fatigue.

“Hey,” Agatha says, appearing beside her as quietly as she always does. “You almost done here?”

Rio startles a little- she’s too tired for any big reaction- and her mind fires off a curse. Shit. Her purse. She forgot. Again.

“Yeah,” Rio sighs, trying not to sound as drained as she feels. “Uh- just gotta take these to Wanda’s car and then I’m good.”

Agatha nods, studying her with those perceptive eyes that always feel like they see more than Rio wants them to. The look softens a little.

“If you wanna start heading over now, I can meet you there,” Rio adds quickly, hoping she sounds casual and not like her mind is completely frazzled.

Agatha accepts the suggestion with a gentle smile and heads toward the parking lot, her steps unhurried.

Rio exhales and lifts a box of tools, the weight nearly making her arms buckle. Her biceps tremble with every step she takes toward Wanda’s car parked near the tent. The sun, now low on the horizon, makes everything feel heavier, the air thick and sleepy. When she reaches the trunk, she shoves the box inside with more force than intended and slams it shut, the sound echoing across the emptying lot.

Wanda materializes behind her almost silently. “Thank you, Rio!” she chirps, voice bright and much too cheerful. “I’ll see you next week!”

Before Rio can sidestep or brace herself, Wanda pulls her into a hug- tight, enthusiastic, and absolutely the last thing Rio has the emotional bandwidth for. Rio stiffens, arms hovering awkwardly before she gives a half-hearted pat on Wanda’s back, counting the seconds until she’s released.

“Drive safe,” Rio says with a forced smile as Wanda finally lets go and heads for the driver’s seat.

As Wanda’s car pulls away, Rio releases a shaky breath- half relief, half exhaustion. She leans forward, hands on her knees, willing her heartbeat to settle. Then her stomach drops.

Agatha.

Agatha is currently en route to her house.

Fuck.

Adrenaline jolts through her sore limbs as she speed-walks to her car, the short distance somehow knocking the wind out of her. She collapses into the driver’s seat, barely pulling the door shut before peeling out of the lot. Her hands grip the wheel tight, knuckles whitening, her mind racing.

By the time she reaches her house, Agatha’s car is already parked at the curb. Rio parks behind it, her chest tightening, and climbs out. Agatha sits waiting on the porch swing, her posture relaxed but her expression unreadable.

Flustered, Rio jogs up the steps, breath hitching, and fumbles with her keys before finally getting the door unlocked. Agatha stands as she approaches, meeting her at the entrance. They don’t say hello. They don’t need to. It’s like they’ve both silently agreed that small talk is pointless now-just empty air delaying whatever comes next.

Rio pushes open the door and steps inside, Agatha following close behind. The quiet of the house wraps around them immediately. Rio heads straight for the kitchen where she knows she left the purse- set neatly on the table so she wouldn’t forget it this morning.

Perfect plan. Not so perfect execution.

She grabs the purse and turns back toward Agatha, hands outstretched- only to freeze.

Agatha is standing at the counter. Over their scrapbook.

Left wide open.

Open to that page.

Her blood runs cold. Her ribs pinch tight.

Shit.

“Rio,” Agatha says quietly, steadying herself with a deep breath. “What is this?” Her voice is deceptively soft, but her shoulders are rigid, her jaw tight. She doesn’t look away from the newly completed page- their photo perfectly centered like a confession, like a wound that won’t close.

Rio freezes. Her stomach swirls angrily- hunger clawing at her ribs, nausea rising, anxiety squeezing her throat shut.

“I- uh-” She can’t even form a lie. How do you go about explaining something like this?

She walks over slowly, places Agatha’s purse on the table, then snatches up the scrapbook, snapping it closed and pressing it to her chest like a shield.

“It’s nothing,” she forces out. “It’s just… our old scrapbook.”

“No, I know what it is.” Agatha’s voice cracks on the edges. “I mean- why is there a new picture of us on this page?”

Rio’s breath stutters. Her fingers tighten around the cover. She stares down at her socks, at a stain on the floor, anywhere but Agatha’s face. She doesn’t answer. She can’t.

Agatha’s tone sharpens, breaking the long silence. “Rio, why won’t you just fucking listen to me?” she seethes, the hurt seeping through her anger. “I explicitly told you to leave me alone. To leave this alone. And then you add to our scrapbook?” Her voice rises, frustrated and raw. “Why do you even still have it?”

Rio looks up, eyes glossy but stubborn. “So what? I’m just supposed to get rid of it? Toss out all our memories like they’re garbage? Like they’re nothing?”

“Umm- yes!” Agatha snaps, as if the answer is obvious, like it’s the only reasonable solution.

Rio’s face twists. “No!”

“I can’t fucking do this anymore!” Agatha’s shout echoes through the kitchen. She groans, grabbing fistfuls of her hair, pacing a few steps like she’s trying to physically shake off her frustration.

“You’re always here. Lingering. Following me around like a lost puppy. It’s annoying. It’s pathetic.”

She takes a deep breath, closing her eyes as she shakes her head slowly. “I’m fucking done! I don’t wanna see you anymore. I don’t wanna see your face.”

The words slam into Rio like physical blows, each one sharp enough to knock the air from her lungs. Her chest tightens painfully, a deep ache settling beneath her ribs. She presses her tongue into her cheek hard enough to hurt, willing herself not to break, not here, not in front of her. Her eyes burn instantly, heat gathering at the corners, but she forces it back with every ounce of control she has left.

Control is all she has.

She keeps her jaw clenched. She keeps her lips sealed. She says nothing. Not a single sound escapes her.

Agatha stands there, breathing hard, her chest rising and falling too fast. Rio can feel the anger rolling off her- deep and familiar- but she sees the moment it shifts. The moment Agatha actually looks at her. The moment the fury hits the wall of Rio’s silence.

There’s a flicker in Agatha’s expression. A crack. Rio sees it even though she’s staring mostly at the floor, because she can’t look directly at her without shattering. Beneath the anger, something else stirs- something tight, anxious, almost worried.

“I’m… I’m sorry,” Agatha whispers, voice suddenly small. “Rio, I didn’t mean- I’m sorry.”

Rio’s stomach twists violently. The apology lands like another strike- not harsh, but devastating. Her throat closes. She feels Agatha step forward, the floorboards creaking under her weight. The air shifts, warming as Agatha reaches out, arms lifting in that old familiar instinct- comfort after conflict, the same pattern they’ve repeated too many times. A hug to patch over damage without ever really fixing anything.

But Rio’s hand shoots up before Agatha can touch her, stopping her cold.

“Don’t.”

Her voice comes out quiet but razor-sharp, slicing through the moment. Even she’s surprised by how steady it sounds when everything inside her is trembling.

Rio continues. “You’re right.”

She feels rather than sees Agatha freeze. She hears the soft hitch in her breathing.

Rio swallows hard, forcing the words out past the lump forming in her throat. “I’m done. I give up.”

Her vision blurs for a second- exhaustion, heartbreak, all of it blending together- but she doesn’t let herself waver. Not now.

She moves around Agatha, her steps slow like she’s trying to delay the inevitable. The room feels impossibly small, suffocating, as she crosses to the trash can. Her foot presses down on the pedal. The lid snaps open with a hollow metal clang that echoes painfully through the silent kitchen.

The scrapbook sits in her hands, heavier than it should be. She stares at the corner of a sticker peeking out from the edge- one they argued about for ten minutes the day they placed it. She can still smell the faint scent of glue that always clung to the pages. The weight of years- of memories, of hope she didn’t want to admit she still had- presses down on her palms.

For a split second, it’s almost enough to stop her.

But only for a split second.

Then she lets it fall.

The lid slams shut above it.

Rio wipes away the single tear that escapes- furious at herself for letting even that tiny trace of emotion slip free. Her fingertips shake as she drags them under her eye. Her legs feel weak. Her heart feels like it’s dropping through her stomach.

She takes a breath that shakes her whole body, trying to regain some piece of control. “I’ll see you out now.”

“Rio-” Agatha tries again, voice cracking, but Rio just shakes her head, sharp and decisive.

Silence stretches thick across the room until Agatha’s soft exhale leaves her lips. A defeated one. A broken one.

She grabs her purse and turns toward the door. Rio follows, each step heavy, each breath harder than the last. She keeps her gaze fixed forward, refusing to look at Agatha’s face- refusing to see whatever expression might unravel her completely.

She opens the door. Agatha steps through. Rio shuts it behind her without a word, the click of the lock echoing throughout the quiet house.

And as soon as that lock clicks-

Rio collapses.

Her knees hit the floor hard, but she barely feels the pain. Her whole body gives out, finally allowed to fall apart. A broken sob rips out of her chest, raw and uncontrollable, and the dam she’s been holding together all day- hell, for months- bursts wide open.

She curls forward, palms pressed to the wood, shoulders shaking violently as she cries. It’s messy, loud, desperate. Grief floods through her- grief for Agatha, for what they used to be, for what they never got to be, for the pieces of herself she had just kept giving and giving until there was nothing left.

She sobs until her throat aches, until her breath stutters, until the weight of letting go crushes her fully.

She cries with the grief of having finally let go of Agatha-

of losing her.

Again.

Chapter 8: Chapter Eight
