Chapter 10: Chapter Ten

Chapter Notes

TW: brief discussions of child loss, sexual assault, and disordered eating
--
anyways... merry christmas to all who celebrate!

See the end of the chapter for more notes

2005

As soon as Evanora enters the room, Agatha rips her hand out of Rio’s. The movement is sharp, almost frantic. She shoots Rio a quick glance, catching the flash of surprise on her face, the hurt that follows a split second later. Guilt twists in Agatha’s chest. She gives her a look she hopes Rio understands- I’m sorry. I don’t have a choice.

Evanora walks over slowly, heels clicking against the hard hospital floor. She stops at the side of the bed, looking down at Agatha with pure disdain, her expression hard and unimpressed, as if Agatha is merely an inconvenience rather than her daughter lying in a hospital bed. Then her gaze shifts to Rio. She clears her throat, raises a single eyebrow, and glares at her unapologetically.

Rio gulps. Agatha can see it- the way her shoulders tense, the way she straightens like she’s bracing for impact. She slowly stands, eyes flicking between the two of them, clearly sensing the hostility crackling in the air.

“I’ll give you guys a minute.” She offers, forcing politeness into her voice. She hesitates, then adds a quiet, “call out if you need anything, I’ll be right outside,” directed at Agatha, her eyes softening before she turns and quickly scurries out of the room.

The door clicks shut. The silence that follows is heavy.

“Who was that?” Evanora spits once Rio is out of earshot.

Agatha’s throat tightens. She looks down at her hands, picking at her fingernails until the skin stings, focusing on that tiny pain instead of the much bigger one sitting in her chest. Eye contact feels impossible. Her heart races, pounding loud in her ears, and tears burn behind her eyes despite her best effort to hold them back.

“Just a friend.” She says, voice barely a whisper.

The word feels flimsy. Inadequate. But it’s all she can safely offer.

This is the last thing she needs to be dealing with right now.

“Hm.” Evanora grunts, folding her hands in front of her, posture stiff and closed off. “When were you planning on telling me you were pregnant?”

The word hits Agatha like a physical blow.

Were pregnant. Past tense.

The question feels detached- like Evanora is asking about the weather instead of the thing that just split Agatha open from the inside. She swallows, throat burning, eyes fixed on the thin blanket pulled up to her waist.

“I didn’t know how,” she says quietly. “I wasn’t sure when-”

Evanora exhales through her nose, a sound halfway between a scoff and a sigh. “Of course you didn’t. You never think these things through.”

Agatha flinches. Evanora’s voice is cold, dripping with disdain like it’s something she’s been waiting to say. Her fingers curl into the fabric, nails digging in. Her pulse thunders in her ears.

“I raised you better than this,” Evanora continues, voice sharp but controlled. “And yet here we are. You leave home and the first thing you do is ruin your life.”

Agatha flinches. “Mom-”

“Don’t,” Evanora snaps. “Don’t try to soften it. You didn’t trip and fall into this. You made choices.”

Tears flood Agatha’s eyes instantly, blurring the room into indistinct shapes and streaks of light. Her heart races, each beat loud and panicked. Her hands shake against the sheets, fingers curling inward as if she can make herself smaller, quieter, just by folding in on herself.

“I didn’t choose it,” she says, voice cracking. “I was raped.”

The words come out quiet. Broken. Fragile in a way that makes her feel exposed and raw. Her stomach churns.

Evanora studies her for a long moment, expression unreadable. Then her mouth tightens.

“That’s a serious accusation,” she says coolly. “And a very convenient one.”

Agatha’s breath stutters. “I’m telling you the truth.”

Evanora tilts her head.

“Just ask Rio,” Agatha blurts. “She knows what happened. She was there after. She-”

Evanora’s eyes harden. “I do not care what that girl thinks,” she cuts in. “She’s not family. And she’s certainly not a moral authority.”

Agatha’s shoulders sag. She bites her lip until she tastes blood. Her chest aches like something is pressing inward, crushing her lungs.

“Who got you pregnant?” Evanora asks.

It isn’t a question. It’s a demand.

Agatha swallows. Her mouth feels dry, her tongue heavy. Saying his name feels like reopening a wound that hasn’t even begun to heal. She hasn’t dared say it out loud since telling Rio, like speaking it might make everything more real, more permanent.

“Ralph Bohner,” she says finally, voice breaking. “He was in my law class.”

Evanora nods once, sharply. “He’s also a law student?” she says. “Good. You’ll make the perfect pair.”

The words don’t register at first. Agatha blinks through tears, confusion cutting through her grief as she wipes her cheeks with shaking hands.

“What?”

“You’re not a child,” Evanora says. “Actions have consequences. You got pregnant by him-intentional or not- so you make it right.”

Agatha’s heart starts to pound. “Make it right how?”

“You marry him.”

Her stomach churns violently, nausea rising as her mind scrambles to keep up. The logic feels warped, cruel, disconnected from reality.

“I-” Agatha shakes her head, tears falling freely now. “I lost the baby.”

She whispers it, the words barely audible, still foreign on her tongue. Detached. Saying it feels unreal, like she’s narrating someone else’s tragedy. She hasn’t even fully processed it herself.

Evanora’s voice is firm, unyielding. She leans in closer now, looming, her shadow falling over Agatha’s bed. “That doesn’t erase what you did.”

Agatha stares at her. “What I did?”

“You had sex before marriage,” Evanora says. “You disgraced yourself. You embarrassed this family. And now you want sympathy because things didn’t turn out the way you wanted?”

Agatha’s chest caves in. “I didn’t want any of this,” she sobs.

Evanora steps closer to the bed, lowering her voice. “Pain doesn’t absolve you of responsibility. Not in the Lord’s eyes. The only way forward is repentance. Marriage. Stability. Anything else is just more selfishness.”

The machines continue to beep steadily beside her as her mother’s words settle heavy in her chest- layered on top of her grief, her trauma, her loss- until it all feels like too much to carry.

“I just lost my baby,” she whispers. “And you’re telling me I need to marry the man who hurt me.”

“I’m telling you how to survive this without destroying your future.” Evanora’s expression hardens. “Don’t forget who’s paying your tuition. College isn’t cheap. And neither is your little dorm. Your health insurance. Your food.” Her lips curl around each word. “Everything you have right now comes from me.”

Agatha’s chest tightens, panic flaring hot and fast. “Mom, please-”

Evanora steps closer, her shadow falling over the bed. “You want to go to law school after this? You want a future that isn’t dictated by your mistakes?” Her voice lowers. “Then you’ll think very carefully about the choices you make next.”

Evanora straightens, expression hardening into something final. “You don’t marry that boy,” she says, “and you’re done. No tuition. No support. No safety net.” She pauses, letting the words settle like a sentence. “You can kiss your dreams goodbye.”

Agatha’s breath comes in short, broken gasps. “You wouldn’t,” she says weakly, even as she knows the answer.

“I already am,” Evanora replies. “So think long and hard about your future, Agatha. Because this is the last favor I’m offering you.”

She turns toward the door, heels clicking softly against the floor.

Behind her, Agatha collapses inward on the hospital bed, clutching the blanket to her chest as a broken, shattered cry spills out of her, the sound jagged with grief and fear and everything she’s just lost.

2025

Saturday – 3 months later

Agatha sits rigid in the passenger seat, picking at the skin around her nails until it stings. She keeps her eyes fixed on the windshield, on the blur of streetlights sliding past, anything but him.

“So,” Ralph says beside her, his voice cutting through the silence. His hands tighten on the steering wheel, knuckles whitening. “Are you going to answer my question?”

“No.” Agatha doesn’t look at him. Her tone is flat, final.

A muscle jumps in Ralph’s jaw. He exhales sharply through his nose, the sound impatient. “You can’t stay mad at me forever, sweetheart.”

Her stomach twists at the word.

“Yes, I can,” she snaps, finally turning just enough to glare at him. “And don’t call me that.”

Ralph scoffs, shaking his head like she’s being unreasonable, like this is an inconvenience to him. He doesn’t apologize. Doesn’t even look at her.

“Whatever,” he mutters.

The car jerks as he pulls into the parking lot, braking harder than necessary. The engine cuts off with a sharp click. He sits there for a moment, jaw tight, eyes forward, radiating irritation.

Agatha stays frozen in her seat, heart pounding, nails aching where she’s torn them raw- counting the seconds until she can get out, until she can breathe again.

Ralph finally climbs out of the car, slamming the door shut behind him hard enough to make Agatha flinch. She waits a beat before following, the cold immediately slicing through her coat as she steps onto the pavement. Snow drifts lazily from the sky, catching in her hair, melting against her lashes. Neither of them speaks as they walk side by side toward the mall entrance, far enough apart to feel the distance lay heavy between them.

The warmth hits them all at once when they step inside- thick, artificial heat covering her skin like relief. Agatha exhales shakily and lets herself be pulled along toward the center of the mall, where Coven for Climate is already in motion.

Her eyes fall on the setup. Folding tables draped in green cloth. Stacks of flyers fanned out neatly. Clipboards crowded with donation forms and sign-up sheets. Calendars printed with upcoming cleanups and meetings, their dates bold and bright. Volunteers move everywhere at once, weaving through shoppers with practiced ease, calling out smiles and soft pitches, their voices blending with the sound of holiday music overhead.

Chicago winters make park cleanups brutal- twenty minutes outside is all anyone can stand before fingers go numb and teeth start chattering- so December has become their month of outreach instead. Fundraising. Education. Showing up where people are already gathering.

For the Saturday before Christmas, that means the mall.

Agatha’s eyes land on the setup near the fountain- a plastic Christmas tree, tinsel glistening under fluorescent lights, two red velvet chairs positioned meticulously. A hand-painted sign reads Meet Santa! in looping letters, complete with snowflakes drawn in the corners. A few kids already hover nearby, tugging at parents’ coats, eyes bright with anticipation.

Her stomach churns.

Somehow- through a combination of poor timing, Rio’s insistence, and having nothing better to do, Agatha and Ralph had been roped into dressing up as Mr. and Mrs. Claus.

Agatha takes a steadying breath before marching over to Yelena, pasting on a smile. Her stomach is already tight, nerves buzzing under her skin like static.

“Hey,” she says brightly, not bothering to check whether Ralph is still hovering behind her. “Any idea where the Santa costumes are?”

Yelena looks up from the box she’s sorting through, a strand of hair slipping loose from her bun. She pauses, thinking, then shakes her head apologetically. “No, I don’t. I’m sorry.”

Before Agatha can respond, Wanda’s voice cuts across the mall, calling Yelena’s name with increasing urgency. Yelena turns, already half gone. “I’d go find Rio,” she adds over her shoulder. “She’ll know.”

And then she’s gone, swallowed immediately by the chaos of setup.

Agatha exhales hard through her nose. “Perfect,” she mutters.

She drifts away from the table, shoulders slumping as she scans the crowd of volunteers across the mall’s open center. Red and green banners hang from the railings above. Christmas music hums overhead, something cheerful and tacky. Kids run past clutching candy canes. It all feels too loud. Too bright. Too much on top of everything else going on.

For the past few months, she and Rio have perfected the art of avoidance.

Short exchanges. Necessary ones only. Logistics, schedules, volunteer counts. No small talk. No checking in. No lingering glances that might open the thought something more.

It’s Agatha’s fault. She knows that. Or at least most of it is.

Still, it doesn’t hurt any less.

She spots Rio near one of the donation tables, mid-conversation with a young couple. Rio’s animated in that way she gets when she’s talking about the organization- hands moving, posture open, smile easy and genuine. Agatha watches her laugh at something the woman says, nodding enthusiastically as she hands them a flier.

God. Of course she looks like that.

Agatha waits until the couple walks away before approaching, turning her face into something neutral. Her throat feels dry all of a sudden.

“Morning,” she says, clearing her throat.

Rio turns.

The smile is immediate- but it doesn’t quite reach her eyes. It’s practiced. Controlled. The kind Agatha recognizes all too well.

“Morning,” Rio replies. “What do you need?”

The words hit her wrong, even though she knows Rio doesn’t mean them cruelly. Even though she knows she’s earned them.

Agatha’s jaw tightens. “Oh, so I can only talk to you if I need something?” she snaps, the words coming out harsher than she intends. Her chest feels tight, too tight, like it could tear open at any second.

Rio blinks, clearly caught off guard. For a split second, something flickers across her face- hurt, maybe- but she covers it quickly, turning it into something safer.

“That,” Rio says slowly, choosing her words with care, “seems to be the pattern lately, yes.”

The truth of it lands like a punch.

Agatha exhales sharply through her nose, shoulders stiffening before dropping just an inch. The anger drains as fast as it came, leaving behind something worse- embarrassment. Regret. The familiar ache of realizing she’s lashed out at the one person who didn’t deserve it.

“I didn’t- ” She stops, presses her lips together, then tries again, quieter. “I’m not trying to be an asshole.”

Rio hums softly. Her gaze drifts past Agatha’s shoulder, out toward the bustle of the mall, like she’s giving her space to back out if she wants to. “Could’ve fooled me,” she mutters, not unkindly.

Agatha winces. “I’m just having a day,” she says, rubbing her thumb into the seam of her coat sleeve. “And you happened to be standing here.”

That finally earns her a look. Rio’s eyebrow lifts slightly. “Lucky me.”

Agatha lets out a breath that’s halfway to a laugh, but it dies in her throat. She swallows. “Yelena sent me to find you. I need to know where the Santa costumes are.”

“Oh.” Rio’s posture shifts immediately- professional, grounded. “Yeah. Storage room behind the north food court. Wanda moved them earlier so the kids wouldn’t see Santa getting dressed.” A pause. “You and Ralph are doing that, right?”

Agatha’s mouth twists. “Unfortunately.”

That does it. The corner of Rio’s mouth tilts upward, just barely. It’s gone almost as soon as it appears, but Agatha catches it- and it hurts in that way good things always do when they remind you of what you’re missing.

“Well, good luck,” Rio says, offering a small, careful nod that feels more like closure than encouragement. She turns away before Agatha can respond, already busying herself with a stack of flyers.

Agatha watches her go for half a second too long.

Then she turns to where Ralph still stands a few feet away, arms crossed, weight shifted impatiently onto one leg. She doesn’t look at him when she flicks her head toward the food court, already starting to walk, already assuming he’ll follow.

They weave through the mall in silence, past kiosks selling candles and phone cases, past families dragging kids in puffy coats and mittens. Agatha’s jaw stays clenched the entire way.

The storage closet is tucked behind the back of the food court. Agatha tugs the door open and steps inside without hesitation. Ralph follows, letting the door swing shut behind them with a hollow click.

The room smells faintly of dust and cardboard.

Boxes are stacked unevenly along the walls, labeled in thick black marker- cleaning supplies, decorations, costumes- Agatha moves straight for the last one, flipping the lid open with more force than necessary.

Fabric spills out in a mess of red and white.

Her hand lands on the Mrs. Claus dress- thick velvet, fake fur trim already shedding slightly. Her stomach lurches. She swallows hard, grabs the white wig beside it, and forces herself not to gag.

She shoves the Santa suit toward Ralph without looking at him. He catches it, silent, expression unreadable.

“We gotta change here,” Agatha mutters. “So no kids see.”

Ralph nods once, jaw tight. The silence stretches heavy.

Agatha hesitates, then looks up at him fully for the first time since they entered. “Turn around.”

“What?” Ralph snaps, brows knitting together.

“You heard me,” she says flatly. “Turn around while I change.”

He scoffs, annoyance flashing across his face. “You’re kidding.”

“I’m not.” Her voice is steady, even if her hands aren’t. “Turn. Around.”

Ralph exhales sharply, muttering something under his breath she doesn’t quite catch- something bitter probably- but he turns toward the wall anyway, shoulders tense. He starts pulling his jacket off with jerky movements, clearly irritated.

Agatha doesn’t waste a second.

She strips out of her coat and sweater quickly, movements rushed and tight, keeping her eyes on the floor. The dress is heavier than she expects, awkward as she steps into it, tugging it up over her hips with shaking fingers.

Behind her, she hears the rustle of Ralph changing too- the sound of clothes hitting the floor, the scrape of boots being kicked off.

She forces the wig on last, shoving it down over her hair, ignoring how wrong it feels, how ridiculous and humiliating this all is.

Agatha swallows, breath shallow, and straightens her spine anyway.

“Done,” she says quietly.

Ralph turns back around, already fully suited up in red, white beard hanging crooked against his chest. Agatha withholds the snarky comment sitting on the tip of her tongue. Instead, she reaches for the door and yanks it open, marching back down the mall corridor with purpose, the hem of the Mrs. Claus dress dragging along the tile behind her.

She pastes on a bright, pleasant smile for the kids. Mrs. Claus can’t look tired. Or angry. Or like she’s barely holding herself together.

The noise of the mall swells as they re-enter the main space. They shimmy through the crowd toward the center display, glittery letters reading SANTA’S WORKSHOP on the top of the constructed backdrop.

“Look! It’s Santa!” a little girl squeals from somewhere to Agatha’s left.

Agatha’s smile widens on instinct. She gives the girl a small wave, bending slightly at the knees to seem friendlier, softer. She doesn’t look at Ralph, but she hopes- briefly- that he’s doing the same.

They finally reach the chairs, which is when she notices Billy.

He’s standing off to the side, bouncing on the balls of his feet, dressed head to toe in green felt. The elf costume is aggressively festive- striped tights, curly shoes with bells that jingle every time he moves, and oversized ears that stick out from beneath his hat. He looks ridiculous.

It takes everything in Agatha not to laugh.

She leans toward him as she passes, lowering her voice. “Love the outfit,” she murmurs, smirk tugging at her mouth. “Very festive.”

Billy beams, chest puffing out proudly. “Thank you. I take my craft very seriously.”

She sits. The chair is stiff beneath her, the dress bunching awkwardly around her legs as she adjusts it. Ralph settles beside her, heavy and silent. The space between them feels vast despite how close they are.

Kids begin to rush forward immediately, a line forming faster than Agatha expects. Parents hover nearby with phones out, already recording. She smooths her hands over her lap, posture straight, chin lifted.

The first kid- a boy with flushed cheeks and a Spider-Man hat- walks up eagerly. Agatha leans forward, voice warm as she gestures him closer, letting Ralph take the lead. She nods along, smiles at the right moments, laughs softly when the kid giggles.

She’s good at this. At pretending.

But every so often, between kids, her smile falters. Her eyes drift- not to Ralph- but outward. To the volunteers moving around the edges. To Billy handing out candy canes. To the far side of the workshop where Rio stands near a table, clipboard tucked under her arm.

Rio doesn’t look over.

Of course she doesn’t.

Agatha swallows, forcing her attention back as another child climbs up onto Ralph’s lap. She tilts her head, offers encouragement, and plasters back on that tight smile that has begun to hurt her face.

After a few more kids shuffle through- sticky hands, excited chatter, the occasional tug on Santa’s beard- the line finally thins, then disappears altogether. The velvet rope sags slightly, abandoned for the moment. The noise around them dulls just enough to make the silence between Agatha and Ralph noticeable.

Ralph groans loudly, slouching back in his chair. “I fucking hate this,” he mutters, tugging at the red coat. “I hate kids.”

Agatha stiffens. Her jaw tightens beneath the forced smile she’s still wearing. “Suck it up, Santa,” she snaps under her breath, shooting him a warning glare.

He scoffs, rolling his eyes. “Why do I even have to be here?” he whines sharply, voice rising. A few nearby shoppers glance over.

Her patience finally snaps. “Because you made a commitment!” she yells back, the words bursting out before she can stop them.

“To Santa?” he shoots back.

“No, to me!” The words rip out of her chest, raw and shaking. Her eyes sting instantly, vision blurring as the weight of everything presses down at once.

A throat clears behind her.

Agatha freezes.

She doesn’t need to turn around to know who it is. Her stomach drops anyway.

“Mrs. Claus,” Rio says evenly, her voice carrying just enough to sound official. Calm. Controlled. “May I speak with you for a minute?”

Shit.

Agatha swallows hard, nodding once. Her movements feel stiff as she pushes herself up from the chair, smoothing the front of the dress with trembling hands. She ducks under the velvet rope, heart hammering like she’s sixteen again and being sent to the principal’s office.

Up close, Rio looks… tired. Clipboard tucked under her arm, brows slightly furrowed- not angry, exactly, but disappointed. Which somehow feels worse.

Rio’s hand clamps around Agatha’s wrist securely, making her heart lurch. The sudden touch yanks her off balance, and she stumbles a step before Rio’s grip steadies her. She guides her a few feet away, far enough that any shoppers’ voices fade into the background hum of the mall.

Rio lowers her voice. “You can’t yell like that. Not here. Not in front of the kids.”

“I know,” Agatha says quickly. “I’m sorry. He just-” She cuts herself off, pressing her lips together. She refuses to cry in a mall dressed like Mrs. Claus.

Rio exhales through her nose, glancing back toward the empty chairs, then back at Agatha. “Do I need to kick him out?” She asks softly, leaning in slightly, gaze lowered. Her shoulder brushes Agatha’s as she shifts her weight, and for a brief moment, the closeness makes Agatha’s stomach flip. All she can focus on is the heat of Rio’s presence pressing in.

Agatha raises an eyebrow. “What?” Her pulse quickens. She can feel the brush of Rio’s sleeve against her arm, subtle but deliberate, and it sends a jolt of awareness down her spine.

“I can send Ralph home if you want. Just say the word.” The words are soft, careful, almost a whisper, but the sharpness behind them carries weight.

“I- no, it- it’s okay.” Agatha stammers, her fingers curling slightly around the hem of her dress. Her chest tightens, a low ache of longing and confusion bubbling up.

“Are you sure? He’s doing a half-assed job at Santa anyway.” Rio’s voice is teasing now, light, but the seriousness behind it lingers in the space between them.

Agatha swallows hard. She hesitates, distracted. There’s something electric in the way Rio leans in, the way her hand lingers on her wrist for a fraction longer than necessary. “Yeah, okay,” she finally says, voice soft, almost breathless. “Go ahead.”

Rio nods, brushing past her, shoulder grazing once more as she strides toward Ralph. The contact leaves a trail of warmth along Agatha’s arm, and she can’t stop the shiver that races down her spine. Her heartbeat quickens, eyes following Rio’s movement, lingering on the curve of her back, the way she moves with quiet authority.

She marches over to Ralph, not even glancing at him, and beckons with a single finger, her movements sharp and deliberate. Her shoes click against the tile as she walks away, a rhythm of authority in each step. Ralph hesitates, muttering under his breath, but finally rises and follows, his jaw tight, his eyes narrowed in annoyance.

Agatha watches from a distance, observing Rio speaking in a low, calm tone, her gaze steady. Ralph’s frown deepens with every word, and then- something snaps. He yells, face red, veins visible in his neck, and storms off toward the nearest exit, hands balled into fists. The tension lingers in the air, but Rio simply exhales, rolls her eyes, and turns back toward Agatha with a small, almost imperceptible shake of her head, the corners of her mouth twitching as if fighting amusement.

Agatha smooths the folds of her bulky dress, tugging at the white trim, feeling the warmth of her flushed cheeks and the lingering adrenaline from watching Ralph’s outburst. She sits back down, her hands folding neatly in her lap, trying to mimic Rio’s calm, but her stomach still churns.

The line of children has grown, and their chatter and laughter fill the mall with energy. One little girl tugs at her mother’s sleeve and calls out, “Where did Santa go?” Agatha’s chest tightens with worry. She looks to Rio for reassurance and finds her already kneeling slightly to meet the children’s height, giving them a bright, genuine smile.

“Hey there, everyone,” Rio says, voice warm and playful. “Santa had a little emergency with one of his toy machines, so he had to step away. But don’t worry! Mrs. Claus is here, and she’ll make sure every wish gets back to him.”

Relief ripples through the line of children, their tense little shoulders dropping as giggles and excited whispers spread. Agatha lets out a breath as she catches Rio’s eye across the space. Rio gives her the smallest nod- but it’s enough. Something in Agatha’s chest loosens, just a fraction. She straightens her back and pastes on the bright, practiced smile she’s perfected.

Rio gently takes the hand of the first boy in line and leads him toward Agatha. He can’t be more than four or five, bundled in a puffy coat with mittens dangling from a string, eyes wide as he looks up at the chair.

“I’m scared,” he tells Rio, his voice barely louder than the hum of the mall.

Agatha’s heart drops instantly, a sharp, aching tug. She stills, afraid to move, afraid she’ll do something wrong.

“Why are you scared?” Rio asks softly, bending down to his level, her voice warm and steady.

“I’ve never talked to Mrs. Claus before,” he says, fidgeting with the zipper of his jacket. “What if she’s not as nice as Santa?”

Rio smiles, easy and reassuring, the kind of smile that makes the kids trust her without question. “Well,” she says, glancing over at Agatha, “I’m actually very good friends with Mrs. Claus here.” She flicks a smile toward her. “And I promise you- she’s very kind.”

Agatha’s chest tightens at the sight. The way Rio speaks to him so naturally, the softness in her expression, the gentleness in her movements- it all comes so easily to her. A sudden, piercing ache hits Agatha as the thought of Nicky rises uninvited. Of the moments they never got. Of the version of this scene that will never exist. She swallows hard, forcing the smile to stay in place.

Rio leans in closer to the boy’s ear, lowering her voice. “Maybe,” she adds, “even kinder than Santa himself.”

The boy giggles, the sound light and infectious, and any remaining fear seems to melt away. He toddles closer to Agatha’s chair, clinging briefly to her leg. Rio scoops him up with ease and gently settles him onto Agatha’s lap before stepping back.

Agatha wraps an arm around his small waist, surprised by how natural it feels, how instinctive. Her smile softens into something real.

“What’s your name?” she asks.

“Oliver,” he says shyly, eyes flicking up to meet hers for just a second before darting away again.

“Well, Oliver,” Agatha says, her voice warm now without effort, “what would you like for Christmas?”

He leans in close, warm breath tickling her ear as he whispers, “A train set.”

Agatha gasps dramatically, hand flying to her chest. “Well that’s a very good choice, dear,” she says. “I’ll make sure to get that back to Santa for you.”

“Thank you!” Oliver chirps, already squirming free. He hops down and runs back to his mom, who beams at Agatha as she scoops him up.

Agatha watches him go, her smile lingering as something tender and bittersweet settles in her chest. When she looks back up, her eyes find Rio again.

Rio is smiling- but it doesn’t quite reach her eyes. They’re glassy, heavy with something she doesn’t let surface for long. She notices Agatha watching and quickly wipes it away, straightening her posture again.

“I’ll leave you to it, then,” she says softly, offering a sad smile before turning and disappearing back into the crowd of volunteers.

Agatha watches her go, the noise of the mall rushing back in as the next child steps forward. She forces herself to refocus, lifting her chin and opening her arms once more, smile bright.

When they finally break for lunch, Agatha is more relieved than expected to peel herself out of the hot, suffocating Mrs. Claus costume. The velvet sticks to her skin as she wrestles it off in the supply closet, the wig itching, the dress heavy in her hands. She exhales once she’s back in her own clothes, shoulders loosening like she’s been holding herself rigid for hours.

By the time she reaches the food court, the group has already clustered around the catering setup. Despite the fact that they’re in the middle of a mall packed with food options, Wanda- of course- had insisted on bringing her own catering. So now they’re all trapped in another painfully slow line, shuffling forward inch by inch while Wanda oversees everything.

Agatha joins the line with a sigh, tapping her foot against the tile, arms folded as she watches volunteers ahead of her take their sweet time. Why does everyone suddenly forget how to move when food is involved?

Her gaze drifts without permission.

To Rio.

Of course.

She’s standing off to the side near Wanda, arms crossed loosely, eyes tracking every movement Wanda makes, who’s currently helping herself to a meal. Agatha’s stomach drops in a way she refuses to acknowledge.

She’s just… watching her put food on a plate. How fascinating could that possibly be?

Agatha scoffs under her breath and looks away, annoyed with herself more than anything. She shouldn’t care. It’s none of her business. So what if Rio’s crushing on another woman? That chapter is closed. Finished. Over.

The words sound hollow even in her own head.

The line moves forward, and Agatha finally grabs a plate, immediately snatching up a sandwich like it might disappear if she hesitates. As she does, she catches sight of Lilia standing close to Rio, speaking to her in a low, serious voice. Rio’s posture is closed off, shoulders tight, eyes downcast. Lilia listens, nods once, then wordlessly places a sandwich onto a plate and presses it into Rio’s hands, offering her a soft, knowing smile.

Agatha frowns, confusion prickling at her. What was that about?

Before she can dwell on it, the line pushes her forward again. At the end of the table, Christmas desserts are laid out like bait- reindeer cupcakes, frosted Santa cookies, chocolate peppermint muffins dusted with powdered sugar. There are even big mugs of hot cocoa, steam curling into the air.

Agatha doesn’t hesitate.

She piles desserts onto her plate, more than her fair share, more than she can justify. She turns away before anyone can comment and scans the food court for somewhere to sit.

Billy, Jen, and Alice are already crowded around one table, laughing about something. She considers joining them- but then she spots Rio sitting alone a few tables away, shoulders slumped, staring down at her plate like it’s personally offended her.

Agatha pauses.

Then, without letting herself think too hard about it, she heads over.

Agatha drops into the chair beside Rio without announcing herself. She doesn’t look at her right away. Instead, she unwraps her sandwich and takes a large bite, chewing slowly, like this is the most normal thing in the world.

Out of the corner of her eye, she sees Rio stiffen.

Rio turns her head, clearly startled. “Hi?” she says, confusion written plainly across her face.

“Hey,” Agatha replies easily, like her heart isn’t hammering in her chest. “Thought you could use some company.”

Rio swallows hard, fingers immediately tangling together in her lap. “Uhh- thanks,” she whispers, the word sounding uncertain, like she’s bracing for something.

Agatha nods toward Rio’s untouched sandwich, following her gaze. It sits there, pristine, the wrapper barely disturbed. “You gonna eat that?”

Rio goes quiet. Agatha notices things she probably shouldn’t- the way Rio’s shoulders are drawn tight, the way her leg bounces rapidly beneath the table. Finally, Rio shrugs. “Probably.”

Agatha raises an eyebrow, unconvinced. She sets her sandwich aside and reaches instead for one of the peppermint muffins. She takes a big bite, powdered sugar dusting her lip as she lets out an exaggerated groan on instinct. “Fuck, that’s good.”

She doesn’t give herself time to overthink it. She grabs the second muffin from her plate and slides it across the table toward Rio, the paper liner crinkling softly. “Here, you can have my other one.”

For a split second, nothing happens.

Then Agatha hears Rio inhale sharply.

The sound catches- fractures- and comes out as a choked sob.

Agatha’s head snaps toward her.

Tears are already spilling down Rio’s cheeks, faster than she can keep up with them. She drags the sleeve of her sweater across her face again and again, red-eyed and blotchy, like she’s trying to erase the evidence before anyone can notice. Her shoulders hunch inward, chin tucked down, body folding in on itself as she fights to keep the sobs quiet- like she’s embarrassed to be seen like this. Like she’s doing something wrong by falling apart.

Agatha freezes.

Her heart drops straight into her stomach, heavy and sickening, and for a moment everything else fades into static. The hum of the food court dulls into a distant roar- the scrape of chairs, the clatter of trays, someone laughing too loudly a few tables away. None of it feels real anymore. All she can see is Rio, breaking right in front of her.

“Rio?” Agatha says quietly, the name slipping out before she can stop it. Her voice comes out softer than she intends, gentler, like a reflex she never quite unlearned.

The sound of her name seems to snap the last fragile thread holding Rio together.

Her shoulders cave completely. A sharp, uneven breath catches in her throat, then another, and suddenly she’s bawling- silent at first, lips trembling as she fights it, then breaking into jagged, uneven sobs she can’t contain. She presses her sleeve to her mouth like that might muffle the sound, but it only makes her shake harder.

Agatha’s chest tightens painfully.

The old panic flares instantly- the familiar, gut-deep urge to reach out, to pull Rio into her arms, to fix this, to make it stop. Her body leans forward before she realizes it, muscles already preparing to move.

But her hands stay glued to her lap.

They don’t get to do that anymore.

Not after everything that’s been said. Not after everything that’s broken. Comfort like that belongs to a version of them that doesn’t exist now- a softer timeline, one where words weren’t used like weapons, where lines hadn’t been drawn and redrawn until nothing was left but distance.

All because of her. Because of her mouth. Her anger. Her carelessness.

Agatha swallows hard, nails biting into her palms as she forces herself to stay still. She tells herself this is what boundaries look like. This is what she asked for.

Then Rio curls in on herself even more, like she’s trying to disappear. Like the weight of her own body is suddenly too much to carry. Her leg bounces frantically beneath the table, knee knocking against the metal in short, desperate bursts.

Agatha’s resolve shatters.

Fuck boundaries.

She moves before doubt can catch up, sliding closer and wrapping her arms around Rio’s shoulders, pulling her into her chest. The contact sends a shock through her- warm and grounding and devastating all at once. Rio feels too familiar in her arms, like muscle memory snapping back into place without permission.

For half a second, Agatha braces herself. Waits for Rio to stiffen. To pull away. To tell her to stop.

She doesn’t.

Instead, Rio collapses into her, a broken, helpless sound tearing out of her as she buries her face into Agatha’s shoulder. Her fingers clutch at the fabric of Agatha’s sweater, gripping tight, knuckles whitening like she’s afraid Agatha might vanish if she lets go. Her whole body shakes, breath hitching unevenly against Agatha’s collarbone.

Agatha’s breath wavers.

She lifts one hand to Rio’s hair, smoothing it back slowly, carefully, like she’s handling something fragile. The motion is instinctive, intimate, something she’s done a hundred times before without thinking.

“I’ve got you,” Agatha murmurs, voice low and steady despite the way her chest aches. She presses her cheek lightly to Rio’s temple, grounding them both. “You’re okay. I’m here.”

She holds her through the trembling, through the uneven breaths, through the quiet devastation of it all. She blocks out the noise of the food court- the chatter, the clinking plates, the world continuing on like this isn’t happening.

For this moment, it’s just them.

Eventually, the sobs soften. Rio pulls back slowly, like it takes effort to put space between them. She wipes at her cheeks with the back of her hand, eyes red and glassy, and forces a small smile that doesn’t quite stick.

“Sorry,” she mutters, voice rough. “I’m being so fucking dramatic.”

Agatha shakes her head immediately, instinctively. Her hand is still warm and steady against Rio’s back. She doesn’t pull it away. “You’re not,” she says quietly. “Are you okay? Like- really okay?”

Rio exhales, the sound almost a laugh but not quite, coming out thin and hollow. “I could be better.”

Agatha’s gaze drifts down to the table. The untouched sandwich. The muffin she offered with such careless hope, like a good dessert could fix something this deep. Something in her chest tightens as the pieces finally click into place, sharp and awful and obvious in hindsight. It hurts in a way that feels deserved.

She inhales slowly. “Can I ask you something?”

Rio stiffens, just a fraction. “Absolutely not.” She tries to play it off like a joke, but the edge beneath the words gives her away.

Agatha tilts her head, voice softening despite herself. “Rio…”

“What, Agatha?” Rio snaps, the name slicing out too fast, too loud.

Agatha flinches. “I- sorry. We don’t have to talk about it.” She swallows hard, forcing the words past the lump in her throat. “I just… I’m here. If you need me.”

Rio’s eyes lift to hers, sharp and searching. “Are you?”

The question lands heavy between them.

Agatha’s own words come rushing back, uninvited and merciless.

I’m fucking done.
I don’t wanna see you anymore.
I don’t wanna see your face.

Her jaw tightens, teeth pressing together like that might keep her from falling apart.

“I’m so sorry, Rio,” she whispers. The apology is small, but it’s raw. “I didn’t mean it.”

Rio stares at her for a long moment, expression unreadable. Then something shifts behind her eyes, defenses snapping back into place.

“Yeah,” she says quietly. “Well, you said it.”

She stands abruptly, chair scraping harshly against the floor. She wipes at her face one last time, shoulders squaring, and walks away without looking back.

Agatha stays frozen, watching her disappear into the crowd.

The sandwich still sits untouched on the table next to her, taunting.

And Agatha feels her heart crack all over again.

Chapter 11: Chapter Eleven

Chapter Notes

TW: disordered eating, brief throwing up (not detailed)

See the end of the chapter for more notes

2005

Rio waits until Evanora’s heels fade down the hallway before she moves.

The hospital corridor is too bright, the fluorescent lights buzzing faintly overhead. Her chest feels tight as she lifts her hand and knocks once, softly, like she’s afraid the sound might shatter something fragile on the other side.

“Agatha?” she calls, already reaching for the handle.

The door gives easily, swinging open just enough for her to slip inside.

The room is dim, curtains half-drawn against the early morning light. The steady beep of a monitor fills the silence, slow and painful. And then Rio sees her.

Agatha is curled in on herself in the narrow hospital bed, knees drawn up, arms wrapped tightly around a thin pillow like it’s the only thing keeping her together. Her face is turned away, buried into the fabric, but the sound of her sobs fills the room- muffled, broken, tearing their way out of her chest. Her whole body shakes with them, sharp and uncontrollable.

Rio’s heart cracks.

She doesn’t think. She just moves.

She crosses the room in a few quick steps and drops to her knees beside the bed, hands hovering for half a second before settling into Agatha’s hair. She strokes it gently, like she’s touching something sacred, something already shattering beneath her fingers.

“Sweetheart,” she whispers, voice barely holding together. “I’m here. I’ve got you.”

Agatha doesn’t respond. Her sobs only hitch harder, like hearing Rio’s voice finally gave them permission to exist.

Tears burn in Rio’s eyes. This trembling, shattered version of Agatha doesn’t match the girl she knows. The sharp wit, the fire, the unshakable confidence. Seeing her reduced to this hurts in a way Rio didn’t know was possible.

She pushes herself up slowly, carefully climbing onto the bed behind her. The mattress dips under her weight. She turns sideways and presses close, tucking herself against Agatha’s back, burying her face into the crook of her neck. One arm slips around her waist, pulling her in firmly, protectively, like if she holds tight enough she can keep Agatha from falling apart entirely.

Agatha clings to her instantly, fingers curling around Rio’s, desperate and shaking.

Rio holds her there, breathing her in. She doesn’t say anything else. She just stays.

After a long moment, Agatha’s sobs soften enough for words to break through.

“She’s making me marry him.”

The sentence comes out fractured, barely audible, like it physically hurts to say.

Rio stiffens.

“Marry who?” she asks quietly, even though something ugly twists in her gut before Agatha answers.

“Ralph.”

The name hits her like a blow.

Her stomach drops, nausea curling sharp and sudden. “What?” she breathes, voice breaking despite herself. Tears spill over now, blurring her vision. “How?”

Agatha finally turns in her arms, face crumpled and wrecked. Her eyes are swollen and red, skin blotchy, tears streaking down her cheeks. Rio has to bite the inside of her mouth to keep from completely breaking at the sight.

“She won’t pay my tuition if I don’t,” Agatha chokes. “She said she’s done supporting me. I can’t afford college by myself, Rio. Not undergrad. Not law school. I can’t-” Her breath stutters. “I can’t do it on my own.”

Rio shakes her head, tears slipping down her face now too. She squeezes her eyes shut for a moment, forcing herself to breathe. This can’t be about her. Not now. Agatha needs her steady- put together.

She cups Agatha’s face gently, thumbs brushing under her eyes, wiping tears away even as more replace them.

“We’ll figure something out,” she whispers, the words instinctive. “We will.”

Even as she says it, she knows she doesn’t believe it.

“How?” Agatha asks, voice small and hopeless. “I don’t see a way out of this.”

Rio’s chest aches. Her heart pounds painfully as she searches for answers she doesn’t have.

“I-” Her voice wavers. She swallows hard. “I don’t know yet. But we’ll find one. Together.”

Agatha nods weakly, like she’s holding onto the word together with everything she has left. She curls back into Rio’s chest, breathing shallow, uneven breaths, fingers fisted in her shirt.

Rio wraps both arms around her, tighter this time.

Inside, panic flares viciously. Her mind races, grasping at possibilities- jobs, loans, scholarships, miracles. Nothing sticks. Nothing feels real enough to save them.

She curses herself silently.

She should be able to fix this. She should be able to protect her. But the truth settles heavy and sickening in her stomach-

She has no control.

All she can do is hold Agatha and hope it’s enough to keep her from breaking completely.

And right now, that feels terrifyingly insufficient.

2025

“Keep it 100 on the land, the sea, the sky,” Billy sings from the driver’s seat, his voice loud and off-key as he attempts the choreography with one hand while the other stays loosely hooked over the steering wheel. The car swerves just enough to make Agatha grip the edge of the seat. Taylor Swift has been playing the entire drive- no skips, no compromises.

Wanda lets out a sharp sigh from the passenger seat, arms crossed tight against her chest. “I hated this album,” she says flatly, staring straight ahead like the song personally offended her.

Agatha scoffs from the back seat, irritation flaring for no real reason other than the sound of Wanda’s voice. “No one asked,” she shoots back.

“I never moved on from Reputation,” Rio adds from beside her, eyes glued to her phone, thumb scrolling mindlessly.

Billy cranes his head around at the red light, grinning. “That makes so much sense for you.”

Rio finally looks up, one eyebrow arching. “What’s that supposed to mean?”

“Oh, don’t worry,” he says easily, turning back as he signals and pulls up to the curb outside the restaurant. “It’s a compliment.”

The car lurches to a stop, and everyone climbs out, the hum of traffic and distant chatter filling the space left behind by the music. The restaurant glows warmly through the windows, already buzzing with noise.

“Thanks again for driving us, Billy,” Rio says as they head toward the entrance, her tone genuine.

“It’s no biggie!” he replies, shuffling along behind them while fixing the tissue paper in his gift bag, making sure it looks presentable. “I can’t drink, so I might as well let you guys enjoy yourselves.”

Agatha snorts. “I still can’t believe you bought Jen and Alice a present. They’re moving in together, not getting married.”

“Well, they organized this whole dinner party for it,” Billy shrugs. “I thought it was only appropriate.”

“Yeah, ’cause Jen’s dramatic as fuck,” Agatha mutters with a smirk.

“And wants any excuse to get drunk,” Rio adds, slipping seamlessly into the conversation just as the hostess approaches.

Agatha stiffens.

Rio is suddenly right there- too close. Close enough that Agatha can feel her warmth, catch the faint scent of her perfume. The proximity sends an unwanted shiver down her spine. They haven’t talked since the food court incident last week. Haven’t really looked at each other, either. The car ride over had been filled with music and jokes and carefully maintained distance.

Agatha’s chest tightens.

She doesn’t know what to say. Doesn’t know how to act normal, or how to support Rio without reopening wounds or saying the wrong thing again. Every word feels like it could be a mistake waiting to happen.

The silence between them is loud.

And it’s killing her.

They trail behind the hostess at an almost painfully slow pace, weaving through narrow aisles and tightly packed tables. The restaurant hums with overlapping conversations, clinking glasses, and bursts of laughter that feel too loud. Agatha keeps her eyes forward, hands flexing tightly at her side, acutely aware of Rio’s presence beside her.

Every now and then, Rio’s arm brushes against hers- brief, accidental… devastating. Each touch sends a sharp jolt through Agatha’s chest, her skin prickling. She tells herself it’s nothing. That it doesn’t mean anything. That it’s just close quarters and bad luck.

It doesn’t work.

They reach the long table tucked near the back, already crowded with familiar faces. Jen, Alice, Yelena, and Lilia are deep in conversation, heads leaned in close, wine glasses half-empty. Rio slides into the open seat beside Yelena without hesitation. Agatha watches, stupidly frozen, as Wanda immediately swoops in to claim the chair on Rio’s other side- literally shoulder-checking Billy out of the way to do it.

Agatha snorts under her breath, rolling her eyes as her stomach twists unpleasantly.

Jen notices them and immediately lights up, pushing her chair back and jumping to her feet. She squeals, crossing the space in seconds before wrapping Agatha in a tight hug. Agatha melts into it, arms circling her instinctively, grounding herself in the familiar warmth.

“Congratulations,” she murmurs into Jen’s ear, her smile real, if a little tired.

“Thanks, Agatha,” Jen says, pulling back with a grin so wide it borders on feral. “I’m so happy.”

Agatha believes her. It’s written all over her face.

After gifts are exchanged and Billy is loudly thanked for being “the most thoughtful human alive,” Agatha turns to take a seat near the end of the table- only for Billy to slide into it at the last second, completely oblivious. She exhales sharply through her nose, biting back a comment, and resigns herself to the only chair left.

Directly across from Rio.

Of course.

She drops into the seat and immediately grabs the menu, holding it up like a shield, blocking her view. Her shoulders tense as if bracing for impact. If she doesn’t look at her, maybe she won’t think about last week. About the food court. About tears and apologies that hadn’t been enough.

She leans toward Billy, lowering her voice. “God, I’m so fucking hungry.”

Billy nods emphatically. “Same. I heard the food’s really good here.”

Agatha groans softly, eyes scanning the page as her stomach growls as a reminder. “I want the steak frites,” she says, tapping the picture eagerly while turning it towards Billy. “Look at that beaut.”

“Oh, you’re gonna convince me,” Billy replies, already searching for it on his own menu.

“Guys,” Jen whines, drawing their attention. “I can’t decide.” She looks around the table dramatically. “What’s everyone getting?”

Alice and Lilia answer first, confident and decisive. Wanda adds hers to the mix with a smile.

“Rio?” Jen asks, turning toward her. “What about you?”

Agatha stiffens before she can stop herself.

Rio glances down at her menu, fingers tightening slightly around the edge. “Um… I don’t know,” she says carefully. “I might not get anything. I’m not really that hungry.”

Something drops hard in Agatha’s chest.

Before she can overthink it, the words are out. “We could share something.” Her voice is casual- too casual- as she finally looks up, meeting Rio’s eyes for the first time tonight. She adds quickly, shrugging, “I’m not that hungry either.”

Billy turns toward her, confused. “I thought you said-”

Agatha elbows him sharply in the ribs.

“Ow- what the fuck?” he mutters, instantly silenced by her glare.

Rio stares at Agatha, disbelief written all over her face. The look clearly says are you serious right now?

Agatha holds her gaze, raising an eyebrow, daring her to call her out.

There’s a long beat. Then Rio exhales, shoulders slumping just a little, like she’s giving up on a fight she doesn’t have the energy for.

“Okay,” she says quietly. “Fine. Sure.”

Relief floods Agatha so fast it almost makes her dizzy. She lets herself smile- small, gentle- nothing too much. “What do you fancy?”

Rio scans the menu more quickly than she probably needs to, eyes flicking over the page. The rest of the table slips back into overlapping conversations, laughter rising and falling in waves, but Agatha hears none of it. Her attention is locked entirely on Rio’s hands, the way her thumb rubs anxiously at the edge of the menu, the slight furrow between her brows.

“Maybe the grilled chicken,” Rio says finally, her voice tentative, like she’s asking permission more than making a choice.

Agatha nods immediately, a little too fast. “Works for me.”

The waiter appears beside them with a bright smile and practiced efficiency, topping off wine glasses with the bottle Alice ordered. The soft glug of liquid fills the brief lull as people start rattling off their orders.

When it’s Agatha’s turn, she clears her throat. “We’re gonna share the grilled chicken,” she says, casual, easy- like it’s nothing at all. Like her chest isn’t tight watching Rio’s fingers twist together under the table. The waiter nods and scribbles it down before disappearing toward the kitchen.

“Okay,” Wanda says brightly, setting her glass down with a decisive clink, “what are our New Year’s plans, guys?”

Alice perks up immediately. “Jen and I were actually just talking about hosting a party at our new apartment.” She turns toward Jen, smiling. “Obviously you’re all invited.”

“Sounds fun!” Wanda replies instantly. “I’ll be there!”

Agatha presses her lips together, resisting the reflexive eye roll. There’s no chance in hell she’s going if Wanda is. She tunes out as Jen launches into an enthusiastic rundown of paint colors, furniture layouts, and mood lighting for their new place. Alice jokes about maxing out her credit card. Lilia hums along, amused, as Yelena laughs softly, sipping her wine.

Agatha hears almost none of it.

Her gaze keeps drifting back to Rio, who has gone quiet again, shoulders drawn in, posture stiff. There’s something uneasy in her expression, a subtle tension that sets Agatha’s nerves on edge. When the food arrives, plates are set down one by one.

Agatha exhales softly when she sees the chicken has been split neatly onto two plates. Relief loosens something in her chest.

No awkward negotiations. No dividing portions. No chance for Rio to insist she take more.

The table quiets as everyone starts eating, conversation dropping to murmurs drowned out by the clink of silverware.

Rio nudges her food around at first, rearranging it more than actually eating. Agatha notices every tiny movement- the way Rio cuts her chicken into careful, uniform pieces, the pause before each bite, the way her jaw tightens like she’s bracing herself.

She does eat, though. Slowly. A good few bites here and there.

More than Agatha.

Agatha realizes, suddenly, that her own food is cooling almost untouched. She barely tastes the bite she forces herself to take, too busy watching Rio- making sure she’s eating- making sure she seems okay.

She pretends to listen as the table erupts into another round of chatter, but her world has narrowed to the small, fragile space directly across from her.

“And how are you doing, Agatha?” Jen asks suddenly, turning toward her as the conversation lulls and forks pause midair.

Agatha blinks, caught off guard. “With what?”

Jen tilts her head, genuinely confused. “With your divorce?”

The words land harshly.

Agatha’s stomach plummets. Heat rushes to her face as she snaps her gaze to Jen, eyes sharp, warning.

Too late.

She feels it immediately- the shift at the table, the sudden attention, the quiet.

Jen’s smile falls. “Oh- shit. You haven’t told them yet?”

Agatha presses her tongue to the inside of her cheek, jaw tightening as she looks around at the startled faces staring back at her. Wanda’s eyebrows knit together. Billy looks frozen. Alice’s mouth hangs open slightly.

“No,” Agatha says stiffly. She spears a piece of chicken a little too hard, the scrape of her fork against the plate loud in the silence. “I haven’t.”

“Fuck,” Jen mutters, horrified. “Agatha, I’m so sorry. I thought you said you were gonna tell them last week so I-”

“Yeah,” Agatha cuts in quickly, not trusting herself to keep it together if Jen keeps talking. “I was planning on it.” She shrugs, brittle. “Didn’t follow through.”

Jen winces. “I’m such an idiot.”

Agatha exhales slowly. “It’s fine. It’s out now.” She forces herself to look around the table, even though every instinct tells her to disappear. Pity meets her from every direction- soft eyes, awkward sympathy, people clearly unsure what the right response is.

Billy clears his throat. “What… what happened?” he asks carefully.

Agatha reaches for her wine glass, fingers curling tight around the stem. She takes a long swallow, barely tasting it. “Ralph’s been cheating on me,” she says, voice steady through sheer force of will, though her heart tightens from saying it out loud again. “For about a year now.”

Silence crashes down hard.

No one speaks. No forks move. Even the surrounding restaurant noise feels distant, muffled.

Agatha presses her lips together, nodding faintly like she’s confirming it for herself too.

“That’s… that’s awful,” Yelena finally says, her voice quiet but sincere. “I’m really sorry, Agatha.”

“Yeah,” Alice adds, shaking her head. “He’s a fucking dick.”

Agatha manages a weak smile. “Thanks.”

Her stomach twists anyway, nausea swimming slowly. She lifts her eyes- and freezes.

Rio is staring at her. Not with pure pity like everyone else- with anger.

Her jaw is clenched tightly, her grip around her fork making her knuckles white. There’s something fierce and protective burning in her eyes, something that makes Agatha’s chest ache in a way she doesn’t have words for.

The conversation slowly crawls back to life after that, splitting into smaller groups as more wine is poured and voices grow louder. Laughter resurfaces, forced at first, then easier as the alcohol does its job.

Rio smiles when someone speaks to her. She nods. She laughs in the right places.

But Agatha watches her.

The way her hands twist beneath the table. The way her shoulders stay tense. The way she barely touches what’s left of her food now.

Eventually, Rio pushes her chair back. “I’m gonna run to the bathroom,” she murmurs, already standing. Her fingers brush her lips in a nervous habit Agatha knows too well.

She moves quickly- too quickly.

Agatha’s chest tightens.

Before she can talk herself out of it, she’s standing too, chair legs scraping loudly. She mutters something about needing air and slips away from the table, following Rio down the short hallway toward the bathrooms.

The restaurant noise fades with each step. The bathroom door shuts just as Agatha reaches it.

She knocks twice, sharp. “Rio?” Her voice comes out softer than she expects.

Nothing.

She tries the handle. Locked.

“Rio?” She leans closer, heart pounding.

Then she hears it- retching from the other side. The raw, broken sound of Rio’s throat carrying out the contents of her stomach.

Her stomach drops.

“Rio, please,” she says, knocking again. “Let me in.”

“Go away, Agatha,” Rio’s voice cracks through the door.

Agatha shakes her head, tears already burning. “No. Please. Just- open the door.”

Silence stretches.

Then, finally, the lock clicks.

The door cracks open, Rio half-hidden behind it. Agatha steps in. The bathroom is small and dim, lit by a single fluorescent bulb that hums faintly overhead. It smells like citrus soap and something metallic underneath, sharp enough to make Agatha’s stomach turn. The door clicks shut behind her, the noise sounding too loud in the sudden quiet.

Rio stands near the sink, shoulders hunched like she’s bracing for impact. Her hands grip the edge of the counter so tightly her knuckles have gone pale, fingers trembling despite her obvious effort to stay still. Her face is blotchy, eyes red-rimmed and glossy, lashes clumped together from tears she’s clearly wiped away more than once.

Agatha doesn’t give herself time to think.

She crosses the space between them in two steps and wraps her arms around Rio, pulling her close with a firmness that surprises even herself. It feels instinctive- necessary. Rio stiffens for half a heartbeat, muscles locking under Agatha’s touch, and Agatha braces herself for rejection.

But then Rio exhales.

It comes out shaky and broken, and her body falls forward like she’s been holding herself upright for too long. Her arms lift slowly, hesitantly, circling Agatha’s waist with a looseness that hurts more than if she hadn’t returned the hug at all. She smells faintly like wine and something unmistakably Rio, and the familiarity nearly knocks Agatha off balance.

They pull apart after a moment, just enough to look at each other.

“You don’t have to keep worrying about me,” Rio says quietly- voice hoarse, throat dry. She avoids Agatha’s eyes, focusing instead on some invisible spot on the tiled floor. “I’m fine.”

Agatha lets out a soft, humorless breath. She tilts her head, studying Rio’s face- the tightness around her mouth, the way her shoulders remain raised. “You might be able to convince yourself,” she says gently, “but you’re not going to convince me.”

Rio’s jaw tightens. She folds her arms over her chest, retreating inward, making herself smaller.

“I’ve got it under control,” Rio insists, though the words sound rehearsed, like something she’s said so many times it’s lost all meaning. “I don’t need help.”

Agatha swallows. The urge to argue, to fix it, burns in her chest. Instead, she stays still. She doesn’t know what to say.

“And how are you doing?” Rio asks finally, evidently trying to shift the conversation. The question is quiet, stripped of its edge. Genuine.

Agatha scoffs before she can stop herself, turning away toward the sink. She grips the porcelain, grounding herself. “I’m great,” she says, too quickly.

Rio frowns. “Agatha-”

“Don’t,” Agatha snaps, the word harsher than she intends. She closes her eyes, forces herself to breathe, then lowers her voice. “Don’t do that. That’s not fair.”

Rio’s brows knit together. “What isn’t?”

“You can’t shut me out when I try to help,” Agatha says, turning back to face her, voice trembling now, “and then expect me to just- spill everything when you ask. That’s not how this works. This isn’t a one-way street.”

Rio’s mouth opens, then closes. She looks wounded, caught off guard. They stare at each other without a word.

Agatha sighs. “Our lives are a fucking mess right now, aren’t they?” She laughs humorlessly.

“Yeah.” Rio responds with a sad smile, voice barely audible.

Silence stretches between them, thick and heavy.

Then Rio speaks, so softly Agatha almost misses it.

“This isn’t the life I imagined for us.”

Agatha’s breath catches. Her heart races. Slowly, she lifts her gaze.

“What did you imagine?” She asks, fearing the answer awaiting her.

Rio looks past Agatha, eyes unfocused, like she’s watching a memory play out in her mind.

“I imagined us buying a quaint brownstone on the edge of town,” she starts, voice trembling but steady. “Close enough to the city that the drive wouldn’t be awful, but far enough that it was quiet at night.” She swallows. “I imagined you as this big hotshot lawyer, coming home exhausted and scattered but still somehow making time for us.”

Agatha’s throat tightens painfully.

“I imagined cooking dinner for you,” Rio continues. “Taking way too long, burning half of it, pretending it was intentional. I imagined Nicky running around in little Paw Patrol pajamas, refusing to brush his teeth, and us trying- badly- to convince him that bedtime was non-negotiable.”

Rio’s eyes shine now, tears spilling freely to match Agatha’s.

“I imagined scrapbooks,” she whispers. “So many of them. Pictures everywhere. Birthdays, vacations, stupid blurry photos that only mattered to us.” She laughs weakly. “I imagined dropping Nicky off at college, coming home to a house that was way too empty. Too quiet.”

Agatha presses her lips together, her vision blurring completely.

“I imagined us growing old together,” Rio says, voice breaking. “Retiring somewhere in the French countryside. Complaining about your ridiculous, loud breathing even though I secretly love it. I imagined us being happy- really happy.” She finally looks back at Agatha. “Because we’d have each other. And that’s all we’d ever need.” She sighs, taking a second to steady herself. “That’s what I imagined.”

The silence afterward is fucking devastating.

Agatha blinks rapidly, fighting the tears that continue to spill over. Her chest aches like it’s caving in on itself.

“Wishful thinking, I guess.” She murmurs, the words barely audible. It’s the only thing she can manage to get out.

Rio nods, her mouth twisting sadly. “Yeah,” she whispers. “I guess so.”

And for a moment, they just stand there- two people grieving the same future, alone together in a restaurant bathroom that’s suddenly far too small.

“We should probably go back.” Agatha mutters, not daring to stay another second in here in case she breaks apart completely.

Rio just nods, staring at her with tears in her eyes, before she turns and walks out the door.

Jen covers the dinner bill, waving off everyone’s half-hearted protests with an easy smile, insisting- again- that it’s fine, that they can afford it, that this is their celebration and she wants to enjoy it. Agatha murmurs a quiet thank you, her lips curving into something that resembles a smile, though it never quite reaches her eyes.

Chairs scrape against the floor as the group stands, bodies swaying slightly- everyone except Billy and Lilia carrying the loose, warm haze of too much wine. Goodbyes blur together in hugs and laughter and overlapping congratulations. Jen’s voice rings bright and happy, Alice’s arm draped around her shoulders, both of them glowing in a way that makes Agatha’s chest ache in a way she doesn’t want to examine too closely.

Outside, the night air is cool and sharp- sobering. Agatha walks beside Rio in silence, the space between them charged and fragile, like one wrong word could shatter it. When they reach Billy’s car, Agatha moves without thinking, stepping forward to pull open the door for Rio. The action is automatic, muscle memory from a life they don’t have anymore.

Rio hesitates for half a second before sliding in. Agatha follows, shutting the door behind her.

The car fills with the low hum of the engine and the faint, distant sound of Taylor Swift playing at a barely-there volume. Streetlights streak past the windows as lit up buildings blur across their vision. No one speaks for a while. It’s the kind of silence that presses down on Agatha’s chest, heavy and suffocating.

“That was delicious,” Wanda finally says from the front, stretching slightly in her seat. “But I, for one, am absolutely stuffed.”

Billy nods, eyes still on the road. “Same. I’m literally gonna give them five stars though.”

Agatha exhales through her nose, staring straight ahead.

Wanda isn’t done. She twists in her seat just enough to glance toward the back, her smile sharp and expectant. “Yeah, even Rio ate a bunch, so it must’ve been good, right?”

The words land like a slap.

Agatha feels Rio stiffen beside her- can feel it, even without looking. Her leg starts bouncing again, fast and frantic, like it’s a distraction from the moment. Heat floods Agatha’s chest, sharp and immediate, her jaw tightening as something ugly and protective claws its way up her throat.

“Wanda?” Agatha says lightly- too lightly.

Wanda beams. “Yeah?”

Agatha smiles- wide and absolutely vicious. “Do you ever shut the fuck up?”

The car goes dead silent.

Wanda’s mouth falls open, shock written all over her face. For a second, Agatha almost expects her to argue back, to fire something equally cruel in return. Instead, Wanda snaps her mouth shut and turns forward without another word, shoulders stiff.

Agatha leans back in her seat, a small, satisfied smirk tugging at her lips. A tiny victory- but she’ll take it.

She finally looks over at Rio.

Rio is staring out the window, jaw clenched, eyes unfocused, the city lights reflecting faintly off the glass. Her leg is still bouncing. She looks like she’s barely holding herself together.

Agatha doesn’t think. She just moves.

Her hand slides over, tentative at first, then settles around Rio’s, warm and steady. She gives a gentle squeeze, grounding, reassuring- I’m here. The contact sends a jolt through her, sharp and electric, like touching something she’s been missing for far too long.

For a terrifying second, Agatha braces for Rio to pull away.

She doesn’t.

Rio’s fingers curl slightly in response, just enough to acknowledge it. Agatha’s heart stutters violently in her chest. Twenty years, and it still feels like this- comforting and terrifying all at once. Like home.

Too soon, Billy slows the car and pulls up in front of Rio’s house.

Rio withdraws her hand, the absence immediate and cold. She murmurs a quiet thank you to Billy, voice small, controlled. He wishes her a good night. Wanda says nothing.

Rio steps out of the car without looking back, closing the door softly behind her.

Agatha watches as she walks up the path, shoulders slightly hunched, keys clutched too tightly in her hand. The porch light flicks on. The door opens. Then she’s gone.

Billy pulls away from the curb.

Agatha stares down at her lap, at her empty hand.

It’s cold now.
And it feels unbearably hollow.

Chapter 12: Chapter Twelve
