2005

Rio clutches Agatha tighter around the shoulder, steadying her fragile frame as they shuffle down the hallway. Agatha’s body trembles with each step, still weak from the surgery, and Rio can feel the slight weight of her exhaustion pressing into her. The fluorescent lights of the dorm hallway flicker faintly, casting long, eerie shadows across the walls, but Rio doesn’t notice- her focus is entirely on Agatha, on keeping her upright, on making sure she doesn’t fall.

When they reach the door, Rio fumbles with the key, her hand heavy from carrying Agatha’s bag and supporting her weight. The lock clicks open, and she pushes the door with a quiet sigh, stepping inside with a sense of relief. She guides Agatha carefully toward the bed, her hands gentle, supporting her as she lowers her down slow and steady, as if afraid the slightest jolt might undo her.

“How are you feeling?” Rio asks softly, her voice barely above a whisper, like she’s afraid to shatter the silence. She tucks the blanket snugly around Agatha, then slides the back of her hand across Agatha’s forehead, feeling the warm heat from her fever.

“I’m okay,” Agatha murmurs, forcing a smile that doesn’t reach her eyes. Rio sees through it immediately- the tension in her jaw, the tight line of her lips, the way her shoulders slump just slightly. She can feel the strain, the exhaustion, and it twists something in her chest.

Rio purses her lips and tilts her head, studying her. She wants to say something- anything- to take away the weight she can see pressing down on Agatha. But all she can do is watch, helpless.

“I think I’m just gonna get some sleep,” Agatha says finally, turning onto her side and pulling the blankets around her, covering half her face as if the world is too much for her to face fully.

“Okay,” Rio replies, her voice soft, cautious. “Do you want me to stay with you?”

She moves forward instinctively, already expecting the familiar, unspoken “yes.”

“No,” Agatha whispers, barely audible, and it stops Rio in her tracks. Her heart lurches. “It’s okay. You can go.”

Rio freezes, her chest tightening as the words sink in. Agatha has never rejected her comfort before- not like this. The thought that she might be shutting her out hits Rio like a punch. She swallows hard, her throat suddenly dry, her face etched with nothing but worry.

She nods slowly, even though Agatha can’t see it, her fingers brushing against the blanket as if to reassure her. “I’ll be back to check on you later,” she says finally, her voice steady but softer, sadder than usual. She runs her fingers through Agatha’s hair for a brief moment, lingering longer than necessary, memorizing the softness, the warmth, before letting go. “Get some rest, my love.”

Rio walks to the door, flicking off the light switch. Darkness swallows the room, leaving only the faint hum of the dorm vents and the shallow rhythm of Agatha’s breathing. An uneasy feeling settles in her stomach, low and persistent, a sense she can’t shake. She tells herself it’s nothing- just normal worry- that they just went thought a lot, but every instinct in her chest screams otherwise.

Before leaving, she steals one last glance at Agatha- curled in on herself, small and vulnerable, the blanket pulled tight- and the sight grips her heart. She can feel the fragility of this moment, the way the air itself seems heavy with unspoken words and fears- ones they don’t want to admit out loud.

But she knows- truly knows- that there’s a chance they might not get better. That the healing she wants to bring, the comfort she wants to give… may not be enough this time.

And there’s nothing she can do about it.

2025

Rio cruises down the street with the music turned low, bass barely humming through the speakers. Normally it would help steady her, give her something to anchor to, but tonight it barely registers. Her stomach aches in a deep, hollow way- sharp one moment, dull and twisting the next- like it’s folding in on itself. Hunger has settled there heavily, gnawing and insistent, radiating outward until it feels like it’s pressing up into her ribs.

Her fingers tap restlessly against the steering wheel, a nervous tick she doesn’t fully notice. It takes an embarrassing amount of effort just to keep her eyes forward, to stay locked on the lines of the road. Everything feels fast and impossibly slow at the same time, like her body is lagging a half-second behind her thoughts.

Her heart thuds hard against her chest- not panicked, just loud- each beat heavy enough that she feels it in her throat. Breathing feels strangely shallow, like she can’t quite pull in a full breath no matter how much she tries. She eases her foot off the accelerator without fully realizing it, the car slowing as a wave of lightheadedness rolls through her.

Her stomach clenches painfully, a sharp reminder of how long it’s been.

When was the last time she ate?
Two days ago?
Three?

The thought slips away before she can pin it down. Her vision blurs at the edges, the streetlights smearing into soft streaks of light. She blinks hard, squints, tries to force the world back into focus. Her head feels floaty, disconnected, like it’s not fully attached to the rest of her body anymore.

Shit. Not now.

She swallows and drags in a deeper breath, gripping the wheel tighter as she lifts her gaze back to the road. The motion sends another dizzy surge through her, dark spots fading in at the corners of her vision.

Instinct takes over when her eyes land on a figure in front of her.

Her foot slams down on the brake.

The car jerks violently to a stop, the seatbelt snapping tight across her chest as she’s thrown forward and then back into the seat. Her heart lurches painfully, hammering hard enough to make her feel nauseous. For a split second, everything goes black.

When her vision clears, there’s a man standing in the crosswalk directly in front of her hood- an elderly man, frozen mid-step, eyes wide with shock. He stares at her like she’s lost her mind. Like she almost ended his life.

Because she did.

He glares and flips her off with a sharp, furious motion as he finishes crossing. Rio mouths a frantic “sorry,” her lips trembling, hands shaking so badly she has to lock her elbows to keep them steady on the wheel.

Fuck.

Her chest tightens- with delayed panic- with the weight of what just happened. Tears spill into her eyes before she can stop them, blurring the man into a vague shape as he reaches the sidewalk on the other side. Her stomach twists again, harder this time, cramping so sharply she hisses through her teeth.

She could’ve killed him.
She almost fucking killed someone.

Her vision wavers as she blinks rapidly, tears streaking down her cheeks. She drags the heel of her hand across her face, angry at herself, exhausted. When she presses the accelerator again, it’s slow and careful, like she doesn’t trust her own body to cooperate.

She needs to eat.

The realization lands with clarity, cutting through everything else.

Not tomorrow. Not later tonight.

As soon as possible.

Her stomach aches in agreement, a deep, hollow pain that makes her feel weak and unsteady. The idea of food makes her stomach churn, but she knows better than to listen to that reflex.

She can’t let it get this bad.
She can’t let herself be a danger to others.

That’s where she draws a line.

Her hands tremble as she drives the car toward Alice and Jen’s apartment, taking deliberate, steady breaths. Each one feels like work. By the time she parks, she’s drained, limbs heavy and slightly numb, like she’s been running for miles instead of merely sitting behind the wheel.

She takes a moment before getting out- just long enough to breathe, to swallow past the tightness in her throat. Then she climbs out of the car, legs feeling a little too shaky beneath her, and walks toward the building.

The buzzer sounds loud in the quiet. The door clicks open.

Inside the elevator, she leans softly against the wall as it ascends, the numbers ticking upward painfully slowly. Fifth floor. She stares at them, forcing her mind to clear, smoothing her expression into something neutral.

Act normal.
You’re fine.

By the time she reaches the apartment door, she’s adjusted her jeweled halter top and pasted on a smile just as it swings open.

Alice stands there, beaming, a gold, glittery 2026 headband sitting crookedly on her head.

“Hey!” Rio says brightly, leaning in to hug her, grateful for the brief, grounding pressure of it.

“Come in, come in!” Alice laughs, pulling her inside.

Rio slips off her boots and looks around, taking in the space- the warm lighting, the cozy decor, the way it already feels lived-in.

“Your place is so nice,” Rio says honestly.

“Thanks! Jen did all the decorating.”

Rio smirks. “Obviously.”

They move toward the living room, where music thumps loudly and people shout over it, laughter spilling everywhere. Rio scans the room, trying to orient herself, trying to stay upright. She pushes down the realization that she’s looking for Agatha’s brown hair in the crowd.

“Is there anyone I know here yet?” she yells over the music.

Alice points toward the corner. “Only Wanda so far.”

Rio’s mouth tightens. “Okay.” She exhales. “I need a drink.”

Alice laughs. “Now that I can help you with.” She drags her to the kitchen and slips behind the counter like a bartender. “What’ll it be?”

“Gin and tonic,” Rio says, then adds quickly, “Diet.”

Alice nods, reaching for the bottles.

Rio leans back against the counter, taking another careful breath, hoping the room will stop tilting long enough for her to get something- anything- in her stomach.

She tells herself she’ll wait. Just a little while longer. That she can’t walk in and immediately head straight for the food like it’s the only thing she came for. Her eyes betray her anyway, drifting to the kitchen counter the moment she steps closer.

Pizza boxes line the island, lids cracked open just enough to show greasy, overloaded slices- pepperoni curling at the edges, cheese stretching into glossy strands. There’s charcuterie laid out neatly, crackers stacked in precise rows, little bowls of olives and nuts. Chips spill from torn bags, surrounded by dips already marked with drag lines from over-eager scoops. A veggie tray sits untouched. And desserts- too many of them- brownies, cupcakes, and cookies sit there like a taunt.

Her stomach tightens immediately, a sharp, hollow pull that feels more like pain than hunger now. It twists low and deep, the kind that makes her mouth water and her throat close at the same time. She swallows hard and forces her gaze away, heat creeping up the back of her neck.

Alice presses a drink into her hand, smiling like everything in the world is easy. Rio accepts it, fingers curling around the cold glass, mumbling a thank you she barely hears herself say. The smell of it makes her stomach clench again.

She moves into the living room with the others, positioning herself carefully- far enough from the kitchen that she won’t be tempted, close enough to other people that she looks normal. A massive Happy New Year banner hangs crookedly on the wall, one corner threatening to peel off. Music thumps through the speakers, bass vibrating faintly through the floor.

She spots Wanda’s red hair almost immediately and heads that way by default. It’s easier than standing alone.

Wanda lights up when she sees her, champagne sloshing dangerously close to the rim of her glass.

“Rio!” she yells, already pulling her into a hug. “So good to see you!”

Rio smiles, returns it lightly. “Hey, Wanda. You too.” She pulls back and asks, “Do you know when the others are getting here?”

Wanda shrugs, taking a sip. “I think Yelena’s on her way. That’s all I’ve heard.” She grins. “Why? You don’t like my company?”

“No- no, of course I do,” Rio says quickly. Too quickly. She feels it even as the words leave her mouth.

Wanda smirks obliviously. “Good. ’Cause I like yours too.” She reaches out, resting her hand on Rio’s arm.

Rio’s muscles tense instantly. She brings her drink up, pretending to take a sip just to create an excuse to shift away. Wanda’s hand drops. Relief loosens something in her chest, but her stomach gives another sharp twist in protest.

Then she hears the door open.

Jen’s excited shriek cuts through the music, unmistakable. Rio’s heart jumps painfully as she turns and sees Agatha step inside with Billy, framed by the doorway light. For a second, everything else fades. She smiles without meaning to.

She turns back to Wanda. “Excuse me a minute.”

She doesn’t wait for a response, already moving through the crowd, threading between bodies, having prepared nothing to say- just wanting to be close. She’s almost there when Jen grabs Agatha’s wrist and hauls her toward the kitchen before Agatha even has the chance to look up.

Rio exhales, the smile slipping. She pivots instead toward Billy, who’s still standing by the door, like that was the plan all along.

“Hey,” she says softly.

Billy looks up, pushing a pair of glittery 2026 glasses onto his face. “Hi, Rio! Oh my god, I love your shirt.”

She smiles, real this time. “Thanks, Billy.” Her voice comes out softer than she means to, sadness slipping in subconsciously.

“You should let me borrow it sometime.”

She raises an eyebrow, laughing under her breath. “Yeah. Sure. It’s yours.”

He literally jumps for joy before walking past her, disappearing into the crowd of people.

Alice calls her over suddenly, waving frantically at the speaker setup. “Rio- help, it just stopped!”

She heads over, crouching down by the speaker and fiddling with the Bluetooth, phone screen blurring for a second as a wave of dizziness rolls through her. Her stomach cramps again- deeper now, more insistent. She presses her tongue to the roof of her mouth, breathing through it.

Food, she reminds herself.
Soon.
She promised herself soon.

Out of the corner of her eye, Rio sees Agatha reappear in the doorway, immediately clocking the poker game set up around the living room table.

“Fuck, yes!” Agatha yells, already moving, grin wide and sharp. “Deal me in.”

Rio can’t help the soft smile that tugs at her mouth. Poker does something feral to Agatha- brings out this reckless, glittering confidence that’s impossible not to watch.

“I’m gonna crush all of you,” Agatha mutters as she drops to the floor, cross-legged, cards already being shoved into her hands.

Rio lets out a quiet laugh she doesn’t realize she’s holding.

She finally gets the speaker working again, the music snapping back to life- loud and pulsing- and the sudden vibration seems to echo straight through her body. Almost immediately, her stomach twists sharply, like it’s had enough of being ignored. Hunger. Deep, gnawing, hollow hunger that makes her feel light and fragile, like she’s made of glass and could shatter at any point.

She exhales slowly. She can’t put it off anymore.

Standing too fast makes the room tilt, the edges of her vision dimming for half a second. She steadies herself on the table, waits for the floor to settle back into place, then forces her feet toward the kitchen.

The spread looks obscene up close, the food staring back at her, taunting.

Her stomach clenches painfully, a deep ache developing under her ribs.

She grabs a plate with a hand that won’t quite stop shaking and immediately looks over her shoulder. No one’s there. No one’s watching.

Which should make this easier.

It doesn’t.

Her eyes flick over the food anyway, her brain firing numbers she didn’t ask for.

Pizza. 285.
Cheese and crackers. 200.
Cupcake. 250.

Her heart starts to race, each beat loud in her ears, as if she’s already done something wrong just by standing here.

No one else is eating.

That’s the problem.

She can’t be the first. Can’t eat alone. Can’t eat more than everyone else. The rules stack up quickly, mercilessly, even as her stomach stabs her in protest.

Her mind flashes- unwanted- to the crosswalk. The man’s shocked face. How close the bumper had come.

Her fault.

She swallows hard, eyes burning, and sets the plate back down like it’s dangerous.

Laughter drifts in from the living room, bright and careless, and something in her chest caves in.

She turns and leaves the kitchen abruptly, almost colliding with the doorway. Her gaze lands immediately on Agatha, hunched over the cards, focused and intense, hair falling into her face as she argues with another player.

Rio stops short.

Don’t.
Don’t interrupt.
That’s annoying. That’s needy. That’s-

The room tilts again, a slow dizzy roll that makes her vision blur at the edges. She sucks in a breath, grounding herself, then moves before she can talk herself out of it.

She kneels beside Agatha, leaning in close so only she can hear, Agatha’s eyes still glued to the game.

“Will you come get food with me?”

The words come out thin, barely there, and the second they do, embarrassment floods her. God. She’s not a child. She shouldn’t need this.

Agatha freezes.

Then she looks up.

Their eyes meet, and whatever Agatha sees on Rio’s face makes her expression soften immediately- surprise melting into something gentle, steady. She nods once, no hesitation, sets her cards down.

“You guys finish without me,” she says easily, already standing. “I’ll play again later.”

She flicks her head toward the kitchen and starts walking like this is the most normal thing in the world.

Rio follows, heart pounding harder now for a different reason.

Agatha’s probably annoyed.
She shouldn’t have asked.

Rio constantly pushes her away and now suddenly she wants her help?
When it’s convenient for her?

Agatha doesn’t slow. She reaches the counter, grabs a plate, then another, and hands one to Rio without a word. No commentary. No pressure. Just solid, quiet presence.

Rio’s throat tightens.

Her stomach still hurts. She’s still terrified.

But she’s not alone.

“What are we having?” Agatha asks, casual enough that it almost feels normal.

Rio inhales slowly, her gaze drifting back over the counter. The food still feels too dense, too much. Her hand hovers for a second before she forces it forward, fingers trembling as she grabs a slice of pizza. The grease seeps through the napkin immediately, warmth pressing into her palm. Her stomach aches sharply at the smell- hunger, raw and painful- making her swallow hard. She moves quickly after that, like if she stops she won’t start again, adding a small handful of celery sticks, carrots, a piece of broccoli.

Safe. Controlled.

Agatha mirrors her without comment, taking a slice, some vegetables, nothing more, nothing less. The quiet solidarity hits Rio harder than any reassurance could. She gives Agatha a brief, grateful smile, barely there, then turns and walks back into the living room before her resolve can crumble.

The music is loud enough to rattle the walls, bass thumping through the floor and into her chest. She sinks onto the end of the couch, plate balanced carefully in her lap, shoulders tense. Her hands feel clumsy as she picks up the pizza, fingers slick with grease, her pulse loud in her ears.

Agatha sits beside her, closer than necessary. Their knees press together, solid and warm, and every time Agatha moves her arm brushes Rio’s. The contact is electric- too much and too grounding all at once- sending a shiver up Rio’s spine, raising goosebumps along her arms. Her heart starts racing, breath catching, and for once the protests in her head go quiet.

She focuses on that instead. On the warmth of Agatha’s leg against hers. On the way her chest feels too tight, too fast. On anything other than counting the calories in each bite.

Every now and then, Rio lets her eyes drift sideways- to Agatha’s hands, to the plate balanced on her knees. She tracks each bite with quiet precision, counting the seconds between chews, making sure Agatha is still eating, that she hasn’t slowed or stopped. She matches her without thinking, lifting her own fork only when Agatha does, swallowing when Agatha swallows.

It’s grounding in a strange way.

When their plates are finally empty, Rio’s stomach lurches- not from hunger this time, but from the sudden awareness of fullness. The sensation is foreign, heavy, sitting low and hot beneath her ribs. Her brain snaps back online immediately, sharp and cruel, already questioning her choices, already replaying every bite.

Too much.
Too fast.
You didn’t need all of that.

But before the spiral can fully take hold, Jen’s voice cuts through the room, loud and ecstatic, Alice right beside her.

“Okay! Charades time! Everyone grab a partner!”

The distraction hits like relief. Rio sets her plate aside, leaving it half-hidden on the coffee table, as if putting physical distance between herself and the evidence might quiet her thoughts.

She barely has time to stand before Wanda appears in front of her, all flushed cheeks and bright eyes, the faint scent of alcohol clinging to her breath. Wanda grabs Rio’s wrists and tugs her upright.

“Rio!” she shrieks, bouncing on her heels. “We should be partners!”

“Oh- I, um- ” Rio starts, her words tangling as Wanda’s grip tightens.

“Sorry.” Agatha’s voice cuts in smoothly, sharp enough to end the debate immediately. She smiles up at Wanda from the couch, sweet and condescending all at once. “Rio and I are already partnered up.”

Wanda’s smile falters. She looks to Rio, brows pinched, waiting for confirmation.

Rio hesitates for half a second- then nods, forcing an apologetic frown into place, though her mouth itches to break into a smile. “Sorry, Wanda.”

Wanda releases her slowly, disappointment written all over her face, before drifting off toward someone else.

Rio exhales and turns back toward the couch, sitting down again without thinking. This time, she’s closer. Close enough that her leg presses firmly against Agatha’s, heat seeping through fabric.

Agatha doesn’t move away.

After everyone is paired up and settled in, Jen and Alice set the box of cards down on the coffee table, pushing aside empty glasses and snack plates to make room.

“Okay, Agatha and Rio, you’re up first,” Jen says, lifting her champagne glass in their direction before taking a sip.

Agatha taps Rio’s knee gently, a quick grounding touch. “I’ll go first. You guess.” She stands and moves to the front of the living room, plucking a card from the box as she passes. She scans it quickly, lips pressing together in concentration, just as Jen flips the sand timer and the grains begin to spill downward.

Rio watches her closely- how Agatha straightens her shoulders, how she takes a steady breath before starting. Agatha sets the card facedown, brings her hands together, then opens them outward.

“Book,” Rio says immediately. Agatha nods.

She holds up four fingers.

“Four words,” Rio adds, taking a small sip of her drink, eyes never leaving Agatha.

Agatha switches to two fingers.

“Second word.”

She then drags her thumb across her throat in an exaggerated motion.

“Die. Death. Kill,” Rio fires off, rapid.

Agatha nods sharply at kill, barely able to stop herself from smiling, and starts to lift her hands again- but Rio blurts, “To Kill a Mockingbird!”

“Yes!” Agatha shouts, the word bursting out of her as she jumps in place with a wide, excited grin. Rio smiles, heat blooming in her chest, as Agatha snatches the next card, the sand still slipping slowly through the glass.

They barely slow down. Agatha’s movements are quick and confident now, acting on instinct, and Rio keeps up effortlessly, guessing almost as fast as Agatha can gesture. Around them, conversations die off. People lean forward, eyes wide, watching the two of them tear through the deck.

“Harry Potter.”
“Star Wars.”
“Little Women.”
“Les Mis.”
“The Great Gatsby.”

Each correct answer lands instantly, like they’re speaking a private language. The other guests stare, mouths slightly open, half-amused and half-baffled, as if they’re witnessing something unfairly synchronized. Rio barely notices them- her focus locked on Agatha, on the way her face lights up with every win, on how easy this feels, how natural.

Finally, the sand timer runs out, the last grains slipping through the glass.

“Time!” Jen calls out immediately.

Agatha lets out a breathless laugh and rushes back over, holding her hands up as Rio high-fives her. The smack echoes louder than it should over the music.

“We fucking crushed that,” Agatha says, grin splitting her face as she drops back onto the couch beside Rio, knees knocking together.

Rio laughs, breath still a little quick. “We really did.”

“Okay,” Jen says, shaking her head in disbelief as she lifts her champagne glass, “I genuinely don’t know how anyone’s beating that… but Billy and Wanda, you’re up.”

Agatha leans in before Billy even reaches the front of the room. Close. Too close. Her mouth hovers near Rio’s ear, her voice dropping into something private, conspiratorial.

“Do you wanna sneak away for a sec?”

Her breath grazes Rio’s skin, warm, and Rio’s stomach flips.

“To do what?” Rio murmurs back, mirroring the closeness without even thinking about it.

Agatha answers by slipping a hand into her pocket and pulling out a pack of cigarettes just enough for Rio to see, flashing her a quick, crooked grin.

Rio nods immediately.

As the room’s attention shifts fully to Billy- already flailing dramatically through his first card- Agatha reaches for Rio’s hand and tugs her up. The contact is instant, grounding and electric all at once. Agatha doesn’t let go as she pulls her through the edge of the living room and toward the kitchen.

Rio’s heart starts racing, her pulse loud in her ears. Agatha’s palm is warm, familiar in a way that makes something twist low in her chest. She thinks- briefly- about the car ride home from the restaurant, about how she told herself that moment hadn’t meant anything- despite the fact that her heart was racing a million miles an hour and she couldn’t shake the feeling for days. She forces the thought away, breathing sharp through her nose as they move.

Agatha slides open the balcony door with practiced ease, the cold air rushing in immediately. They slip outside and shut it quietly behind them, the muffled sound of laughter and music sealing them off from the rest of the party.

The cold hits Rio all at once, biting at her exposed skin. She shivers, rubbing her hands along her arms.

Agatha pulls the cigarette pack out again and offers it to her. “Here.”

“Thanks,” Rio whispers, taking one. Their fingers brush, brief but undeniable.

Agatha flicks the lighter, shielding the flame from the wind as she leans in to light Rio’s cigarette first. The tip flares orange. Rio inhales, the smoke burning her throat before settling into something familiar, steadying. Agatha lights her own and exhales slowly, smoke curling into the night air.

They stand side by side, shoulders almost touching, breathing in sync without realizing it.

Rio watches her. The way the moonlight catches the sharp line of her cheekbones, the way her eyes glisten. Then she notices the tension in her jaw. The stiff, controlled way she inhales, like she’s holding herself together by force alone.

“So…” Rio starts before she can think too hard, her voice careful. “How are you doing?”

Agatha doesn’t look at her, but Rio sees her tense up anyway. “With what?”

“With Ralph and… everything,” Rio says quietly, her heart racing.

“I’m fine.”

The word is flat. Practiced.

“Are you sure?”

Agatha hums in response, a vague, dismissive sound, eyes fixed on the street below. “Mhm.”

Rio nods, even though it doesn’t feel like enough. Her head feels light- maybe from the drinks, maybe the nicotine, maybe the way being this close to Agatha still does something to her she can’t control.

“Okay,” Rio says, exhaling smoke. The breath leaves her thinner than she means it to. Her hand trembles slightly as she lowers the cigarette, the embers falling to the ground as she taps. She swallows. “I know you hate me and everything, Agatha, but… just know I’m here. If you need anything.”

The words hang between them, fragile in a way that makes Rio immediately regret saying them. Her stomach twists. She keeps her eyes forward, jaw tight, already preparing herself to take the hit of rejection she’s come to expect.

Agatha goes still.

The silence is worse than any sharp reply. Rio’s pulse spikes, loud in her ears. She becomes suddenly aware of her own breathing- too fast, too obvious- and forces it slower. Her shoulders tense, instinctively curling inward, like she can make herself smaller before Agatha pushes her away.

Agatha takes another drag, then lets the smoke out slowly before breathing in cold, clean air.

“I don’t hate you.” Her voice is soft, sad.

The sentence lands and Rio’s chest tightens in surprise. For a split second, she’s sure she misheard. Her breath stutters, caught halfway in, and she has to consciously pull air into her lungs.

“You don’t?” she asks, barely audible, the question slipping out before she can stop it. It’s fragile and uncertain, like she’s afraid Agatha might take back the words if she presses too hard.

Agatha shakes her head, slow and deliberate. “I don’t.”

Relief hits Rio all at once, sudden and overwhelming. It spreads through her chest like warmth, sharp enough to make her dizzy. Her heart stutters. She hadn’t realized how tightly she’d been holding herself together until this moment.

A quiet, involuntary smile tugs at her mouth before she can stop it. She quickly reins it in, instinctively cautious.

Agatha continues, her voice low and raw. “I’ve tried, Rio. God, I’ve tried.”

Guilt flickers, but it’s gentler now, threaded with something warmer. Rio nods faintly, her throat tight, emotion pressing close to the surface. Agatha tilts her head, her gaze settling fully on Rio. The moonlight sharpens her features, all familiar edges and tension.

“But you know what I’ve learned?”

Rio’s breath catches again, her fingers tightening unconsciously around the cigarette. She exhales slowly, grounding herself before answering. “What?” Her voice is almost a whisper, trembling just enough to highlight the pounding of her heart.

Agatha draws in a long drag, the cigarette tip glowing orange in the dark. She exhales slowly, smoke unraveling between them. Her eyes never leave Rio’s.

“That it’s so much harder to hate you than it is to accept how much I still care about you.” She finally admits, her voice trembling with a mixture of fear, relief, and something else Rio can’t quite name.

The words land all at once. Rio’s knees feel weak, her chest full, breath coming easier than it has in years. She resists the urge to rush forward- to reach out. She’s learned better than that.

“Agatha…” she breathes, her voice warm and unsteady, a sense of relief flooding through every syllable.

Suddenly, the door swings open, and Lilia’s head peeks out, bright and energetic. “What are you guys doing out here?” she asks, her tone playful, teasing. “Come on, it’s almost midnight!”

They hurry inside without a word, following closely behind Lilia. The living room is alive with anticipation, crowded with friends, clinking champagne glasses, and the TV counting down.

“10… 9… 8…” The room chants along with the timer, voices overlapping, laughter and excitement bubbling. Rio glances at Agatha, who keeps her gaze on the screen, lips moving silently along with the numbers, eyes reflecting the flickering lights.

A thrill pulses through Rio, and she can’t help the grin that spreads across her face, wide and uncontainable. “3… 2… 1… Happy New Year!” The room erupts, cheers and shouting drowning out everything else, and Rio doesn’t hesitate. She moves instinctively, pulling Agatha into a tight hug, arms wrapped around her as if to make up for all the years of distance and doubt.

Agatha stiffens for a heartbeat, then melts into the hug, resting her head against Rio’s shoulder. For the first time in what feels like forever, there’s no fear, no danger in the closeness. There are no undertones of sadness or pity in the embrace.

It’s real. Raw.

The chatter of the crowd fades behind them, leaving only the steady warmth of each other.

Chapter End Notes

everyone say 'we love you yaz, you're so kind to us!'

Chapter 13: Chapter Thirteen

Chapter Notes

this is me realizing im only good at writing angst...

TW: brief discussions of child loss, rape, and disordered eating, brief depictions of violence (not too detailed)

See the end of the chapter for more notes

2006

Rio knocks softly on the door. Waits a beat.

Nothing.

She knocks again, a little louder this time, heart tightening in her chest as the seconds stretch. The door finally opens.

Agatha stands there, looking small and fragile. Her hair is unbrushed, falling limp around her face. Dark circles pool beneath her eyes, skin dull and drawn. She’s wearing the same clothes as yesterday- wrinkled and slept in.

Rio forces her mouth into a smile that doesn’t quite stick and lifts the container in her hands. “I brought you soup,” she says softly, like raising her voice might shatter something between them.

Agatha blinks at it, then at Rio. “Thanks,” she murmurs, voice rough and croaky, eyes glassy like she’s been crying for hours.

Rio steps inside, easing the door shut behind her. The dorm room feels heavy, air stale and unmoving.

Too quiet.

They move together toward the bed without saying a word. Agatha sits, then sinks back under the covers, clutching the soup tightly to her chest.

Rio takes a breath, steadying herself. “How are you doing?” she asks quietly, already knowing the answer.

“I’m fucking great,” Agatha says, letting out a short, sharp laugh. She smiles, but it doesn’t reach her eyes. It’s fragile. Hollow.

Something inside Rio cracks.

“What can I do, sweetheart?” The words come out before she can stop them, her voice trembling despite her best effort. Tears sting behind her eyes.

Agatha shakes her head, scooping up a spoonful of soup. Her hands visibly tremble as she pulls it towards her mouth. “There’s nothing you can do.” She swallows hard, then inhales deeply, like she’s bracing herself. When she looks up at Rio again, her composure fractures.

“I think the pregnancy distracted me from everything,” she explains. “I was so focused on getting ready for Nicky, on being a good mom,” her voice wavers, “that I could push everything else away. Push Ralph away. Push the thoughts of him- of him all over me away.”

She shivers violently, like the memory is crawling across her skin.

Rio doesn’t hesitate. She climbs onto the bed, close enough now to feel Agatha’s warmth and gently takes her shaky hand.

“But now that it’s over…” Agatha continues, tears spilling freely down her cheeks. “There’s nothing to distract me.” Her breath breaks. “Nicky’s crib is just sitting there. Empty. Like it’s mocking me.”

Rio’s chest tightens, painful and sharp, as she rubs slow circles over Agatha’s hand with her thumb.

“I can’t stop seeing Ralph’s face,” Agatha sobs. “It just flashes in my head, over and over. And I stopped showering because every time I do, I see the scar from the surgery.” Her voice cracks completely now. “And it reminds me that Nicky isn’t here. That he’s gone.”

Rio’s throat burns. She can barely breathe.

“I can’t push the thoughts away anymore,” Agatha whispers, breaking down. “It’s getting worse, Rio. I’m scared.” Her whole body trembles as she cries. “I’m so fucking scared.”

That’s it.

Rio wraps her arms around her, pulling Agatha into her chest, cradling her like she could hold her together. Agatha collapses into her, face buried against Rio’s shoulder, sobs tearing out of her. Rio strokes her hair over and over, the motion instinctive, desperate.

“I’m here,” Rio whispers, pressing her cheek to Agatha’s head. “I’ve got you. I’m here.”

The words feel useless. Inadequate. Almost cruel.

I’m here.

As if that fixes anything.

She curses herself silently. Agatha is in so much pain- unimaginable pain- and all Rio can offer are words. No solutions. No miracles. No way to make it stop.

It’s bullshit.

But she keeps holding her anyway. Keeps whispering it like a promise she refuses to break. Eventually, Agatha’s sobs quiet, her breathing evening out as exhaustion drags her under. Rio stays exactly where she is, breath shallow, until she’s sure Agatha is asleep.

Then she squeezes her hand- quickly, gently- before quietly sneaking out with a heavy heart, leaving her to rest.

Back in her own dorm, Rio crawls into bed and pulls the blankets tight up to her chin, like she’s trying to protect herself from the world. She squeezes her eyes shut.

And finally, she lets herself cry.

She can’t do it when she’s with Agatha. Agatha has it so much worse. Rio needs to keep it together, needs to be the steady one- the support. She can’t make it about herself.

So she cries here instead- quiet, shaking sobs muffled into her pillow. She cries for Nicky. For the life they almost had. For the way she once imagined them as moms, a big happy family. She cries for everything Agatha went through that she couldn’t stop.

She couldn’t save her from any of it.

Her stomach growls suddenly, low and insistent. Hunger gnaws softly now, demanding attention. Rio frowns.

She considers getting up. Making food. Doing the normal thing.

But the thought feels overwhelming. Like too much effort for a body already hollowed out.

She stays where she is.

The pain in her stomach intensifies, and after a moment she realizes- distantly- that it’s almost comforting. A clean, simple sensation. Something she understands.

It distracts her.

Just for a little while, she tells herself. I’ll eat later.

And so, she stays in bed.

Stays there as her stomach grows emptier and emptier, clinging to the ache like it’s the only thing keeping the rest of the pain at bay.

2026

“You can drop me here,” Agatha mumbles, already spotting Rio waiting on the sidewalk ahead.

Ralph doesn’t respond. He just nods once and pulls the car to the curb, the tires crunching softly over ice and slush. Agatha pushes the door open and steps out onto the pavement, the cold biting her skin immediately.

“I’ll pick you up at three,” Ralph says flatly. “Don’t be late.”

Agatha straightens, shooting him a stubborn look over her shoulder before slamming the door shut. The car pulls away almost immediately, taillights disappearing down the parking lot as if he can’t leave fast enough.

She exhales, breath fogging in the air, and turns toward Rio.

Rio is still watching the car, arms crossed tight against her chest, a deep frown carved into her face. When she finally looks back at Agatha, there’s confusion there- and something sharp, something edged with irritation she hasn’t bothered to hide.

“He’s going golfing with his friends,” Agatha says quickly, before Rio can ask. “So he needs the car today.”

Rio nods once, lips pressing together. “I could give you a ride home later, if that’s easier.”

Heat rushes to Agatha’s cheeks, unexpected. She smiles anyway, small and instinctive. “Thanks, but we already figured out timing and everything. I don’t wanna change plans on him last minute.”

She glances around the packed high school parking lot, the large building looming ahead, windows glowing warmly against the gray sky. “Where are the others?”

“They’re already inside,” Rio answers, shivering as a gust of wind cuts through them. She tips her head toward the entrance just as snow begins to fall- soft, slow flakes catching in her dark hair and clinging to the shoulders of her coat.

Agatha raises an eyebrow, eyes flicking over Rio. “And you’re out here in the cold because…?” she trails off.

Rio shrugs, a crooked smile tugging at her mouth. “Didn’t want you getting lost on your way in.”

Agatha laughs, the sound light and easy. “You really have that little faith in me?”

“Yep,” Rio jokes immediately, then huffs out a breath. “Now let’s go, I’m freezing.”

They turn toward the school together, boots crunching over thin ice as they walk side by side. Their arms bump once- then again- small, accidental touches that send warmth shooting under Agatha’s coat despite the brutal January wind.

She doesn’t move away.

Neither does Rio.

“Hey,” Rio says softly, a nervous edge threading through her voice that gets Agatha’s attention.

Agatha looks over, and Rio meets her gaze, eyes searching. “About what you said at New Year’s…”

Agatha’s stomach twists, tight and immediate, like her body knows what’s coming before her mind catches up. Her thoughts snap back to the balcony- the cold air, the cigarette smoke, the way the words had spilled out of her without permission, dramatic and unguarded like they belonged in some cheesy movie scene.

God. How embarrassing.

She braces herself, shoulders subtly tensing. Rio might’ve been happy in the moment, but that was before she’d had time to think.

What if she’d changed her mind? What if this is her shutting it down?

“Sorry,” Agatha murmurs quickly. “I’d had a little too much to drink.” The lie tastes thin as it leaves her mouth. She winces, already regretting it.

She catches the flicker of hurt on Rio’s face- quick, unguarded- before it smooths into something carefully neutral.

“You didn’t mean it?” Rio asks. Her voice is sad, barely a whisper.

Agatha’s heart drops. Hard.

“No- no, of course I meant it,” she says immediately, the words tumbling over each other. “I meant every part, Rio. I just-” She exhales sharply, shaking her head. “I’m just saying I wouldn’t have blurted it out like that. I would’ve… y’know- worded it better.”

Rio nods, the tension in her shoulders visibly easing. “Okay. Good.” She lets out a quiet, shaky sigh, fingers curling into the hem of her sleeve. “’Cause I’ve really missed my friend.”

Agatha smiles on instinct, the expression settling easily onto her face, familiar and practiced. But something sharp still shoots through her chest at the word.

Friend.

Friends is good. Friends is what she wants, right?

So why does it sting?

“What I wanted to say was…” Rio continues, her voice dropping, careful, like she’s afraid the wrong word might shatter something fragile between them. She swallows. “I’m sorry. For how things worked out between us. For what I did.” She shivers, whether from the cold or the weight of it, Agatha isn’t sure, and when she looks up, her eyes are glossy- big and painfully familiar. “Not a day has gone by that I haven’t regretted it.”

Agatha’s heart stutters, skipping a beat. Sorrow seeps into her chest, slow and heavy, tangled with something quieter- acceptance- understanding. She takes a breath, nodding once, grounding herself. “It’s okay,” she says, and she’s surprised by how much she means it. “I’ve been holding a grudge all these years, but… we were just kids.” Her voice softens. “You were a scared kid, too. You were struggling. I mean-” She gives a small, helpless shrug. “I probably would’ve done the same thing.”

Rio nods, her composure finally cracking. Tears pool at the corners of her eyes, threatening to spill. Agatha offers a sad, understanding smile- one that carries years of hurt and forgiveness all at once.

After a beat, Rio returns it, just as fragile, just as real.

They approach the school and step into the front office, warm air washing over them, a relief after the bitter cold outside. Wanda, Yelena, and Billy are already there, visitor nametags stuck awkwardly to their coats. Agatha and Rio grab their own from the desk, peeling the backing off and pressing the labels onto their shirts before rejoining the group.

“Okay, team,” Rio says, slipping smoothly into organizer mode, “we’re hitting the junior classrooms first. They’re the most crucial.”

Wanda frowns. “Why? Are they not all the same?”

Rio smirks. “Juniors always need last-minute volunteer hours before college apps in the fall. They get desperate.”

Yelena snorts.

“Desperate enough to do manual labor,” Rio adds. “So we usually get a bunch of new volunteers.”

Wanda laughs. “Okay, smart.”

“I’ve got a list of classrooms,” Rio continues, tapping her phone. “If we have time at the end, we’ll hit the other grades.”

Everyone nods, and they head down the hall just as the bell rings. Passing period explodes around them- teenagers flooding the narrow hallway from every direction, loud and fast and completely unconcerned with personal space.

Someone bumps into Rio, shoving her sideways into Agatha. Their hips collide, flush and unavoidable, and Agatha’s breath catches despite herself. Another elbow slams into her side.

“Fuck,” she mutters under her breath, leaning toward Rio instinctively. “I do not miss this.”

“Me neither,” Rio groans back, trying to squeeze through a brief gap in the moving mass of kids.

The hallway is loud- overlapping conversations, locker doors slamming, someone laughing way too aggressively somewhere behind them. Agatha catches fragments of sentences as they pass, most of them making no sense- the words complete nonsense. It makes her feel fucking old.

“I can’t believe Byler is bones,” a girl beside her says loudly to her friend, shouting over the noise.

Agatha frowns and leans closer to Rio, lowering her voice just enough to avoid being overheard. “What the fuck is a Byler?” she whisper-yells.

Rio snorts before she can stop herself, biting her lip as she shakes her head. “Don’t worry about it.”

Agatha raises an eyebrow in response, still frowning as they finally reach the classroom door.

Wanda and Yelena appear seconds later, breathless from shoving through the crowd of teenagers. Wanda scans the hallway behind them, frowning. “Did we lose Billy?”

Agatha looks around before waving a hand dismissively. “He’s a smart kid. He’ll find us eventually.”

They step into the classroom, and Rio immediately shifts. Her posture straightens, shoulders squaring as she heads for the teacher with confidence. Agatha watches it happen- how easily Rio slips into control, how natural it looks on her. Her stomach tightens, heat blooming low as she bites her lip softly. Agatha and the others trail behind her a few seconds later, slipping into their familiar roles.

“So it’ll just be a brief explanation of our organization,” Rio finishes, calm and professional, “and then we’ll take any questions. We won’t take up too much of your class time.”

The teacher nods, smiling, and gestures for them to go ahead as students continue filtering in, voices slowly lowering as they take their seats.

“Wanda,” Rio murmurs as they walk toward the front, “you’re good to give the speech?”

“Yeah! Of course, Rio!” Wanda replies, a little too loudly- a little too enthusiastically, as she beelines for the front of the classroom. Agatha frowns, scoffing.

Rio turns to her, reaching into her bag. “Can you help me hand out the flyers?” she asks, pulling out a stack and passing them over.

“Yeah,” Agatha says easily, taking them. Their fingers brush- brief, accidental- and a sharp shiver shoots up Agatha’s spine anyway. She hates how predictable her body is.

They split off to opposite sides of the room as Wanda launches into her speech. Agatha hands a flyer to each student with a polite smile, nodding along absently as Wanda talks about community outreach and volunteer hours. She rejoins Rio near the back just as they finish.

Wanda is still talking, so they slip into two empty chairs in the back row. Their legs brush softly when they sit, close enough that Agatha’s heart rate spikes before she can stop it. She inhales slowly through her nose, grounding herself. Get a grip.

She digs into her bag, pulling out a granola bar purely for something to do with her hands. She unwraps it, takes a bite, then glances sideways at Rio before holding it out silently, offering her a bite.

Rio shakes her head, and Agatha’s stomach twists despite herself. She studies her for a second, opening her mouth to say something-

“I ate breakfast,” Rio whispers quickly, forcing a reassuring smile.

Agatha squints at her, unconvinced, but looks away instead. She doesn’t want to push. Doesn’t want to overstep. Billy slips into the classroom then, moving quietly down the aisle before dropping into the seat beside them.

“Where’d you get to?” Agatha whispers, amused.

“I hid in the bathroom until passing period was over,” he mutters.

Agatha blinks. “Because…”

“I get stressed around other people my age.”

She stares at him. “What the fuck? Why?”

Billy shrugs. “I don’t know. Teenagers are scary.”

Rio shoots them both a warning look and presses a finger to her lips before Agatha can respond. Agatha bites back a laugh and finishes her granola bar just as Wanda wraps up.

“If you’re interested in volunteering,” Wanda says brightly, “raise your hand and Yelena here will come around and take your information.”

A few hands go up. Wanda beams. “Okay! That’s all- thank you, everyone!”

The applause is scattered and half-hearted as the teacher steps back to the front, already launching into the day’s lesson. Yelena moves through the rows collecting emails, and the rest of them file quietly out into the hallway, heading toward the next classroom on their list.

Agatha walks a step ahead of the group, eyes flicking over the room numbers as she searches for the right classroom. She barely hears the jog of footsteps until someone falls into stride beside her.

Her heart jumps.

Rio.

Agatha glances sideways as they walk, Rio’s arm brushing hers again. The contact is light, casual- and still it sends an unwanted spark through her. She scowls faintly at the floor, annoyed at herself. Rio just smiles, unbothered.

“This is bringing back memories,” Rio says with a sigh. “Sitting in the front row of all my AP classes, cramming for environmental science tests at lunch, trying to get a head start on homework…”

Agatha grins despite herself and nudges her shoulder playfully. “Okay, nerd.”

Rio laughs, shooting her a mock glare. “Hey- so were you.”

“How dare you,” Agatha says flatly, though her mouth curves into a smirk. She knows Rio’s right.

“Since when were they friends?” Wanda’s voice floats up from behind them, far too loud to be accidental.

Agatha’s shoulders stiffen. She keeps her gaze forward, pretending she doesn’t hear, though her chest tightens immediately.

Billy answers in a whisper that still carries. “I think they made up at the New Year’s party.”

“Weird,” Wanda scoffs.

Agatha can feel her eyes on the back of her head. She swallows, jaw tightening.

Bitch.

She shoves the feeling down hard as they reach the next classroom.

Rio lifts a hand to wave at the teacher, slipping easily into that competent, in-charge mode that makes Agatha’s pulse kick up without permission. Wanda darts forward then, all bright smiles, and rests a hand on Rio’s arm as she speaks.

Agatha notices immediately. Of course she does.

“Agatha,” Wanda says sweetly, “would you like to give the speech this time?” She tilts her head. “I can hand out the flyers with Rio. I’m just feeling a little nervous.”

Liar.

Agatha’s lips press into a thin line. She looks to Rio, eyebrow lifting. Rio looks mildly confused too, her gaze flicking between them.

“Uh… I guess I can,” Agatha says after a beat, her voice careful.

She turns and heads to the front of the classroom, spine straight, refusing to look back. She can feel Rio behind her anyway, following quietly. The students settle into their seats with the usual chatter.

A boy in the front row fake-coughs as she approaches. “Smash,” he mutters, not quite under his breath. Laughter ripples throughout the room.

Agatha whips her head toward Rio instinctively, heat flaring in her chest. Rio’s glare snaps to the kid. Her posture shifts- controlled and unmistakably authoritative. He meets Rio’s eyes.

“What?” he laughs, nudged by his friend. “She’s a milf.”

Agatha’s stomach drops, embarrassment surging hot and fast.

“Did no one ever teach you manners?” Rio snaps, unforgiving, tilting her head. “Sit your ass down and listen.”

The room goes quiet as the boy’s face turns red.

Before she can say anything, Rio steps up beside her. She places a steady hand on Agatha’s arm and leans in, voice low.

“Just give a brief overview of what we do,” Rio murmurs. “Volunteer dates, responsibilities, then questions.”

Agatha nods, throat tight. The touch lingers just long enough to make her skin buzz, heat creeping up her neck. She hates how grounding it feels.

Rio gives her a small, reassuring smile before walking away, beginning to hand out fliers.

Agatha inhales, steadying herself. She starts her spiel, forcing the butterflies down, ignoring the heat still burning under her skin- from the touch, from the way Rio had shut down the room without hesitation.

Agatha tells herself to focus.

She fails.

As she goes over the volunteer duties, Agatha’s eyes betray her.

They drift toward the back of the room, where Rio leans casually now that the flyers are handed out, one shoulder pressed against the wall. Her tank top has ridden up just slightly, exposing a thin strip of skin at her hip.

It’s nothing. Barely anything at all.

It makes Agatha’s pulse spike anyway, heat blooming low in her stomach before she can stop it.

She forces herself to keep talking, to keep her voice steady, even as Wanda joins Rio at the back- too close. Close enough that their arms brush. Close enough that Wanda leans in and murmurs something directly into Rio’s ear.

Rio laughs.

It’s soft, brief- but it lands like a punch.

Agatha’s mouth goes dry. Her smile falters for half a second before she forces it back into place. She swallows, eyes snapping back to the class like if she looks away long enough, the feeling will pass.

It doesn’t.

“Any questions?” she asks, and the words come out thinner than she means them to, her voice cracking just slightly at the end.

Two girls raise their hands. Agatha answers easily- automatic- with a smile plastered across her face.

Still, her chest feels tight.

When she wraps things up, she doesn’t head toward the back wall. She can’t. Instead, she drifts off to the side, pulling out her phone and pretending to scroll. Her thumb moves aimlessly over the screen she isn’t really looking at as she swallows hard.

It’s stupid. It’s nothing.

So why is it bothering her this much?

A minute passes. Maybe two.

Then Rio appears beside her, warm and familiar, smiling like she always does. “You did great.”

Agatha looks up, caught off guard as she slides her phone away. She smiles back, real this time, some of the tension finally draining from her shoulders. “Thanks.”

They file out of the classroom together in silence, falling naturally into step at the back of the group as they head toward the next one.

“He wasn’t entirely wrong, y’know,” Rio says eventually, glancing over at her with a slight smirk.

Agatha blinks. “Who wasn’t?”

“That boy. Who called you a milf.” She huffs out a laugh as she elbows Agatha lightly, teasing.

Agatha’s heart drops despite the humor. She exhales, shaking her head slowly. Her voice comes out barely a whisper. “You need a kid for that.”

She keeps her gaze fixed straight ahead. She can’t look at Rio- not when saying it makes her throat tighten, not when his name echoes in her mind, unspoken but loud.

There’s a beat of silence. The lightheartedness evaporates completely- quickly- as if it was never even there, and the space between them grows heavy, charged.

Agatha hears Rio’s breath catch before she speaks.

“Nicky may not be here, Agatha, but you’ll always be a mother.” Rio says softly, and there’s no teasing now, no smirk. Just honesty.

The words hit harder than Agatha expects- hard enough to knock the air from her lungs. Her vision blurs, the hallway smearing at the edges as tears sting her eyes. She swallows, forcing them back, forcing herself to stay upright, to keep walking like nothing’s wrong. A smile finds her anyway, small and fragile, barely holding together.

“Yeah,” she murmurs. “I guess.”

Rio reaches for her hand.

She squeezes it once- quick, grounding- before letting go as they reach the next classroom. The contact is brief, gone almost before Agatha can register it.

Still, it sends a jolt straight through her.

Agatha lingers a moment longer than she needs to, heart racing, pulse loud in her ears. Her hand still tingles where Rio’s had been, warmth echoing on her skin. She curls her fingers slightly, as if she could hold onto it, and forces down the feeling rising in her chest- that familiar feeling that’s much too dangerous to name.

After hitting the rest of the classrooms, they sign out of the building. It’s taken longer than they expected, and the moment they step outside, the air feels sharper- thicker with the threat of after-school traffic and something else Agatha can’t quite name.

Wanda, Yelena, and Billy peel off quickly, shouting hurried goodbyes as they jog toward their cars. Agatha barely hears them.

She spots Ralph immediately.

He’s parked right in front of the school, leaning against the car with his arms crossed, jaw set in a deep frown. The sight of him makes her stomach drop. She exhales through her nose and walks over anyway.

She can feel Rio behind her. Silent. Close.

“I’m sorry,” Agatha starts, already bracing herself. “We were running a little behind-”

“I don’t give a fuck,” Ralph snaps, cutting her off. His voice carries, loud enough that a few heads turn. “When I say three, I mean three, not three-thirty.”

Something sharp snaps in Agatha’s chest.

She scoffs, tired- so fucking tired- of swallowing herself to keep the peace. “And when I say ‘I do,’” she fires back, “I mean ‘don’t stick your dick in other women!’”

Ralph’s face darkens.

Before Agatha can react, he steps forward and grabs her arm, fingers digging in hard enough to hurt.

“Hey!” Rio shouts immediately, approaching.

Ralph releases Agatha and whips around, eyes locking onto Rio. “What the fuck do you want?”

Rio steps closer, positioning herself just slightly in front of Agatha without even thinking about it. She crosses her arms and raises an eyebrow, unimpressed. “I’m making sure Agatha gets home safe.”

“Who said she wasn’t?”

Rio’s mouth twitches- not amused. “I just made an assumption based on your previous track record.”

That does it.

Ralph steps into her space, fists clenched, face flushed with rage. Agatha’s heart slams into her ribs as she moves instinctively toward them. “What did you just say, bitch?” He yells.

For a split second, Rio just breathes.

Deep. Steady.

Then her fist flies.

The punch lands squarely across his face with a sickening crack, knocking him back into the side of the car. Rio shakes out her hand once, flexing her fingers as if annoyed more than hurt.

Ralph staggers, clutching his nose. When he pulls his hands away, they’re slick with blood.

Agatha’s heart is racing, adrenaline flooding her veins. Fear tangles with something else- something hot and dizzying. She steps toward Rio without thinking, eyes scanning her, needing to know she’s okay.

Rio doesn’t hesitate. She grabs Ralph by the front of his shirt and slams him back against the car, forearm braced across his chest.

“You have no idea how long I’ve been waiting to do that,” she hisses, voice low and lethal as she leans in. “Now leave us the fuck alone.”

Heat rushes to Agatha’s cheeks, sharp and overwhelming. Her breath goes shallow as she watches Rio- fierce, controlled, and unafraid in a way that makes Agatha’s knees feel weak. There’s still adrenaline buzzing under her skin, her arm throbbing faintly where Ralph grabbed her, but it all blurs beneath the image of Rio standing between her and him like it’s the most natural thing in the world.

Rio gives Ralph one final shove, the motion decisive, final. She doesn’t look back at him again, as if he’s already irrelevant.

She turns toward Agatha instead, nodding toward her car. “Come on,” she says, voice steady. “I’m not letting you go home with him.”

Agatha lets out a breathless scoff, the sound brittle. She doesn’t move. Her feet feel rooted to the ground. “What else am I supposed to do?” She asks, though the question is more panic than protest.

“You can stay with me tonight,” Rio says easily, already starting to walk away.

Agatha’s heart jumps violently in her chest, a sharp, dizzy lurch that steals her breath.

Rio takes a few steps before realizing Agatha hasn’t followed. She slows, then stops. Turns.

Agatha meets her gaze.

Rio stands there with her hair slightly disheveled, chest rising with slow, controlled breaths. Her knuckles are red, smeared faintly with blood, and the sight of it sends a strange, heated twist through Agatha’s stomach. Her eyes are locked on Agatha’s with quiet certainty- not demanding, not pleading. Just… waiting.

Rio tilts her head slightly, eyebrow lifting. “Are you coming?”

Chapter 14: Chapter Fourteen
