2006

“Agatha!” A voice yells from outside the dorm, fists pounding on the door with a sharp, urgent rhythm. “Agatha!”

Agatha jumps out of bed, her heart pounding. She stumbles across the room, the blanket slipping from her shoulders, and yanks the door open. Standing there is Rio, her smile impossibly wide, eyes shining with excitement. Agatha blinks, taken aback.

“Rio? What are you doing here?” Her voice trembles slightly as she frowns, a mixture of confusion and lingering fatigue from the day.

Without an answer, Rio barrels past her into the room, shutting the door firmly behind her. The click echoes, sealing them off from the hallway. She grabs Agatha’s arms, holding on tightly, almost vibrating with energy. “I went to the financial aid office and explained everything,” she blurts, words tumbling out in a rush. “I told them your situation- about your mom refusing to pay your tuition, how you don’t have another option, all of it. They said that normally they can only do so much mid-year, but this is exactly the kind of case they can review for a special circumstances appeal. You’d need to fill out some forms, submit proof of your situation, things like bank statements and tax info, but… if they approve it- Agatha- they could offer you a substantial amount of financial aid. I’m talking grants and scholarships that combined would cover a huge chunk of your tuition!”

Agatha takes a step back, pacing toward her bed, running her fingers through her hair as the words sink in. Her heart leaps. “Hold on, Rio. Slow down. What exactly are you saying?” Her voice shakes slightly, disbelief mixing with hope as she turns to face her fully.

Rio takes a step closer, gesturing with her hands as she continues, almost spilling over with excitement. “I’m saying… forget Evanora and her bullshit ultimatum. You don’t need her money. You don’t need to marry Ralph.” She takes a steadying breath. “You could still finish undergrad without being buried in debt for years, Agatha. They said the aid wouldn’t cover everything, sure- you’d still need a job, maybe take out some loans- but it would be enough that you could breathe. Like… really breathe.”

Agatha’s chest tightens. Her mind is spinning with financial aid forms, deadlines, loans- but beneath it all, an unfamiliar feeling of relief begins to spread across her shoulders. She looks up at Rio, breath hitching. “Rio Camila Vidal, you better not be messing with me right now.”

Rio shakes her head violently, tears brimming in her eyes. Her voice softens, almost a whisper, but comes out steady. “I swear I’m not.” She raises a hand like a pledge. “This is real. I promise.”

Agatha swallows hard, tears threatening to spill, her chest rising and falling in uneven breaths. Relief crashes over her like a wave, lifting a weight she’s carried for weeks. Her voice cracks. “I… I don’t need to marry him?”

Rio shakes her head again, a small, reassuring smile tugging at her lips. “You don’t. You don’t have to marry him,” she whispers, letting each word hang between them, giving Agatha time to feel it sink in.

Agatha laughs, a breathless, shaky sound that’s half disbelief and half relief, and throws herself into Rio’s arms. She clings to her as if letting go isn’t even an option. A sob escapes her chest, raw and uncontrollable, and Rio tightens her hold, pressing her cheek against Agatha’s hair, murmuring soft, grounding words.

And for the first time, Agatha believes them.

It’s all going to work out.

2026

The car hums quietly as Rio drives, streetlights flicking past the windows in quick flashes. The air between them is tense and adrenaline-filled, but not uncomfortable. Agatha sits in the passenger seat, shoulders slightly hunched, eyes gazing through the window without really looking at anything.

They slow to a stop at a red light, the brake creaking faintly under Rio’s foot. She exhales, loosening her grip on the steering wheel just a little.

“Hey,” Agatha’s voice is soft, enough that Rio almost misses it at first. She turns her head instinctively. “Thanks for what you did back there.”

Rio glances over at her, then back at the light, the corner of her mouth lifting. “Of course. That son of a bitch deserved it anyway.”

Agatha lets out a quiet chuckle, the sound brief but genuine, and it sends an unexpected flip through Rio’s stomach. “Yeah, he did.”

The light stays red. Rio takes the moment, stealing another look at Agatha. There’s a tension in her posture that hasn’t fully faded, like she’s holding herself together by sheer will.

“Are you okay, though? Really?” Rio asks. She furrows her brow, her voice gentler now, eyes dropping to Agatha’s hands. Agatha’s fingers are tangled together in her lap, thumbs playing with each other like she doesn’t quite know what to do with them.

“Yeah, I’m good now.”

Rio raises an eyebrow, unconvinced.

“Promise.” Agatha reassures, then hesitates before continuing, the words tumbling out faster. “If it’s too much of a bother, I really don’t have to stay with you- I can call someone else or-”

“You’re not a bother.” Rio cuts in almost immediately, the response sharp with sincerity. Her heart drops at the idea that Agatha might ever believe that.

Agatha looks over at her then, really looks, and offers a soft smile in return- a real one.

Rio doesn’t notice the light turn green right away. The world seems to narrow to that single expression, the way Agatha’s eyes soften, the tension in her shoulders easing just enough to be visible. It takes much longer than it should for her to tear her eyes away from that smile, and when the car behind them honks, she jolts back into motion, pressing her foot onto the accelerator, heart thudding just a little harder than before.

Rio pushes open the front door and yanks the key free as she steps inside, placing it down on the wooden side table as she enters. Agatha follows a second later, the sound of her footsteps soft as the door slams shut behind her.

Rio drops her bag by the door and kicks off her shoes without looking back, muscle memory guiding her toward the kitchen. She opens the fridge, the pale light spilling out across her face as she stares inside, eyes darting over the minimal options. Agatha takes a seat at the counter, folding her hands together as she watches Rio move around the space with ease.

“Can I get you anything? Soda? Wine?” she asks, brow furrowing at the nearly empty fridge. She curses herself silently for not going shopping.

“Rio.” Agatha pipes up from behind her, voice soft, laced with concern.

Rio turns, one hand still gripping the fridge door. “Yeah?”

“Your hand.” Agatha nods her head toward her knuckles, now swelling an aggressive red. “It’s bruising, are you okay?”

Rio follows her gaze, lifting her hand and flexing her fingers, inspecting the damage with more curiosity than concern. “Huh.” she notes. “Yeah, I barely feel it.”

Agatha’s brows knit together. She’s already standing, already moving closer. “Do you have an ice pack?” she asks, stepping into Rio’s space and opening the freezer door before Rio can answer.

“Oh, don’t be ridiculous, it’s not that bad.” Rio insists, a chill running up her spine as Agatha stands so close her arm brushes against hers. “I’m fine.”

She reaches out to shut the freezer door, but Agatha bats her hand away without looking at her. “Stop, let me help you.” The tone of her voice leaves no room for argument. Rio gulps.

Agatha pulls out the ice pack, the plastic crinkling in her hands, and grabs a paper towel from the counter, wrapping it carefully. She tilts her head toward the bar stool. “Sit.”

Rio obeys, lowering herself onto the stool with a quiet huff, mostly because she doesn’t trust herself not to smile like an idiot if she stays standing that close to Agatha- and mostly because, as much as she hates to admit it- it feels good to be taken care of.

Agatha takes her hand gently and holds the ice pack to it, her touch careful, deliberate. Rio lets her. Agatha’s skin is warm against hers, and the contrast sends heat rushing up Rio’s neck and into her cheeks, sharp and sudden despite the sting of the ice. Agatha twists Rio’s limp hand slightly, adjusting the ice pack along her knuckles, making sure to move tenderly, making sure it doesn’t linger in one place for too long.

Rio doesn’t realize she’s holding her breath until her chest starts to burn. She inhales shakily, heart thudding as Agatha’s fingers graze over hers, just barely, as she leans in with quiet concentration. Her long, wavy hair falls forward, framing her face, and Rio can’t look away. She watches the way Agatha’s brow pinches in focus, the faint crease between her eyebrows, the way she softly bites her lip without meaning to.

“Do you have bandages?” Agatha asks, the words snapping Rio out of her trance so abruptly that she jumps.

“Uh- yeah,” she mutters. “But it’s just a little bruising, I really don’t need-”

“Where are they?”

“Bathroom. In the medical kit under the sink.”

“Hold this here.” Agatha says, guiding Rio’s own hand back toward her as she holds the ice pack in place.

Rio takes it as Agatha darts off toward the bathroom, her footsteps light but purposeful. Left alone at the counter, Rio lets out a shaky breath, her pulse still racing as the cold seeps back into her skin.

Moments later, Agatha returns, moving quietly. She gently takes the ice pack from Rio’s hand and sets it carefully on the counter. Agatha retrieves the bandages and begins to wrap them slowly around Rio’s knuckles, fingers purposeful but tender, taking care not to press too hard.

“Is that too tight?” she whispers, eyes meeting Rio’s.

“No,” Rio’s breath catches, sharp and warm in her chest. “No, that’s good. Thanks.” She smiles, small and real, before Agatha releases her hand. Rio feels a pang of disappointment at the sudden loss of contact, her chest deflating the instant she lets go.

Agatha’s gaze lingers for a beat, looking at her a moment too long before her mouth curls into a grin. “I’ll take that wine now.”

Rio smirks, immediately pushing herself up from the stool and rifling through the cabinet for two glasses. “White?” she asks over her shoulder.

“Obviously.” Agatha mutters, voice teasing.

Rio opens the fridge again, the soft hum filling the quiet kitchen, and pulls out the bottle. She pours them each a generous glass and slides one toward Agatha, who’s settled back at the island, gaze attentive.

“Thanks,” Agatha says softly, lifting her glass for a sip. Rio follows, letting the cold wine ease the lingering tension in her shoulders.

“Dinner.” Rio blurts suddenly, almost as if she’s had an epiphany, setting the glass down. “I can cook for you.”

“Oh, you can?” Agatha teases, doubtful, her brow arched.

“Hey.” Rio shoots her a mock glare. “I’m an excellent cook.”

“Mhm.” Agatha sips again with a smirk, and Rio’s stomach twists in response, the flutter of nerves bold and apparent.

“Chicken?” Rio suggests as if there’s an abundance of options, though knowing it’s the only thing left in the fridge.

“Sure.” Agatha responds, a knowing smile tugging at her lips. Rio crouches slightly to rummage through the lower cabinets, searching for a pan as she gathers the ingredients.

Her heart pounds as she hypes herself up, hoping her barely adequate cooking skills won’t let her down.

The stakes feel impossibly high.

Rio flips the chicken. The underside comes up black and charred.

She stares at it for a second, shoulders sagging. “Shit,” she mutters, taking a long sip of her wine. When she looks up, Agatha is perched over the island, elbows on the counter, a small, knowing grin tugging at her lips as she eyes the pan.

Rio sighs and grabs two plates, awkwardly coaxing the chicken onto a spatula before letting it thud onto the ceramic. She gives the mashed potatoes a half-hearted stir before dropping a measly spoonful onto each plate. Balancing one in each hand, she circles the island and sets one down in front of Agatha.

“M’Lady,” she says, offering a sheepish grin as she slides onto the stool beside her and reaches for her wine.

“Thank you,” Agatha replies sincerely. She cuts into the chicken, hesitates, then brings a piece to her mouth. Rio watches closely as she chews.

“Well?” Rio asks after a beat, biting her cheek like she’s bracing for impact. “How is it?”

Agatha chews longer than necessary, expression carefully neutral, then swallows. “It’s very… cooked,” she says at last, clearly working to be kind.

“Fuck,” Rio mutters, sawing into her own piece. Then she glances sideways at Agatha, fighting a smirk. “Y’know, I actually did it on purpose.” Her tone is deadpan. “It’s supposed to be burnt.”

Agatha arches a brow. “Oh yeah? The recipe called for a side of ash?”

“Mhm,” Rio says, grin breaking through. “I think it really ties the whole meal together.”

Agatha nods gravely. “Oh, absolutely.” There’s a pause before her voice softens. “It’s not that bad.”

“You don’t have to lie,” Rio responds, shaking her head. “We can just order takeout.”

“No, look, it’s edible. I’m eating it.” Agatha takes an exaggeratedly large bite- and immediately coughs, choking as she spits it back onto the plate. She stares down at the chicken, lips pressed together. “Oops.”

Rio bursts out laughing, already pushing off the stool. Wine glass in hand, she heads for her phone. “Yeah,” she says, still amused. “I’m ordering pizza.”

Agatha doesn’t argue.

“What do you want on it?” Rio asks, rifling through her phone for the number. Her fingers hover over the screen for a fraction too long, heart ticking faster than it should.

“I’ll have whatever,” Agatha responds casually, shrugging.

Rio looks up, eyes meeting hers, and the panic sharpens for a second. “Please don’t make me decide,” she blurts, forcing a light, playful smile even as her insides are quietly pleading. The idea of making a decision- of calculating toppings, of mentally tallying calories- squeezes her chest. She can feel her stomach knotting, a low panic bubbling under her laugh.

Agatha catches her gaze and holds it, her eyes soft but knowing, reading every flicker of tension in Rio’s face. She doesn’t push, doesn’t question, just nods slowly, understanding without needing explanation. “Okay. Let’s just do pepperoni then.”

Relief floods Rio in a rush at the easy answer. She smiles at Agatha before stepping aside to make the call.

The doorbell rings, sharp and impatient. She rushes over, wine glass still in hand. At the door, a young delivery driver stands there, pizza in his hands, floppy grin across his face. Rio snatches it, murmuring a quick ‘thanks’ as she drops some cash into his palm. The scent hits her immediately- warm cheese, greasy pepperoni, dough still hot and steaming. Her stomach twists, and a small, guilty thrill runs up her spine.

She carries the box into the living room, carefully balancing it alongside her drink, and sees Agatha lounging on the couch, legs folded beneath her, wine glass cradled in one hand. The soft light from the lamp catches the curve of her jaw, the casual confidence in the tilt of her head. Rio’s chest tightens, her pulse skipping a beat before she can rein it in.

“Alright, round two. Let’s hope this one’s better,” Rio says, setting the box on the table. She lifts the lid, and her stomach flutters again as her eyes fall on the greasy, steaming pizza. She takes a shaky breath. Don’t be dramatic, she tells herself. Just eat. This isn’t a big deal.

She grabs a slice, placing it on her plate. Agatha mirrors her, shifting slightly so that their knees brush. The contact is brief, but Rio feels it in a sudden jolt up her spine. She swallows hard, forcing herself to focus on the pizza.

“So… how’s work?” Rio asks, taking a hesitant bite, trying to start conversation to distract herself from the sudden anxiety rising in her stomach.

“Boring as hell,” Agatha groans, taking a large bite of her pizza. “I gotta say… even your park cleanups are more fun than this bullshit.”

Rio laughs. “Wow, that’s saying something. You hate volunteering.”

“I do,” Agatha says softly, shaking her head, letting a quiet laugh escape. “I really, really do.”

Agatha bites again- and the layer of cheese lifts- causing a huge dollop of red sauce to slip off the pizza, sliding down the front of her bright white shirt.

“Shit,” she mutters, eyes widening as she jumps up.

Rio reacts instantly, grabbing a napkin from the table. “Here,” she hands it over, but Agatha’s frantic wiping only makes it worse, red sauce spreading further across her shirt.

“We should wash that before it stains,” Rio suggests, standing up, as she heads toward the laundry room. Agatha follows closely, and Rio feels the warmth of her presence brushing against her arm. Her pulse flutters.

“Give me your shirt,” Rio orders once they reach, holding out her hand while rifling through the cabinet. Her fingers close on the stain remover just as Agatha drops the shirt into her palm. She sprays the red patch generously, watching it soak in before tossing the shirt into the washing machine with her own laundry. She starts the cycle, exhaling slowly, trying to shake the tension.

She’s unsuccessful.

When she turns back, Agatha is leaning against the doorframe in nothing but jeans and a black lace bra.

Rio freezes. Her eyes betray her for a fraction of a second, sliding down Agatha’s exposed torso before she jerks them back up, heat surging to the back of her neck. Her stomach tightens, pulse pounding, and every rational thought vanishes for a beat.

“Uh… sorry,” she mutters, stepping past her, trying to keep her voice even. “I’ll get you something to change into.”

She rushes up the stairs, flustered, her heart hammering, as Agatha follows a step behind her, quietly. Rio bursts into her room and heads straight for the dresser, fingers fumbling through the drawers until she finds an old, oversized Fleetwood Mac t-shirt. She grabs it, holding it out to Agatha.

Their eyes meet for just a moment. Rio’s heart skips a beat. She tears her gaze away immediately, staring down at the drawer instead, tongue pressed nervously into her cheek.

“Do you want sweats too?” she stammers, reaching toward her PJ drawer, voice just a notch higher than normal.

“Sure, thanks,” Agatha replies, voice seemingly calm and patient, a small smile tugging at her lips.

Rio digs out the grey sweatpants and holds them out. Agatha takes them, brushing her fingers against Rio’s briefly. The contact is fleeting but warm, leaving a jolt in Rio’s chest that she tries- and fails- to ignore.

“I’ll just- ” Rio gestures vaguely toward the door before stepping aside. “I’ll wait downstairs.” She walks out, shuts the door with a soft click, and exhales shakily. Her shoulders loosen slightly as she descends the stairs, though her chest still feels tight.

Back in the living room, Rio collapses onto the couch, setting her plate in her lap. She picks up a slice of pizza with a shaky hand, hesitates, and sets it back down. Her stomach growls audibly. She ignores it, biting her nails as she debates whether it’s worth it to eat.

“Rio?” Agatha’s voice calls from the stairs.

“Hm?” Rio spins around on the couch, forcing herself to meet her eyes.

“What’s this?”

Rio follows her gaze and freezes. The scrapbook in Agatha’s arms makes her stomach lurch, a mixture of guilt and panic twisting inside her. The scrapbook. She’s really gotta learn to stop leaving that out.

“I- um-” Her voice is small. Her heart is pounding, stomach twisting.

“I thought you threw this out?” Agatha asks, walking over, a mixture curiosity and amusement in her tone.

“I did.” Rio swallows hard, avoiding her gaze. “I… got it back out as soon as you left.”

Agatha laughs softly, warm and teasing, and Rio’s eyes snap up, caught in the moment.

“Please don’t be mad,” she whispers, barely audible.

Agatha’s smile shifts, gentler now. Real. “I’m not mad.”

“You’re not?”

She shakes her head, still smiling. “I was kinda hoping you’d kept it.”

Rio exhales, the breath leaving her slow and shaky. “Yeah?” Her voice comes out softer than she means it to.

Agatha nods, fingers tightening briefly around the scrapbook. “I mean, it’s painful as fuck to look through,” she admits, her voice faltering just slightly. “But… it’s our life- our experiences, our memories.” She pauses, swallowing. “What I’m trying to say is… I’m glad you kept it.”

Something flips hard in Rio’s stomach- at the sincerity, at the weight of it, the understanding. “Yeah,” she murmurs, barely above a whisper. “Me too.”

Agatha smiles again, then deliberately sets the scrapbook down on the table- out of her hands- like she’s choosing not to linger there any longer.

Only then does Rio notice the clothes- her clothes- on Agatha, and something in her chest tightens. Her eyes drift down before she can stop herself, taking in the familiar fabric now reshaped by Agatha’s body- the way the sweatpants hang just right on her hips, the way the t-shirt slips off one shoulder, worn from years of Rio’s own use. It feels intimate in a way she isn’t prepared for, seeing pieces of herself on Agatha like that. Her stomach flutters, heat pooling low and steady, and she forces her gaze back up, heart pounding harder than it has any right to.

“So,” Agatha says, tone suddenly lighter as she flops back onto the couch- right next to Rio.

Her heart stutters again.

Agatha’s thigh presses against hers, solid and warm. “What movie are we watching?”

Rio raises an eyebrow, a soft chuckle slipping out despite herself, even as her entire focus narrows to that point of contact. “Oh, we’re watching a movie?”

Agatha nods, unbothered. “Mhm. Your pick.”

Rio tilts her head back, staring up at the ceiling like she’s actually thinking. “How about Mamma Mia?”

Agatha’s grin is immediate, wide and unguarded. “Are you just picking that because you know I like it?”

“What?” Rio scoffs, failing miserably to suppress her smile. “Never.”

A lie. A bad one.

“Okay, I guess.” Agatha laughs, shrugging. “If you insist.”

Rio grabs the remote, scrolling until the familiar blue screen fills the TV. She presses play.

“I have a dream~” The song blares through the speaker.

Agatha audibly giggles, excitement bubbling out of her like she can’t stop it.

Rio watches her from the corner of her eye, the fondness catching her off guard. She smirks, settling back into the couch, letting their legs stay tangled like it’s the most natural thing in the world.

An hour into the movie, Rio hears the subtle change from next to her- the softening of Agatha’s breath, the way her movements slow gently. Then Agatha’s head tips, careful and almost hesitant, and comes to rest softly against Rio’s shoulder.

Rio sucks in a sharp breath at the sudden contact, her stomach flipping so hard it makes her dizzy. Every muscle in her body goes rigid. She doesn’t dare move, not even to adjust her posture, afraid that the smallest shift might wake her. Slowly, she moves her gaze toward Agatha, keeping her head perfectly still, her heart pounding loud enough that she’s sure Agatha must hear it.

She’s sound asleep.

The realization hits Rio all at once, warm and terrifying. She lets out a shaky breath through her nose, barely audible, and focuses on staying as still as humanly possible. Agatha’s weight is soft but grounding, the steady rise and fall of her breathing brushing against Rio’s collarbone. A few strands of her hair graze Rio’s cheek with each exhale, sending a quiet shiver down her spine.

Rio forces her eyes back to the movie. She can hear the dialogue, the familiar songs, but none of it sticks. Her mind stays locked on Agatha- on the warmth seeping through her shoulder, on the way her head fits there like it belongs. She becomes acutely aware of her own breathing, slowing it, matching Agatha’s rhythm, terrified of moving too much or breathing too loudly.

Time stretches. The couch feels smaller, the room quieter, the moment impossibly delicate. Rio lets herself exist inside it, holding still as if the world might shatter if she doesn’t.

The next song crashes in loud and immediate from the TV, bright and sudden, shattering the quiet bubble they’d fallen into. Agatha startles awake with a sharp inhale, lifting her head abruptly.

Rio’s chest deflates all at once, the warmth gone, the weight gone- her shoulder suddenly empty. The absence feels louder than the music.

Agatha blinks, disoriented, rubbing at her eyes as she turns toward Rio. Her brow furrows slightly as the memory catches up with her, realization dawning slow across her sleepy brain.

“Oh,” she murmurs, mid yawn, “sorry.”

“It’s okay,” Rio whispers, the words automatic. She shrugs, trying desperately to sound casual. “You can stay if you want.”

Agatha’s eyes flick down to Rio’s shoulder, the spot where her head had rested only seconds ago. Something soft passes over her face. She smiles, small and unguarded, biting her lip gently like she’s thinking about it. Then she looks back up.

Their eyes meet.

Neither of them looks away.

The air between them changes- thickens, tightens- like they’re trapped in this moment, and the rest of the world doesn’t exist. Rio feels her pulse spike instantly, loud and insistent in her ears, her heart threatening to beat straight out of her chest. Agatha tilts her head just slightly, studying her, gaze steady and searching.

Her eyes drop to Rio’s mouth- then lift again almost instantly.

Rio inhales sharply, her breath catching as she presses her tongue into her cheek, trying to ground herself- and failing miserably. Her eyes trace Agatha’s hair, the way it frames her face just right before spilling down her back in waves. She notices the sharp line of her jaw, the gentle curve of her nose. Then her eyes- wide and steady- locking onto Rio like she can see straight through her.

Her gaze betrays her, drifting lower, landing on Agatha’s lips. They’re soft- delicate. Just slightly parted, as if they’re asking for something Rio doesn’t trust herself to resist.

Agatha leans in- not much, barely anything at all- but it’s enough to make Rio’s entire body light up. Instinct takes over. She mirrors the movement, closing the distance by a single inch.

Her lips part before she even realizes.

Then a knock at the front door explodes through the house.

They both jump. Rio pulls back immediately, heat rushing to her face, her heart slamming wildly against her ribs. The moment shatters, shifting into awkward silence and unfinished breath.

Rio opens her mouth to say something- anything- but the words won’t come. Instead, she bolts to her feet and heads for the door, moving too fast while not looking back. Her pulse refuses to slow. Her cheeks burn. She drags in a breath, then another, trying and failing to compose herself before swinging the door open.

Lilia stands there, arms overloaded with bags, a serious look already set on her face. Before Rio can even form a word, she barrels past her and into the house.

“Okay, I’ve been really worried about you lately, Rio,” she starts immediately, heading straight for the kitchen. She drops the bags onto the counter with a dull thud, plastic crinkling. Rio trails after her, still flushed and rattled, her heart nowhere near steady. “So, I made a bunch of meal prep for you to keep in the freezer. It won’t take much effort in the mornings, and I’m really hoping you’ll consider-”

She stops mid-sentence.

Lilia turns, finally registering Rio’s expression- wide-eyed and visibly tense. Her gaze flicks past her, landing on Agatha, who’s twisted around on the couch, watching the scene unfold.

“Oh- ” Lilia blinks. “I’m sorry. Is this a bad time?” Her eyes bounce between them.

“No, it- it’s fine,” Rio stutters. “It’s fine, Lilia.”

“Oh my god, I’m so sorry,” Lilia says immediately, already backing toward the door. “I’ll go. I’ll just leave the food here.” She pauses, then steps close, cupping Rio’s face gently in her hands. “Promise me you’ll try to eat some?”

Rio nods, throat tight.

“Okay. Goodnight,” Lilia says softly, then glances toward the living room. “Goodnight, Agatha!”

The door clicks shut behind her.

Rio leans back against it, exhaling hard, her head falling against the wood as the last of her composure slips. She closes her eyes for a second too long.

“That was sweet of her,” Agatha says quietly from the couch.

Rio looks over, pushing herself off the door. “Yeah,” she breathes out, shaking her head. “She does stuff like that.”

She walks back toward the couch- then stops short, standing there instead, hands useless at her sides. Her stomach twists, nerves clawing up her chest.

Agatha looks up at her, tilting her head slightly. “So,” she whispers, hesitant now, like she’s stepping onto thin ice. She takes a steadying breath. “Where were we?”

Rio sucks in a sharp breath.

Everything in her screams to move. To close the distance. To kiss her. To end the ache.
Her heart beats wildly as her body itches to lean closer.

But her brain keeps her frozen in place.

You just made up. You’re finally on good terms again.

What if Agatha’s rushing into this without thinking?
What if she regrets it tomorrow?
What if this kiss sends them back to square one?

“I should probably be heading to bed,” Rio says instead.

She hates herself the moment the words leave her mouth.

She watches Agatha’s face fall, the hope there dimming slightly, and it hurts more than she expects it to.

“It’s only eight o’clock,” Agatha says softly, brow lifting.

“You’ve been drinking,” Rio blurts, grasping for something- anything. She presses her tongue into her cheek. Her stomach twists.

Jesus Christ.
Why does she keep talking?

Agatha studies her. “I’ve had half a glass of wine.”

“You’re going through a divorce.”

The laugh that slips out of Agatha is quiet and hollow- humorless. She looks back up at Rio with a sad, knowing smile. “You’re really finding every excuse not to kiss me, huh?”

Rio’s heart drops straight into her stomach. “No- Agatha, I didn’t mean it like that, I just-”

“It’s fine,” Agatha says gently, but her voice fractures anyway. She looks away, fingers picking at her nails, retreating into herself inch by inch. “I get it. I’m damaged goods.”

Rio feels it like a physical blow- her heart dropping straight into her stomach, breath knocked clean out of her lungs. Tears sting her eyes before she can stop them. She shakes her head hard. “Don’t,” she says, voice breaking. “Don’t you dare say that.” She steps forward without thinking. “You are not-”

“I’m gonna go to bed.” Agatha pushes herself up from the couch, cutting her off before Rio can finish. She exhales, tired and resigned.

She turns toward the stairs, already walking, already pulling away, refusing to look back at Rio as she heads for the guest room.

Rio’s stomach churns violently. Her breathing turns shallow, uneven. “Agatha,” she calls out, the name slipping from her mouth like a plea.

Agatha pauses at the bottom of the stairs- just for a second. She turns, and the look she gives Rio is quiet and devastating. Like she’s already accepted the disappointment.

“Goodnight, Rio.” She turns away and disappears upstairs. The guest room door shuts with a soft click that somehow sounds impossibly loud.

Rio doesn’t move.

She stands there in the living room, staring at the closed door, the silence pressing in from all sides. Her hand flies to her mouth as the first sob breaks free, muffling the sound so it won’t carry upstairs. Her shoulders shake as she swallows the rest of it down, tears spilling anyway, hot and relentless.

She hates herself.

Hates herself for speaking, for hesitating, for ruining everything.

Again.

So much for making up.

Chapter 15: Chapter Fifteen

2006

A sharp knock echoes through Agatha’s dorm room, loud enough to make her flinch. Her head snaps toward the door as her heart slams violently against her ribs, pulse roaring in her ears.

Shit.

She scrambles off the bed, hands unsteady as she twists the cap back onto the vodka bottle and shoves it beneath her pillow. The world tilts just slightly as she sucks in a breath, steadying herself before stumbling toward the door.

The room sways as she reaches for the handle. She blinks hard, forcing her vision to focus, and pulls it open, revealing Rio standing there.

“Hey,” Rio says softly, smiling like this is normal, like Agatha hasn’t been actively unraveling for the past hour. “Do you wanna go get dinner?”

Agatha’s throat tightens. “Um,” she whispers, voice thin, fragile. “I’m kind of tired. Sorry.” Her gaze flickers to the floor, the wall- anywhere but Rio’s eyes

“Oh.” Rio hesitates. She studies Agatha’s face, eyes narrowing just slightly. “Are you okay?” she asks at last.

“Yeah,” Agatha says too quickly. “Why?”

“I just-” Rio exhales. “We haven’t really spent time together in a while.” Her voice drops, careful. “I just thought that, I don’t know… after your financial aid appeal got accepted, things might’ve started to get better.”

Something sharp twists in Agatha’s chest.

“That money doesn’t fix everything, Rio,” she snaps quietly. “Not having to marry Ralph doesn’t fix everything.”

“No- I know,” Rio says quickly. “I know that. I just thought… maybe it would be a weight lifted, right? Like a light at the end of the tunnel?”

Agatha shrugs, small and much too casual. “I guess.”

But her stomach churns as she says it.

She’s learned better than to believe in light at the end of anything. Learned better than to get her hopes up. Things don’t work out for her- Agatha Harkness never gets her happy ending. There’s always another condition, another consequence that gets in the way.

She can’t rely on this working.

Unlike Rio, apparently.

“I just want you to be okay,” Rio says, tired now.

“I’m okay,” Agatha replies, voice flat, void of any emotion.

It’s a lie. A thin, pathetic one.

She’s not okay- not even close.

All she can think about is what happens if this falls apart- if the appeal somehow isn’t enough, if something else goes wrong, if she has to fall back on the ultimatum she’s been trying not to think about.

What happens to Rio then?

Does she just get left behind? Forced to watch Agatha marry Ralph? Watch her smile through it, and pretend it doesn’t kill her?

The thought makes her chest ache, breath hitching painfully. The what-ifs claw at her, relentless.

It finally got too much. So for the past hour, she’s been trying everything to quiet her mind.

Rio steps closer and pulls her into a hug, firm and grounding. Agatha exhales shakily, hesitating before sinking into it, her forehead brushing Rio’s shoulder.

Then Rio stiffens.

She pulls back just enough to look at her, brows knitting together. “Have you been drinking?”

Agatha swallows. Her mouth is dry. She doesn’t answer, just drops her gaze to the floor.

“Agatha,” Rio says gently, but there’s an edge there now. “This isn’t a solution.”

Agatha’s head snaps up. Anger flashes hot and fast, a defensive reflex. “Get off your fucking high horse.”

Rio recoils like she’s been slapped. Hurt flashes openly across her face, her mouth falling slightly open. “I’m just worried about you, sweetheart.”

Sweetheart.

The word hits her square in the chest.

Her mind spirals once again to the possible marriage- a future she doesn’t want but might not be able to escape. Images pile up until she can barely breathe- Rio with someone else. Or worse, Rio stuck loving her while she’s trapped. Rio waiting, hoping, hurting.

She can’t do that to her.

“Don’t be,” Agatha spits, forcing the words past the lump in her throat.

Before Rio can say anything else, Agatha slams the door shut.

The lock clicks loudly, cutting through the tense silence.

She leans back against the door, heart racing, her chest tight, blinking hard as she stares at absolutely nothing.

It’s for the better, she tells herself.

It has to be.

 2026

Sunday

Agatha wakes before the sun has fully risen- maybe for the first time in her life.
That’s what happens when you go to bed at eight, she guesses.

Careful not to make a sound, she slips out from under the covers. The room is dim and still. She pulls on yesterday’s jeans and lifts Rio’s Fleetwood Mac shirt over her head, folding it once before carrying it downstairs. In the laundry room, she finds her own shirt warm in the dryer- Rio must’ve started the cycle after everything went sideways last night. Agatha drops the pajamas into the basket and pulls her shirt back on. It smells fresh from Rio’s detergent. Just like Rio. The scent hits her all at once, sharp and dizzying, and she has to pause until it passes.

She moves through the house slowly, padding across the floor in her socks, every step measured so she doesn’t wake Rio upstairs. In the kitchen, she rummages quietly until she finds a pad of sticky notes and a pen.

Thanks for letting me stay. I’ll see you next week.

She stares at it for a second longer than necessary, then sticks it to the counter. Her chest feels tight as she grabs her bag and coat and eases out the front door, shutting it gently behind her until the lock clicks.

Outside, she calls an Uber, not wanting to inconvenience Jen this early in the morning. The cold bites immediately. She hugs her coat closer, shivering, silently cursing herself for not thinking to wait inside.

Too late now.

Her mind wanders and guilt settles heavy in her stomach as she waits. For sneaking out. For last night. For the way she’d spiraled so fast. She hadn’t even been angry- just… embarrassed. Utterly mortified, actually. She doesn’t blame Rio for pulling back. Why would she? Who would want to kiss her, really? She’d convinced herself there was something there and then acted surprised when reality caught up.

I mean, she’d held a grudge against Rio for twenty years, for god’s sake. What had she honestly expected?

For Rio to still be in love with her? To want her, even after all that?

What a fucking moron, she thinks.

She can’t face her right now. Can’t look her in the eye without wanting to curl up in a hole and disappear after how badly she’d misread everything. Agatha exhales, the breath fogging white in the early morning air, rubbing her arms as she waits.

Saturday

Agatha pulls up to the park- luckily, Ralph didn’t need the car this time- and she can feel the sense of relief. It’s a small thing, but compared to last week it feels like a victory, one less weight sitting on her shoulders. Rio had insisted they come back for park cleanup again, citing a “warm spell.” Apparently, low forties counts as a “perfectly decent temperature to work in.”

Agatha had dressed for it, at least. Extra layers, thick socks, gloves shoved into her coat pockets, even knowing the volunteer event had been shortened by a couple of hours for safety. Still, she takes a slow, steadying breath before stepping out of her car, leaving behind the comfort of the heated seat. The cold hits fast and sharp, seeping through her winter jacket. She shivers briefly before stepping out of the shade, thankful for the large patches of sun streaking across the pavement.

She walks toward the tent set up in the middle of the park, boots crunching softly against frozen ground. And as much as she hates to admit it, she’s missed this- the excuse to keep her head down and her hands busy. Manual, mind-numbing labor is far preferable to pasting on a pleasant smile and convincing innocent strangers to join the organization. Here, she can just exist in her own little bubble.

She spots Rio near the front of the crowd, scanning faces as volunteers trickle in. Their eyes meet- briefly, accidentally- and Agatha’s stomach twists hard. She drops her gaze immediately, heat creeping up her neck despite the cold.

She’d thought a full week without seeing Rio would dull the embarrassment, take the edge off the memory. It hadn’t. If anything, it had given it room to sharpen.

Agatha slips quietly into the group, standing shoulder to shoulder with the other volunteers, hands shoved deep into her coat pockets. Rio’s voice cuts through the murmur from the front.

“Okay, I think most people are here, so we’ll get started,” she says, clear and confident, voice carrying easily across the park. “I know it’s still cold- and you probably would rather be anywhere else- but I wanted to take advantage of the higher temperatures while they’re here.”

A few people chuckle, others nod, their breaths releasing slow puffs of white air.

“If you need extra layers, Wanda kindly brought some jackets, hats, and gloves,” Rio continues, glancing briefly to the side. “Please grab something if you need it. Try to stay moving to keep warm, and if you need a break, the tent has a heater set up.”

She checks her notes, then looks back up. “Our main focus today is river cleanup, so if we could get a few extra people over there, that’d be great.” A small smile. “Otherwise, you’re free to get started. Thanks, everyone.”

The group disperses, breaking into smaller clusters heading toward different tasks. A large chunk moves off in the direction of the shallow river. Agatha catches sight of Jen among them and latches onto the excuse instantly, following before she can second-guess herself.

She keeps her head down as she walks, scurrying away before Rio has a chance to cross the distance between them. She needs time- just a little- to pull herself together. To quiet the reel of memories replaying uninvited- the pause, the hesitation, the rejection that still makes her chest tighten and her stomach churn.

She’ll be able to face Rio- just… not quite yet.

She scurries toward the river with the rest of the group, the air sharp enough to sting her lungs. Jen and Alice are already there, clustered together near the bank, their bodies angled inward like the rest of the world doesn’t exist. Agatha slows as she approaches them, past volunteers tugging on tall rubber boots and snapping gloves over numb fingers, trash grabbers clacking as they’re handed out.

She offers Jen and Alice a small, tight smile that they don’t look up to notice. Her eyes flick briefly across the river- muddy water sliding lazily over rocks, litter caught in branches along the edge. Then she catches sight of Wanda a short distance away, standing stiffly with Lilia. Whatever Lilia says makes Wanda’s mouth curl downward, her shoulders tightening. Agatha frowns, unease pricking at her spine, but she forces her attention back to the task at hand.

Jen and Alice, meanwhile, have abandoned the idea of helping entirely. They sit perched on the riverbank, knees tucked up, faces too close, whispering and giggling like this is a date instead of a volunteer event. Agatha exhales sharply through her nose.

“Are you guys actually going to help or are you just going to sit there looking cute?” she mutters, already reaching for her own grabber.

Jen looks up, batting her lashes in mock innocence. “We’re gonna sit here,” she says. “Care to join us?”

Agatha snorts. “Hard pass. I don’t feel like getting scolded by Rio for slacking off right now.”

Jen’s smile turns sly. “Since when do you care what Rio thinks?”

Agatha’s chest tightens, breath catching just enough to be noticeable. “I- uh- I don’t,” she says too fast, shaking her head. “Maybe I just want to help my community.”

Jen laughs. “Sure.”

Agatha opens her mouth, already lining up a sharp retort back at her. She stops herself, not nearly having the energy. Instead, she turns toward the river, stepping slowly as she carefully makes her way to the edge.

The water is cold and slow-moving, scattered pieces of trash tangled in the mess of twigs and weeds at the bank. Agatha steadies herself, crouching slightly as she reaches out with the metal grabber, snagging soggy wrappers and half-buried bottles from the shallow water.

The repetitive motion helps.

Focusing on the work- on the cold air biting at her cheeks, on the ache settling into her shoulders- keeps her mind from drifting where it wants to go. Away from Rio. Away from the look on her face the other night. Away from the humiliation of having thought, even for a second, that she still might be wanted.

It quiets the noise.

For a little while, at least.

By the time they break for lunch, the thoughts have crept back in anyway, insistent and cruel. Agatha takes them out on the trash, stabbing at it with more force than necessary, metal clanging sharply against rock. Her jaw aches from how hard she’s been clenching it.

She drops the tool to the grass and doesn’t look back, heading toward the center of the park with Jen and Alice. The tent is buzzing with movement- coolers opening, foil crinkling, Wanda darting around with purpose as she sets up the food. Agatha notices the way Wanda keeps glancing toward Rio, something tight and calculating in her expression. Her stomach twists unpleasantly.

She grabs a plate, piles it with food without really looking, and pours herself a cup of Fanta. The cold, sugary fizz is a small relief as she takes a long sip. Jen, Alice, and Yelena are already settled at a picnic table, leaning in close, mid-conversation. Agatha drops into the seat at the end, barely managing a single bite of her sandwich before Wanda slides into the seat across from her.

Agatha looks up and rolls her eyes.

“Guys,” Wanda snaps, setting her plate down hard, “guess what I found out earlier?”

Jen lights up instantly. “Ohhh, gossip. Spill.”

Wanda folds her arms, clearly enjoying the attention. “I was talking to Lilia, and apparently Rio had a woman over last week.”

Agatha’s chest tightens, heat flaring just beneath her ribs as worry creeps in, sharp and unwelcome.

Wanda barrels on. “Isn’t that fucking insane? I mean, how many girlfriends has she had in the past couple years? Four? Five?”

Jen hesitates. “That’s… not that many-”

Wanda waves her off. “It’s embarrassing. Like, have a little dignity, y’know?”

Agatha’s fingers curl tighter around her cup. Her jaw locks so hard it almost hurts.

This bitch.

“And honestly,” Wanda continues, voice thick with contempt, “at some point you’ve gotta stop whoring yourself out-”

Agatha doesn’t think. She just moves.

The cup travels with her hand in a clean arc, bright orange liquid sloshing out as it flies. It hits Wanda square in the chest, soaking into her crisp white shirt.

Wanda shrieks, leaping to her feet. “What the fuck, Agatha?!” Her voice shoots up an octave as she frantically tries to wipe at the stain. “This shirt was seventy dollars!”

Agatha blinks once, then smiles sweetly. “Oops.”

Jen chokes on a laugh, clapping a hand over her mouth as Wanda lets out a furious groan and storms away from the table.

“Rio!” Wanda shrieks, marching toward the tent.

“Oh my god, she’s telling the teacher,” Jen snickers, hopping up and motioning for Agatha to follow.

Alice grabs Agatha’s arm and tugs her up. “Come on,” she says, taunting. “Don’t wanna lose your recess privileges.”

Agatha sighs but follows, heart already pounding harder.

“Agatha threw her drink on me!” Wanda complains loudly, gesturing at her shirt as Rio turns toward them, attention shifting to the group.

“I did not,” Agatha says flatly, crossing her arms. “She just happened to walk into it.”

“No, you threw it,” Wanda snaps, glaring, her eyes dark. “We all saw.”

Agatha turns to Jen, palms up. “Did you see that, Jen?”

Jen shakes her head immediately, smirk barely contained. “Nope.”

Agatha looks back at Rio and Wanda, smug. “See?”

“No- she did, Rio, I swear!” Wanda insists, voice rising.

Rio exhales slowly, rubbing at her temple like she’s already exhausted. “Wanda, go get a change of clothes.”

Wanda huffs but stalks off, cheeks flushed bright red.

Rio turns to Agatha, lowering her voice. “Can I speak with you?”

Agatha’s stomach drops. She follows anyway, heart thudding in her ears.

“I get that you’re mad at me,” Rio says quietly, once they’re far enough away from the others, “But you can’t take it out on Wanda. It’s not fair.” She sighs. “If you’re angry, take it out on me. I can handle it. But I can’t have you harassing the other volunteers.”

“But she-”

“Do you understand?” Rio cuts in.

Agatha’s jaw tightens, anger now developing hot in her stomach.

What kind of fucking power trip is this?

“Yes, ma’am,” she says, voice flat and cutting, sarcasm dripping from every syllable.

For a split second, Rio looks stunned. Hurt flashes across her face- raw and unguarded- before she smooths it away like it never existed.

“Thank you,” she says, nodding once.

Agatha turns and walks off, chest tight, her stomach churning. The adrenaline drains quickly, leaving something hollow and sore behind. She knows she crossed a line with Wanda. Knows she shouldn’t feel satisfied.

And yet- some small, ugly part of her does.

Agatha finishes her food mechanically, barely registering the taste, before standing and heading back toward the tent for seconds. The cold has crept back into her fingers now that she’s stopped moving, and she welcomes the excuse to stay busy. She reaches the buffet table and slides another sandwich onto her plate, the paper crinkling loudly as she handles it.

Rio’s voice carries from a few feet away.

Agatha freezes.

She doesn’t turn fully- just tilts her head slightly, pretending to examine the food options as she listens. Rio is standing with Jen near the edge of the tent, their bodies angled away from the others, shoulders tense. Agatha lingers, suddenly very invested in whether or not there’s any mustard left.

“Oh, come on, she threw a drink on her, Jen,” Rio says, clearly flustered. There’s an edge to her voice Agatha recognizes- frustration layered over guilt.

“Yeah,” Jen replies easily, folding her arms, “only ‘cause I didn’t get to her first.”

Agatha’s grip tightens on her plate.

“What do you mean?” Rio asks. “Why would-”

“She didn’t tell you?” Jen cuts in.

Rio pauses. “Tell me what?”

Jen sighs like she’s annoyed this even needs explaining. “Wanda heard you had a woman over last week. She was losing her mind about it- jealousy is not a good look on her by the way. She started calling you a whore, saying you need more dignity, all this ridiculous shit.”

Agatha’s chest tightens, sharp and hot.

Jen shrugs. “I’m just saying- it was kind of deserved.”

There’s a beat of silence.

“Agatha did that for me?” Rio asks quietly.

The question lands heavy, like it’s knocking the air out of the space between them.

“Yeah,” Jen says, more gently now. “I kinda figured she would’ve told you.”

“No,” Rio murmurs. “She didn’t.” She exhales, long and shaky. “I thought it was because-” She stops herself, words dropping off. “Nevermind. God. I’m such a dick.”

Agatha’s stomach twists painfully.

Jen clicks her tongue. “Well. Now you know.”

Rio nods slowly, like the realization is still settling in, reshaping something she thought she had figured out completely. Then she turns.

And her eyes meet Agatha’s.

Agatha realizes too late that she’s been staring- frozen mid-motion, sandwich forgotten, not subtle in the slightest. Her heart slams into her ribs as heat rushes to her face.

She looks away instantly and scurries off with her plate, resisting the urge to look back.

“Okay, everyone,” Rio calls out, clapping her hands together to grab attention as people finish tossing wrappers and clearing up lunch, beginning to gather around her. “Like I said earlier, today’s a shorter volunteer day, so let’s push through for one more hour and then you’ll be free to go.”

A collective groan ripples through the group, half-joking, half-relieved.

Before Rio can continue, Wanda’s hand shoots straight up from the front of the crowd.

Agatha feels her jaw tighten instantly as she looks over.

Rio spots her and exhales through her nose. “Yes, Wanda?”

“Rio,” Wanda says, voice sweet and sickening, “could I please switch from river cleanup to park cleanup? It’s sort of become a hostile work environment for me.” She punctuates it with a smile- and a pointed glare in Agatha’s direction.

Agatha scoffs under her breath.

“Yeah,” Rio says after a beat, clearly tired. “Sure.”

Wanda’s mouth curls into a smug little grin. “Thanks so much.”

Agatha rolls her eyes so hard it almost hurts. Beside her, Jen leans in and lets out a very fake cough. “Suck up,” she mutters.

Agatha snorts loudly.

The group disperses again, bodies drifting back toward their assigned tasks. Agatha heads for the river with the others, shoulders tense, irritation buzzing under her skin. If she’d been in a bad mood this morning, she’s in a downright foul one now.

She picks up her trash grabber and steps into the tall rubber boots, wading carefully into the shallow river this time. Cold water sloshes around her calves as she steadies herself, breath fogging in front of her face. She focuses on the work- yanking plastic bags from the reeds, prying a crushed can from the mud, wrestling a tangled net free from a cluster of sticks.

Each movement is sharp and deliberate, like she’s trying to work something out of her system. The cold bites harder out here, creeping through the layers she’d meticulously chosen this morning. She exhales heavily, over and over, as if she can push the feeling out with her breath.

Still, her gaze drifts.

She glances back toward the tent by pure instinct. Her eyes find Rio immediately.

Wanda is there now too.

Rio is kneeling in the dirt, sleeves pushed up slightly as she pulls weeds from the ground, and Wanda crouches beside her, far too close. She’s smiling, talking far too quickly, with one hand resting casually on Rio’s shoulder like it belongs there. Rio nods at something she says, her head focused down on her task.

Agatha’s stomach twists hard, sharp and ugly.

For a split second, she wants to march over there. To interrupt. To say something- anything- to break the moment apart and pull Rio’s attention back where it belongs.

Where it should belong.

She freezes, fingers tightening around the handle of her grabber as she shakes her head to push out the thoughts.

No.

Why should she care?

Her and Rio are just friends. She made that painfully clear.

Besides, Rio is allowed to talk to whoever she wants. Touch whoever she wants, be with whoever she wants.

Agatha has no claim here. Absolutely no right whatsoever.

She swallows hard, forcing the thought to settle even though it burns. A shaky breath leaves her lungs as she turns back to the river, stabbing the metal tool into the water with more force than necessary.

She keeps her head down. Keeps telling herself to just let it go.

Even as her chest continues to ache.

When the long, excruciating hour finally ends, Agatha doesn’t waste a second. She grabs her supplies, hands stiff and numb, and trudges back to the tent. She dumps everything into the plastic bin with more force than necessary, barely sparing a glance at the people milling around, chatting and laughing like the day hasn’t completely wrung her dry.

All she wants is the silence and comfort of her warm car.

She keeps her head down and starts toward the parking lot, moving fast, hoping she can slip away unnoticed before anyone thinks to stop her.

She only makes it a few feet.

Just her luck.

A hand closes gently around her arm, warm even through her sleeve. Familiar. Her stomach drops before she even turns.

She pivots, and there’s Rio- close, closer than she’s prepared for- looking at her with those big, earnest eyes that have always been her undoing.

“Agatha,” Rio says softly, like she’s testing the sound of her name.

Agatha presses her lips together, heart twisting painfully in her chest. She doesn’t have the energy for this right now. “Hm?”

“I’m sorry for earlier,” Rio says, exhaling as if she’s been holding it in for ages. Her hand shifts, resting more securely on Agatha’s arm, the touch casual and comforting and entirely too much. A chill creeps up Agatha’s spine at the contact. “Jen told me what Wanda said. Why didn’t you explain-”

Agatha arches an eyebrow, a bitter edge creeping into her voice before she can stop it. “I would’ve told you if you weren’t so quick to defend your little girlfriend.”

The words land harder than she intends. She regrets them immediately- but not enough to take them back. Her stomach twists anyway.

Shock flickers across Rio’s face. “She’s not my girlfriend,” she says quickly, her hand dropping away like she’s been burned.

Agatha huffs out a laugh, sharp and humorless. “Yet.”

“She-” Rio starts, then pauses, studying her, her tongue pressing against the inside of her cheek. Her eyes narrow slightly before a slow smirk tugs at her lips. “Wait… are you jealous?”

“No,” Agatha says immediately, too fast. She looks away, gaze skittering across the ground, the tent, anywhere but Rio’s face. Heat floods her cheeks. She lets out a soft, awkward laugh that convinces absolutely no one.

“You are,” Rio says, openly grinning now.

“I am not,” Agatha snaps, folding her arms tight across her chest in defense.

“I don’t want her to be my girlfriend, Agatha. I promise.” Rio’s voice shifts- lower, sincere- completely stripped of teasing.

Agatha’s stomach churns, confusion flashing across her face. “Why?” she asks, quieter now, eyes fixed on the dirt as she nudges a pebble with her boot anxiously. “She’s gorgeous, she loves the planet, she-”

“She’s not you,” Rio cuts in.

Agatha’s head snaps up. Her breath catches. “What?”

“She’s not you,” Rio repeats, voice steady, brown eyes locked on hers.

Agatha’s heart slams against her ribs. “I-” she stammers, words hanging in the air, her pulse roaring in her ears. “What does that m-”

“Rio! Agatha!”

Lilia barrels toward them, bright and oblivious, slicing clean through the moment. Again. “A bunch of us are going to the club later and you’re invited,” she says cheerfully. Then she turns to Rio. “Also, we need your help with these boxes, and I am freezing, so we should really get this done as fast as possible, please.”

Rio opens her mouth to protest, eyes still on Agatha- like she’s trying to finish the sentence she started- but Lilia grabs her arm and starts hauling her away toward the other volunteers.

Agatha just stands there.

Her heart doesn’t slow as she watches them walk off. It thuds loud and uneven, like it’s trying to chase Rio down all on its own even as her feet remain glued in place. The words echo in her head, over and over, refusing to settle- refusing to disappear.

She’s not you.

Chapter 16: Chapter Sixteen

Chapter Notes
