2007

Thursday

Rio lies on her back, staring at her ceiling fan as she reaches for her phone with a trembling hand. Her fingers hover over the screen for half a second before she dials Agatha’s number, her thumb moving quickly.

Her stomach growls at her, sharp and hollow, a deep ache curling in on itself. She presses her lips tightly together and ignores it.

The phone rings.

Her heart sinks lower and lower in her chest with every unanswered tone. She holds her breath without realizing it, her shoulders pinched tight, waiting- hoping-

The call cuts off.

Rio exhales shakily, her hand falling back onto the mattress as the screen goes dark.

She didn’t pick up. Again.

Her throat tightens. Tears sting behind her eyes, threatening, but she refuses to let them fall. She sets the phone beside her pillow carefully, using the little strength she can muster.

Her gaze drifts to the clock on the wall across the room, its ticking suddenly too loud in the quiet apartment.

1:20.

Ten minutes until class.

She should already be dressed. She should be pulling on shoes, grabbing her bag, scurrying out the door.

She has to get out of bed.

But her body doesn’t respond.

Her limbs feel heavy, useless, like they’re glued to the mattress. Even the idea of sitting up feels impossible. Instead, she turns onto her side and curls inward, drawing her knees tight to her chest, wrapping herself in the soft blankets.

She shivers, though the room is warm.

She doesn’t have the energy to stand. To go to class. To do homework.

She doesn’t have the energy for much anymore.

Her eyes slide shut, tiredness washing over her despite her struggle against it. Her stomach twists again, clawing at her insides, begging for her attention.

She ignores it.

She always does.

Friday

The hallway outside of Agatha’s dorm is cold and deserted. Rio stands in the middle of it, carefully balancing a small bakery box in her shaky hands.

She makes the mistake of looking down.

The frosting is piped neatly; pastel swirls topped with a generous number of sprinkles sit on each cupcake. Taunting her.

Her stomach tightens violently, a deep, aching pull that makes her slightly lightheaded. She swallows hard and looks away, fixing her eyes on the door instead, ignoring the thoughts.

She exhales slowly through her nose before knocking.

The sound echoes down the hall, too loud for what she intended. Her heart races immediately, pounding against her ribs as she waits.

Nothing.

She knocks again, softer this time. “Agatha?” Her voice is soft and gentle, like she’s afraid of startling her. “Agatha, I know you’re in there.”

Silence.

Then finally, at last, she catches movement. Soft footsteps travel from the other side of the door, shuffling against the carpet. They get close enough- loud enough- that Rio can imagine exactly where Agatha is standing.

Her chest tightens.

Agatha is there.

But she doesn’t open the door.

“Please let me in,” Rio says, her voice cracking despite her effort to keep it steady. “Please, my love.” She steps closer until her toes nearly touch the door, close enough to hear Agatha breathing, the sound uneven and shallow. “I brought you cupcakes.”

There’s no answer.

“Agatha, I- I don’t understand.” Her hand lifts on instinct, pressing flat against the cold wood. It’s the closest she can get. “I thought we fixed things. We got you your money.” Her voice wobbles. “Why won’t you talk to me?”

There’s a pause- long enough that Rio considers turning to leave. She sighs, hesitantly pulling her hand away.

“I’m scared, Rio.”

Agatha’s voice comes out quiet and hoarse, barely audible. The sound of it makes Rio’s chest lurch painfully.

“It’s not gonna work out,” Agatha continues. “Something’s gonna go wrong.” Her voice fractures. “I can feel it.”

“I won’t let that happen,” Rio says immediately, words spilling out before she can stop them. “We can work this out together. You don’t have to do it alone.” She leans closer to the door, forehead nearly touching it. “I’m right here.”

Silence stretches again.

“You’re just gonna get hurt,” Agatha says finally, crying now, the sound unmistakable. “I can’t let you get hurt.”

“Agatha, I won’t,” Rio insists, shaking her head even though Agatha can’t see it. “Nothing’s going to go wrong, sweetheart. I promise.”

Muffled sobs leak through the door. Rio’s hands curl into fists at her sides, nails biting into her palms. Every instinct in her screams to break the door down, to pull Agatha into her arms and prove she’s not going anywhere.

“Just let me in,” she whispers, pressing her forehead against the wood, eyes squeezed shut.

Silence.

“Agatha?” Her voice breaks completely this time.

Nothing.

Slowly, Rio steps back. Her hands tremble as she crouches down and sets the box of cupcakes on the floor, lining it up carefully against the door in hopes that Agatha will decide to accept it.

She waits as she stares at the door.

She imagines it flying open- imagines Agatha finally running into her arms, wrapping her in a tight embrace.

It doesn’t happen.

The tears finally spill over, turning the world around her into a blur.

And eventually… Rio turns and walks away.

2026

Saturday

By the time Rio finishes moving the last of the boxes with Lilia, Agatha is gone.

The realization hits her all at once, sharp and hollow. She stands there for a second longer than necessary, hands still gripping the cardboard, eyes scanning the parking lot like Agatha might reappear if she just looks hard enough.

She doesn’t.

Rio exhales a shaky breath, her chest tight- her heart still racing from earlier.

She’s not you.

Why the fuck would she say that?

The words hadn’t been planned. They hadn’t even been slightly thought through. They’d just slipped out, completely unfiltered, dragged up from somewhere deep in her chest before she could stop them. She presses her tongue into her cheek now, utterly embarrassed, as she replays it over and over in her mind.

Idiot.

Agatha’s already upset- already hurt from the other night, from everything that’s been hanging between them for weeks. Blurting out her deepest feelings like that wasn’t going to help anyone.

It was reckless.

It was exactly the kind of thing that would send Agatha running in the opposite direction.

Maybe it already has.

Maybe that’s why Agatha left- why she didn’t wait for her.

The thought makes Rio’s stomach flip unpleasantly. She swallows, pushing it down, refusing to let it settle. She busies her hands instead, picking at her nails as she walks to her car, boots crunching against the gravel. The cold air bites at her face, grounding her just enough to keep her moving.

She climbs into the driver’s seat and shuts the door. For a second, she just sits there, forehead resting against the steering wheel, eyes closed.

Get it together.

Her phone buzzes in her pocket. She pulls it out, squinting at the screen, seeing that Jen’s texting the group chat with details for the club later- where they’re going, what time, etc.

Who’s in?  She adds.

Rio groans softly and bites her lip. Her whole body feels heavy- completely exhausted in that bone-deep way that isn’t fixed by a good night’s sleep. The idea of loud music and flashing lights makes her want to crawl into bed and not move for twelve hours. She probably won’t even go.

Another notification pops up.

It’s from Agatha.

I’m in.

Rio’s breath catches. Her thumb hovers over the screen as something warm and electric spreads through her chest, cutting straight through the exhaustion. She stares at the message longer than she needs to.

On second thought…

Me too.

She types back immediately, not allowing herself the time to overthink it, hitting send before hesitation can creep in. The corners of her mouth lift despite herself, her smirk small and unsteady.

She sets her phone down, starts the car, and pulls out of the parking lot, the sun already dipping lower in the sky. All she wants now is to get home, shower, and lie down for as long as she can before tonight- before she has to see Agatha again and figure out what the hell she’s supposed to say to her.

Because whatever this mess is between them- it’s clearly not going away.

By the time Rio steps out of the shower, her stomach is growling, loud enough to make her wince. She reaches for the towel on the hook- then freezes.

The bathroom mirror is fogged over, but not enough to hide her. Her hand stalls midair, the towel hanging uselessly as her eyes drift over her reflection. The familiar knot tightens in her stomach- part hunger, part dread.

She studies herself automatically.

Her waist is too wide. Her arms look swollen instead of lean. Her thighs are too close- threatening to touch no matter what she does.

Her pulse races faster as she frowns, her jaw tightening, eyes flicking from one flaw to the next without a second of hesitation. She bites her lip hard enough to hurt.

Stop.

She tears her gaze away and yanks the towel around herself, wrapping it tight, like she can hide from her own mind if she covers up enough.

It’s easier not to look. Easier not to think at all.

Once she’s dressed, she heads downstairs, taking the steps a little too fast. Her head spins briefly, the world tilting just enough to make her grab the railing before she forces herself onward. The kitchen light feels too bright as she opens the fridge, eyes skimming over the shelves.

They’re full. For once.

Thanks to Lilia’s handiwork, there are containers neatly stacked, labeled, and portioned. A large variety of chicken, rice, pasta, and other quick meals stare back at her. Rio’s stomach twists again, her brain immediately doing the math without her asking it to- adding up calories, remembering rules she didn’t consciously set but follows anyway.

She shuts the fridge door and turns away, her pulse racing far too fast for someone just standing in their kitchen.

Then she stops.

She closes her eyes and exhales slowly through her nose, shoulders rising and falling. Last time she went out on an empty stomach, she’d gotten blackout drunk, convinced Agatha to drive her home, threw up in front of her, and- much to her distain- asked her why she didn’t want her.

It hadn’t been a good look.
She really doesn’t recommend it.

With a quiet groan, Rio turns back, opens the fridge again, and grabs the pasta before she can second-guess herself. She shuts the door a little harder than necessary, holding the container with a shaky hand.

One step at a time, she tells herself.

Just eat.

She rushes down the stairs, nearly missing the last step, fumbling with the doorknob as she twists the lock with shaking fingers. The door clicks shut behind her once she leaves, and she jogs down the front steps to the Uber waiting at the curb, its headlights flooding the sidewalk in light.

She pulls the door open and climbs into the backseat, breath still a little uneven. Agatha, Jen, and Alice are already there, the car warm and faintly smelling like perfume and winter coats. All three glance up as Rio slides in and shuts the door, the Uber easing away from the curb.

“Girl,” Jen says immediately, arms crossed, unimpressed, “we’ve been here like ten minutes.”

“Sorry,” Rio mumbles, eyes dropping to her lap as she tugs her coat closer around herself.

Ten minutes had felt like nothing upstairs. Ten minutes had been her standing in front of her mirror, changing outfits over and over until the floor was covered with rejected clothes. Nothing had looked right. Shirts clung too tightly to her chest and stomach, skirts rode up her thighs no matter how she adjusted them. Every option just felt wrong.

By the time Jen texted we’re outside, she’d still been frozen, staring at herself, pulse racing. In the end, she’d grabbed the safest things she could think of- a black graphic tank, a grey denim skirt, and the first pair of boots she found by the door- more out of panic than intention.

She shifts in her seat, suddenly hyper-aware of the hem of her skirt against her legs.

From the other side of Jen, Agatha looks over, meeting her eyes. She offers a small, tentative smile.

It hits Rio straight in the chest.

Her heart flips, warm and utterly traitorous. She smiles back automatically, then quickly looks away, pressing her forehead lightly to the cold window as downtown Chicago rushes past outside. Streetlights blur together, neon signs flickering in her peripheral vision. She watches the city go by instead of thinking, instead of spiraling again.

Soon enough, the Uber slows, pulling up in front of the club. Even from outside, the music leaks through the walls- low and heavy, vibrating faintly in the air. They pile out onto the sidewalk, the cold wind biting through their clothes, completely unforgiving after the warmth of the car.

They hurry toward the entrance, boots clicking against the pavement. Once inside, the sound swallows them whole. The bass pounds through the floor, rattling in Rio’s skull. Colored lights flash across the room as she scans the bodies packed together, the smell of sweat and perfume and alcohol hanging thick in the air.

Wanda spots them almost immediately.

“Hi!” she shrieks, darting over and throwing her arms around Rio before she can react.

Rio stiffens, startled, then hesitantly lifts her arms and gives Wanda a brief, awkward pat on the back before pulling away as politely as she can. “Hey,” she mumbles, barely audible over the music, pasting on a small smile.

Instinctively, her eyes flick to Agatha.

Agatha’s gaze lingers on Wanda for half a second too long, something sharp and unreadable flashing behind her eyes before she looks away, scanning the room instead.

“I am way too fucking old for this,” Agatha yells over the music, addressing no one in particular.

“Old is a state of mind!” Wanda shouts back, already backing toward the bar. “Let’s do shots!” She spins and scurries off, enthusiasm bursting out of her.

Rio snorts despite herself, covering it with a quick cough.

Lilia pats Agatha’s shoulder as she follows Wanda. “Baby, if you’re old, that makes me ancient.” She leans in close, grinning. “And I’m not ready to be ancient yet.”

Then she’s gone too, disappearing into the crowd instantly.

Rio stands there for a moment, the music pounding in her ears, her heart still racing. She glances back over at Agatha.

Now that they’re out of the car, she really sees her- and Jesus fucking Christ, it takes everything in her not to drop to her knees right there. Agatha’s wearing a tight red shirt, the zipper running straight up the center, stopped just low enough to expose a sharp V of skin. Her collarbones catch the light when she moves- sharp and taunting. Her black leather shorts sit high on her waist, hugging her hips tightly and leaving a generous amount of thigh bare.

Rio’s gaze betrays her, dragging down Agatha’s long legs, all the way to the strappy black heels wrapped smooth around her ankles.

She gulps, heat flooding her face, and snaps her eyes back up before she can get caught staring. She makes herself take a breath, rolls her shoulders back, and forces her expression into a failed attempt at nonchalance.

“I could use a drink,” she offers, tossing in a casual smirk.

Jen immediately wedges herself between them, grinning. “Me too- let’s go!” She grabs both of their wrists and tugs them toward the bar, Alice trailing after them through the crush of bodies.

They reach the counter, the surrounding seats loud and crowded. The bartender nods at them mid-pour before turning his attention over. “What can I get y’all?”

“I’ll do a mojito,” Agatha says easily, then glances at Rio.

“Vodka soda,” Rio answers with a polite smile, fingers picking at the edge of her nail. She ate dinner earlier, so has to go with the low-calorie option to even it out.

Jen and Alice shout their orders, and the bartender works fast, sliding their drinks across the bar. They grab their glasses and weave through the crowd, following closely behind one another, before finally collapsing into a booth tucked away near the corner.

Rio slides in first.

Agatha sits right beside her.

Their legs brush- barely- but it’s enough. A sharp chill shoots up Rio’s spine, her breath catching before she can stop it. She stills, gripping her glass a little tighter, pretending she doesn’t feel it- pretending she isn’t painfully aware of every inch of space between them.

Chatter fills the air quickly, laughter bouncing off the walls as it blends with the steady pulse of bass overhead. The neon club lights flash and blur across the room, painting their faces in vibrant colors. Halfway through their drinks, Wanda and Lilia reappear- Wanda stumbling slightly as she makes her way to the booth, cheeks flushed with excitement.

“Guysss,” she singsongs, waving her arms at the others dramatically, “let’s go dance!”

Jen leans back in the booth, her eyebrows shooting up, a flabbergasted look plastered across her face. “Girl, we aren’t even one drink down.”

Alice tilts her head, smirking. “Enjoy your shots, Wanda?”

Wanda gives a quick nod, a mischievous smile on her lips.

“She stole a couple of mine,” Lilia adds, crossing her arms with a raised eyebrow as Wanda’s giggles bubble up uncontrollably.

“Damn, girl,” Alice says with mocking disbelief, lifting her brows as she looks between the two.

“Yeah, so I’m gonna need this,” Lilia whispers conspiratorially, reaching forward to snag Alice’s half-empty drink before she has a chance to react.

“Hey!” Alice protests, lunging to grab it back, but Lilia has already pulled it away, taking a long sip as the others burst into laughter.

Agatha lets out a soft giggle, glancing over at Rio at the same time Rio looks at her. Their eyes meet briefly, the world around them fading for a heartbeat, before they both tear their gazes away, each feeling the weight of their unspoken words.

Rio’s body stiffens slightly, a flicker of the earlier conversation lingering awkwardly between them.

“Okay, if you guys are gonna be lame, I’ll go dance by myself,” Wanda pouts dramatically before darting off toward the crowded dancefloor, her laughter trailing behind her.

“Okay, bye,” Jen whispers, rolling her eyes with exaggerated annoyance.

“I don’t know, I feel kind of bad for her,” Alice says, shooting Jen a pointed look, her tone softening.

Lilia nods in agreement, but Jen’s expression hardens. “Hey- no. Are we all just conveniently forgetting what she said about Rio earlier?”

A sudden heat flares in Rio’s cheeks, a mix of embarrassment and defensiveness.

Please don’t make this a big thing.

“It’s fine,” she murmurs, offering a small, tight-lipped smile that doesn’t quite reach her eyes.

“No, it’s not fine,” Jen snaps back, her voice rising slightly. “She was being a bitch. Who is she to judge you for having a girl ov-” She drops off mid-sentence, realization finally flashing over her. “Wait, back up,” she says slowly, her tone shifting to offense. “Rio, you had a woman over and you didn’t tell us!” She leans forward, eyebrows now furrowing in playful outrage. “Girl, spill! Who was it?”

Rio’s gaze darts to Agatha for less than a second, then quickly back to her lap. Her voice wavers, quiet and uncertain, as she picks at her fingernails. “I, um- it was no one.”

Jen frowns, lips pressing into a thin line before a laugh escapes her, shaking her head. “Damn, okay then.” She reaches for her cocktail, finishing it in one quick gulp. “That’s my cue to get another drink.” She pushes herself off the couch, grabbing Alice’s wrist to pull her along too.

“And I need my first,” Lilia adds, following them toward the bar with a grin- leaving Agatha and Rio alone- surrounded by the loud hum of conversation and blaring music.

Agatha toys with the edge of her glass, eyes flicking up at Rio briefly and then back again. Rio’s fingers tap lightly against her own drink, her gaze fixed on a far wall, yet still hyper-aware of Agatha’s occasional glances.

She finally chugs the rest of her drink, wiping her mouth before turning to Agatha, not allowing herself any hesitation. “Wanna go dance?” she asks, cutting through the tension as her pulse quickens.

Agatha studies her for a second, eyes searching Rio’s, before she nods. “Yeah.”

They climb out of the booth and make their way to the dance floor, their bodies pressed close together, the air now thick with heat and the tang of sweat. They weave into the crowd, the bass of the music overtaking them as they start to move. People jostle them from all sides, leaving them with almost no control over where they’re going, but Rio barely notices.

Her heart races as she’s pushed closer and closer to Agatha- their torsos brushing, hips nudging, faces so near that she can feel Agatha’s breath ghosting along her neck. Every touch sends sparks shooting through her body, leaving her breath shallow, pulse stuttering.

For just a moment, the room fades. The music dulls; the people disappear.

All she can see is Agatha.

All she can feel is Agatha.

Until a young man slips into her line of sight, moving toward Agatha and sliding his hands onto her hips. Agatha frowns, momentarily frozen, but Rio doesn’t give her a chance to respond. She scoots instinctively closer, placing her own hands gently but firmly on Agatha’s waist, shoving the man’s grip away with ease- without even a second thought.

He grimaces. “Who are you?” he spits, irritation flaring in his voice.

The audacity.

“I’m Rio,” she shoots back over the music, raising an eyebrow, hands still protectively on Agatha’s hips. “Who are you?”

“I’m Brandon,” he replies, voice wary.

“Well, hands off, Brandon,” Rio says, glaring at him, “she’s with me.”

Brandon scoffs, muttering under his breath as he backs off- obviously butthurt- and disappears into the crowd.

Agatha tilts her head, looking at Rio with narrowed eyes, expression unreadable. It hits Rio that her hands are still lingering on Agatha’s waist. Her mouth opens slightly, brow furrowed as she studies her. Rio jerks her hands back, heart skipping.

Why the fuck do you keep doing this? Rio thinks. Control yourself.

Agatha slows her movements, her eyes locked on Rio. “Can I talk to you?” she says, leaning close enough that Rio can feel her words brush against her ear.

Rio’s stomach twists, nerves shooting through her chest. 

Fuck.

She nods, letting Agatha grab her wrist and pull her off the dance floor as sparks shoot through her at the contact. They weave through the crowd toward the bathroom, the music fading into a low hum as Agatha pushes the door open and pulls her inside, locking it behind them.

Rio paces, immediately moving to the sink and leaning against the cool countertop, her fingers tapping lightly as she forces herself to meet Agatha’s gaze. She gulps, heart hammering in her chest, each beat louder than the faint hum of the bass still seeping in from the dance floor. This is it, she thinks, stomach twisting. I finally pushed her over the edge.

Agatha stands a few feet away, shoulders squared, her chest rising and falling quickly as she stares at Rio. Her hands tap lightly at her thighs as if she’s nervous, but her voice cuts through the quiet like a knife. “You’re getting on my fucking nerves.”

Rio’s chest tightens. Her heart skips a beat. She fucking knew it, she thinks, panic welling up inside her stomach.

“What?” Her voice comes out barely above a whisper, quiet and uncertain, with a tremor in it that she can’t hide.

“Enough with the mind games.” Agatha steps closer, the space between them shrinking by mere inches, heat radiating off her in waves. Her eyes lock on Rio’s, intense and unyielding as they study her. “Just admit it already. Either admit it or tell me I’m crazy- that I’m imagining this.”

Rio’s throat tightens. Her stomach knots, a rush of heat creeping up her spine. Her hands ball into fists at her sides, nails pressing into her palms anxiously. “Imagining what?” she asks, frowning, though her voice quivers.

Agatha takes a shaky breath, shoulders rising and falling as if she’s gathering all her strength, all her courage. “That you want me.”

Rio’s chest flips, a sudden jolt of dizziness running through her. She swallows hard, blinking rapidly, trying to steady herself. She catches the sincerity in Agatha’s eyes, the way her lips are parted slightly, the subtle shift of her weight forward as if drawn magnetically toward Rio. She opens her mouth to deny it, to play it safe, to keep the walls up- but instead the words slip out before she can stop them.

“You’re not crazy.”

The reaction is instantaneous. Shock, relief, disbelief, all flashing across Agatha’s face in a rapid succession. Her eyes widen, lips parting slightly as she exhales, releasing almost a soundless laugh of astonishment. Rio watches, pulse hammering, as Agatha takes a deliberate step closer, the space between them shrinking until they’re only inches apart.

“So admit it,” Agatha whispers, voice low and trembling, carrying both urgency and something softer- vulnerability hidden behind her fragile mask of courage.

“What-” Rio begins, voice catching, but Agatha cuts her off, leaning in slightly, the warmth of her breath brushing against Rio’s face.

“Say it, Rio.” Her hands land on the counter on either side of Rio, framing her, effectively trapping her in. The heat radiating off Agatha’s arms and body presses against Rio’s senses, her close proximity dizzying. Rio can feel the pulse of her heart through her chest, the faint brush of her fingers against her palms, and the smell of her- warm and intoxicating.

Rio swallows again, shivers running up her spine as the tension coils tight in her stomach. Her pulse races, and for a moment she closes her eyes, breathing in, steadying herself. She finally opens her eyes and exhales, her voice barely audible, coming out raw and real.

“I want you…” she admits, her voice barely steady. “I crave you.”

Agatha looks genuinely stunned, like the words knocked the air straight out of her lungs. Her eyes search Rio’s face, wide and vulnerable. When she speaks, her voice is quiet- a little broken. “Really?” she whispers. “Me? Not Wanda, not-”

Rio cuts her off before she can finish, panic and urgency flaring in her chest. “Ever since you came back into my life…” She exhales a shaky breath, shaking her head as the words tumble out now, unfiltered and completely unstoppable. “I haven’t even considered thinking about other women- not Wanda, not anyone.” Her cheeks burn as she swallows. “…except you. You’re all that’s on my mind. You consume me, Agatha. Day and night.”

The confession settles between them, heavy and alive. The room seems to close in, the distant hum of the club dissolving into nothingness- the only remaining sound being the fast thumping of their hearts. Rio feels something inside her loosen- a tightness she didn’t realize she’d been carrying finally easing off.

Tears well in Agatha’s eyes as a small, disbelieving smile pulls at her lips. “You mean it?”

Rio nods slowly, deliberately, holding her gaze. “I mean it.”

She notices Agatha’s eyes flick down to her lips- linger there for just a second too long- before drifting back up. Agatha doesn’t step away. She doesn’t pull back. Rio’s breath catches as she looks into her eyes, those deep pools of blue she’s tried so hard not to drown in. Heat floods her skin, her stomach, every nerve lighting up as Agatha studies her, her breath warm against Rio’s mouth.

Rio gulps, pressing her tongue into the inside of her cheek, her own eyes betraying her as they flick to Agatha’s lips.

In all her life, she’s never felt a hunger like this.
Never something so sharp, so consuming.

She learned long ago how to bury her cravings- how to shove them down, ignore them as they relentlessly gnawed and demanded her attention.

But this time, she can’t.

This time… she concedes.

Rio lifts her hand slowly, deliberately, cupping Agatha’s cheek. Her thumb brushes lightly against her skin as she pulls her just a little closer, moving carefully, giving Agatha every chance to stop her- to pull away.

She doesn’t.

Instead, she leans in, closing the distance, letting Rio guide her lips closer to her own.

Rio’s eyes flutter shut as she finally bridges the space between them.

Their lips meet.

And it feels like fireworks exploding inside her, all-consuming, like everything she’s been holding back finally ignites at once.

She lets out a soft sigh into Agatha’s lips as Agatha presses closer, her body pinned firmly against the counter. The cool edge digs into Rio’s lower back, a sharp contrast to the heat radiating between them. Without thinking, Rio’s hands slide up, fingers threading into Agatha’s hair. She grips a fistful of it, holding on like it’s the only thing anchoring her- like if she lets go she might completely lose it.

Her stomach flips violently as Agatha brushes her tongue, just barely, along Rio’s bottom lip before pulling away again. It’s brief- almost teasing- but it unravels her. Rio’s mind goes blissfully blank. She forgets how to think, how to breathe. The world narrows until there’s nothing left but the feeling of Agatha’s mouth against hers, the press and pull of it, the soft heat that makes her chest ache.

She doesn’t know how she survived twenty years without this. Doesn’t know how she’s supposed to survive another second now that she’s felt it again.

Agatha’s hand drifts slowly down Rio’s waist, unhurried but deliberate, before circling around to her back and tugging her closer. Their stomachs press together, the contact intimate and grounding all at once. Rio’s breath stutters, a soft, instinctive groan slipping out of her before she can stop it, her grip tightening in Agatha’s hair as her whole body reacts before her mind can catch up.

The cool surface presses against the backs of her legs. Her thighs spread without thinking, muscle memory and want taking over as Agatha immediately steps between them, gripping low on Rio’s hips.

Rio’s legs wrap around Agatha’s waist, locking her in place, pulling her closer until there’s no space left at all. A soft moan slips from her into the kiss as it deepens, unrestrained now, desperate and hungry.

She melts into it- into her. Every nerve is lit up, every thought stripped away until there’s nothing left but this feeling, this moment, this impossible closeness she never wants to let go of.

Then a sharp knock echoes through the room.

They break apart instantly, heads snapping toward the door as they both inhale hard, lungs burning as they try to catch their breath.

“Hurry up in there, I have to pee!” a voice calls.

Agatha’s hands linger- just for a second- before she drops them and moves toward the door. She opens it a crack, poking her head out.

“Alice!” Agatha says, her voice pitched with forced cheer.

Rio’s heart drops. She slaps a hand over her mouth, smothering a sound that’s half gasp, half laugh.

“Oh- Agatha, I’m sorry, I didn’t realize it was you!” Alice says from the other side.

“Oh, no, that’s okay,” Agatha replies quickly, waving her off with a smile. “Just give me a few minutes, hon!”

She closes the door slowly, then whips around, the smile vanishing as she strides back toward Rio.

“Shit,” Agatha mutters, looking up at her- still perched on the counter- as she bites at her nail. “What do we do?”

Rio lets out a quiet giggle before clapping her hand over her mouth again. Agatha crosses her arms.

“It’s not funny, Rio.”

“It’s a little funny,” Rio says, smirking. “We’re like teenagers sneaking around, trying not to get caught.”

“No, it’s not,” Agatha snaps back, pacing. “Because if Alice finds out, she’ll tell Jen, and Jen will tell Yelena, and Yelena will tell Wanda, and Wanda will throw a tantrum and cause a fucking scene, and then everyone will know.” She stops, frowning. “Is that what you’d want?”

Rio’s smile fades. She looks down, her legs swinging slightly, heels knocking softly against the counter. “No,” she whispers finally.

“Right,” Agatha says, nodding once, crossing back over to her as she rests her hands on Rio’s thighs. “So… what’s our plan?”

“Okay, come on,” Agatha says, flicking the lock and pulling the bathroom door open. Her arm wraps tight around Rio’s waist as she leans into her, steering her forward intentionally.

Rio looks up and her eyes land on Alice’s. She lights up immediately, plastering on an exaggerated grin. “Aliceee!” She slurs, reaching forward as if to hug her, only for Agatha to keep her firmly pinned to her side. Agatha’s hand slides soothingly up and down Rio’s arm, thumb brushing slow, absentminded circles into her skin.

Alice raises an eyebrow.

“Sorry, Alice,” Agatha sighs, shaking her head as she steps aside to let her pass. “She had a little too much to drink.”

Rio wipes her mouth with the back of her hand, swiping away nothing at all, before fake-stumbling into Agatha’s side. Their hips knock together, deliberately- naturally. Agatha doesn’t even flinch.

She turns toward Rio, smiling down at her, arm still locked securely around her waist as she brushes a loose strand of hair out of Rio’s face. “We’ve learned our lesson though, haven’t we, Rio?” she says, voice dripping with mock patience.

It’s condescending enough to sell the act- but Rio catches the glimmer in her eyes, the unmistakable spark that tells her Agatha is enjoying this far more than she should.

“Fuck you,” Rio shoots back, her words clumsy on purpose. She turns back to Alice, who’s inching fully into the bathroom now, and beams at her. “You’re so prettyyy,” she drawls, leaning in to clumsily boop Alice on the nose.

Alice laughs, shaking her head as she pulls the door closed. “Thanks… okay, I really have to pee. I’ll see you guys later.” The door shuts quickly. “Feel better, Rio!” Alice calls through it, followed by the sound of the lock clicking and then silence.

Agatha doesn’t waste a second. She steers Rio down the hall and away from the bathroom, the music becoming loud and shattering again, and the moment they’re out of sight, they both lose it- laughing hard, folding over as they try to catch their breath.

“Holy fuck,” Rio manages between giggles, her voice suddenly perfectly clear as she yells over the booming speakers. “That was good.”

“Should we become actors?” Agatha asks with heightened confidence, grinning widely.

“Oh, definitely,” Rio says, smirking as her laughter begins to fade.

She bites her lip, the humor dissolving into something heavier as Agatha’s laughs start to trail off too. The air shifts as they look at each other, the space between them charged again.

“So.” Rio starts softly. “Now what?”

Agatha shrugs. “We go find the others, get another drink,” she says, casual to the point of cruelty.

Rio takes a shaky breath.

That’s not what I meant, she thinks.

She doesn’t say it.

Instead, she nods and starts walking back toward their booth- back to their friends, to small talk, to pretending she doesn’t care about Agatha nearly as much as she does.

Agatha immediately falls into step beside her. Their hands brush, just barely, and the contact sends a shiver straight up Rio’s spine, her stomach flipping violently.

She glances over at Agatha- at the curve of her nose, the way her hair falls just right, the way her skin glows under the soft gleam of the club lights.

It takes everything in her not to grab her and pull her right back into that bathroom.

She doesn’t give a damn who sees.

Chapter End Notes

basically im the nicest person ever and you all love me for this.

Chapter 17: Chapter Seventeen

Chapter Notes

TW: violence, brief mentions of disordered eating, child loss

sorry this took forever, ive been sooo busy, but hope you enjoy!

2007

It’s been four months since Rio has seen Agatha. Four months since she’s heard her voice, since she’s smelled her skin- that intoxicating smell of vanilla lotion and faint remnants of lavender shampoo, something that still lingers in her memory no matter how hard she tries to scrub it out. Four grueling, unbearable months that are stretching on endlessly, each day blurring into the next.

She’s stopped by Agatha’s dorm more than once. Lingered too long in the hallway, pretending to check her phone while her heart pounds so loudly she’s afraid it’s going to jump out of her chest. She’s stood outside her door like a creep, staring at the wood grain, imagining Agatha just on the other side. One time she even raised her fist, knuckles hovering mere inches from the door, breath caught in her throat before she pulling away at the last second.

What’s the point?

Agatha has made it clear she doesn’t want to see her again- for whatever stupid, self-sacrificing reason she’s convinced herself of.

So, Rio forces herself to stay away. Forces herself to respect it, to give her space, even though every part of her begs to fight it.

It’s what Agatha wants. That has to matter more than how Rio feels.

Instead, she focuses on her classes, on her work. It takes everything she has just to keep up, but in a way, she’s grateful for it. The exhaustion leaves less room to think. Less room to feel.

It’s a distraction- from Agatha, from the constant ache in her stomach, from the spiral of self-hatred that loops through her head when she’s alone. And for now, she tells herself, that has to be enough.

She’s just finishing up her last assignment when a sharp knock rings through her door.

Rio startles, fingers freezing on her keyboard. She blinks at the screen, then exhales and pushes herself out of bed. The room tilts the second her feet hit the floor, her head spinning from getting up too fast. She pauses, one hand braced against the mattress, forcing herself to breathe until her vision clears.

Another knock.

Her heart starts racing as she crosses the room, bare feet padding softly against the floor. Her mind betrays her immediately- imagining Agatha on the other side, standing there with that familiar, careful smile, eyes warm and forgiving like none of this time apart ever happened.

Her chest leaps painfully.

She grabs the handle and yanks the door open.

Her smile drops the second her eyes land on the blonde girl standing there instead.

“Carol,” she sighs, forcing her lips into something resembling a grin. “What’s up?”

“There’s a party in room 504,” Carol announces, already stepping past her without invitation. “We’re going.”

Rio groans softly as Carol heads straight for her closet, rummaging through hangers before yanking out a black mini skirt and tossing it at her. Rio stumbles back as it hits her chest, barely managing to grab it before it slides to the floor.

“I’m not going,” Rio protests, holding the skirt limply at her side.

“Mmm,” Carol hums, shaking her head. “Too late. I’ve already decided we are.”

Rio straightens, crossing her arms over her chest and lifting an eyebrow. “I’m the RA. You know I’m not supposed to go to those,” she reminds her flatly. “It’s literally my job to shut them down, I’m not sure why you even told me.”

“Rio, come on,” Carol pleads, grabbing her wrists and tugging her closer. “Have some fun, break the rules once in a while.” She gives her a stern look. “You graduate in a month, and basically all you’ve done the past three years is study. Don’t you want the authentic college experience?”

Rio stiffens slightly at the words.

“It’s one night,” Carol continues, eyes bright. “Just let loose. You’ve been so uptight lately.”

Rio studies her friend’s face- the excitement in it, the genuine hope that this might be the thing that snaps her out of whatever fog she’s been stuck in. She looks down at the floor, jaw tight, the familiar ache pressing heavy in her chest.

She exhales.

“Fine,” she mutters. “Just this once.”

Carol lets out a screech and throws her arms around her. “Yay! It’ll be fun, I promise.” She pulls back, already reaching for another hanger. “Now get changed, we’re leaving in 5.”

Rio stands tucked into a corner, drink clutched loosely in her hand, watching the rest of the students lose themselves in the noise. Laughter spills over the music, bodies pressed together and moving in time beneath the faint streaks of moonlight. Her head spins slightly from the tequila, even though she’s only had a single shot. The alcohol sits heavy in her stomach, sloshing uneasily as her muscles tense around the familiar ache.

Her gaze drifts over the packed bodies. The party has long since outgrown the single dorm room, spilling out into the common area of the floor, now overcrowded with students from other buildings- too many people with nowhere to disappear.

Rio briefly considers sneaking out.

Then the elevator doors slide open. And Rio’s breath catches sharply in her throat.

Agatha steps out, laughing with a couple of other girls, framed by soft light for just a second too long. She’s wearing flared jeans and a tight black halter top, the fabric hugging her frame and leaving her shoulders and back bare. Absolutely fucking beautiful in the way that still feels unfair.

Rio’s stomach flips violently as she stares, mouth parting before she can stop herself.

Tears sting her eyes at the sight of her- really seeing her- for the first time in far too long. Her heart races, pounding against her ribs as instinct takes over, her body already leaning forward, ready to cross the room to her.

She stops herself mid-step.

She can’t.
She shouldn’t.

Agatha doesn’t want this. Doesn’t want her.

Rio swallows hard and tips her glass back, downing the rest of her drink in one go. She wipes her mouth with the back of her hand and retreats deeper into her corner, as if the distance might dull the ache.

It doesn’t.

Her eyes betray her immediately, tracking Agatha as she moves immediately toward the table littered with half-filled bottles and scattered shot glasses. Rio watches her pour a shot, toss it back with practiced ease. Then another. And another.

Her chest tightens painfully.

Thirty minutes later, Agatha is on the floor.

Rio watches as she doubles over, clutching her stomach, laughter spilling out of her uncontrollably as she smacks one of her friends’ arms, struggling to breathe through it. A small, sad smile tugs at Rio’s lips. It’s been so long since she’s seen her like this- this happy. Since she’s seen that wide, unguarded grin. Heard her real laughter.

A redheaded girl steps backward suddenly, tripping over Agatha and nearly falling.

Rio recognizes her instantly- Natasha, Agatha’s problematic roommate from freshman year.

“Jesus, get off the fucking floor,” Nat snaps, glaring down at Agatha, making sure none of her drink spilled.

Agatha jerks her head up, eyes flashing. “Fuck off, Natasha.”

Natasha scoffs, turning to the friend beside her, drink sloshing in her hand. “This is the roommate I was telling you about,” she says loudly, rolling her eyes. “The one who was always calling the RA on me for no reason.”

She enunciates, making sure Agatha hears it as she flashes her a glare.

The other girl leans in, eyebrows raised, voice pitched just loud enough. “Did you hear she was pregnant?”

The words hit Rio like a punch in the chest.

Her stomach drops, memories flooding back without warning- the fluorescent hospital lights, the antiseptic smell, the sobs that wracked through Agatha’s body as she held her tight. Nicky.

For a second, she’s not here anymore.

Then the girl speaks again.

“She like lost the baby.”

Rio’s heart sinks even further. Tears burn behind her eyes.

Who the fuck do they think they are?
Talking about Nicky like he’s gossip. Like he’s their entertainment.

She looks at Agatha.

Her gaze is locked on the floor now, her body unnaturally still, her breathing shallow. She looks hollow- like she’s slipped somewhere far away.

“Serves her right,” Natasha scoffs. “I mean, that’s what you get for being a bitch, right? What goes around comes around.”

Heat explodes under Rio’s skin.

She barely registers the movement before Agatha is on her feet.

Her fist connects with Natasha’s face with a sickening crack, sending her stumbling backward into the table. Natasha lowers her hand slowly from her cheek, already reddening.

“You bitch!” she shrieks, lunging forward and shoving Agatha hard.

Agatha barely budges.

In the next heartbeat, Natasha is on the ground, Agatha straddling her waist as she rains blow after blow down on her face.

The room erupts into noise- a mixture of cheers and chaos. Some people scream encouragement, others try desperately to pull Agatha off. Voices overlap, drowning out Natasha’s cries completely.

Everything blurs.

Rio stands frozen for half a second too long, heart slamming wildly against her ribs, mouth hanging open. Then she shakes herself out of it and pushes forward, forcing her way through the tightly packed crowd, panic clawing through her chest as she fights to reach her.

Because she knows Agatha won’t stop on her own.

“Agatha!” Rio shouts, shoving someone hard out of the way as she reaches her. She grabs onto Agatha’s upper arms, drops down close to her ear. “Agatha, stop,” she pleads, voice breaking. “You have to let go.”

She tugs at her again, but Agatha doesn’t budge.

Rio catches a glimpse of her face- tears streaking down her cheeks, eyes wild and shattered- and it splits her straight open. “Agatha, come on. Please.” The word comes out barely louder than a breath.

Agatha finally turns her head.

Their eyes meet, and for the first time tonight, Agatha actually sees her.

Rio feels it immediately- Agatha’s arms going slack, her fists unclenching as they pull away from Natasha. Agatha pushes herself up shakily, knuckles already red and swelling. Rio’s hand is still on her arm without realizing it.

Agatha jerks away and steps back.

Rio barely registers anything after that. Natasha’s friends rush in, helping her sit up, voices overlapping and sharp. Rio’s head spins- whether from adrenaline or tequila on an empty stomach, she can’t tell.

“Don’t worry, Nat,” one of them says tightly, gripping her hand. “The police are on their way.”

Rio’s head snaps toward her. “What?”

A chorus of protests erupts around them.

“You called the fucking cops?” a guy yells.

The girl frowns. “Yeah, she was beating her up! What was I supposed to do?”

“Not call the cops!” he snaps. “Come on- let’s go.”

People scatter instantly. Students flood toward the elevator, others grabbing bottles off the table and shoving them into cabinets, under pillows, anywhere they’ll fit. The room explodes into frantic movement.

Then the elevator doors open.

Two officers step out.

Everything freezes.

Drinks hover midair as bottles still sit half-capped on the counter. Someone swears under their breath. A few people try to back away, retreating into rooms to disappear.

Rio’s heart slams painfully against her ribs.

“We got a call about an assault?” One officer asks, looking around the room before eyeing the bottles.

Natasha’s friend waves them over immediately. “Yeah. Agatha beat up my friend for literally no reason.”

One of the cops kneels in front of Natasha, gently tilting her head, asking her to follow his finger as he examines the bruising.

“I’m gonna need to take a statement,” The other officer says. “Is there someone here I can talk to?”

Before Rio can even register the question, a panicked guy nearby points straight at her. “Talk to Rio, she’s the RA.”

Her stomach drops.

“Rio?” The officer turns to her, nodding. “Can I talk to you?”

No.
No, please, no.

“Okay,” she says instead, the word barely audible.

Her eyes drop to the cosmopolitan still in her hand. With shaking fingers, she sets it down on the counter. The officer watches the entire time, eyebrow raised.

She swallows hard and follows him to a quieter corner of the room.

He pulls out a notepad, pen clicking open. His expression is calm but firm. “Tell me what happened.”

2026

Saturday

For the rest of the evening, it takes everything in Agatha not to grab Rio and kiss her until her legs give out. Now that she knows what it feels like again- now that she’s had a taste- it’s all she can fucking think about. The memory sits heavy and vivid in her chest, nerves still hot in her hands and mouth. It makes it impossible to focus on anything else.

So, when Jen asks her a question and her mind finally snaps back into the present, all Agatha can manage is a blank, delayed, “Huh?” as she looks at Jen like she’s just been dropped into the conversation mid-sentence.

Jen’s laugh bursts out first, quickly echoed by the rest of the table. “Agatha, where do you go?” she asks, shaking her head with a grin. “I said- how’s the divorce going?”

Agatha exhales slowly, the word divorce landing like a dull weight in her stomach. “We’re finalizing on Friday.” She lifts her glass and takes a long sip, suddenly needing the burn of liquor desperately more than she has all night.

She feels Rio’s gaze snap toward her instantly- sharp and concerned- but she doesn’t look back. If she does, she knows she won’t be able to control herself.

The table goes quiet for a beat, the energy dipping. Agatha stares down at her hands, at the condensation slicking her glass, hating the way the attention presses in on her.

“You know we’re all here if you need anything, right?” Jen adds more gently.

Agatha looks up, meeting her eyes, and offers a small, practiced smile. “Thanks, Jen.” She takes another gulp of her drink. Then, forcing an attempt at lightness, she lets out a short, humorless laugh. “Gee. Way to kill the mood.”

Jen takes the hint immediately, swiveling her attention away. “Okay- new topic.” She turns to Rio. “And how are you feeling now, Rio?”

Agatha’s eyes flick over without permission, her heart jumping the second she catches Rio’s side profile- her fingers wrapped around a tall glass of water, her posture relaxed but alert.

Rio had kept up the drunk act when they rejoined the group. She didn’t want Alice questioning how she was suddenly stone-cold sober after supposedly throwing up in the bathroom. Unfortunately, that meant Lilia cutting her off and shoving water into her hands until she agreed to drink it.

Agatha had nearly lost it when Rio protested, sloppily crossing her arms over her chest like a sulky teenager. The only upside was when Rio had drifted closer, letting her head tip lazily against Agatha’s shoulder. No one batted an eye- because who isn’t touchy when they’re drunk, right?

It had sent a shiver straight down Agatha’s spine. She doesn’t think she’d breathed the entire time Rio was pressed against her, knowing she was secretly in full control of herself. Knowing she wanted to be that close.

Now, Rio sits a few inches away on the couch, close enough to feel her aching presence, but still too far for Agatha’s liking. Her shoulder feels cold without her.

“Much better,” Rio says brightly, smiling as she looks up at Jen. “Very sober.” She tips the glass back and finishes the water with an exaggerated gulp, then adds with a wink, “I could go for another drink.”

“Let me buy you one!” Wanda pipes up from across the seating area, already leaning forward with enthusiasm.

Rio’s gaze darts to Agatha, panic flickering briefly in her eyes. She looks back to Wanda just as fast, lifting a hand to wave it off. “Oh- thank you, Wanda, but that’s okay.”

“I insist,” Wanda says, tilting her head, her smile soft but unwavering.

Rio looks back to Agatha again, her mouth parting slightly like she’s searching for an excuse that doesn’t exist.

Agatha shrugs, a sly smile tugging at her lips. “She insists,” she says lightly, trying to withhold a laugh, eyes locked on Rio’s.

Her stomach flutters, the memory of the bathroom flashing quickly through her mind as their eyes meet. It’s a secret that lingers heavy in the air between them, something sort of powerful.

So what if Wanda wants to buy Rio a drink? She had her pinned against a counter twenty minutes ago.

Rio shakes her head at her, subtly but with a slight smirk, like she can hear Agatha’s thoughts. She shoots her a glare that clearly says bitch, then turns back to Wanda with a resigned sigh.

“Okay,” she says, giving in. “Sure, Wanda.”

Wanda practically springs to her feet. “Cool!” she beams, already halfway turned toward the bar. “Vodka soda?”

Rio hesitates- just a fraction of a second too long- before nodding. “Sure,” she says, voice seemingly light but practiced.

Agatha turns toward her, one eyebrow arching. “What ever happened to the cosmopolitans?”

Rio’s gaze flicks down immediately, like her eyes have somewhere else to be. Her fingers pick at the skin beside her thumb repeatedly. Agatha notices instantly- because of course she does. She always notices. Her attention follows the movement, then drifts back up just in time to catch the brief tightening of Rio’s jaw, the way her mouth sets before she looks away.

“I outgrew them.” Rio shrugs, casual on the surface, but practiced.

Liar.

The word lands sharp in Agatha’s head.

“I’ll get one with you.” She blurts it out before she can stop herself, the offer rolling over her tongue on instinct.

Rio looks back at her then, really looks, her eyes searching. They linger on Agatha’s face for a beat too long, as if she’s weighing some heavy decision. Agatha holds her gaze, heart beating a little faster under her ribs. After a moment, Rio takes a shaky breath and nods once.

“Okay.” She turns back to Wanda, who has gone very still, watching the exchange with a raised brow and unmistakable interest. “We’ll have two cosmopolitans, Wanda.”

Wanda stares at her for a second, unimpressed, face falling flat. Then she turns toward the bar, muttering, “I wasn’t offering to pay for Agatha, but sure whatever,” just loud enough to carry back.

Agatha presses her lips together, failing to suppress a laugh. It slips out anyway, soft and warm, and she catches Rio glancing at her from the corner of her eye. For the briefest moment, she sees the tension in Rio’s shoulders eases, just a fraction, and Agatha’s stomach flutters.

“Agatha! Let’s go!” Jen shouts from a few feet away, barely audible over the music as she waves her arms frantically over her head.

Agatha squints at her, head swimming just enough that it takes effort to focus. “Go where?” she yells back.

“Karaoke bar across the street! Grab the others!” Jen cups her hands around her mouth to project the noise, already halfway turned toward the exit.

Agatha pivots to Rio and Alice, tapping them both on the shoulder to get their attention as they slow their dancing. “Apparently we’re leaving,” she says with a shrug.

“Nooo,” Alice groans, immediately throwing her arms around Rio’s shoulders and clinging on sloppily. “Don’t make me go,” she whines, pressing her mouth near Rio’s ear like a sulky child.

Agatha rolls her eyes, smiling despite herself. “To another bar, Alice. Relax.” She jerks her chin toward the door and starts weaving her way back to Jen, stumbling just slightly as she goes.

Jen does a quick headcount, spinning in a slow circle. “Okay- everyone here?” She grins when no one answers and turns on her heel. “Let’s go, bitches!”

The group funnels toward the exit in a loose, uncoordinated line, most of them just following Jen on instinct, not really knowing what’s going on. Agatha and Rio end up near the back. As Agatha steps forward, a hand settles lightly on her lower back.

Rio.

The touch is brief, barely there, but it sends a sharp shiver up Agatha’s spine, heat blooming under her skin. The hand slides to her waist, guiding her gently toward the door, then disappears just as quickly as they step outside. It’s innocent enough- quick enough that no one notices.

But Agatha doesn’t think about anything else as they cross the road, streetlights washing over them in streaks of yellow, her head still buzzing from that single point of contact.

The karaoke bar is quieter than the club. The music hums softly instead of pounds through her skull. Agatha glances toward the stage just in time to see a guy finish absolutely butchering “Piano Man,” and she laughs under her breath.

They claim a couple of empty tables near the front. Jen is already halfway to the song queue, dragging Rio with her. Alice drops into the seat beside Agatha and nudges her shoulder. “You gonna sing?”

Agatha scoffs. “That’s funny.”

“Oh, come on.”

“I sang karaoke on my birthday. That’s all you’re going to get.”

Alice rolls her eyes and heads for the bar instead.

When Jen and Rio come back, Rio slips into the seat next to Agatha, close enough that Agatha’s breath catches. Their eyes meet for half a second, which is somehow long enough to make Agatha chest leap, and she has to look away..

If it was hard not to touch her before, it’s unbearable now.

Pull yourself together, Agatha.

Alice returns with a round of tequila shots, setting them down with a grin. “Drink up.”

“More shots?” Agatha asks, eyebrow raised.

“What, are you scared?”

Agatha snorts and reaches for one, taking the jab personally. Her fingers brush Rio’s as they grab their shots at the same time. Her pulse jumps.

They clink the glasses together, downing the tequila simultaneously. Agatha bites into her lime immediately afterwards, the burn spreading warm through her chest.

A few songs later, she’s doubled over with laughter as Jen absolutely commits to “Total Eclipse of the Heart.” She watches her friend on stage, loud and confident and only slightly off-key. When the song ends, Jen bows dramatically to scattered applause.

Agatha whoops and claps, grinning wide.

Rio climbs out of her seat as Agatha glances back to the screen, the lyrics to Andante, Andante glowing softly. Agatha’s mouth falls open slightly as she watches Rio make her way to the stage. When she reaches the microphone, she turns back toward Agatha, holding out a hand with a smirk, head tilted just enough to be an offering.

Agatha’s heart stutters as memories crash into her all at once- late nights in college, Rio singing it to Nicky against her stomach, Agatha murmuring it back to her as Rio drifted to sleep. Rio at her birthday party, frozen, terrified to sing it out loud again.

Their song.

Agatha shakes her head once, a reflex more than a decision.

Rio doesn’t drop her hand. “Come on,” she says softly, eyes wide, pleading in that way she knows Agatha has never been able to resist.

Those fucking eyes.

Agatha hesitates for only a second longer. Then she exhales, pushes herself to her feet, and joins her on stage. The microphone is placed into her hand, cool and solid. She looks over at Rio, who smiles, and it knocks the breath clean out of her.

Rio lowers her microphone and leans in just enough to murmur, “Mi niña linda,” The words audible only to Agatha.

My pretty girl.

Agatha’s chest tightens painfully. She hasn’t heard those words since college. Her eyes burn as Rio pulls back, lifts the mic, and starts to sing.

“Take it easy with me, please
Touch me gently like a summer evening breeze
Take your time, make it slow
Andante, Andante
Just let the feeling grow…”

Rio’s gaze never leaves Agatha. Not once. It’s steady and intimate- absolutely devastating. Agatha feels it everywhere- down her spine, in her stomach, in the back of her throat. Rio arches a brow at her, just slightly.

Shit. Her cue.

Agatha swallows and brings the microphone up, her voice a little unsteady but there, catching as she locks her eyes back onto Rio.

“Make your fingers soft and light
Let your body be the velvet of the night
Touch my soul, you know how
Andante, Andante
Go slowly with me now…”

She takes a steady breath before joining in on the chorus, her voice sliding into Rio’s like it’s always belonged there, falling into place without effort. It’s soft and familiar. The harmony settles between them, and suddenly everything else fades away- the chatter from the nearby tables, the clink of glasses, the scattered laughter from the back of the room.

All Agatha can see is Rio- her mouth shaping the words, her eyes fixed on her like the rest of the room doesn’t exist, like this is just the two of them again. The music wraps around them, pulling memories up from somewhere deep and hidden until her chest feels too tight to even breathe.

When the music finally fades and the applause swells, reality rushes back in all at once. Agatha blinks, her gaze tearing away from Rio as if she’s just remembered where they are and how she was somehow convinced to sing karaoke. She exhales shakily, sets the microphone down, and makes a beeline back to the table.

She drops into the seat beside Lilia with an embarrassed laugh that’s a little forced, and immediately catches the look Lilia gives her- an arched brow with a knowing smirk. Her eyes flick between Agatha and Rio.

Agatha looks away fast, suddenly fixated on her hands, heat crawling up the back of her neck. She doesn’t know why she feels so flustered, so exposed at the fact that Lilia’s knows the weight of what just happened.

They didn’t do anything wrong… she’d just really thought they were being more subtle.

She senses Rio slide into the seat beside her and sucks in a quiet breath, chest warming instantly, like her body recognizes the close proximity before her even brain catches up. She turns her head just enough to look at her. Rio’s already watching her, a small smile tugging at her mouth as she sticks her tongue into her cheek, eyes flicking over Agatha with an unmistakable, teasing glance.

That does it.

Agatha’s pulse jumps, ringing loud in her ears. She swallows, clearing her throat of absolutely nothing. “I’m going to the bathroom,” she announces to the table, pushing up from her chair a little too fast, the legs scraping softly against the floor.

As she passes Rio, she lets her hand trail along her shoulder- brief and casual to anyone else watching, but deliberate. With just enough pressure. Just enough contact to send a message.

Follow me.

She doesn’t look back to see if it lands. She just stalks toward the bathroom before she can second-guess herself, before fear or common sense can talk her out of it. The door shuts behind her with a soft click, sealing her into the quiet hum of fluorescent lights and distant music seeping through the walls.

She exhales, hands braced on the counter for a second as she steadies herself. Her reflection looks flushed- eyes bright, lips already parted like she’s waiting for something. She lifts her hands to her hair, fluffing it, adding a little volume like that might somehow give her more courage. From her bag, she pulls out a lip gloss and smooths on a thin layer, pressing her lips together, watching the shine catch the light.

She smiles at herself in the mirror- nervous but anticipatory- then leans back against the counter, arms folded loosely, gaze locked on the door.

Any second now.

Her heart beats faster with every passing moment. She imagines the door opening. Rio slipping inside, that familiar look in her eyes. The way she’d smile like she knows exactly what she’s doing.

A minute passes.

Then another.

There are no footsteps, no handle turning.

Agatha bites her lip, fingers tapping against the counter despite herself. The thrill dulls, twisting into something uneasy. Her stomach rolls, a faint wave of nausea creeping in as doubt starts to whisper in her mind.

Maybe she didn’t notice. Maybe she didn’t understand.

…or maybe she just doesn’t want her.

With a quiet huff, she pushes herself off the counter and opens the door, stepping back into the bar.

Her eyes land on Rio, still seated at the table. Wanda has slid in beside her now, her chair angled too close, her hand resting comfortably on Rio’s arm as she talks. She’s leaning in, smiling wide, her mouth moving a mile a minute.

Rio catches Agatha’s eye from across the bar. Her eyes widen instantly, flaring with panic, a silent help flashing between them. She looks back at Wanda and nods awkwardly, trying to subtly pull her arm free. Wanda doesn’t notice- or just doesn’t care.

Jealousy flares hot in Agatha’s cheeks before she can stop herself. She’s moving on instinct, already crossing the space and dropping into the empty seat on Rio’s other side, wedging herself neatly into the conversation. She flicks a glance at Rio first, just to let her know she’s there.

“What are we talking about?” she asks, smile fixed a little too tightly on her face.

“I was just telling Rio how great she was up there!” Wanda beams. “You know, I’ve always loved ABBA.”

“Oh, have you?” Agatha replies.

“Mhm!”

“What’s your favorite song?”

Wanda doesn’t hesitate, still smiling obliviously. “Probably Dancing Queen.”

Agatha rolls her eyes and lets out a fake cough, muttering, “basic,” just under her breath.

Rio immediately shoots her a look and lightly smacks her arm. “Agatha,” she warns, tongue pressing into her cheek. “Be nice.”

Agatha bites the inside of her lip, forcing herself not to fire back.

“Oh, that’s okay, Agatha,” Wanda says easily, slurring her words slightly, waving it off without a care in the world. “What can I say? I like the classics.”

The karaoke machine switches tracks, and Wanda perks up instantly. “Oh! My song!” She grabs another shot from the table, downs it without hesitation, and hops up toward the stage, already swaying a little.

Rio turns to Agatha the second she’s gone. “What was that?” she asks, eyebrow raised.

“What was what?” Agatha frowns.

Rio tilts her head, pitching her voice high in mockery. “’What are we talking about? Oh, have you?’”

Agatha crosses her arms, still a little embarrassed from hanging around in the bathroom like an idiot mere minutes ago. “Okay, that is not what I sound like.”

Rio smirks, flicking her eyes up to her. “You’re jealous.”

“I am not.” Agatha shoots back, but her heart jumps.

“Oh, you so are,” Rio laughs. “It’s okay. It’s cute.”

“Cute?” Agatha splutters but can’t withhold her smile. “How dare you.”

Rio opens her mouth, clearly ready with another jab, but Wanda’s voice cuts through the bar before she can get it out.

“Guys, I need a partner- it’s a duettt,” Wanda slurs into the microphone, the last shot clearly tipping her over the edge.

The group exchanges glances. No one moves.

“Anyone?” Wanda asks again, wobbling slightly.

Agatha notices Rio drop her gaze to her hands, suddenly very interested in the grain of the table.

“Okay,” Wanda mutters into the mic, forcing a laugh. “It’s fine.”

The music starts. She begins to sing as the lyrics to ‘The Last Time’ light up on the screen behind her.

“I find myself at your door
Just like all those times before
I'm not sure how I got there
All roads, they lead me here
I imagine you are home
In your room, all alone
And you open your eyes into mine
And everything feels better…”

Her voice wavers as she looks around the room, visibly shrinking under the attention. Agatha exhales through her nose. As much as she hates to admit it, she kind of feels bad for her.

She’s… not totally unbearable when she’s drunk.

The chorus comes around, and Wanda shifts slightly to the side, clearing her throat as she tries to take the other part.

“You find yourself at my door
And just like all those times before…”

Something twists uncomfortably in Agatha’s chest. Before she can overthink it or talk herself out of it, she’s pushing back her chair and climbing onto the stage.

She leans into the mic and takes over the verse.

“You wear your best apology
But I was there to watch you leave
And all the times I let you in
Just for you to go again
Disappear when you come back
Everything is better…”

Wanda stares at her, stunned, before her face splits into a wide, grateful smile. Her eyes glisten, and Agatha gives her a small, reassuring look as she sings. Her gaze flicks briefly to Rio, who looks utterly baffled, like she’s trying to figure out what the hell Agatha is doing.

Agatha just shrugs at her.

She turns back to Wanda for the bridge, letting herself sink into the music as their voices blend. The harmonies sound pretty good, but she’s not exactly in a state to judge after a few too many drinks.

“This is the last time you tell me I've got it wrong
This is the last time I say it's been you all along
This is the last time I let you in my door
This is the last time, I won't hurt you anymore
Oh-oh oh-oh oh-oh oh-oh
Oh-oh oh-oh oh-oh oh-oh…”

They lean in towards their mics, hands gripping them, as they sing at each other, pouring every ounce of drama into the moment. When the next verse hits, they spin away in sync, hair whipping as they laugh. Wanda reaches out dramatically, and Agatha takes her hand, twirling before throwing the back of her hand to her forehead with an exaggerated sigh.

“This is the last time I’m asking you this
Put my name at the top of your list
This is the last time I'm asking you why
You break my heart in the blink of an eye…”

They finish the song breathless, backs pressed together, striking an over-the-top final pose. Feeble applause scatters from the crowd. Jen is already on her feet, whooping and clapping like she’s just witnessed a Broadway show.

Agatha feels Wanda laugh beside her before she wraps her arms around her in a quick, tight hug and hops off the stage, heading back to her seat. Agatha follows a moment later, dropping back down next to Rio, who’s currently eyeing her.

“What?” she asks, still catching her breath.

“Nothing,” Rio says softly, smiling. “I just… didn’t expect you to do that.”

Agatha grimaces. “I'm drunk... and I felt bad.”

Rio nods. “I know. It was sweet.” She tilts her head, smirk creeping in. “You’re going soft.”

“I am not.” She crosses her arms.

“No, it’s cute,” Rio teases, voice low so that the others don’t overhear. “You’re cute.”

“Shut up,” Agatha fires back immediately, though she can’t suppress the grin that creeps onto her face and the warmth rising in her chest.

She’s screwed.
