2007

“Rio?” The officer says again, sharper this time. “I asked what happened.”

Rio sucks in a shaky breath that barely fills her lungs. Her fingers won’t stay still. Instead, they pick at the broken skin around her nails until the familiar sting appears, giving her a small sense of relief. She keeps her eyes down, fixed on the red crescents that are now carved into her skin.

She sees the edge of his badge out of the corner of her eye and her heart stutters.

She’s never spoken to a cop before- never been questioned.

She’s never even gotten so much as a speeding ticket.

“Sorry,” she breathes, the word coming out almost silent. She forces herself to look up, to meet his gaze like she’s supposed to, trying to act like someone who has her shit together. Her throat closes around the lump now sitting there. “Natasha was talking shit- making comments about our-” her voice catches, and she swallows before correcting herself. “Agatha’s baby. That she lost.”

The officer’s pen pauses mid-scratch. “Agatha who?”

Rio clears her throat, digging her nails into her palm hard enough to almost break skin.

“Agatha Harkness.” Her voice is barely audible.

The pen starts moving again as the officer scribbles down the name.

Rio watches the motion carefully. Guilt blooms deep in her stomach, the feeling almost unbearable.

He’s a cop, she reminds herself, she’s the RA.

She doesn’t have a choice.

Right?

“What next?” the officer presses.

Rio’s head spins. The noise in the common room- muffled student voices, someone crying, the distant hum of the vending machine- all of it blurs together until it’s just a painful ringing in her ears.

“Agatha punched her.”

The officer looks up, waiting for more.

Rio forces air into her lungs before speaking again. “Natasha called her a bitch and shoved her back pretty hard.” Her voice shakes despite her best effort to keep it steady. “Then the next second Agatha was on top of her and everyone was shouting until I pulled her away.” She finally looks up at him fully, eyes glassy and wide. “Please don’t get her in trouble. Please.”

He doesn’t even acknowledge her plea.

“And where is Agatha now?” He asks.

“I-” Rio starts before turning instinctively, scanning the room, and her heart drops low in her chest.

Agatha is standing across the common room, half-hidden in the corner near the tattered couch. Her arms are wrapped tight around herself like she’s trying to hold all herself together. She looks so small, like she could break any second- nothing like the girl who’d been swinging fists five minutes ago.

Their eyes meet.

Tears streak silently down Agatha’s face, but her gaze hardens when she sees Rio. A look of complete and utter betrayal flashes across her face.

Rio’s breath stutters as she registers the change. Her mouth opens, then closes again almost immediately. She tries to push every ounce of apology and guilt she has through her expression alone.

I’m sorry.
I didn’t mean to.

Please don’t look at me like that.

She doesn’t have to answer the officer- he follows her line of sight immediately and nods.

“Thank you for your cooperation, Rio.”

The words feel like a slap.

Rio watches in slow-motion as he crosses the room and the officer moves in beside him. They speak to Agatha in low voices that Rio can’t quite make out. Agatha nods once, stiff and mechanical, like her mind is already somewhere else.

Then the click of metal fills the room, ringing through Rio’s ears.

Rio’s heart drops into her stomach as the cuffs snap around Agatha’s wrists.

Agatha flinches, and it breaks something in Rio entirely.

Rio moves before she can think twice. “Agatha!” The name tears out of her throat. She stumbles forward, finally breaking free of the frozen haze she was trapped in. “Agatha!”

One of the officers steps neatly into her path, his palm out to stop her abruptly.

“Ma’am, please step back,” he says, voice firm and bored. “We’re going to have to take her into custody at this time. It’s a misdemeanor charge, so you’ll be able to post bail once she’s processed.”

The elevator doors slide open with a soft ding.

Rio barely hears him- her eyes are locked on Agatha as they guide her forward.

“I’m so sorry, mi amor,” Rio whispers, words breaking apart as they leave her mouth. She shakes her head helplessly as tears spill down her cheeks.

Agatha doesn’t respond as she looks up to meet Rio’s eyes.

Instead, her gaze just turns harder, void of any ounce of warmth.

And the doors slide shut.

“You think I have a spare five hundred dollars to spend?” Rio yells, voice echoing loudly off the cold station walls. “I’m a broke college student, for god’s sake!”

The officer behind the desk pinches the bridge of his nose in annoyance. Rio’s been pacing in front of him for twenty minutes straight, trying to find any other way out of this.

“I’m sorry, ma’am, but that’s the bail price.” He sighs, leaning back in his chair. “Is there someone else you can call? A parent maybe? Her mom?”

“No.” The word comes out fast and sharp.

The officer’s eyebrows lift.

Rio forces herself to breathe. “No,” she repeats, quieter now. Her fingers curl into the fabric of her hoodie. “We don’t need to get her mom involved.”

Agatha would never forgive her.

She drags in a slow breath, running her hands over her tired face.

“I’ll pay.” She finally concedes.

“You’re sure?” he asks, doubt written all over his face.

Rio nods, even though her stomach is already twisting at the thought of her bank account.

“Yeah,” she says hoarsely. “It’s fine. I’ll figure it out. Just-” Her voice cracks. “Just let her out.”

Rio stands outside the station, a dying cigarette balanced between her fingers as she takes a slow drag, smoke leaving her lips in an unsteady stream. Her head turns on instinct when the door creaks open behind her. Agatha steps out, arms wrapped tight around herself as she’s hit by the cool night air.

“Hey,” Rio says, swallowing hard as she turns fully toward her. She flicks the cigarette to the concrete and the spark fizzles out on the cold pavement. Agatha’s eyes skim over her, briefly, before she walks straight past, her jaw set. “Agatha-” Rio starts, moving after her, but Agatha spins around sharply, stopping her in her path.

“Don’t.” The word is stripped of any warmth, though her voice trembles just slightly.

“Agatha, I’m so sorry, I didn’t have a choice.” Rio steps forward again, voice breaking as Agatha lets out a hollow, disbelieving laugh.

“Didn’t have a choice?” Agatha repeats, her voice rising.

“It was a cop, Agatha. We were all underage drinking, everyone there witnessed the fight.” Rio rambles, tilting her head, eyes wide and pleading for forgiveness. “…I was just doing my job.”

“Bullshit,” Agatha spits, pure disdain dripping from the word. “I don’t remember ‘ratting out your ex-girlfriend to the police’ being a part of your job description.”

The word ‘ex’ hits Rio square in the chest.

Rationally, she knows.

She knows that they’re not together- that they haven’t been for months.

Agatha made that painfully clear.

But hearing it out loud for the first time?

It fucking wrecks her.

Tears flood her eyes instantly. She takes a shaky step forward, hand reaching out before she can stop herself, her muscle memory kicking in.

“Please, my love,” she whispers.

Agatha steps back immediately.

“I’m done.”

Rio grabs her wrist on instinct. “Agatha, it was a mistake,” she pleads, voice cracking wide open. “An honest mistake. But I paid for bail… I-I’ll convince Natasha not to press charges, we’ll figure this out. Nothing bad will happen.”

Agatha goes still.

“Nothing bad will happen?” she says slowly, her voice low and dangerous. She yanks her wrist free. “Are you fucking for real right now? Do you know how many times you’ve told me that?”

Rio’s stomach drops.

“This is still on my record, Rio,” Agatha continues, voice shaking with anger. “Do you know what this could do to my law school applications?”

Shit.

Rio’s mouth opens, her throat suddenly dry. No words come out.

Agatha laughs again, humorlessly. “My chances are shot. My future- my dream- is basically out the window. Thanks to you.”

Rio’s heart constricts painfully in her chest.

“Agatha, I am so, so sor-”

“Don’t bother.”

The words slice clean through her. Her breath catches.

“Maybe there’s still a chance,” Rio tries weakly. “You never know-”

“There’s no chance. It’s over.”

“Please let me make it up to you.” Rio’s voice breaks as she reaches for her again, more careful this time. “What can I do? Just- tell me what to do.”

Agatha yanks her arm away forcefully.

“Let me go.”

Her voice is empty now, the anger dissipating to pure numbness.

“Stop making my life hell.”

Rio’s hand drops limply to her side.

She opens her mouth- to argue, to beg, to say anything that might fix all this- but nothing comes out.

Agatha takes a shaky breath, then turns and starts down the sidewalk without a goodbye, her shoulders locked and rigid.

Rio’s feet stay rooted to the concrete, waiting for Agatha to stop, to turn around, to hesitate- anything.

She doesn’t.

Rio’s hand flies to her mouth, muffling the broken sob that finally tears free as her whole body starts to shake.

The streetlight flickers overhead, filling the street with momentary shadows.

And Rio stands there, frozen in place.

Alone.

2026

Saturday

Rio pushes open her front door, shoulder hitting the wood frame a little harder than she means to. The hinges creak as she stumbles through the entrance, kicking the door shut behind her with the heel of her boot.

Her bag slips from her fingers and lands on the floor with a dull thud. She shrugs out of her jacket next, her movements loose and slightly off, and lets it fall wherever it wants.

Through the front window, she watches the Uber’s headlights disappear down the street, red taillights shrinking until they vanish completely, taking Agatha and Jen with them.

The house is cast into dark shadows. Rio swallows as she stands there, everything a little too quiet now that she’s alone.

Her stomach churns, heavy with the night’s liquor. The dinner she forced herself to eat earlier is still sitting there, large and unmoving- or at least, that’s what it feels like.

Her hand drifts to her abdomen without thinking as she glances down.

Her stomach looks a little too bloated, a little too round for her liking.

Rio’s breath catches in her throat and her brow furrows hard as a familiar wave of anxiety creeps up her spine. Her fingers curl slightly against the fabric of her shirt.

Before she can think too hard about it, she’s moving- feet quick and uneven as she heads for the stairs. She nearly misses the first step, catching herself on the railing with a sharp exhale before rushing the rest of the way up.

The bathroom light flicks on brightly after a few fumbling attempts to find the switch.

Rio steps in front of the mirror and yanks her shirt up, turning sideways.

She stares, her chest rising and falling a little too fast.

She shouldn’t have eaten earlier.

Or she shouldn’t have drank so much.

Especially not the cosmopolitan Agatha had somehow talked her into getting, her voice coaxing in Rio’s ear.

She was supposed to stick to vodka sodas.

Rio’s fingers dig into her palm hard enough to sting as she keeps staring at her reflection. Like if she looks long enough something might fix itself, like her brain might suddenly rewire.

It doesn’t.

Her vision starts to blur at the edges.

You’re okay, she tells herself quickly. You’re fine. It’s just a small setback.

The words feel fake.

She doesn’t believe them.

Panic spikes sharp in her chest, and suddenly she’s moving again, dropping automatically to her knees in front of the toilet before she can give herself time to hesitate.

Her breath comes out shaky.

This’ll help. This’ll cancel it out.

Rio squeezes her eyes shut and leans forward, shoulders tense. She sticks two fingers into her mouth, hitting deep into the back of her throat, causing her to retch.

A fresh slate.

Her body jerks forward, harsh and uncomfortable, as the liquid from her stomach finally comes up, splashing into the bowl in front of her. Her other hand grips the edge of the porcelain, knuckles pale.

Again.

Rio inhales sharply through her nose, chest heaving.

Fingers back in.

Repeat.

The motions are familiar and automatic- like muscle memory has taken over where her brain has checked out.

The liquid thins, coming up in smaller amounts.

Her throat burns now, raw and scratchy, but the anxious buzzing under her skin hasn’t fully settled yet.

Just one more.

One more and it’ll be enough.

Rio squeezes her eyes shut and leans forward again, stomach clenching hard in attempt to expel what little contents are left.

Her body finally gives up.

She slumps sideways against the toilet seat, cheek pressed to her forearm, her fingers still loosely curled against the cool porcelain. Her breathing is slow as she tries to ignore the hollow, aching protest in her stomach.

After a minute or two she pushes herself upright, her legs shaking slightly as she stands. She flushes, watches the water swirl away, then turns back to the mirror.

Her eyes are red and glassy- her mascara faintly smudged beneath them. Loose strands of hair have fallen messily around her face, sticking slightly to the sweat on her skin.

Rio studies herself for a moment, shoulders dropping as the tension fades away.

It’s almost… pretty.

Proof she fixed it. Proof she did something right.

The clawing thoughts in her brain quiet just a little.

She turns off the bathroom light and drifts back down the hall toward her bedroom, movements slower now. Her club clothes hit the floor in a soft pile. She reaches for her worn pajama pants and the oversized Fleetwood Mac t-shirt draped over the back of her chair.

She pulls it over her head, the soft fabric falling over her comfortably.

Rio lifts the collar to her nose and inhales deeply, making her chest tighten.

It still smells like Agatha.

Faint now, but it’s there.

She’d pulled it straight from the laundry basket after Agatha left last week, carefully setting it aside before anything else could touch it- before the scent could fade.

Rio’s lips curl into a smile instinctively.

Her mind drifts back to the bathroom at the club- to warm hands and soft laughter and the dizzying, electric press of Agatha’s mouth against hers. Her stomach flips.

Rio crawls into bed and pulls the blankets up to her chin, curling slightly onto her side. The room is quiet except for the soft hum of the heater and the steady sound of her breath.

For the first time all night, her aching thoughts fade away- and with the scent of Agatha overwhelming her nose, Rio lets herself drift into a comforting, deep sleep.

Sunday

Rio wakes early, the light of morning sun already leaking through the edges of her curtains. For a moment she just lies there, staring at the ceiling, body heavy and warm under the blankets.

If she stays, she knows what’ll happen.

She’ll roll over and open her phone and suddenly three hours will be gone.

With a soft groan, Rio forces herself upright, pushing the blankets off quickly. Her stiff muscles protest the movement, but she swings her legs over the side of the bed anyway.

It’s Sunday.

Which means she has shit to do.

She pulls on a pair of old jeans from the floor, not bothering to change out of the oversized Fleetwood Mac shirt still hanging loose around her frame. Downstairs, the house is quiet in the way that makes every little sound feel too loud. The hardwood creaks under her feet as she pads into the kitchen.

Rio moves instinctively, filling the coffee maker and pressing the button to make the machine sputter to life.

She leans back against the counter, arms folding loosely across her stomach as the dark liquid begins its steady drip into the mug, the smell bitter smell hitting her immediately.

Her eyes are still half-closed when the knock echoes through the kitchen.

Rio frowns immediately, pushing off the counter. Who the hell?

She leaves her coffee mug untouched and heads for the front door, rubbing sleep from the corner of her eye as she pulls it open.

She freezes.

Agatha stands on her porch, looking just as tired, a duffel bag slung over one shoulder.

Rio blinks at her, her brain still trying to catch up to what she’s seeing.

Her mouth opens, ready to ask why the hell Agatha Harkness is on her doorstep at eight in the morning, but Agatha beats her to it.

“Hey.”

The word comes out quietly with a soft, crooked smirk that doesn’t quite reach her eyes.

Rio’s brows lift slowly. “Hey…” Her gaze drifts deliberately from Agatha’s messy hair down to the duffel bag, then back up again. “…Why are you on my porch?”

Agatha exhales through her nose, shifting the bag slightly higher on her shoulder.

“Can I stay here?” she asks, voice quieter now. She lifts the duffel up a little, in case Rio somehow hadn’t noticed it. “Just for a couple days.”

Rio’s heart skips a beat, her answer coming out way too fast.

“Of course,” she says immediately, already stepping back to make room for Agatha to enter. Then she asks more carefully, “are you okay?”

Agatha’s mouth twists.

“Yeah, just… Ralph’s a fucking dick.” She shakes her head, but there’s no anger behind it- more just exhaustion. “I got a whole lecture for coming home late last night and waking him up by ‘opening the door too loud.’”

Her eyes drop to the wood of the porch as she takes a small pause before continuing.

“I just…” Agatha swallows. “I can’t be in that house right now.” She shifts her weight, seemingly hesitant with her words. “I move into my new apartment Wednesday, so I’ll be out of your hair in no time.”

Something tightens in Rio’s chest. She steps back fully, opening the door wider.

“Stay as long as you need,” Rio says gently. “I don’t mind.”

Agatha finally looks up at that- then she smiles- small, but real this time.

“Thank you.”

Rio shuts the door behind her as Agatha steps inside, the familiar sound of the lock clicking into place feeling strangely loud in the quiet house.

She gestures toward the staircase. “You can set up in the guest room.”

Agatha stills for half a second. Rio catches it- the flicker of something unreadable crossing her face. Agatha studies her a beat too long, then looks away.

“Right,” she says, voice carefully neutral. “Thanks.”

Rio watches her head up the stairs, duffel bumping softly against her hip with each step until she disappears down the hall toward the guest room.

Rio lingers in the foyer for a second, faintly puzzled.

But she shakes it off and heads back into the kitchen, grabbing a second mug and starting the coffee maker again. The machine has just begun to drip when footsteps sound on the stairs.

Rio glances up.

Agatha reappears, already looking more settled but just as exhausted, and drops onto one of the breakfast bar stools with a quiet huff. She drags a hand down her face, blinking slow.

Rio turns, leaning her hip against the counter.

“Coffee?”

Agatha’s head snaps up immediately. She nods hard, eyes lighting up desperately.

“Fuck, yes please.”

Rio grabs the already-full cup and slides it across the counter toward Agatha. She takes her own seat diagonal from her, close enough to feel the warmth of her presence, causing a faint blush to rise in her cheeks.

Agatha speaks. “You’re sure you don’t mind my being here?” She asks, raising a brow. “I would’ve stayed with Jen, but now that she’s moved in with Alice I felt kind of weird.”

Rio nods with a small smirk. “Yeah, you don’t wanna be third wheeling.”

“Or have to hear them fucking at 1 a.m. from the room over.” Agatha adds pointedly, causing Rio to stifle a laugh.

“I promise, I don’t mind.” Rio answers gently, before taking a breath and studying her face. “You sure you’re doing okay?”

Agatha wraps both hands around the mug as she nods slowly. “I’ll be fine,” she says, her voice rough around the edges. She lifts the coffee and takes a careful sip before grimacing slightly. “Once I get past this hangover.”

She exhales hard into her hands, dragging them down her face. Then her eyes flick back up, narrowing slightly.

“How are you fine?” she asks. “You drank just as much as I did.”

Rio’s stomach twists sharply. For half a second, her fingers tighten around her mug. She swallows, throat suddenly dry.

“Uh-” She clears her throat. “I threw up when I got home.”

Agatha’s brows pull together, pausing slightly.

“Lucky,” she finally mutters, dropping her forehead briefly toward the counter. “I swear to God I’m never drinking again.”

Rio snorts, throwing her head back slightly. “We’ll see about that.”

Agatha huffs weakly into her coffee but doesn’t argue. They fall into an easy silence after that- the comfortable, peaceful kind. Steam curls from their mugs as the clock ticks softly on the wall.

Rio tries very hard not to stare- not to make it obvious how much she’s replaying last night in her head, over and over again.

By the time their cups are empty, some of the gray has faded from Agatha’s face. She still looks tired, but at least some life has been brought back into her.

Rio pushes back from her stool and stands.

“Come on,” she says, already moving toward the door. She tilts her head in a silent command.

Agatha blinks at her. “What?”

“We’re going on a walk,” Rio says simply, grabbing her jacket from the hook.

Agatha lets out an offended scoff. “Absolutely not.”

Rio just smirks as she shrugs into her sleeves. “It’ll make you feel better. Exercise is good for hangovers.”

She pulls the door open and glances back over her shoulder, eyes glinting.

“Plus,” she adds sweetly, “I’m letting you crash here, so technically you have to do what I say.”

Agatha groans at the statement, knowing she’s not getting out of this one. With all the dramaticism she can muster, she shoves herself off the stool and stomps over, yanking a random jacket off the wall hook and shoving her arms through it.

Rio bites the inside of her cheek to keep from laughing.

She holds the door open with a small, teasing smile. “After you.”

Agatha shoots her a sharp glare on the way past, but there’s no real heat behind it. She pulls the jacket tight around herself as she heads down the front steps toward the sidewalk.

Rio locks the door quickly and jogs a few steps to catch up.

The morning air is cool and refreshing, the neighborhood still quiet except for a few birds chirping loudly from somewhere in the trees. The sun is bright, stretching long shadows across the pavement as they fall into step beside each other.

Rio glances sideways.

Agatha is scowling. Hard.

Rio lets out a laugh, shaking her head. “You’re so dramatic.”

“I am not,” Agatha snaps immediately, shooting her a narrow-eyed look before facing forward again.

Then Agatha speeds up abruptly.

Rio’s brows knit together as she watches her stride ahead a few quick steps.

Agatha suddenly bends down, and Rio slows, keeping her eyes locked on her movements.

Agatha snatches up a crushed soda can from the edge of the sidewalk and in one swift motion, tosses it neatly into a trash can a few feet ahead.

Rio stops walking entirely, her mouth falling open.

Agatha turns back toward her, frowning slightly. “What?”

Rio stares. “You just picked up trash,” she says flatly. “Willingly.”

Agatha blinks once, then glances back at the trash can like she’s only just now processing what she did. She shrugs one shoulder.

“I guess I did.”

Rio steps forward again, grin spreading slow across her face as she pokes Agatha lightly in the arm.

“Look at you.”

Agatha immediately swats her hand away, but she pointedly looks anywhere but at Rio now. There’s the faintest twitch of a smirk at the corner of her mouth.

“Stop,” she mutters softly.

Rio crosses her arms, smile widening. “Agatha Harkness,” she says in an exaggerated whisper, “do you care about the planet?”

“Mhm,” Rio hums, deeply unconvinced.

2007

“I’m sorry, but the decision is final.”

The words echo through Agatha’s head, her throat suddenly dry.

“No- no, it can’t be.” Agatha leans forward in her chair, her fingers curling tightly around the edge of the desk, her knuckles flexed and white. “There’s got to be something we can do, right? Some sort of compromise?”

Her heart is already racing, panic rising fast in her chest, but she keeps her voice as steady as she can manage. She can’t let them see that she’s vulnerable, that she’s about to have a complete and utter breakdown.

“Unfortunately, it’s not up to me.” The counselor gives a small, practiced shake of her head as her eyes brush over Agatha. “We have a strict student code of conduct here, and your actions were in direct violation of it. Your financial aid offer was conditional, Agatha.”

Her stomach drops even lower, the final sliver of hope she had left fading away completely.

The look in the counselor’s eyes is almost pitying, but her voice stays flat and emotionless as she begins straightening the stack of papers on her desk, if only to busy her hands so she doesn’t have to meet Agatha’s gaze.

“These,” she adds, moving on to reorganize her pencil holder, “are the consequences of your behavior, I’m afraid.”

Agatha’s throat tightens painfully as tears well behind her eyes.

Pull yourself together, Agatha.

She blinks fast, willing the tears back, because crying in front of this woman would be her absolute final straw.

“How am I supposed to pay for college without my scholarship?” Her voice comes out more fragile than she intends, shaking slightly at the edges. She swallows hard, her jaw clenching. “You’re basically telling me I can’t stay.”

For a brief second, something kind of like sympathy flickers across the counselor’s face, but it’s gone almost immediately.

“You can always take out loans.”

Agatha lets out a short, humorless laugh. “And be in crippling debt for the rest of my life?”

The panic in her chest is overwhelming now, pressing hard against her ribs, the feeling completely suffocating. She can barely take in a breath.

“I’m a college counselor, Agatha, not a financial advisor.” The old woman’s tone sharpens just slightly. She studies Agatha’s face for a moment longer this time, her expression shifting just an inch as she takes in the way Agatha’s hands tremble in her lap.

The counselor sighs, quieter now.

“What about your mom?” she asks softly. “She was covering the fees originally, right?”

The question hits Agatha hard, her jaw locking so tight that it begins to ache. A familiar heat floods her chest, panic rising quickly at the mention of Evanora.

“My mom and I don’t talk anymore,” she spits, the words coming out sharper than she intends. She leans back in her chair and crosses her arms tightly over her chest, frowning at the counselor as if she was supposed to know. Silence stretches across the small office, thick and unbearably uncomfortable. For a second, Agatha thinks she might reconsider- or at least offer any piece of helpful advice.

“Well,” the woman says instead, pushing back from her desk and standing before smoothing down the front of her blazer, “I’d advise fixing that.”

Agatha’s chest goes tight. Her vision blurs again, this time much harder to fight off. The counselor moves to the door and pulls it open, holding it wide in a polite but unmistakable signal that the meeting is over.

Agatha stays frozen in her chair for half a second, staring at the file on the desk, like maybe if she refuses to leave the room then this won’t be real. Or maybe someone will walk in and help her, finally taking her side.

No one does.

Her throat burns as she forces herself to stand, her legs unsteady beneath her. She shuffles toward the exit, her shoulders stiff and awkward as she tries to hold together the last of her composure.

“Otherwise,” the counselor finishes, already halfway back to her desk, “I’m afraid you’re out of options.”

Agatha taps her fingers against the wood of the table in an anxious, frantic rhythm, matching the pounding of her heartbeat filling her ears. Every few seconds she glances up at the door of the coffee shop, eyes flicking toward it on instinct. Each time it stays closed, her racing pulse slows just slightly, relief rushing through her for a beat until the sense of impending doom returns.

She takes a shaky sip of her coffee, the bitter liquid sliding hot down her throat. It does nothing to settle the knot twisting painfully in her stomach- in fact, it makes the nausea even worse. Her hands feel cold despite the warmth of the mug between them as she sets it back down on the table.

Maybe she won’t come, Agatha thinks for a brief second.

But of course she will. Evanora Harkness never ignores an opportunity to make her feel small.

The bell above the café door suddenly dings, the sharp sound slicing through the chatter of surrounding customers, catching Agatha’s attention and making her head snap up immediately.

Her gaze lands on the bony, grey-haired woman now standing stiffly in the entrance. Evanora’s posture is perfectly straight, her coat draped neatly over her shoulders as her sharp glare sweeps across the room.

It’s a terrifying sight.

For a second, Agatha considers ducking under the table.

But before she has the opportunity, Evanora’s gaze finds hers. Agatha gulps, trying to mentally prepare herself for what’s about to come. Evanora begins walking over, her steps quick and stiff, the heels of her shoes clicking loudly against the linoleum tiles. Her face is completely flat- no smile, no hint of warmth, not even a single acknowledgement of Agatha’s presence.

She reaches the table and slides into the seat across from Agatha without a word, placing her purse neatly on the floor beside her chair.

“Mother,” Agatha says, nodding hello.

She tries to make her voice sound steady, show that she’s not intimidated, but the word comes out a shaky whisper. She swallows hard, forcing the next words out of her suddenly dry throat. “Thank you for meeting me.”

Evanora’s expression doesn’t shift even slightly.

“What do you want, Agatha?” she asks flatly.

Agatha’s stomach twists. She begins picking at the skin around her fingernails, a nervous habit she’s still not been able to break. Her fingers sting from how hard she’s been digging into them, the past wounds not even fully scabbed over yet.

“You were right, mother,” she whispers. The words taste fucking awful coming out of her mouth.

“What was that?” Evanora questions, now seemingly intrigued.

Agatha forces herself to say it again, even though every part of her brain begs not to.

“You were right,” she repeats, her voice trembling. “I messed up. I sinned.”

Evanora scoffs quietly, leaning back in her chair. “I’m glad you finally came to your senses.”

Something uneasy surges through Agatha’s chest, but she pushes it down quickly, following the script she’d prepared on the way over.

“I can make it right,” she says quickly. “I will make it right.”

These are the consequences of your behavior, I’m afraid.

Her throat goes dry.

I’m afraid you’re out of options.

The shift in Evanora’s expression is immediate. The smugness fades away, replaced by pure disdain, her lips forming a scowl.

“That’s what this is about?” Evanora’s voice sharpens, rising just enough that a few nearby customers glance over.

Agatha flinches instinctively. “No, mother-”

“You’re just after my money?” Evanora cuts her off, her voice laced with disgust. “Bad behavior is not rewarded, Agatha.”

“No.” Agatha shakes her head quickly, panic rising in her throat. Tears blur her vision as her lip begins to tremble. “Please, I can be good.” Her voice cracks completely now as tears spill down her cheeks. “I can be good,” she repeats desperately, the words barely a whisper.

Evanora watches her without sympathy.

“No,” she says calmly. “You cannot.”

The words hit hard, Agatha’s chest dropping. She stares down at her hands, her nails now having broken the skin around her fingers. Her voice comes out barely audible. “Why do you hate me still?”

Evanora doesn’t hesitate. “There’s an evil in you, Agatha,” she says coldly. “Marrying that boy doesn’t change that.”

Agatha’s chest tightens painfully. “But I’ll repent,” she whispers. “I’ll do better. I promise, I’ll be better.”

For a long moment Evanora says nothing. Agatha searches her face, stupidly hoping for even a flicker of warmth.

There is none.

Her mother exhales slowly. “Okay.”

Agatha’s head snaps up. “Okay?” she stammers.

“Okay,” Evanora repeats, her voice flat. “You marry him, and I’ll pay your tuition.”

The relief that floods Agatha’s chest is so intense it almost makes her dizzy.

“Thank you, mother,” she breathes, the words tumbling out quickly. She forces a weak smile even though her face is still wet with tears.

Evanora stands, picking up her purse with smooth deliberation. She takes a step away from the table before pausing. Slowly, she turns her head over her shoulder. “But one more mistake, Agatha,” she says, her voice calm and unwavering, “and you’re done. For good.”

The threat hangs heavy in the air between them.

“So make this one count.”

She walks out of the coffee shop without looking back, the bell above the door ringing again as it closes behind her.

Agatha sits there for several seconds, frozen in place.

Only when her mother disappears completely down the street does the tightness around her lungs finally release. She exhales shakily, taking in her first real breath since the conversation started.

Her hands tremble as she digs through her bag, her phone nearly slipping from her fingers when she pulls it out.

She bites the inside of her cheek hard as she punches in the number, each ring echoing through her ear. After a few seconds, the line clicks.

Ralph’s voice comes through the phone, low and rough in a way that makes her stomach immediately twist with nausea.

“Agatha?” Her foot begins tapping anxiously against the floor. “Hello?” He says again.

Agatha closes her eyes for a moment before she finally forces herself to speak.

“Hey,” she says quietly. Her throat feels completely dry. “Do you wanna go out?”

Her touch is soft but purposeful, and Agatha tilts her head to the side to give her better access. Rio’s mouth is warm against her skin. Her head falls back completely as Rio’s teeth graze lightly along her neck, a breathy groan slipping out in response. Rio’s hands guide her hips gently, moving them together in a slow rhythm. Agatha’s brain is completely gone now, barely able to concentrate on a single thought.

A flash of lightening fills the room, followed by a loud crash from outside.

Agatha jumps violently, suddenly snapped from the trance as her hand flies to her chest.

“Jesus Christ.” Rio blurts, both of them spinning toward the window. “What was that?” She frowns.

Agatha reluctantly slides off Rio’s lap as Rio stands quickly, rushing toward the window. Agatha follows closely behind, peering out of the rain-streaked glass. A nearby tree lay uprooted across the lawn, its branches scattered everywhere, the trunk just barely missing the side of house.

“Shit,” Agatha mutters quietly, looking at the mess outside, the wind howling loudly now, slightly bending the remaining trees. “They weren’t kidding about this storm.”

Rio shakes her head slowly. “No, they were not.” She glances down at her watch. “It’s getting late,” she says. “We should go to bed.”

Agatha nods. She tries not to let the disappointment show on her face. “Okay.”

Agatha gathers the empty wine glasses from the table, carrying them back to the kitchen while Rio shuts off the TV. The house falls into a silence again. They walk upstairs together, side by side, their arms brushing slightly. When they reach the hallway, Agatha steps toward the guest room. She hesitates. Her hand rests on the doorknob as she turns slightly back toward Rio.

Rio tilts her head just a little. “Well… goodnight, Agatha.”

Agatha masks her disappointment, pausing before forcing a soft smile. “Goodnight, Rio.”

She pushes the door open and slips into the room, climbing under the cold covers of the guest bed. She rolls onto her side, squeezing her eyes shut as she pulls the blanket up around her shoulders, trying not to think about the fact that she has to get up for work in the morning.

She flips onto her back, her brain wide awake and racing with thoughts of Rio. Agatha exhales slowly, focusing instead on the sound of the rain on the roof above her, hoping the steady rhythm will lull her into a sleep.

Suddenly, her ears catch a noise from outside in the hall, the soft movement of light feet across the carpet. She hears the faint flick of the bathroom light in the room next to hers, followed by the careful, slow sound of a door trying to be shut quietly. A couple of second later, her eyes flicker open at the sound of retching, the noise quiet but unmistakable in the otherwise silent house.

She props herself up on her elbows, her brows furrowing as her ears strain to pinpoint the sound, the heart beginning to beat just a little faster.

Maybe she’s mistaken?

The noise comes again, clearer this time and more strained.

Is Rio throwing up?

Unease settles heavy in her chest as she pushes the covers off and climbs out of bed, the floor cold under her feet as she walks quickly out of her room and into the hallway. She makes her way to the bathroom door and raises her fist, giving a light knock against the wood.

“Rio?” She calls softly.

“Hm?” She hears from the other side.

“Are you okay in there?” She asks, her voice betraying her slightly as it wavers. “Are you sick?”

She hears the toilet flush, then the rush of the faucet turning on, water running for a moment too long before it abruptly stops. The door suddenly swings open, making her flinch slightly. Rio stands there with one hand braced against the wooden frame, her posture tense and jaw tight. Her eyes are red and glassy and her lip trembles just slightly before she presses her mouth into a hard line.

“What?” Rio says.

Agatha’s breath hitches, and she hesitates for a second before speaking, her voice coming out soft. “Were you throwing up?”

Rio scoffs immediately, but Agatha catches it- that split second of panic that flashes across her face as if she’s been caught doing something she wasn’t supposed to. “No.” She says, her voice flat, a hint of what seems to be warning behind it, as if to say don’t do this.

“I just thought I heard-”

She’s cut off by Rio brushing past her, shoulder knocking lightly into hers as she moves down the hall. “Go back to bed, Agatha.” The words are final. Rio disappears into her room and shuts the door before Agatha even has the chance to respond, the soft click echoing through the quiet hallway. Agatha stands there for a moment, completely still, her arms hanging uselessly at her sides as her chest tightens.

Rio can deny it all she wants, but Agatha knows what she heard- knows what she saw written all over her face the second that door opened.

Her stomach churns with unease as the realization settles deep in her chest.

Of course, she knew Rio struggled with food... but not like this, not to this degree. She curses herself silently for being so fucking oblivious.

Rio’s not okay.

Not even close.

Monday

Agatha wakes before her alarm, the soft morning light pouring through the curtains and settling across the room. She groans under her breath as she rolls onto her side, her eyes puffy and heavy from a night spent tossing and turning, kept up by thoughts of Rio.

She exhales slowly, dragging a hand down her face as she reaches for her phone, squinting at the screen while she checks her emails- an unwelcome reminder that she has work waiting for her in just a couple of hours.

Fuck Mondays.

She pushes herself out of bed, movements slow and groggy as she makes her way downstairs, one hand gliding along the wall for balance. Her eyes barely stay open as she practically drags herself to the coffee machine. Somehow, she manages to get a pot brewing and leans back against the counter, arms loosely crossed as she watches the dark liquid drip steadily into the mug she’d grabbed from the cabinet.

Agatha’s lips twitch as she notices the design on the side- a cartoon turtle holding a lesbian flag. She lets out a quiet laugh, reading the text printed beneath it.

What do lesbians and sea turtles have in common?
They both choke on plastic.

Her chest warms as she stares at it for a second longer than necessary.

It’s so Rio.

“Morning.”

Agatha startles, her head snapping up at the sudden voice, heart jumping slightly as her gaze falls on Rio standing in the doorway of the kitchen, already dressed and ready for the day.

“Hey,” she responds, her voice soft as she offers a small smile. She’s suddenly very aware of the lingering tension between them after last night.

Rio gaze flicks to the coffee machine, and the now full mug sitting beneath it. “I see you found my favorite mug.” She says, raising an eyebrow as she looks back at Agatha, a faint smirk tugging at her lips. “Didn’t brew me a cup then?”

Agatha’s face goes hot. “Sorry, I didn’t know you were up yet.”

Rio’s already moving, grabbing another mug from the cabinet with ease. She glances over her shoulder, her expression softening. “I’m joking, Agatha, it’s fine.”

Agatha nods, looking down at her hands as she picks at the skin around her nails again. “Right.”

A heavy silence settles between them as Rio waits for her coffee, the only sound being the quiet drip of liquid, the low whir of the machine, and the soft rain still falling outside. Rio turns, leaning back against the counter as she waits, her voice breaking through the quiet.

“I’m heading to the park with some of the other volunteers.” She says. “We want to check for any damage and clear up the debris from the storm last night.” She pauses for just a second, like she’s debating whether or not to add more. “If you’d wanna come with me.”

Agatha looks up, offering a weary smile. “I have to go into the office.”

Rio nods immediately. “Of course.” She turns back to her coffee, taking a sip before checking the time and pouring it into a to-go cup, her movements quick and efficient. She grabs her coat from the back of the chair and heads toward the door, Agatha tracking her movements closely without meaning to.

“You’re not eating breakfast?” The question slips out before Agatha can even think about it.

Rio pauses mid-step, her hand hovering just inches from the doorknob as she slowly turns back toward her. “I’ll stop for something on the way,” she says, her tone smooth and practiced.

Agatha studies her for a moment, chewing lightly on the inside of her cheek. “On second thought,” she says, pushing off the counter and abandoning her coffee as she crosses the room toward her, “I think I’ll come with you.”

Rio’s brow lifts slightly. “I thought you had work.”

“I’m the boss,” Agatha shrugs, “I can do what I want.”

Rio watches her now, her eyes narrow and calculating. After a second, she nods slowly. “Okay.”

Whatever enthusiasm was there earlier is gone as she pulls open the door, stepping outside with Agatha following close behind.

The drive to the park is tense, the air in the car thick with unspoken words. The only break in the silence comes at the Dunkin drive-thru, where Agatha has to remind Rio- twice- that she said she’d get food. Agatha ended up picking the place and the food for the both of them, seeing as Rio was adamant on eating the same meal as Agatha.

Now Rio drives one handed as she bites into the breakfast sandwich, her chewing slow and meticulous. Agatha watches her movements more closely than she should, noticing the way she chews much too deliberately, the way her left foot bounces against the floor of the car anxiously as she drives.

The rain has eased off into a light mist by the time they reach the park, leaving everything coated in a dull, grey haze. The sky hangs low and heavy, making the entire place feel muted and lifeless. Rio pulls into the parking lot, and they step out of the car to find a small group already gathered.

Rio walks over immediately as she begins to converse with a group of volunteers. Agatha follows a step behind, offering small smiles to Alice and Lilia when she reaches them- even to Wanda when she joins a moment later.

Wanda leans in slightly. “Since when do you and Rio carpool?” She asks, her voice a low whisper.

“Since today,” Agatha replies, not offering anything more.

Wanda frowns. “Why-”

“Thank you all for coming so last minute,” Rio cuts in, her voice clear and projecting as she shifts seamlessly into organizer mode. “I appreciate the effort, especially on such a dreary Monday.” Agatha glances at her, watching the way she carries herself with ease. “We’ll try and make it quick,” Rio continues. “Just scan for any damage- fallen trees, uprooted plants, anything like that- and clean up debris where you can.”

The group nods and begins to disperse.

Agatha looks around, taking in the aftermath of the storm- branches scattered across the grass, leaves clinging to wet ground, the occasional larger limb snapped clean off and lying where it fell. A few yards away, an uprooted tree blocks part of a dirt path.

Rio scans the area too. Agatha watches her closely.

She sees the moment her attention catches on something in the distance, far across the lawn towards the wooded area. Rio’s face changes instantly. Her expression drops, eyes narrowing as she focuses.

“No,” she breathes, barely audible, the word laced with some sort of panic. Before Agatha can even ask, Rio takes off running, her legs barely keeping her upright.

“Rio?” Agatha calls after her, her voice tightening with worry as she immediately runs after her, Alice and Wanda following close behind.

Rio has a head start, so by the time Agatha nears the tree line, Rio is already there, stopping to an abrupt halt as her legs give out beneath her, dropping to her knees in the dirt. Agatha’s chest twists with worry, a pain shooting through her chest at the sight.

She pushes forward faster, closing the distance as she watches Rio scramble with something in front of her, her hands digging into the soil, her shoulders shaking. When Agatha finally reaches her, her gaze drops to what Rio is desperately trying to fix- a large cluster of flowers, now uprooted from the soil- roots exposed and petals scattered across the ground.

Agatha frowns. She hadn’t paid much attention to them before, barely even registered they were here, but now notices how unnatural they look compared to the surrounding woods. They seem deliberately placed, as if they’d been planted.

She squints at them, trying to place what type of flowers they are.

They look so familiar.

Hibiscus? Hydrangeas?

Then it clicks.

Azaleas.

That’s how she knows them. Rio’s favorite flower.

Agatha remembers the way she talked about them that night, how excited she’d been, how she’d gone on and on and on until Agatha had gone home and looked them up just to try and understand the appeal.

“Rio?” Agatha says softly, lowering herself to her knees beside her. Rio has stopped trying to fix them now. Her hands are covered in mud, sitting uselessly in her lap as tears stream freely down her face. “Rio, honey, what’s wrong?”

Rio doesn’t answer. Her voice just breaks into a sob instead, her eyes locked on the ruined flowers in front of her. The sight just about breaks Agatha’s heart, her stomach twisting with nausea. She moves closer, wrapping her arms around Rio, pulling her in as she collapses against her, her face pressing into Agatha’s shoulder just as the others reach them. Agatha runs her fingers through her hair, holding her tightly.

Why she’s so upset about some flowers, Agatha doesn’t understand. But she doesn’t need to.

“It’s okay,” she murmurs softly.

Rio leans into Agatha’s chest as her tears soak through her shirt.

“Oh, Nicky…” Rio whispers, the words barely audible, “oh, my baby...”

Agatha stills. Her heart skips a beat at the name, her throat suddenly feeling like it’s closing up.

Nicky?

Her grip wavers just slightly.

What the hell is she talking about?

2007

Rio’s fist knocks sharply against the wooden door, the sound ringing out across the empty hall.

“Agatha?” She calls out, her voice echoing off the dorm walls. There’s no response. “Agatha, it’s Rio.” She tries again, stupidly, as if that would somehow be what gets her to open the door. Her fingers toy nervously with the edge of her graduation gown as she glances down at the floor. Her hair is curled perfectly, her makeup is caked on smooth, her cap is pinned neatly into place- everything is perfect.

Well, almost everything.

“Agatha, I know you don’t want to talk to me.” Her voice shakes. “I know you hate me, I just-” she sucks in a breath. “I couldn’t bring myself to leave without saying goodbye.”

There’s silence, causing her stomach to twist. She waits anyway, hopelessly.

“I- I don’t even know if you’re in there right now, but if you are…” her voice wavers, “please Agatha, please just open the door.”

Nothing. Absolutely nothing.

Rio turns her wrist, glancing down at the time on her watch.

She has to go. Graduation isn’t going to wait for her, and she knows her mother will never let her hear the end of it if she misses walking across that stage.

Her gaze drifts to the white, crisp envelope sitting in her hand. To the curled handwriting spelling out my love across the front. To Rio’s last hope. Her Hail Mary. Her pathetic, final attempt at an apology.

She swipes her thumb across the paper, tracing the dried water stains left behind by tears she tried not to let fall while writing it last night. Crumpled up pieces of paper still lay in the corner of her room, covered with desperate lines that weren’t anywhere near good enough for Agatha. Filled with hundreds of words strung together in paragraphs, all just trying different ways to say sorry.

Sorry.

As if sorry would ever fix any of this.

Slowly, she crouches down in front of the door, taking a deep breath. The fabric of her gown pools around her feet as she inches the envelope toward the thin crack beneath it. Her fingers hover there for a moment, preparing to push it through before she freezes, something inside her deflating completely.

What’s the point?

Agatha wants nothing to do with her. She made that clear countless times. Maybe it’s for the better if she just… goes quietly. Grabs her diploma and gets the hell out of here without looking back. Doesn’t force Agatha to read some stupid letter she probably doesn’t even want.

Maybe it’ll make this easier for the both of them.

It’s not like she’ll ever have to see her again.

At least, that’s what she tells herself as she slowly stands back up, the letter still clutched tightly between her fingers.

It’s for the better. She repeats it as she tucks the envelope inside her purple gown.

It’s for the better. She says it over and over, like a mantra, until the words stop sounding like words at all. Until they feel strange and foreign on her tongue. Until she almost believes them.

It’s for the better. She repeats it as she walks out of Agatha’s dorm for the last time, without looking back. Because if she does, she’s afraid she won’t ever leave.

It’s for the better. She repeats it while sitting in the stadium surrounded by hundreds of other graduates, all smiling and joyful as if this is the happiest day of their lives. She repeats it as families fill the stands above them, the students searching for familiar faces in the crowd. She repeats it while secretly searching too.

Just in case. Just in case Agatha miraculously changed her mind and is sitting up there too.

She’s not.

Rio bites down hard on her tongue as she walks across the stage, diploma trembling in her hand. The ceremony passes in a blur. She doesn’t even remember marching up there, doesn’t recall them reciting her name, can’t imagine the cheers of the crowd as she becomes a college graduate. Maybe her mother cheers too. Rio isn’t sure. Because all she can think about is Agatha, about the future they were supposed to have together.

She tells herself she’s making the right choice. The one she’s supposed to make. The one Agatha would want.

By the time she steps out of the stadium, the sun is low in the sky and the crowd of people swallows her whole. Her family rushes over to her, showering her with congratulations and hugs. She forces a smile, pretending she’s happy, pretending she’s proud of herself for what she’s accomplished like everyone is telling her to be.

But really, all she can focus on is the ache inside her chest, the tightness squeezing her lungs so hard she can barely breathe.

Because she can’t be leaving. If she’s leaving, it means it’s really over with Agatha.

And she knows it has been for a while now. But all of a sudden? It feels painfully real.

Because Agatha was supposed to be here with her- sitting in those stands beside her family, watching her walk across that stage, watching her take that diploma. Telling her how proud she is as she plants a delicate kiss on her cheek when it’s all over.

She was supposed to be here. But she’s not.

And Rio will never see her again.

The realization settles heavily into her stomach, making her feel physically sick.

It has to be for the better, she tells herself one last time. It has to be.

2026

Once Rio had stopped heave-crying, Agatha managed to convince her to get off the ground. It takes more effort than she’d like to admit. Rio clings onto Agatha’s arms for balance as her legs shake beneath her, jeans coated with streaks of dirt and grass. Her face is blotchy from crying, her eyes red and swollen. She looks so fragile that Agatha makes sure to move carefully.

Alice and Wanda stand a few feet away, faces drawn with concern. Wanda chews nervously on the inside of her cheek while Alice hovers awkwardly, seemingly unsure whether to try to help or give them space. Agatha’s gaze flicks over to them and she offers a quick, tight smile.

“Could you give us a minute?” She asks quietly, still holding Rio upright.

The pair nods in response and scurry away without argument, leaving Agatha and Rio alone. For a moment, neither of them move. Rio keeps her eyes fixed on the ground. Agatha guides her toward a nearby picnic table, one hand resting gently against her back. They drop down onto the wooden bench, shoulders just barely touching.

It’s strangely quiet, and Agatha glances over.

Rio’s head is bowed low as she picks at the skin around her fingernails, some of them already raw and pink. The sight makes Agatha’s heart constrict. She reaches out and brushes back the strand of hair hanging in front of Rio's face, delicately tucking it behind her ear so she can make out her eyes. Rio doesn’t pull away. Agatha’s chest tightens as she notices her cheeks are still wet with tears, streaks of mascara staining her skin. She brushes her thumb gently beneath one eye, then the other, wiping away the residue.

“What happened, Rio?” Agatha asks softly, her heart racing. She can’t let it show how terrified she is of the answer, of what Rio could possibly say about Nicky right now. Agatha swallows hard and pushes the thoughts away. Because this isn’t about her. Not this time.

Rio turns her head, finally tearing her gaze off the ground and meeting Agatha’s eyes. Her mouth opens just slightly, as if she’s about to say something, but closes just as fast.

Agatha’s brow furrows. “You can talk to me.” The words are barely above a whisper.

Agatha begs silently for her to say something. Anything. To finally open up. If she’s not ready to talk about the food, she hopes, at least, she’d maybe talk to her about Nicky.

Their son.

Rio takes a shaky breath, her eyes drifting away again. “Those flowers were for him,” she finally says, her voice breaking immediately. “For Nicky.” She pauses as Agatha’s breath catches. “I plant them every year on the…” Her eyes drift back to her itching fingers, “on the day we lost him.”

The last words barely make it out. Agatha’s air is knocked from her lungs, her heart constricting painfully as she thinks back to the perfectly planted flower bed. Well, used to be perfect. “You did that for him?” Agatha asks quietly, her voice almost inaudible.

Rio nods, just slightly.

“Rio, I…” Agatha swallows. “I had no idea.”

A bitter laugh escapes Rio’s mouth. “Why would you?” She still refuses to meet Agatha’s eyes. “It’s not like we were friends.”

The words sting, knowing they are painfully true. Agatha looks down at her hands now. “I know,” is all she can muster. Silence settles between them, the only thing tying them to the rest of the world being the sound of distant chatter. “I’m sorry.” The phrase sounds pathetic coming out. But what else is she supposed to say? It’s her fault. She’s the one who pushed Rio away.

Rio only shrugs, as if none of it matters anymore. As if that’s the last thing on her mind.

Agatha continues, her chest aching. “We can replant the flowers.” She says quickly. “We can fix this.” She places a soft hand over Rio’s, rubbing her thumb gently across her knuckles. “He’d understand.” Tears burn behind Agatha’s own eyes now, telling herself this just as much as she’s telling Rio.

Rio shakes her head. “No, he won’t.” Her voice cracks. “I let him down. Again.” A muffled sob escapes her mouth, her shoulders hunching inward as she curls further into herself, trying desperately to disappear. She looks so small. “I alwayslet everybody down.”

Agatha’s heart breaks. Rio drops her face into her hands as her body shakes with sobs. Her sound of her crying is quieter now, but somehow worse. Agatha slides closer and rests a hand against her back, rubbing slow circles between her shoulder blades. “That’s not true, Rio.”

“It is.” Rio drags in a shaky breath. “I let you down.” Agatha squeezes her eyes shut. “I couldn’t protect you. I couldn’t save Nicky, and I can’t even keep these stupid flowers safe.” The last sentence comes out as a broken sob.

She reaches for Rio without thinking, pulling her closer. “You didn’t let me down.” Rio doesn’t answer. “You didn’t. You did everything you could.” Rio shakes her head weakly. “I couldn’t see it at the time, but I do now.” Agatha squeezes her arm, takes a breath. “You could never let me down, Rio.”

And finally, she means it.

“Is she okay?” Wanda asks as Agatha approaches. Alice turns as well, worry etched across her face.

Agatha nods as she meets them on the park trail. “It took some convincing, but she’s on her way home to rest.” She tells them.

“I bet,” Wanda laughs, “she’s stubborn, that one.”

Agatha frowns immediately, shooting Wanda a look that the redhead is completely oblivious to. She’s correct, of course, but that doesn’t mean she has any right to say it. “Oh, give her a break.” Agatha shoots back, her tone coming across much more callous than intended.

Wanda’s eyes widen slightly. “I didn’t-”

Luckily, Jen and Billy call out from a few yards away before Wanda can finish defending herself. And before Agatha can say something she’ll inevitably regret.

They jog over, completely oblivious to the tension. “Hey guys!” Billy greets, much too chippy for Agatha’s liking.

She narrows her eyes at him. “Shouldn’t you be at work right now?”

“You’re not there.” He says flatly.

“And?”

“And I’m your assistant, so what would I even do?”

“Sit at your desk and do nothing. Like you do every other day anyway.” She pitches her voice higher, her tone laced with mockery.

Billy frowns, crossing his arms over his chest. “That was mean.”

“It was accurate.” Agatha mumbles, her face pinched.

Jen raises an eyebrow. “Okay…” Her gaze drifts between them. “Someone’s in a bad mood.” Agatha rolls her eyes and Jen gives her a look that very clearly says behave yourself before glancing around the group. “Where’s Rio?”

Agatha opens her mouth to answer, but Wanda beats her to it.

“She had a little bit of a meltdown, so she went home.”

The words are delivered sweetly enough, but they make Agatha’s blood boil anyway. Maybe Wanda doesn't mean anything by it, but the way she says it makes it sound like Rio threw a temper tantrum because somebody took away her favorite toy.

The smile on Wanda’s face is what finally tips her over the edge.

“I will choke you.” She glares, the words flat and dripping with distain. “I’m not even joking.” Wanda visibly startles, taking a baby step backwards. The reaction is somewhat satisfying.

“Agatha.” Jen snaps immediately. She’s got her hands planted firmly on her hips. “Let’s take a walk.” She flicks her head to the side, telling Agatha to come. She sighs dramatically but follows anyway. Once they’re finally out of earshot, Jen’s expression softens. “What’s going on with you?”

“Wanda.” Agatha answers immediately, the name leaving a sour taste in her mouth.

Jen shakes her head. “No, you’re taking it out on Wanda, she’s not your problem.” She gives her a knowing look. “So what is it really?”

Agatha groans. She hates how Jen always does this. How she can see right through her every goddamn time. “There’s just… tension.” She whispers finally.

“Between you and Rio?” Jen clarifies, though the answer is obvious.

Agatha nods. “It seems like every time things start to go right, the universe says ‘fuck you, Agatha!’ and ruins it all.”

Jen nods, understanding. “One step forward, three steps back.”

Agatha pauses mid-step, slowly turning to her with a puzzled expression. “Are you quoting Olivia Rodrigo at me right now?”

Jen looks at her astounded. “Girl, that’s an actual saying.” She stares, raising an eyebrow. “Did you really think Olivia invented it?”

“No.” Yes. She pauses, covering for herself. “Just seems like something you’d do is all.”

“Right…” Jen says, sounding deeply unconvinced as they continue walking.

Agatha kicks a pebble across the trail. “Maybe it’s karma finally catching up to me.” She thinks out loud. “Or my mother’s getting her priest to send evil spirits my way again.”

“Again?”

“Long story,” Agatha waves a hand, brushing the question off before adding, “and I’m worried about her.”

Jen glances over. “Why?”

“She just…” Agatha takes a breath, choosing her words carefully. “She doesn’t seem like she’s in a good place. Mentally.” She pauses. “And she won’t let me help her. And even if she did, I don’t think I’d know how.”

“Have you suggested she try therapy?”

Agatha snorts immediately, throwing her head back with a laugh. “Oh, as if that would ever go down well.”

Jen slows her pace once again, turning back to her, serious. “But if you’re really worried about her, isn’t it worth a shot?”

Agatha doesn’t answer right away, her smile faltering. She knows Jen is right. As always. She clears her throat, staring down at the trail. “I guess.” She mumbles.

“At least think about it.”

Agatha nods reluctantly before glancing back toward the others. They’re scattered around the park now, picking up fallen branches and hauling debris into trash bags. “We should probably actually help clean up this shithole.”

Jen groans dramatically at the thought of manual labor. “I genuinely don’t know why I keep agreeing to this.”

Agatha laughs. “Me neither.”

“Bitch, you’re legally obligated- ow!” She’s cut off by a sharp jab to the ribs, curtesy of Agatha’s elbow. “Rude,” Jen mutters, rubbing her side.

Agatha fights back a smile. “Better watch it,” she responds, before grabbing a trash bag and heading toward the others, letting her focus settle on the cleanup instead of the knot of worry twisting in her chest.

The cleanup. That’s something she can do to help Rio.

Wanda looks up as Agatha approaches, immediately taking a small step backwards, her face wary of Agatha’s movements.

Agatha fights the urge to roll her eyes. Knowing what she has to do, she clears her throat. “Wanda.” She blinks, clearing her throat again.

God, this is humiliating.

“I’m sorry.” The words come out stiff and awkward. “I shouldn’t have snapped at you.” She pauses. “Or threatened violence. I’m just having a stressful day.”

Wanda stares at her for a second before her pursed lips break into a much too wide smile. “Oh, that’s okay, Agatha! We’ve all been there!” Before Agatha can respond, Wanda takes a step forward, lifting her arms. Unfortunately, she doesn’t react fast enough because a second later she’s trapped in a tight hug that seems to put way too much pressure on her lungs. Agatha frowns, but manages to warp it into something resembling a smile by the time Wanda finally pulls away. “Friends again?” Wanda asks hopefully.

Agatha’s throat stifles a laugh. Since when the fuck were they ever friends? Acquaintances maybe, but friends feels like a stretch.

“Friends.” She concedes anyway with a stiff grin. Wanda beams. Agatha swears she feels her eye twitch.

Once the ordeal is finally over, which Agatha is fairly certain went on for literal hours rather than two minutes, she grabs a trash bag and immediately gets to work collecting debris.

She focuses on the task in front of her, gathering broken branches and scattered leaves, letting her hands stay busy.

And for the first time all day, for a brief few moments, her thoughts quiet. And that’s enough.

“Good work today guys!” Wanda calls out, waving goodbye as she heads to her car. The sun is just starting to set, painting the sky in shades of pink, and Agatha is exhausted. Despite the cold temperatures, sweat clings to her forehead, making strands of hair stick awkwardly to her face as she tries to peel them away.

She trails after Alice, dragging her feet slightly as she crosses the parking lot. The second she climbs into the passenger seat, she lets out a heavy sigh as the pressure is finally taken off her feet.

“Thank you for giving me a ride.” Agatha says as Alice pulls out of the parking lot and onto the busy, rush-hour filled streets.

“No problem,” Alice glances over briefly, “you’ll have to remind me where your house is though.”

“Oh- I-” Agatha stammers, turning to look over at her. “I’m actually staying at Rio’s.”

Alice raises an eyebrow. “You are?” She squints slightly, her voice laced with undertones of intrigue.

“Only for a couple of days,” she says quickly, clearing her throat. “Until I can move into my apartment.”

“Right.” Alice nods. Agatha turns away before she can say anything else, choosing instead to focus on the line of stopped cars outside the window. Silence stretches for a moment until Alice adds, “I’m glad you two are getting along again.”

Agatha's shoulders tense and she doesn't answer. Mostly because she doesn't know how. Getting along again feels like an oversimplification of whatever the hell is happening between them lately.

Alice continues anyway.  “She really likes you, y’know?”

That gets Agatha’s attention.

She whips her head back around, heat rising in her cheeks as she stifles a smile.

“She said that?” Her voice comes out quieter than intended, and much more hopeful too.

Alice smiles. “She doesn’t have to.”

Agatha frowns. “What’s that supposed to mean?”

“I can just tell.” Alice responds, glancing over at her.

Agatha looks away again immediately, because she’s pretty sure she’s bright pink right about now, and she can’t let Alice see how giddy she is over four measly words.

“I mean,” Alice continues casually, “you should see the way she lights up when she talks about you. It’s like a little kid on Christmas morning.”

Agatha bites down on the inside of her lip, her gaze falling down onto her lap, at her hands folded neatly together. It takes everything in her to not let her mouth break into the widest grin right now.

As she watches the city pass by outside the window, everything feels a little different somehow- a little brighter, softer.

Like maybe the world isn’t completely determined to make her miserable. Like maybe, despite what it seems, not all hope is lost.

When they finally get out of the city traffic, it’s smooth sailing, and they pull up outside Rio’s home before Agatha can really register that they’re here. She pushes open the door and thanks Alice again as she climbs out onto the sidewalk.

Despite the ache in her legs and the lingering exhaustion from spending the afternoon hauling branches around a park, there’s a pep in her step as she heads toward the front door. Alice’s words replay in her head.

She really likes you, y’know?

Agatha bites back another smile.

God, she’s pathetic.

She raises her fist and knocks on the door, rocking back on her heels as she waits. A smile tugs at the corner of her lips. She hears shuffling from the other side, followed by the familiar click of a lock.

Then the door swings open and the smile immediately falls from her face. Rio stands on the other side, looking awful. Her face is pale and tired, but the kind of tired that goes beyond needing a good night’s sleep. There are dark circles beneath her eyes and a large glass of wine clutched in her hand. It’s the Band-Aid plastered across her forehead that really catches Agatha’s attention.

Rio doesn’t say a word as she steps aside, allowing Agatha in before shutting the door behind her. Agatha doesn’t even make it two steps into the house before speaking.

“What did you do?”

Rio rolls her eyes instantly. “Hello to you to.” She brushes past Agatha and heads toward the kitchen, downing the rest of her wine in one swallow. Agatha follows, watching closely as Rio grabs another bottle from the fridge and tops off her glass, keeping her hands busy and her eyes fixed anywhere but Agatha.

“Rio.” Her voice comes out serious.

Rio slams her glass down onto the counter. “What?” The word echoes through the room. She finally looks up, both palms braced firmly against the countertop.

Agatha actually jumps slightly at the sharpness of her tone. She clears her throat, attempting to regain her composure. “You’re hurt.” She nods toward Rio’s forehead. “How?”

Rio scoffs. “I took a fall. It’s not a big deal.”

Agatha raises an eyebrow. “So, you tripped?”

Rio’s gaze immediately falls back down to her wine glass. She doesn’t respond.

“Did you faint?”

There’s more silence, but Agatha catches the flash of panic that crosses Rio’s face.

There’s her answer.

Agatha takes a step closer.

“Don’t-” Rio puts a hand between the,. “Don’t say anything.”

Agatha tilts her head, eyes wide and unblinking, her stomach twisting with uneasiness and an underlying sense of guilt.

Rio looks over at her, sighing. “Don’t look at me like that.”

“Like what?”

Rio lets out a bitter laugh. “Like I’m some injured puppy in need of saving.” Her grip tightens around her wine glass. “I don’t need help.”

“I didn’t say that.” Agatha keeps her voice a steady whisper.

“But you were thinking it.” Rio spits back, finally looking up. It’s a statement, not a question. “It’s written all over your face.”

There’s a pause. A brief period of tense silence filled only by the hum of the refrigerator, a clock ticking in the room over, and the loud pounding of Agatha’s heart as she gathers the courage to muster her next words. She knows where this conversation is headed. And she knows Rio isn’t going to like it.

“Rio, I think you should talk to someone.” Her voice wavers slightly as Rio stiffens. “A professional.”

The words are barely out of Agatha’s mouth before Rio lets out a short laugh, throwing her head back with a grin. Somehow it’s scarier than her yelling.

“You want me to see a fucking shrink?” Her smile is wide in disbelief. “You think I’m fucking crazy? Is that what this is?”

“No one said you were crazy,” Agatha takes a slow breath. “I just think you would benefit from-”

“Why don’t you just send me to the fucking mental hospital?” Rio’s voice raises now. “At least that way I’ll be somebody else’s problem, and you can finally get a goddamn break, right?”

Agatha’s stomach drops. Does she really think she’s a burden?

“Rio…”

“There’s nothing wrong with me.”

“Rio.” Her voice comes out firmer this time, her heart racing as she attempts to get even a word in.

“There’s nothing wrong with me!”

Agatha steps forward. Rio immediately throws her hand up again, not allowing Agatha any closer. “I’m fine.”

Agatha’s chest aches, not believing those words, and knowing deep down, Rio doesn’t either. Before she can stop herself, Agatha reaches out and grabs Rio’s wrist, pulling her hand back down. Rio freezes and Agatha forces her to look at her, her gaze serious.

“Rio.” Her voice softens. “I need you to listen to me.” For once, Rio doesn’t interrupt, she just stares back quietly. Agatha can see the emotions flashing across her face- anger, defensiveness, terror- she notices each and every one of them. “Rio, you don’t eat.” Agatha swallows. “And when you do, you throw it up.” Rio’s jaw clenches. “You fucking fainted today, and thank god it was only a little bump to the head because who knows how badly you could’ve gotten hurt.” Rio looks away and Agatha tightens her grip slightly on her wrist. “And now what? You’re sitting here getting drunk alone on a Monday night?”

Neither of them speak. The room is filled with silence until Rio gulps.

Agatha continues, her voice dropping to a whisper. “As much as you say the words, I know you’re not fine.” She takes a deep breath. “And you know it too.” She watches something flicker across Rio’s face, and for a split second, Agatha thinks she might actually get through to her. “You don’t have to talk to me if you don’t want to.” Her eyes burn with tears. “Honestly, I get why you wouldn’t. But you need to talk to someone.”

Rio is completely silent for a second, taking heavy breaths through her nose as her gaze is fixed on the floor. Then she tears her wrist away. “Fuck you, Agatha.” The words come out quiet. Rio storms past her, shoulder colliding hard enough with Agatha’s to make her stumble.

A second later, she’s gone, the sound of footsteps fading up the staircase followed by the slam of a bedroom door.

The kitchen fills with silence as Agatha is left alone, eyes unfocused on a spot in front of her, tears threatening to fall.

She lets out a heavy, exhausted sigh and reaches for Rio’s abandoned wine glass, taking a long swig. She sets it back down and leans against the counter, pressing the heels of her palms into her eyes, chest feeling tight.

Well, that went fucking great.
